# Deviations from the reference server

The reference (EVE.js) is matched by default (`docs/design/adr/0001-external-compatibility-only.md`).
A deliberate difference goes here, with the evidence that the client cannot tell or that it
is better off, and how it is tested. A behaviour is either:

- **contract**: the client can observe it; eve.rs matches the reference;
- **tolerated**: the client can observe it but accepts a better behaviour; eve.rs may differ,
  and the difference is listed below;
- **internal**: the client cannot observe it; eve.rs is free.

## Project status

Summary of this file, as of 2026-10-13. The task list with the same areas is the "Project status" table at the top of `docs/project/TODO.md`. **Status** is the state of the area; **Project rule** is the rule a deviation stands on; **Cut** lists what the owner or the evidence has cut, with the entry that records it. A cut item without an entry here or in its section is a bug in this file.

| Area | Status | Project rule | Cut (entry) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Login, characters, sessions (Register 1 to 22) | Tolerated deviations, each tested. | Client-observable behaviour matches the reference; a better behaviour is allowed only where the client cannot tell (contract, tolerated, internal). | Slot and name rules kept as the server's; the staff-role default and the reference's branding text are not copied (Register 7, 8). |
| Space: undock, flight, warp, jump, probes (Register 15 to 28, 53 to 55) | Tolerated; revisit with combat and NPCs. | The client moves its own balls from the commands it is sent; the server's copy decides only what the client can see. | Ambient ships and sentry guns of the reference are not spawned (Register 23); the props are not sent (Register 16). |
| Modules, weapons, reloads, drones (Register 32 to 34, 56 to 58; sections "Module rules", "Launchers", "Command bursts") | Tolerated; open rows are listed in the TODO sections. | Numbers from the SDE dogma; rules from CCP or the wiki, cited in the section. | Missile launchers and the local-cycle modules do not reload by themselves (Register 57); the reference's loadouts and missile fire are not planned (Register 52). |
| NPCs and their AI (Register 42 to 54) | Tolerated; the behaviour trees are eve.rs's own. | The reference's NPC loop is not a tree; the trees are the client's parts. | The reference's behaviour profiles are not used (Register 53); the generated NPC catalogues are not part of the authored data (Register 52). |
| Mining, tractor, bombs, bubbles (sections "Mining tracks", "Tractor beam", "Bombs, bubbles and warp disruption") | Built; graphical-client checks open. | As above. | Entosis link and structure-only modules wait for structures. |
| Fighters, scanners, breacher pods (sections "Player fighters", "Scanner modules", "Breacher pods") | Built; the graphical client has not run them. | As above. | Fighter abilities are built from the squadron type's own dogma, a change from the reference (section "Player fighters"). |
| Structures (sections "Structure ..." and ST-0 to ST-11) | Partly built; owner decision 8 limits new structure work. | Structure rules as cited in each section. | Anchoring, control, deliveries and structure compression (open in TODO section 43 to 48). |
| Market (sections "Market slice M1" to "M9") | Built and live-walked (M9). | The 7.5% sales tax on the server; rules replayed from the decided fixture. | The client's own tax flag cannot be served (M2). |
| Agents and LP (sections "Agents slice AG-1" to "AG-5") | Partly built; LP store offers and research rates have no source in build 3396210. | Sources cited per slice (ESI and the EVE University wiki). | CONCORD LP exchange refused (no CCP source); research rates and datacore price (no source read). |
| Missions (sections "Mission static data", "Epic arc availability", "Mission execution X1" to "X16") | Built; arc 29 is not flipped on. | Owner decision 2: arcs with generic kill sites are held back unless `EVE_MISSION_INCLUDE_GENERIC_ARCS=1`. Owner decision 3: 51 mission level tags are rule-derived (provenance `rule`). Owner decision 1: an in-space agent is placed by rule (no coordinates in the SDE). | Courier and fetch objectives of the 1,130 non-arc courier missions (Mission execution X11, X13b); bonus window and LP of kill missions (Mission execution X1); name-based level tags (X12). |

| Zarzakh and Pochven (sections "Zarzakh ...", "Pochven ...") | Partly built. | Client-only system data from the embedded client export; no invented values. | Gate guns without published numbers; heat, tidal forces and Point of No Return; landmarks and the Black Monolith look-at; gate sentries that shoot; the Deathless Shipcaster jump; bounty and loot of the Pochven hulls. |

Verification run (2026-10-13), the same merged tree and database as the TODO verification: the Rust workspace has 1479 passing tests and no failures; the .NET suite has no failing project in its last run (the Host, Services.Space, Market, Core, Dogma, Inventory, Space and Planets projects were rerun on the merged tree, and the others were not rerun after the final merge).

## Register

| # | Behaviour | Reference does | eve.rs does | Evidence | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Password check at login | Accepts any password by default (`devSkipPasswordValidation` is true) and creates an account on first use | Checks the password hash by default; an unknown user name still creates the account with the hash it presented; `EVE_DEV_SKIP_PASSWORD=1` restores the reference | The client always sends the hash of the password typed, so only a wrong password can tell the two apart (it is refused, as a real server does) | tolerated (S1); `tools/dev/run-eve-live.sh` runs the wrong-password test |
| 2 | Session ids | `ms * 15 + clientID % 15`, which can repeat within a millisecond | The same value, moved up past the last one issued | The client reads `sessionID` as an opaque long; a repeat replaced a live session in the front (found by a test) | internal (S1) |
| 3 | Character slots | The screen says 3 (`maxCharacterSlots`) but nothing stops an account making more: the recording has four on one account | Refuses a character beyond the three, with a `CustomInfo` message; two creations racing for the last slot cannot both win | The limit is stated to the client in the selection data, so a server that keeps it is what the screen promises | tolerated (S2); marked `DEVIATION (slots)` in `tests/test_login_to_character.py` |
| 4 | Reserved names | Reserves the staff prefixes (`CCP `, `GM `, `ISD `) and also "Elysian", the name of its own system character | Reserves the staff prefixes only | Nothing in the client refers to "Elysian"; a player may want it | tolerated (S2); the `Elysian` case of the name test |
| 5 | When two names are one | Names that differ by case or spacing are one name (`recorded pilot` is taken by `Recorded Pilot`) | The same, and names that differ only in Unicode compatibility form (full-width letters, combining marks) are one too (NFKC) | The client shows both spellings alike, so allowing both makes look-alike names | tolerated (S2); `dotnet/tests/Eve.Characters.Tests/NameTests.cs` |
| 6 | Account creation date on the selection screen | A constant (the year 2019) | The time the account was created | The client displays it as the account's age; a real date is what the field means | tolerated (S2) |
| 8 | A character's description | New characters have the text "Character created via EveJS Elysian", the reference's own branding | An empty description | The client shows the description in the character sheet; text that names another project is not something a player wrote | tolerated (S3); the `description` of the public record and `GetCharacterDescription` in `tests/test_login_to_character.py` |
| 9 | Asking about a character or station that does not exist | A made-up id gets a made-up record: `charMgr.GetPublicInfo(1)` returns the name of the last character chosen, `stationSvc.GetStation(1)` returns Jita IV | `None`, as for any call the server cannot answer | The made-up record is a side effect of the reference filling every field with defaults; a real server has no such answer, and the client asks about ids it has been given | tolerated (S3); `dotnet/tests/Eve.Services.CharacterInfo.Tests`, `Eve.Universe.Tests` |
| 10 | What is in the inventory of a ship | `GetContainerContents` of a ship's inventory lists the ship itself (the contents of the hangar it is in), as the reference answers it from the station's list and not the ship's | It lists what is in the ship: the fitted modules and what is carried (a new ship: its three starter modules) | A ship does not contain itself; the client reads a ship's contents by `List(flag)` and `GetContainerContents(itemID, station)` of the inventory manager (`containerContentWindow`, `panelFitting`), never by this call on the ship's own bound inventory | tolerated; `tests/test_login_to_character.py` compares the shape of the rowset and counts the three lines. The rest of what a ship's inventory answers (`List` as a set of rows without custom info when asked for everything, an empty set for a flag with nothing in it, `ListByFlags`, `GetCapacity`) is the reference's, recorded in `inventory.json` and `cargo.json` |
| 11 | The character's attributes "as the ship changes them" (`GetAllInfo.shipModifiedCharAttribs`) | Trade premium (443) is 0 for a character that has Trade trained (its own brain says -10% of 1), and the Gallente rookie ship's mining-drone bonus (428) is left out (100) | Every modifier that reaches the character is applied: 443 is 0.9 with Trade at level 2, and 428 is 120 for the Gallente rookie ship | The client's own rules (`dogma/attributes`, the effects of the static data) say so, and the reference's brain and its attribute contradict each other; the numbers are read for market fees and mining-drone yield only | tolerated (S3); the two values are asserted in `ReferenceTests` and replaced in the expectation of `test_a_new_characters_dogma_is_that_of_the_reference` |
| 12 | Drone limits on the ship | A ship's attributes list the character's drone limits: `maxActiveDrones` (352) and `droneControlDistance` (458) | Only the character has them | The client reads both from the character (`GetAttributeValue(session.charid, ...)`, `clientDogmaLocation.py:1436`, `ghostControllerMixin.py:509`); the ship's copy is never asked for | tolerated (S3); replaced in the expectation of `test_a_new_characters_dogma_is_that_of_the_reference` |
| 13 | Starting a skill by its item (`CharStartTrainingSkill(itemID)`) | Looks the item up in its item store, which has no skill items, so it answers `None` and trains nothing | Starts the skill: the item id of a character's skill is its id times 100000 plus the type (the id the client was given in the skill's entry), so it names the skill; an item that is not one of the character's skills starts nothing | The client passes the id of the skill entry it holds; a server that ignores it is a bug, not behaviour (the queue call the current client uses, `SaveNewQueue`, works on both) | tolerated (S3); `Starting_a_skill_by_its_type_or_by_its_item_runs_it_and_anything_else_starts_nothing` in `Eve.Services.Skills.Tests`, marked DEVIATION in `test_the_skill_queue_is_what_the_reference_does` |
| 14 | Which skill shows as training | The first skill of the queue is marked `inTraining` in the skill list even when the queue is paused (after `AbortTraining`, or saved with `activate=False`) | Only a skill that is training is marked; a paused queue marks none | The client shows the flag as "in training" with a running timer; a skill that is not gaining points is not training | tolerated (S3); marked DEVIATION in `view_problems` of `tests/test_login_to_character.py` |
| 15 | Where a ship appears when it undocks | The first of the authored undock points of the station's type that suits the ship's size (`stationGraphicLocators`, taken from the client's graphics; 44 station types), on the station model, facing along the point's direction (rotated by the station's orientation) | The same: `crates/eve-space/data/undock_points.json` is that table (`tools/dev/extract-undock-points.py`), the point is the first of the ship group's size (else a generic one, else the first), turned by the station's authored orientation (two stations); a type the table does not know puts the ship a third of the station's radius out along that orientation or away from the star | The recorded ship's place and heading agree with ours: it stands on the line out of our undock point along the direction both face, to within a metre (`the_ship_comes_out_where_the_reference_put_its_ship`); the reference's ship had flown for about a second when the bind first showed it | resolved (S4e): kept for the record; the fallback for an unknown station type is tolerated |
| 16 | What the system's ballpark holds | Customs offices (ids from 1.2e12) and, one stamp after the state, eight "props" per station (type 3739 to 3741, group 99) that the reference's own data adds | The stations, planets, moons, belts, the star and the stargates of the static data, and the ship | Neither is in the static data; the client draws the bodies it is sent and needs none of them to fly, dock or jump | tolerated (S4); the comparison in `payloads_match_reference.rs` leaves out ids from 1e12 and the props' second `AddBalls2` |
| 17 | How a ship turns | The heading turns in steps of the reference's own turn rule (fast for small ships, slower the more agile the hull is not), while speed follows the carbon integrator | The heading is the one asked for at once; speed follows the carbon integrator as in the reference | The client turns and moves the ball itself from the actions it is sent, and the server's copy is only used to answer `UpdateStateRequest` and to decide whether a ship is close enough to dock; the reference's turn rule is its own approximation, the client's rule is in the native destiny code that cannot be read | tolerated (S4); revisit with warp and combat, where the heading matters to the server (S5) |
| 18 | The bound objects announced at a docking | At an undock and again at a docking it tells the client to let go of every object bound to the session (`OnMachoObjectDisconnect`), the ones it announced before too | Announces each object once: at the undock those of the station, at the docking those bound in space since (`beyonce`) | The client drops an object when told and rebinds when it needs it; a second notice for an object it has dropped changes nothing | tolerated (S4); `collapse` in `tests/test_undock.py` counts the notices as one run |
| 19 | How `beyonce.GetFormations` is cached | Hands out a cache object (`CachedObject`, a pickle of the formations under the proxy's node) that the client fetches from `objectCaching` when it needs it | Answers the formations inline in the `CachedMethodCallResult`, as the reference does for a result it does not cache at the proxy | The client keeps either under the same call for the length of the session; the fetch needs the pickle and the CRC of the client's own Python, which is not worth reproducing for two fixed formations | tolerated (S4); `compare_answer` in `tests/test_undock.py` |
| 20 | How a ship comes to rest at what it follows | It follows in the destiny code's own way: the recorded ship passed the docking range at 54 s (1.9 km off the surface), drifted out to 2.8 km and settled at about 2.47 km after 95 s, in a damped swing about the range it was given (2 500 m) | It eases in to a point 0.1 % inside the range without a swing (its speed asked for falls with its distance to the point, over three times its reaction time), so a ship told to follow at the docking range is within it; orbits keep their radius by drawing the ship in or out along the radius | The follow and orbit laws are in the client's native destiny code, which cannot be read, and the client moves the ball itself from `FollowBall` and `Orbit`; the server's copy of the motion only decides whether the ship is near enough to dock, and a swing that goes in and out of the range is no use to that | tolerated (S4); revisit with combat and NPCs, where the server has to know where a following ship is (S5) |
| 21 | What is taken out of the ballpark while a ship is in warp | Sends `RemoveBalls` (in a `PackagedAction`, some seconds into the warp) for the eight props of each station and the customs offices, which it added to the ballpark (#16) | Nothing: it never added them | Follows from #16; the ball the client keeps or drops does not matter to a ship that has left the station behind | tolerated (S4); the replay leaves the update out on both sides (`tests/test_warp.py`) |
| 22 | How a ship lines up for warp | Checks once a tick whether the ship's velocity points at the destination (within 0.01 of the cosine) at more than three quarters of its top speed, the heading turning by its own rule (#17), then works the warp out from where it is | The same checks once a second from the command, the velocity turning under the carbon integrator with the thrust along the wanted heading at once; the warp itself (accel, cruise, decel, dropout at 100 m/s or half the top speed, the tail to rest at 0.01 m/s) is the destiny kernel's model, as the reference's | The client aligns and warps by itself from `WarpTo`; the server's copy tells when the ship has dropped out and is at rest. The recorded ship dropped out at 38.0 s and stopped at 82.6 s after the command; ours at 38.0 s and 83 s (`a_warp_drops_out_and_comes_to_rest_when_the_reference_ship_did`, `tests/test_warp.py`) | tolerated (S4); revisit with combat, where the server has to know where a ship in warp is (S5) |
| 7 | A session's `role` | Every account gets the staff roles and a chat colour by default (a development setting), on top of a base mask | The account's role from the identity module (0 for an ordinary player) with the same base mask `0x6000000080000000`; the initial state carries the account's role, the session change on choosing a character carries the combination | The client uses the bits to show staff tools; a normal account should not have them | tolerated (S2); `role` is compared by type only in the differential test |

| 23 | Ambient ships, gate guns and the like in a system's ballpark | Adds ships of its own (ids from 98005 00000 100, groups 301 and 182, with modules), sentry guns near the gates (group 99, ids from 8.4e15) and, for the ship that arrives, puts them in the first `AddBalls2` of its bind and their numbers in the update that follows | None: a system holds the static data's bodies and the ships of players; the first `AddBalls2` of a ship that jumped holds the ship alone and the numbers update the ship's own | They come from the reference's NPC code, not from the static data, and nothing here can see, target or be hurt by them yet; the client draws the balls it is sent | tolerated until NPCs (S5); the replay leaves ambient ids (98005…) out of `SetState`, takes the ship's own slim item from the reference's `AddBalls2` and the ship's own numbers from its update (`tests/test_jump.py`) |
| 24 | What is sent around a jump besides the essentials | Sends the ball's position again 0.5 s after the jump-out effect (`SetBallPosition`, `SetBallVelocity`, `SetSpeedFraction`, `Stop`); a few seconds after the ship binds the new system it sends one more `AddBalls2` and a last position update; the ship arrives cloaked (the jump cloak is in its ball) for 60 s, its end (a movement or the time) told with `OnJumpCloakCancelled` and `OnInvulnCancelled` | Sends the essentials in the reference's order (the effect and stop in one update, `OnJumpQueueUpdate`, the objects let go, `OnItemChange` with the ship at the new system, `OnJumpCloakUpdated`, the session change) and, on a movement or after 60 s, the two notices that end the cloak; not the repeated position updates; the ball is not cloaked | The position updates repeat what the client has (the ship stood still, and arrives where it was told); the cloak matters when other ships can see the ship, which none can yet | tolerated (S4); revisit with other ships in a system and the cloak (S5); the replay leaves the 0.5 s update out on both sides |
| 25 | Where a ship lands by a gate and where it appears after a jump | Draws both with the clock's random numbers: a warp to a gate ends at a random point of a half sphere (2.5 km) on the gate's sphere on the side the ship came from, and the ship that jumps appears in a 45° cone in front of the gate at its radius, the ship's radius and 12 km | The same rules with draws of its own, seeded by the ship, the gate and the moment, so the same calls give the same points | The client works out where its ball goes from the aim it is sent, and the points are only bounded, never fixed: the recorded ship stopped 5.4 km from the gate's centre (2.5 km from its surface) 28° off the axis of the gate it appeared at, which is inside our bounds (`the_recorded_ship_came_to_rest_by_the_gate…`, `the_recorded_ship_appeared_in_the_cone…`) | tolerated (S4) |
| 26 | When a ship that jumps is in the other system, and what a wrong gate says | The later of four seconds after the command and three after the effect's stamp (the recorded ship: 4.87 s); a gate that is not in the ship's system is `WRONG_SOLAR_SYSTEM` (the message "denied, ship changed") | The same rule from the stamp our scene gave the effect (which usually is the moment's own, so four seconds: the recorded jump is within a second of it); a gate that is not in the system is `STARGATE_NOT_FOUND` ("targeting attempt cancelled") because the simulation only knows its own system | The stamp the reference gives the effect runs one or two seconds ahead of the clock, ours the clock; the two messages are both shown for a gate the pilot cannot use | tolerated (S4); `tests/test_jump.py` allows 1.6 s |

| 27 | When a ship is in another's view | Streams what is near a pilot in boxes of 7 864 320 m (its public grid, with 250 km bubbles inside as the unit of ownership), keeps a ship that warps in view until it is 50 000 km away and takes it out at the first one-second sample beyond that; ships come into view one stamp apart for each pilot (a stamp behind, some updates), and a ship that goes out of view is taken out in a `PackagedAction` | Ships are in each other's view when they are in the same box of 7 864 320 m, or when one is in warp and they are within 50 000 km; the sample is taken at each poll (a quarter of a second) and one stamp serves everyone; a ship that goes out of view by warp is taken out in a `PackagedAction` too, one that docks, leaves or jumps in a plain `RemoveBalls` | The recorded watcher was told a ship that had warped off was gone 7 seconds into its warp (the ship, going 50 000 km in that time); a box is what the reference's constants say and no recording shows a ship moving between boxes without warp, which takes hours | tolerated (S4); revisit with NPCs, where the bubbles decide who is shot at (S5) |
| 28 | What the ones who watch a ship are told of its warp | At the order the ship's heading (`GotoDirection`); when it enters warp (about 9 s later for the recorded ship) `OnSpecialFX`, `SetSpeedFraction` and `WarpTo`, in that order; nothing at the drop-out or the rest is recorded | The same two moments; the drop-out (`SetBallMassive` twice) and the rest (`SetBallPosition`, `SetBallVelocity`, `SetSpeedFraction`, `Stop`) are told to the watchers as they are to the pilot, which is a guess: the recorded warp took the ship out of view long before it dropped out | tolerated (S4); to be recorded with a short warp inside a watcher's box |
| 29 | A lock that cannot be had while it is going | When the target leaves, gets out of range or either ship warps while a lock is going, the pilot is sent the service notification `target.FailLockTarget(targetID)` (it closes the client's request), and the lock is dropped | The lock is dropped and nothing is sent: the host carries no notifications to a client-side service yet, so the client's request runs until it asks again. A lock that was held is let go of and told as in the reference (`OnTarget lost`, `otherlost`, `clear`) | from the reference's code (`space/runtime/scene/targeting.js`); the held case is recorded (`oracle/fixtures/scenarios/target.json`, the warp with a lock) | tolerated (S5a); to be recorded and done with the service notifications |
| 30 | When a lock is had | The tick that finds the time up (a tenth of a second) | The poll that finds it up (four a second): up to a quarter of a second later; the time itself is the reference's formula (`40 000 000 / scan resolution / asinh(signature radius)²` ms, at most 180 s) with the ship's numbers from the dogma engine | `tests/test_target.py` compares the moments within a second on both servers (the recording: had 3.79 s after it was asked for, for a ship of 52 m locked from 522.5 mm, where the formula says 3.55 s; 0.76 s for the station) | tolerated |
| 31 | What a ship that leaves is told of its locks | Not recorded: what a ship is sent when it docks or jumps with locks held | Its own client is told nothing (it is leaving the space session); the ships that had it locked are told `OnTarget lost` before the removal, and the ones it had locked `otherlost` | the ships that had it locked: recorded (`target.json`, the watcher docks) | to be recorded |
| 32 | What is told when a module's effect starts and stops | Besides the values of the module and of the ship, `OnModuleAttributeChanges` tells the range (attribute 54) of a module that has nothing to do with the effect (the miner's), at every start and stop | Tells the module's speed factor, capacitor need and cycle, and the ship's mass, top speed and signature, as recorded; not the other module's range | `oracle/fixtures/scenarios/module.json`; the value the client holds is already what it is told | tolerated (S5b) |
| 33 | Which effects run | Every effect of an online module, and a name that is not one of the module's starts its default effect (a call for `targetAttack` on the afterburner started the afterburner); a weapon at a player answers `SafetyActivated` (the safety switch), a miner at a ship `DeniedActivateTargetNotPresent` | Only the propulsion effects (`moduleBonusAfterburner`, `moduleBonusMicrowarpdrive`) and the turrets (`targetAttack` of a module with the effect `turretFitted` that needs no charge: see #56) run; any other effect of a module that needs no target, and one with a target, answer "<module> cannot be activated."; a name that is not the module's effect is refused the same way; a module that needs a target and is given none is refused as in the reference ("You need an active target to activate that module."); a weapon at the ship of another pilot in empire space answers `SafetyActivated` as the reference does (`WeaponSafety`) | `module.json` (the refusal of the miner is the reference's own; `tests/test_module.py` compares it as an error only, and the refusal of the gun is compared in full), `weapon.json` | tolerated until repairers, mining and the turrets that take a charge run (S5c, COMBAT-1) |
| 34 | How the capacitor's charge is told | Every half second, from a tick a tenth of a second after the change, with the value at that tick and the value a tick before, while the charge is below the capacity less a gigajoule (then it is full) | The same times and values on the simulation's clock, sent with the poll (a quarter of a second): the values follow the same curve (`C1/Cmax = (1 + (sqrt(C0/Cmax) - 1) e^(-5 dt/T))^2`), the moments can be up to a quarter of a second later | `tests/test_module.py` compares the charge of every step within half a gigajoule and the number of times it was told within two | tolerated |
| 35 | The shield and the capacitor across an undock and a dock | A ship leaves its station with its shield and capacitor full, whatever `/dmg` left in the hangar, and with the armor and hull it had (`health.json`: the damage state in the stream is `((1.0, tau, time), 0.4, 0.65)` after a heavy `/dmg`); what a ship has when it docks is not recorded (the item keeps a condition state, and the reference persists the entity's on damage, healing and shield ticks) | The same on the way out; a ship that docks keeps what the simulation says it has (shield, capacitor, armor and hull), and a ship that jumps keeps it in the simulation | `tests/test_health.py` compares the damage state given for the undocked ship and the numbers of `GetAllInfo` in the hangar and in space | undock: closed; dock: not recorded |
| 36 | The `slash` service | `SlashCmd` runs any of the chat commands (`/help`, `/giveitem`, `/npc`, `/dmg`, `/heal`, …) and answers with the message; the bare `/` answers with the list of commands as a user error | Only `/dmg` and `/heal`; any other command is answered `Unknown command: <text>. Use /help.` (a message, not an error), `/help` and the bare `/` are not special, `ReportSlashCommandUsage` is answered `None`; the chat feedback the reference also sends over the chat channel (its system message) is not sent | A GM command is a development tool; the client shows the returned message | tolerated (grows with the commands the modules need) |
| 37 | How the shield's charge is told | Every half second from the tick after a hit, the value at that tick and the value a tick before (the stored fraction, rounded to six places at a hit, is what it goes on from), until the shield is full; the tick that fills the shield is told when it is a telling tick | The same values and moments on the simulation's clock, sent with the poll (a quarter of a second): up to a quarter of a second later | The values follow the same curve as the capacitor's; unit tests pin four recorded values (`201.865819`, `201.917433`, … within half a thousandth: the reference's ticks are not exactly a tenth of a second apart); a live comparison needs damage in space, which only the reference's NPCs do | tolerated |

## Candidates to examine

Each is matched to the reference today. None is decided; the evidence needed is in the last column.

| Behaviour | Why it may not need to match | Evidence to collect |
|---|---|---|
| A handler that throws is answered with a `None` result, not an error | A `None` where the client expects data is probably worse than an error the client can report | What the client does on each service's `None` and on an error response |
| A service call may reach any method of the JS class by name | Only the methods the client calls matter | `docs/reference/spec/services.tsv` against the reference's method set |
| A received `str` is echoed as `buffer` (`JsSemantics.AsReceived`) | The Python 2 client's `str` is bytes, so it may not tell the two apart | The client's marshal reader for opcodes `str` and `buffer` |
| JavaScript number and truthiness rules in ported handlers (`parseInt`, `\|\|`) | They are accidents of the language unless the client feeds the value | Which inputs the client can send |
| `charMgr.GetPublicInfo`'s `empireID` is taken from a table of four corporations (a Caldari school's pupils are shown under the Gallente empire, 500004) and is the faction for every other corporation | The table looks like a mistake; the client reads `empireID` only to pick an empire icon (`RACE_ICONS[empireID]`) | Whether the character sheet gets `empireID` from this record, and which icon a Caldari character should have |
| A station's `serviceMask` and `reprocessingEfficiency` are the same for every station on the reference | The data has the services and the efficiency of each operation; a constant may show services a station does not have | Which of the two the client's station window and reprocessing window rely on |
| The points of a starting skill at level II and IV are rounded (1,414 and 45,254 for rank 1), while the training queue rounds up (1,415 and 45,255) | A skill trained from level I to II ends with 1,415 points while a starting skill of the same level has 1,414; the game rounds up | Whether the client compares a skill's points with its level thresholds (`skillPoints >= threshold`) anywhere it would show a starting skill as a point short |
| Session and cache objects are sent in the reference's order | The client may not depend on the order | A run of the client with the order changed |
| One JSON document per legacy table, with a synchronous cache-first API | It is storage and internal structure | Nothing observable; decided by the design of each domain module |

## Owner-directed economic changes (2026-10-01)

| # | Behaviour | Reference does | eve.rs does | Evidence | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 100 | Credit capacity and corporate distributions/liquidation | Corporate primitives clip an over-cap credit; a full debit or zeroed corporation wallet can therefore lose value | Rejects the complete operation before writing; every recipient and division shares a PostgreSQL transaction | Owner's explicit reliability direction; clipping examples reproduced as regression tests, with a clipping mutation caught | owner-requested; domain verified, native-client acceptance pending |
| 101 | ISK balance ceiling | Wallet ceiling is 10000000000000 | Exact decimal ceiling 1145141919364364, enforced in APIs and DB | Owner's explicit value; exact-ceiling and one-cent-over checks | owner-requested; domain verified |
| 102 | Long-lived settlement receipts | Authority keeps at most 1024 receipts | Durable settlement table has no receipt eviction; changed terms are errors | Replay after 1026 settlements changes no balance | internal persistence change; verified |
| 103 | What a bound can inventory answers after goods were taken out of it | The reference keeps one bound context for the session: after the pilot has taken part of what is in a can (a call on the ship's inventory), a `List` on the handle of the can answers nothing (the recording lists the can empty although 70 of 95 are in it) | The handle of the can lists what is in the can: the rest of the stack | A client that lists a can again after it took from it sees what is there (the loot window refreshes by `OnClientEvent_MoveFromCargoToHangar` and `OnSlimItemChange`); an empty list would hide goods the pilot can take | tolerated; `tests/test_cargo.py` asserts the 70 that are left in place of the recording's empty list |
| 104 | Cans and what may be taken out of them | The reference jettisons from every hold a client lists as jettisonable (the cargo hold and the specialised holds), gives a can's loot rights to the pilot, its corporation and its fleet (`lootRights`), and keeps the can on its own timers across a restart (the same here: the two hours are in the database) | Only the cargo hold is jettisoned (the specialised holds are not served yet); a can's contents are taken by its owner and the owner's corporation, anyone else is refused with `SafetyActivated`; a can lasts two hours on the host's clock (kept in the database, and put back in space when the simulation is made again) | Fleets and the safety's levels come with the crimewatch (ADR 0009); the specialised holds come with their bays | open; the can's range (2,500 m to its surface), its two hours and the three minutes between cans are the reference's |
| 105 | The drone bay in space: what the client is told when the bay is listed | The first time a pilot in space lists its drone bay (`List(87)`) the reference primes the dogma attributes of each drone for the client (`OnGodmaPrimeItem` for the ship with the drone's item and its attributes, then `OnItemChange` for the drone) | `List` and `GetCapacity` of the drone bay answer in space as they do docked (a Python `set` when it is empty, rows, `util.KeyVal(capacity, used)`) and tell nothing more | The client's drone window needs the drone's attributes once drones can be launched; the drone's dogma state belongs to launching them (the next drone increment), where it is told for every drone that comes into the bay or leaves it | `oracle/fixtures/scenarios/drones.json` (docked: the bay and the room asked for, a drone put in it as a split stack, a second refused with `NotEnoughDroneBaySpace {type, free, required}`, tritanium refused with `Only drones can be placed in the Drone Bay.`, the drone taken back; in space the bay and the room by their answers), `tests/test_drones.py`, `DroneBayTests` | tolerated until drones launch |
| 106 | What an NPC leaves when it is destroyed | The reference rolls the NPC's loot table when it dies (`rollNpcLootEntries` of `npcLoot.js`, with a random draw it does not seed) and puts what came up into the wreck it makes in the same step as the explosion; its tables can also give fixed entries, guaranteed and required drops and build on a base table (the tables it generates for capital NPCs), and its wreck is a record of its own store (persisted unless the NPC was transient) | The host rolls the table (`NpcLoot`, a port of the rules the authored data uses: rule tables with selectors, weights, an empty chance, sizes and grades, the plain random draw `generic_random_any`, and a table without rules, which gives nothing) against the published types of the static data, makes the wreck an item of the pilot who dealt the last blow (of the NPC's corporation when no pilot did) with the loot in it, and asks the simulation to put it in space (`PlaceWreck`, link version 5: `NpcIdentity.host_wreck`), so it is kept like any wreck (two hours, restored with its scene, taken out with what is in it) and its slim item says `isEmpty` as the list does; a pilot can follow, orbit and align to a wreck or a can that lies in the scene | The reference's wreck is made in the tick of the explosion; the host's is put in space a few milliseconds later (one database write: the client sees the ship go and then the wreck come, in two updates), and an NPC spawned without the host making its wreck (a test host) still gets the scene's empty wreck. Idle drones take up the fight against an NPC that attacks the pilot (aggressive, no focus fire). Direction reuses a prior vector or falls back to +Z, and velocity/warp state are not retained | Rust sim tests verify visual-only priming and full-bind continuation; C# tests verify the first-List command is one-shot; no end-to-end PostgreSQL or V24.01 client test was run | limitation; position restore only |

**Welcome-mail identity:** EveJS uses seeded `GM ELYSIAN` (`senderID=140000004`) and EVEJS-branded copy. eve.rs sends the durable welcome mail from the new character's starter NPC corporation with neutral text, because `140000004` overlaps eve.rs's player-character ID range. The message, mailbox status, type-1004 notification, and unread count are preserved.

## Work cycles and module effect ends (2026-10-08)

Protocol 23 (ADR 0013). The client cannot see the host's round trip, so most rows are internal; the rows that the client can see say so.

| # | Behaviour | Reference does | eve.rs does | Evidence | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 147 | How a mining or salvage cycle is settled | The reference settles the cycle inside the module's own runtime (`miningRuntime.js`, `moduleCycleSchedule.js`) | The simulation ends the cycle, parks the module and asks the host (`SimModuleWorkCycle`). The host decides the yield and answers (`SimModuleWorkResult`). The same cycle id is sent again every 2 s of simulation time (`WORK_RESEND_TICKS`, the interval is chosen, not taken from EVE). Cycle ids come from a per-process counter, so a restarted host never confuses a new cycle with a settled one | ADR 0013 rules 3 and 4; `crates/eve-space/src/modules.rs` tests; `dotnet/tests/Eve.Services.Space.Tests/WorkCycleHostTests.cs` | internal. The resend interval is not matched against the reference's timing |
| 148 | A manual stop while a work cycle is parked | The reference short-cycles ore and gas at a manual stop (`scene/modules.js:3918-3935`) and not ice | A deactivate while parked ends the effect at once. An ore cycle is settled for its share of the run (the mining amount scaled by the elapsed share); an ice cycle runs to its boundary, as the reference does | Rust test `deactivating_a_parked_work_cycle_stops_it_now_and_tells_the_host_once`; `a_short_cycled_ore_cycle_...` and `a_short_cycled_ice_cycle_...` | done for ore and ice. A gas cloud is mined through the same cycle (commit feb36a8); its manual-stop short cycle has no test |
| 149 | A host restart while a cycle is parked | The reference's behaviour here is not stated by the static data, the client's logic or the EveJS code read for this row | The simulation keeps the park and re-sends the same cycle id; the restarted host answers from `space_work_cycles`, so nothing is granted twice. ADR 0013 rule 6 says the simulation drops the parked effect on disconnect: that is not implemented | `WorkCycleHostTests.A_restarted_host_answers_a_repeat_from_the_ledger_in_postgresql` | tolerated (no double grant) pending a decision on rule 6 |
| 150 | A module's effect that ends on its own (cycles done, capacitor short, target gone, forbidden) | The reference drops the effect on the server and the client sees it stop | The same client messages; the simulation also reports each end once (`SimModuleEnded`, kind 53) so the host clears the module and re-sends the ship (FP-C1) | Rust `every_end_of_an_effect_is_told_to_the_host_once_natural_or_asked`; `A_module_the_simulation_ends_is_taken_out_of_the_ship_the_host_sends` | contract on the client side; the host message is internal |

Its cloak is disallowed while it is fitted (`disruptionLanceDisallowCloaking` 5425; EveJS `entityHasCloakDisallowingFittedModule`) | The debuff is a `Strike::debuff_ms` (protocol 28): each covered ship is disrupted at the pulse, and warp (`WARP_DISRUPTED`), jump (`JUMP_DISRUPTED`) and docking (`DOCKING_DISRUPTED`) are refused while it lasts; the remote repair a ship takes from another ship is halved (`mend_remote`). The siege is read from the ship's own state: a fitted module carrying `moduleBonusSiegeModule` that the pilot has started (its state is active in the snapshot), so an offline siege module does not enable the lance (`ACTIVE_SIEGE_MODULE_REQUIRED`). The cloak handler refuses `CLOAK_DISALLOWED` while a fitted lance carries 5425. The tether does not exist in eve.rs, so there is nothing to refuse (row 142) | `SuperWeaponEffectsTests.A_disruptive_lance_needs_an_active_siege_module_...` (PgFact, real SDE: alone and with an offline siege module refused; with an active siege module the strike with the 60 s debuff and the 30 s and 120 s windows), `SuperWeaponEffectsTests.A_fitted_disruptive_lance_disallows_the_cloak_...`, `ModuleEffectsTests` (the lance is in the implemented set), Rust `weapons::tests::a_disruptive_pulse_disrupts...`, `scene::tests::a_disrupted_ship_is_refused_warp_docking_and_jump...`, `simlink_fixtures` (a disruptive lance frame, 60 000 ms) | the siege is the module's state in the host's snapshot (the modules are the state owners; no separate siege table); a siege module switched on through a path other than the pilot's own activation is not seen (no such path exists in eve.rs) |
| 140 | The buff registry (decision D4, `Eve.Dogma` timed collections from `sde.dbuffCollections`) | EveJS's command bursts, the Phenomena Generator and the P.A.N.I.C. fleet effect apply timed collections to recipients (`commandBurstRuntime.js`, `dbuff/common/registry.py`); the disruptive lance does not (no collection, 139) | Not built on main (protocol 32). No effect in eve.rs consumes it: command bursts need fleet recipients (D3) and the area type (D1), neither built, and the P.A.N.I.C. effect needs the disallow flags (D10). Building a registry with no consumer would be a store nothing reads (AGENTS rule 5). The disruptive lance uses the strike's debuff instead, which the reference does too | none (refused: the command bursts stay `UNSUPPORTED_EFFECT`, the P.A.N.I.C. stays `EFFECT_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`) | the registry is the next step of the command-burst work (decisions D1 and D3), after the area type and the fleet recipients |
| 141 | The Gravitational Transportation Field Oscillator (HOG, 40634): the sources and their weight | The record of the sources read for row 135: CCP static data (the SDE attribute values and the English description), the V24.01 client callsites (`positionalControl.py`, `shipmodulebutton.py`, `superWeapon.py`, `controlPaths.py`), the CCP Citadel 1.9 patch note (the 2016 expansion added the oscillator as a teleportation; the 1.9 fix stops tethered ships from being affected), the EVE University wiki (no page for the oscillator was found in the search), item databases (everef, EveTools, eve-kill: the same figures as the SDE), and one player forum post (a random spot in the same system, about 30 s without warp) | Resolved as in row 135: the SDE's attribute and the client decide the point count, the SDE's 60 s block applies to capitals, and the random destination and the 30 s block are not used | none of the code paths depend on the forum post | the wiki page for the item is not in the index consulted; the tether exclusion (142) is taken from the patch note |
| 142 | The tether exclusion of the transport field (CCP Citadel 1.9), and the structure variant (Standup Gravitational Transportation Field Oscillator, 47068, category 66) | CCP patch note: the oscillator's teleportation and warp scramble do not affect tethered ships. The SDE: 47068 is a structure module (one per structure) with the same effect 6474 | The tether exclusion is not applied: tethering has no state in eve.rs (no tether command, attribute or host state exists), so no ship can be tethered to exclude. The structure variant is refused by name with `STRUCTURE_SUPERWEAPON_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` (`StructureHogEffect`, module and wiring only, O5): no structure host exists, so the refusal is the only honest answer; it is never a success | `SuperWeaponEffectsTests.A_hog_builds_a_transport_field_...` (the structure refusal, real SDE) | a tether state, and its exclusion from the field, when tethering is built; a structure host for 47068 |
| | 143 | Ship modifiers in a mutated item's `GetDynamicItemInfo` attributes | The item's dynamic attributes on the source base, with the ship's modifiers applied to a fitted or bayed item through the ship's dogma | eve.rs answers the item's own dogma (source base, rolls, result skill layer) from a scratch world with no ship modifiers (`DynamicItemRpcService.cs`). The fit, the drone prime (`OnGodmaPrimeItem`), the launch and the simulation use the ship's world | `MutatedDroneHostTests` and `MutationHarnessTests` check the prime and the launch with the modifiers that reach the drone | RESOLVED (2026-10-09): an item fitted or bayed in a ship is read in that ship's world (`CharacterShips.LoadShipAsync`), so the ship's modifiers reach it; an item in a hangar is read alone, where no ship acts on it |

## Mining tracks (2026-10-09)

- **Belt ore by security (D8).** The SDE places each belt and gives its size, but no ore. A belt now holds the ores of the EVE University wiki's asteroid belt rows (wiki.eveuniversity.org/Asteroid, which says it is being updated for Catalyst) for its system's true security (`BeltOres`, `dotnet/src/Eve.Ships/BeltOres.cs`): 1.0 to 0.9 Veldspar and Scordite; to 0.7 Pyroxeres; to 0.45 Plagioclase; low security Pyroxeres, Kernite, Jaspet and Omber, and Hemorphite and Hedbergite below 0.25; null Kernite and Pyroxeres, Bistot from -0.5, Arkonor from -0.6. The rows are per security class letter, and the SDE system carries the number, so they are merged by true security (the best-supported reading). Each ore is one rock of 15,000 m3, the floor of EVE's estimate for a field (`miningRuntimeState.js`, `estimateOriginalQuantity`). shortcut: the belt's radius is not passed to the host, so the rock does not scale with it. Mercoxit (Deep Core Mining) and the Drone regions are not seeded. Evidence: `BeltOresTests`, `SpaceContainerTests.A_belt_arrival_seeds_each_ore_its_system_security_holds_as_a_persisted_sde_asteroid`. Status: tolerated (the client sees the rocks' names and sizes only).
- **Belt reseed decided from the store.** The in-memory "seeded" flag was never cleared, so a belt emptied in one process stayed empty until a restart. A belt is now seeded when the store holds no live rock in it; the in-flight guard only covers two arrivals at once. Evidence: `SpaceContainerTests.A_belt_whose_rocks_are_all_depleted_is_seeded_again_when_a_pilot_arrives`.
- **Daily goals run every day (X-6).** EVE.js rolls each pilot a rotation of daily goals (`dailyGoalsState.js`, `DAILY_ROTATION_SIZE`); here "Mine 2000 units of Ore" and "Salvage 5 Wrecks" run for every pilot every period, from 11:00 UTC (`DOWNTIME_HOUR_UTC`), as content (`DailyGoalRecorder`). The targets are EVE.js's (`dailyGoalsCatalog.js`). Ore counts only (EVE.js counts ore only). Evidence: `DailyGoalTests`. Status: tolerated until the rotation is built; no client window reads the goals yet.
- **The mining ledger is a table (X-7).** EVE.js keeps the ledger in its own game-store file; here it is `mining_ledger_entries` and `mining_ledger_observers` (migration 0003, space schema), written by the subscriber `mining.ledger` and read through the rowset read side. Shape: the same fields, the same order (newest first), the same visibility (a pilot's entry after 10 minutes, `miningCharacterLedgerDelayMs`) and the same 90-day retention. Differences: (a) the numbers are `PyLong` where the game store returned `PyInt` (the client reads them as integers; the marshal type differs); (b) an observer is listed when its first visible time has come and its newest entry is within the retention, which is exact when its entries are all visible or all retained, and approximate when a visible-but-expired entry and a retained-but-hidden entry sit together; (c) the observer names are sorted by the database collation, not the culture's; (d) the game store's rows are copied at each start (adding only the entries that are not there), a shortcut that reads the whole table, and the rows stay there as an archive. Evidence: `MiningLedgerTests` (replay, rollback, visibility, prune, import), `MiningLedgerServiceTests` (the wire fields, order, roles, empty shapes). Status: tolerated; the observer path has no writer, because a ship's laser has no observer structure.
- **Gas clouds sit beside belt rocks.** CCP keeps harvestable gas clouds in gas sites found by scanning, and EveJS places them through its mining-resource
  sites (`miningResourceSiteService.js`). Here a published cloud is seeded beside each belt's rock on a pilot's arrival, when no live cloud is in the belt.
  Site generation is not built.
- **Yield of a cloud.** The SDE material of the cloud (`typeMaterials`), else the compressed gas it is named for (`Compressed <name>`, group Compressed Gas).
- **Quantity of a rock and of a cloud.** 1,000 units each, a fixed shortcut. EveJS estimates a rock's quantity from its radius and a template
  (`miningRuntimeState.js`, `estimateOriginalQuantity`). The source of per-belt contents is decision D8.
- **Crystal volatility is rolled by the host.** EveJS rolls it in the process (`applyCrystalVolatility`). The simulation does not carry the roll, so no wire field
  is added for it. The host draws it with `SpaceContainers.VolatilityRoll`.
- **Crystal damage** is kept as the crystal item's custom info, and is not announced to the client (EveJS `notifyMiningChargeDamageChange`). A crystal at full
  damage is removed (`applyCrystalVolatility`).
- **Crystal consumption.** None per cycle, as in EveJS (`executeMiningCycle` takes no charge per cycle).
- **Crystal yield.** EveJS reads the crystal's yield multiplier (attribute 782) in its module snapshot and does not read the waste bonuses (attributes
  3159 and 3160, `miningDogma.js`, `buildMiningModuleSnapshot`). eve.rs checks the crystal's type list at activation and does not apply the multiplier
  directly. Whether the multiplier reaches the yield through dogma is not checked in this code path.
- **Passive lock.** The lock stays on the target after the targeter stops. EveJS's deactivation path for `passiveTargetingEffect` was not read.
- **Auto-target at a full lock list.** The sweep passes over the ships it cannot lock, silently. A refusal the sweep did not ask for is not sent.
- **Mining ledger.** One entry per granted cycle. The entry id is the event id, which keeps a replayed event from writing twice. A character's entry is
  visible 10 minutes after its cycle (EveJS `miningCharacterLedgerDelayMs`) and kept for 90 days. Observer rows are written as described in the ledger-table entry above.
- **Yield destination.** The general cargo hold. EveJS prefers the mining hold (`resolveDestinationFlagForPlayer`).
- **Client text.** `TARGET_INVALID_FOR_MODULE` and `CHARGE_NOT_COMPATIBLE` have no dedicated text in EveJS's switch, so the client shows the default
  "Failed to activate" text. The salvage notices use EveJS's names (`SalvagingFailure`, `CustomNotify`); their text in the client is not checked here.

## Tractor beam (protocol 30)

- **A pulled wreck is a ball that follows its puller (D6).** EVE.js `applyTractorActivationCommand` sends `SetBallFree`, `SetBallMass` (10), `SetMaxSpeed`, `SetSpeedFraction` (1) and `FollowBall` to any ball it pulls, wreck or can alike. The client's tactical display reads a ball's `followId` whatever the ball's kind (eve-logic `tacticalNavigation/actionDisplay.py`, `_GetMode`). eve.rs sends the same presentation (`tractor.rs`), and the follow step is generalised to any body (`motion::Kinematics`), not a new motion kind.
- **Inertia of the pulled wreck stays at the ball default (1.0).** EVE.js's beam does not set it; eve.rs's `BallEntity` default is 1.0 (`ball.rs`).
- **Thrust of a light body (a fix to the motion model; ships unchanged).** The thrust is the one whose equilibrium is the speed (`speed * friction / inertial mass`). The old `agility_seconds` floor of 0.05 s held a 10 kg body to about 0.1 m/s. Ships sit far above the floor, so their motion is unchanged (the ship motion tests pass).
- **Hold distance 500 m, the beam's constant (EVE.js `TRACTOR_FOLLOW_RANGE_METERS`).** The speed is the module's `maxTractorVelocity` (attribute 1045).
- **Release.** EVE.js `stopTractorTarget` restores the ball's top speed, zeroes the speed fraction and velocity, and sends `Stop`; it leaves the mass at 10. eve.rs does the same, restoring the top speed of a ball that lies still (0).
- **Loot rights (D13): checked at activation, not every cycle.** EVE.js checks `hasTractorLootRight` every cycle, through the space loot entitlement (`moduleRuntimeCallbacks.hasLootRightForTarget`). eve.rs checks the wreck's stored rights when the beam is activated (`SpaceContainers.MayPullAsync`, the rule of `MayTakeFromAsync`: abandoned, the pilot's own, or its corporation's). The rights change only by abandonment during a pull, which widens them. Choice: the stored snapshot, as the salvage credit uses it.
- **Two pulls on one wreck.** The later beam takes the wreck over; the earlier beam's end leaves it alone. EVE.js does not state this case; this is the simplest rule that keeps one follow target per ball.
- **A puller that leaves.** The pull is let go at the next settlement (`Scene::release_orphaned_pulls`). EVE.js ends the beam with the ship's removal.
- **Corpses are not tractorable.** EVE.js gives a corpse its own runtime kind (`getRuntimeInventoryEntityKind`: the biomass group, scooped to the cargo hold), and `TRACTORABLE_ENTITY_KINDS` names only wreck and container. eve.rs refuses a corpse as not there (`TARGET_NOT_FOUND`).
- **Mobile Tractor Units (group 1250)** are not ship modules and their runtime (EVE.js `mobileTractorUnitRuntime.js`) is not built; the handler is for the ship's `tractorBeamCan`.

## Not yet matched

Behaviour of the reference that eve.rs does not have yet, and where it will come from. These are gaps, not
decisions.

| Behaviour | Comes with |
|---|---|

Not there: the character's own attribute reads (`GetCharacterAttributes`, `GetAttributeValue`...), `GetRequiredSkillLevels`, `CreateNewbieShip`, activating, overloading and deactivating modules (`ActivateModule`, `Deactivate`, `ChangeStatusOfModules`...), charge loading and `GetLocationInfo`. Space login attaches the active ship from a saved pose and primes visual AddBalls2 on the first active-ship List before a later full bind; full native-client ballpark and Dogma bootstrap are not checked in the graphical client. A ship is undamaged with a full capacitor and shield (`ShipCondition.Full`), the pilot's learning attributes are all 20 (no remapping, implants or boosters) and its security status 0 | S3 (skills), S4 (space) |
| Item ids of a new character's ship and modules are small integers (from 1990000000), not the reference's longs (from 9.9e12); the client treats both as integers | S3 (ids), if the client turns out to care |
| The skill handler runs the queue (`SaveNewQueue`, `CharStartTrainingSkillByTypeID`, `CharStartTrainingSkill`, `AbortTraining`, `CharStopTrainingSkill`, the queue and history reads, points that grow with the clock, levels that finish while the client watches or is away) and matches the reference for it, checked at its own speed and at a speed 60,000 times faster (`training.json`, `training_fast.json`). Not there: buying and injecting skills (`PurchaseSkills`, `InjectSkillIntoBrain`, `IsSkillAvailableForPurchase`, `GetDirectPurchasePrice`), injectors and extraction (`InjectSkillpoints`, `ExtractSkills`, `SplitSkillInjector`, `CombineSkillInjector`), free skill points (always 0), `GetSkillChangesForISIS`; the learning attributes are all 20 (no remapping, implants or boosters), an account trains one character at a time (no extra slots), a lapsed subscription trains at half speed but the alpha clone's skill-point cap and the skills only Omega may train are not enforced, and the queue is settled when something asks or when its timer fires while the character is in the game (timers are not restored after a host restart: the next login settles the queue) | S3 (skills: books and injectors), S5 (subscriptions) |
| `charMgr` notes, contacts, blocked senders, settings and titles are those of a player that has none (empty lists, `GetOwnerNote`/`GetNote`/`GetPrivateInfoOnCorpChange` not answered); security status is 0 and there are no bounties | contacts, notes and corporation modules (S5) |
| The inventory broker: `GetInventory` for the hangar, `GetInventoryFromId` for the station and a ship of the character in the hangar, `List`, `ListByFlags`, `GetItems`, `GetItem`, `GetSelfInvItem`, `GetContainerContents` on them, and `Add` between the hangar and the character's ship (fitting into a slot, unfitting, cargo), in one transaction with the module state (`Eve.Ships`), answered with the item's id and told to the client the way the reference does (`OnMultiEvent`, then `OnItemsChanged`). What `Add` does is checked against the reference only for taking a fitted module out (the headless client cannot fit: the reference answers `None`); fitting and the cargo hold are written from the client's contract and the fitting rules the reference's validator lists, and are covered by unit tests only. Not there: ships other than the active one for the cargo hold's room (a ship in the hangar that is not the one the character flies holds what its type says, without the character's skills), overheating, `MultiAdd`, `MultiMerge`, the character's and corporations' inventories, containers, the fighter and fuel bays and the drone bay's moves (its size is told, `GetCapacity(87)`), `SetLabel`, `GetAvailableTurretSlots`, item ownership rules | S3 (inventory: stacks, charges), S5 (corporations) |
| The space slice: a ship undocks from its station, is told the system's balls and its own, flies (`CmdSetSpeedFraction`, `CmdGotoDirection`, `CmdStop`, `CmdAlignTo`, `CmdOrbit`, `CmdFollowBall`) and docks (`CmdDock`, accepted within 2.5 km of the station's surface, done four seconds later). A `CmdDock` from beyond the docking range makes the ship follow the station to the docking range and is answered with the user error `DockingApproach` (the distance, rounded half up); as in the reference the ship does not dock when it gets there, the pilot asks again. The protection of a ship that has just undocked ends, and the client is told, when the pilot's first movement is accepted or after 30 seconds, whichever comes first (a refused command costs nothing). The `onlineModules` argument of `Undock` is ignored (the starter modules are online already), and station/structure guest join and leave broadcasts are now sent to registered observers. Warp: `CmdWarpToStuff('item', body, minRange=)` to a body of the system (a planet, a sun or a big moon at the point the client works out for it, another body short of its place by the reference's stop distance); a warp that is too near, or to nothing, is the message "You cannot warp there right now.", a ship that is in warp is told `ShipInWarp` when asked to warp or stop, and the first accepted movement of any kind ends the undocking protection. Stargates: a warp to a gate ends at a random point by it (#25) and `CmdStargateJump(from, to, shipID)` takes the ship through: the effect and the stop, `OnJumpQueueUpdate` (kept by the host: the pilots that jumped to the system in the last three minutes), the answer (the bound object and the time it can be used from), and four or five seconds later the objects bound to the old system let go, the ship's item at the new system (`OnItemChange`), `OnJumpCloakUpdated` and the session change (solar system, constellation, region if it differs, location); the client then binds the new system and is sent it, with the ship at rest in front of the gate; the cloak's end is told (#24). A jump that cannot be done is the reference's message (`TargetingAttemptCancelled`, `DeniedShipChanged`, or `CustomInfo` for too far from the gate). Not there: the gate's activation states (every gate is open), the criminal and weapons timers that forbid a jump, scripted heavy interdictors, contraband inspection, abyssal and jump-bridge restrictions, the cloak itself in the ball, warp to bookmarks, scanned sites, fleet members and other kinds of place, warp disruption and cloaks, `CmdWarpToStuffAutopilot`, weapons, drones, NPCs, other ships in the system are seen as #27 and #28 say (players; no NPCs, no ambient ships #23), time dilation other than 1.0, damage (a ship is undamaged), the protection of a ship that has just undocked is announced and ended, and enforced against locks (nothing can hurt a ship yet), the reference's damping of repeated `CmdGotoDirection` echoes is only reproduced for a repeat of the current direction, space login restores a persisted pose at rest and primes AddBalls2 on the first active-ship List before the later full bind; velocity and warp state are not persisted, and native-client startup has not been run yet | S5 (combat, NPCs, cloaks) |
| Modules (S5b, `crates/eve-space/src/modules.rs`): an effect of a module is started (`Activate`) and stopped at the end of its cycle (`Deactivate`), goes on cycle after cycle and costs capacitor at the start of each, and changes the ship's mass and top speed while it runs; the afterburner and the microwarpdrive are among them (the numbers are the client's formula: the ship is heavier by the module's mass addition and faster by its speed factor times its speed boost factor over the new mass). The capacitor recharges along CCP's curve and its charge is told as in the reference. Also run now: repairers, weapons, mining, electronic warfare and cloaks (TODO sections 6 to 10). Not there: overheating, the effect's range and its end when the target goes, `EffectCrowdedOut` (the group limit while running, TODO section 9), `ModuleReactivationDelayed`, a module that is put offline while it runs, and #32 to #35 | S5c (combat) |
| Targeting (S5a, `crates/eve-space/src/targeting.rs`): ships and stations can be locked, unlocked, given up and cleared, from `dogmaIM` bound to the system (`AddTarget`, `CancelAddTarget`, `RemoveTarget`, `RemoveTargets`, `ClearTargets`, `GetTargets`, `GetTargeters`), with the reference's refusals (itself, not found, not targetable (the star, planets, moons, belts, gates), a ship under its undocking protection, a ship in warp, out of range, the limit of locks) and its events (`OnTarget` `add`, `lost`, `clear`, `otheradd`, `otherlost`; the damage state of what is locked, sent to everyone who can see it). Not there: refusals for jamming, cloaks, tethers, recalibrating sensors and dungeon scope, `lost` with a reason for a ship that explodes, locks on anything but ships, stations and mineable rocks (NPCs, drones, containers; a wreck is looked up on the same path). A mineable rock locks through the same path with the same range and visibility checks, and a depleted rock refuses a new lock and drops the one held on it (`a_mineable_rock_in_range_is_locked_and_an_out_of_range_one_is_refused`, `a_depleted_rock_refuses_a_new_lock_and_drops_the_lock_held_on_it`). Not checked: the client's view of a rock lock, and whether rock ids (item ids from 1,990,000,000) can collide with system body ids; a rock is looked up before the bodies, and the SDE is not loaded in this checkout to check the body id range. The reference's re-timing of a lock when scan resolution changes, `OnTarget clear` for a ship that docks with locks held (not recorded), and #29 | S5 (combat) |
| Health (S5c-1, `crates/eve-space/src/health.rs`): a ship has a shield, armor and hull (points and resonances from dogma; what it has taken as fractions, kept per ship and per module in `Eve.Fitting`); a hit goes through the layers as the reference's `applyDamageToEntity` does (each layer's resonances, what a layer cannot take goes on to the next, a resonance of nothing skips the layer, what is kept of a hit is rounded to six places and what is told is not), the shield recharges along the capacitor's curve and its charge is told to its pilot every half second, the damage state of a hurt ship is told to everyone who sees it, has it locked or flies it, and `/dmg` and `/heal` (`slash`) hurt and mend the ship in the hangar and `/heal` mends it in space (`ship.Undock` asks nothing of the presets: a ship leaves full-shielded, full-capacitored and as damaged in armor and hull as it was). Recorded with one character (`oracle/fixtures/scenarios/health.json`) and replayed by `tests/test_health.py` on the reference itself and on eve.rs; 30 Rust tests of `health.rs`, one of the sim's, the host's on top; deviations #35 to #38. Not yet: anything that hurts a ship in space (weapons, missiles, NPCs, smartbombs, environments: `Scene::damage` is the only way in and tests are its only caller), the destruction of a ship (`OnShipDeath`, wreck, pod), `OnDamageMessage`, repairers and boosters, the other `slash` commands | S5c (weapons and NPCs) |

The client's quirks are kept on purpose: `ModifyOrbitVelocity` sets the custom velocity only when the velocity it worked out is nothing (`if not customVelocity`), the scripted loader called without a scope type raises a `TypeError`, `SetShieldRatio` sets the shield's capacity and not its charge, and Python 2's integer division (`microWarpDriveDuration / 1000`) and `math.floor` returning a float (additional loot amounts) are followed. | The reference's optional AES branch of the crypto request (its own comment says it never worked) | not planned |
| Precursor weapons (`targetDisintegratorAttack`): the damage ramp is kept and applied by the simulation (`weapons.rs`, EVE.js `precursorTurrets.js`), but EVE.js also sends the client the ramp's attribute overrides (`damageMultiplierBonusCurrent` 5804, `damageMultiplierBonusMaxTimestamp` 5818) and the effect's graphic info (`multiplierBonusPerCycle`, `multiplierBonusMax`, `buildPrecursorTurretGraphicInfo`); eve.rs does not send them yet, so the client's ramp display is not checked | the special-FX graphic info and the attribute notifications of a precursor effect |
| Precursor weapons stop at the end of a cycle whose target is beyond the optimal range (EVE.js `executeTurretCycle` returns `stopReason: range` and fires nothing); eve.rs keeps the effect running and its shots miss | the turret cycle's range stop |
| Vorton arcs (`ChainLightning`): the arc reaches the ships of the simulation only. EVE.js also chains to drones, fighters, structures and orbitals (`isVortonArcTargetKind`) and needs local interaction (`canEntitiesInteractLocally`), which the simulation's drone and structure damage paths do not provide yet | drone/fighter damage and structures in the simulation |
| Warp disruption field generator, warp bubbles, interdiction probes, stasis burst, bombs, defenders, warp core stabilizer and interdiction nullifier: now run, see "Bombs, bubbles and warp disruption (2026-10-09)" below. Still refused: F.O.F. missiles and the mobile warp disruptors (the rows that follow) | that section |
| F.O.F. missiles (`fofMissileLaunching`, groups 394 to 396) on the served launchers 506, 509, 510, 511 and 1245: refused `FOF_MISSILE_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` (`MissileEffect.RefusedFiringEffects`). The SDE and EveJS give no rule for which hostile an F.O.F. missile takes (no `fofMissileLaunching` handler exists in EveJS, and the client's request carries no target) | a target-selection rule from a captured V24.01 trace of an F.O.F. launch |
| Mobile warp disruptors (`anchorDrop`, `anchorLift`, group 361): not deployable. The SDE has no lifetime (attribute 1088 is absent); the EveJS table (2, 7 and 14 days, `mobileWarpDisruptorRuntime.js`) is the only source and is not yet taken. The deployable store (its own schema) and the anchoring and expiry jobs on `DurableScheduler` are not written | the deployable store and jobs (not started in the 2026-10-09 track A session) |

## Bombs, bubbles and warp disruption (2026-10-09)

Decisions of the bombs and bubbles brief (`bombs_decisions_brief.md`, D1 to D14) and the bubble wire of protocol 34 (ADR 0013). The sources are the rank of
AGENTS.md: the SDE (type and effect attributes, read from the pinned build), the V24.01 client (`eve-logic`), and EVE.js. Where they disagree the reading below is
the one chosen, and the reason is given. "No trace" means that no V24.01 recording of the scenario exists in the repository; nothing here is checked in the
graphical client.

| Status | What differs or was decided | Evidence and reason | Open item |
|---|---|---|---|
| DECIDED | Bomb (D1): released at the launcher's ship when the cycle starts, it goes off where it is `explosionDelay` later (12 s, 7.5 s, 15 s by charge). Blast radius is the charge's `aoeCloudSize` (400 m; 5000 m for the Focused Void). Its kind comes from the charge: neutralising if `energyNeutralizerAmount` is above zero, ECM if a scan strength bonus is, otherwise damage | client `missile.py` `Bomb.Release` (the impact is the bomb's place at release); the SDE attributes. EVE.js has no bomb code, so this is the only reading with a source | no trace of a launch and a detonation; the explosion's client effect is `TerminalPlayDestructionEffect`, not a bomb-specific guid |
| DECIDED | The blast is one `Area` (ADR 0011 addendum, 2026-10-09): centre, surface radius, the launcher's own ship spared as a smartbomb spares its own. The recipients are the simulation's own ships, not a host list, because the positions at detonation are the simulation's state. A damage bomb hurts each ship in the area through `damage_ship`, an ECM bomb jams through `jam_at`, a neutralising bomb takes capacitor through `take_capacitor` | ADR 0011 decision 5 (host lists) is for fleet and hostile filters, and a bomb has none | a fleet or hostile filter would come as a host list in a later wire batch |
| DECIDED | Defender launcher (D4, Defender Launcher I, 512): target-less. Each cycle its defender missile (the charge's flight) goes for the nearest hostile missile in its reach and shoots it down, or hurts it by the charge's four damages when it does not destroy it (`DefenderMissile::intercept`) | the defender charge is `defenderMissileLaunching` with no target in the client request; EVE.js's point defense keys on `pointdefense`, which is not in the SDE; the intercept maths already exists in `missiles.rs` | the interception is shown as the missile's explosion at once, not as a defender missile flying out |
| DECIDED | Defender damage is the charge's own, not scaled by the pilot's missile damage multiplier (212) | a defender is not a missile that hits a ship | none |
| DECIDED | Field generator penalties (D5): the mass bonus (1131), the velocity modifier (1076) and the signature bonus (554) are the generator's own type attributes, applied as percentages of the ship's own numbers. They were read from the SDE for all eight generators of group 899: the mass and velocity bonuses and the propulsion bonuses (1164, 1270) are zero on every one; the signature bonus is +50 % on seven and +40 % on the Clutch Restrained (37610) | `eve-logic` `warpDisruptSphere.py` (`MODIFIER_CHANGES`) names the attributes; the SDE has the values on the type | the propulsion part (speed factor and speed boost bonuses of the propulsion modules) is refused `EFFECT_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` on a generator that has a non-zero value; none of the eight does |
| DECIDED | Hazard restriction (D7): attribute 5561 is not enforced. The client's rules (`moduleRestrictions.py`) check the empire (1074) and high-sec (1970) only, and EveJS applies its hazard check to one system | `eve-logic` and EveJS | a server reference for the hazard rule |
| DECIDED | Bubble and bubble immunity (D9): a bubble holds a ship that is in its range when the ship starts a warp. No owner is spared (EveJS has none). A ship already in warp is not dropped (EveJS checks at the four warp-start callers only) | EveJS `warpCommands.js` (`findActiveWarpDisruptorForEntity` at the warp-start callers) | none |
| DECIDED | Refusal reasons (D10): a bubble refuses with `WARP_DISRUPTED_BY_BUBBLE`, sent to the client as the user error `WarpDisrupted` (EveJS `beyonceService.js`). A standard cloaking device refuses with `WARP_BLOCKED_BY_CLOAK`, sent as the notice "You cannot warp while cloaked. Deactivate your cloaking device first." (EveJS). The check order is EveJS's: distance, then bubble, then cloak | EveJS `warpCommands.js` and `beyonceService.js` | none |
| DECIDED | Immunity and stabilizer (D8): the sim gets what dogma gives the module with it active against offline: an immunity (attribute 1538 above zero: `WarpImmunity`) or the change of the scramble status (attribute 104: `ScrambleStatus`, -2 for the stabilizer). A scramble point the stabilizer cancels is not a scramble (`scrambled_with`) | `DogmaModifierEffect` builds the same modifiers for the client; `WarpModifierEffect` adds the simulation's part | the modifier of an interdiction disruptor (attribute 1539) that the client's fitting warnings describe is applied by dogma only if an effect of the world carries it; no such effect is modelled here |
| DECIDED | Cloaks (audit D, M-3): a cloak that is not warp-safe (`cloaking`, `cloakingPrototype`) holds its ship out of warp; the warp-safe one (`cloakingWarpSafe`, covert ops) does not. The flag is on the wire (`SimAction` tag 5) | EveJS `shipCloak.js` (`isEntityWarpBlockedByCloak`) | none |
| DECIDED | Interdiction probe (D11, group 589 with a warp disruption charge): released at the ship and stood still (EveJS's probe spawn: the ship's position, zero velocity), a source of `warpScrambleRange` (20 km; 10 km for the Surgical) for its `explosionDelay` (120 s; 180 s), then removed with `RemoveBalls`. Simulation-only: a restart drops it (LIMITATION, ADR 0012 addendum) | EveJS `interdictionProbeRuntime.js` (lifetime equals the SDE delay, verified) | the probe persists only in the simulation |
| DECIDED | Stasis webification burst (WP-11b, charge group 4088, 56748): each cycle, every ship within the charge's `doomsdayAOERange` (15 km) of the launcher's ship, but its own, is webbed by its `speedFactor` (-20 %) for `doomsdayAOEDuration` (30 s). It is a burst: no probe is put in space, and nothing checks who the ships belong to | the SDE attributes; the reference has no burst handler to compare (`torpedoLaunching` is not offensive in the SDE, so no aggression is made by it) | the friend-or-foe rule of the burst is not written anywhere the sources show |
| LIMITATION | Probes and bombs are simulation-only: their lifetime is the simulation's own clock, so a restart drops them (ADR 0012 addendum). A 120 s probe is not a `Deployed` row, because the 30 s sweep would make it up to 25 % late | ADR 0012 decision 2 and its addendum | persistence of timed objects, if an owner wants a restart to keep them |
| DECIDED | Focus scripts (WP-3, group 908, a charge of the field generator): the script's effect runs on the target ship as a projected scramble. Its reach is the generator's warp scramble range (attribute 103, 16 km on the Generator I) with the script's own range bonus (1327: +50 % for the disruption script, -20 % for the scrambling one), which dogma applies to the module's attribute 103 itself (effect 3648), so the range is read once and not multiplied again. Strength: the module's warp scramble strength (attribute 105, 100 on the Generator I), as the script's own modifier gives it (effects 6849 and 6848: a ModAdd of the target's warp scramble status by the modifying item's 105), so a target is scrambled at once. The scrambling script stops a microwarpdrive (its EffectStopper), as the scrambler does | the SDE effect rows (`dogmaEffects.modifierInfo`) | the other modifiers of the scripts (the maximum velocity multiplier, attribute 1470, and the 1973 and 1974 pair of the disruption script) are not run; the range and the scramble are |
| LIMITATION | Protocol 34 (ADR 0013): one bump carries the cloak's warp-safe flag (tag 5) and the actions of tags 16 to 22: the bubble, the immunity, the scramble status, the probe, the bomb, the stasis burst and the defender. Fixtures in `oracle/fixtures/simlink/messages.json` cover each | `tools/dev/gen-simlink-fixtures.py` | the bubble's client visual is the effect's own FX (`effects.WarpDisruptFieldGenerating`), with no client message; no trace has been compared |
| NOT RUN | The client was not driven for this track: no MITM capture of a bubble, a probe, a bomb or a defender was made, because the headless client has no scenario that fits one, and the graphical client is not on this host | `tools/mitm/README.md` | a capture when a scenario can be driven |

The recovery distance is measured from the centre of the ship to the centre of the squadron (EVE.js measures the distance between the surfaces: a few metres differ). | `fighters.rs` tests, `SpaceFightersTests`, `SimCodecTests`, `messages.json` | the graphical client has not run any of these |

The neutralizer's signature falloff reads attribute 2451 from the squadron's own type, as a ship's neutralizer does.
