## Module rules (track B, 2026-10-09)

Decisions taken where the sources left a choice (EveJS is the trusted reference; CCP docs and the SDE settle the numbers):

- Module rules are enforced in the simulation from the rules the host sends with each effect (`ModuleRules`, flag 32). The host works them out from the type's
  attributes once, in the effect wrapper (`ShipEffect`), so no handler repeats them. Early deactivation (906), the core requirement (3265), the group limit (763)
  and the reactivation delay (669) are the four rules sent. Disallow activate on warp (1245) stays on the host (`ModuleRestrictions.CheckWarp`), as on main.
- Locks from running modules (warp 104, docking 2354, activation block 1349) are read from the running effect's `on_changes`, which the host already sends,
  so they need no wire change. The Networked Sensor Array's block of the capital micro jump drives comes through its modifier (5700, skill 83464).
- Refusal wording: where EveJS has a text it is used (docking, cloak, industrial core, module blocked as the client's `ModuleIsBlocked`). Where EveJS shows a
  client user error that needs data the refusal does not carry (the reactivation delay with `timeSinceLastStop`, the group limit with `count`), the pilot is
  told in a notice of ours, recorded in TODO section 16.
- Dock refusal: EveJS's `DOCKING_DISALLOWED_BY_MODULE` text is shown. The other silent dock refusals (immobile, doomsday, disrupted) are unchanged.
- Repeat cap (disallowRepeatingActivation 1014): the server's cycle budget is one (EveJS `moduleCycleSchedule.js`). The repeat the client is told for the
  effect's FX is also one. EveJS keeps the client's repeat for the FX, so this differs for the FX only; the client's view was not checked.
- Hostility (FP-3, FP-C6): a computer (every percentage on its side) is not hostile; a disruptor is. A guidance disruptor is hostile when its velocity or
  explosion velocity is lowered, or its delay or explosion radius raised, the same signs as the SDE's bonuses. EveJS gives no separate rule for it.
- Spool (wiki gap WG-1): the disintegrator's bonus resets when its target changes and when its effect stops (EveJS `precursorTurrets.js` 157-164 and
  `moduleEffectShutdown.js` 165, 319). EveJS has no reset when the target leaves optimal range, so none is added; the wiki line is not applied.
- The mwd_blocked special case (another ship's scrambler, `Incoming`) stays. It is not an own-module overlay, so the 1349 overlay does not replace it.
- A burnt-out module (its own effect sets its damage to its hit points, the energizer) does not start again (MODULE_INCAPACITATED). The state is kept in the
  simulation and is lost on a host restart; the host's module condition is not updated by it.
- Stall guard (M-1): a module moment that comes back at the same tick for the same module ends that module's effect (Ending::Forced). A parked work cycle
  whose action has gone stops the effect. The budget is 10,000 moments a ship per settle; the rest is due at the next settle.
- ProtocolVersion 32 to 33: one bump for the module rules (flag 32). The fixture generator and `messages.json` carry the frames.

## Launchers A3: festival, countermeasure, F.O.F. (track A leftovers)

- F.O.F. (auto-targeting) missiles: the rule is the EVE University wiki's ("Missiles", auto-targeting missiles): the launcher goes for "the nearest enemy ship
  or drone that has previously fired on you", and no lock is needed. The simulation takes the enemies that the scene's aggressions name as having fired on the
  shooter, the nearest of them within the missile's flight range (speed times explosion delay, from the SDE). The wiki gives a "hard maximum range" with no
  number; EVE Ref lists 200 km for a cruise F.O.F. The SDE number is used (numbers from the SDE, rules from the wiki). Excluded: the shooter's corporation and
  alliance, the shooter's own owner, ships in warp, and ships in undock protection. Standings and war declarations are not consulted (the simulation has none),
  so the rule is the wiki's wording only. A drone's shot is an aggression of its controller's ship, so the controller's position stands in for the drone.
  Ties go to the lower ship id. Nothing in reach: nothing fires and nothing is charged.
- Breacher pods (launcher 4807; SDE numbers, wiki rules): the pod's damage-over-time has the SDE values dotDuration 40 s (attribute 5735), a cap of 600 damage
  per tick (5736), and 0.6% of the target's hit points per tick (5737). The wiki gives the tick as one second ("damage per second cap", "percentage of target
  HP damaged per second"), so the tick is one second. The wiki's rules: a pod does nothing unless it reaches its target; it can be killed by a smartbomb before
  it arrives; it hits only player ships and NPCs; one DoT per target at a time, while several ships can carry DoTs from one launcher. The per-tick bonuses (5749, 5750) are
  skill bonuses to the same numbers and are not read yet. The wiki's "75 seconds with max skills" is a skill-modified duration; the base duration is the SDE's
  40 s, and the duration bonus is not read from the SDE yet. Not built yet: the DoT needs a protocol change, since a pod's DoT is not in the missile the wire carries.
- The F.O.F. launch is sent as the target id -1 (`AUTO_TARGET_ID`), so no wire change was needed. The simulation resolves it at the launch.
- The wiki's remark that F.O.F. missiles only engage after the pilot has been aggressed is from forum posts, not from CCP or the wiki's own page: not applied.
- Festival launchers (501, charge group 500, `snowBallLaunching`): the snowballs are launched at a target, do no damage (the charge's damage is zero or
  negative, clamped to zero), and make no aggression (the SDE marks the effect not offensive, `isOffensive` false). A launch with no damage makes no
  aggression in the simulation; a damaging launch always does. Structure festival launchers (1974, category 66) stay refused (structure phase).
- Countermeasure launcher (308, unpublished): it fires torpedoes and missiles (group 89) or defenders (group 88). The loaded charge chooses the handler:
  `MissileEffect` for the first, `DefenderEffect` for the second. Groups 84 and 91 accepted by the launcher have no types in the SDE.

## Module rules, track B2 (2026-10-09)

Each item names the source that decided it. The wiki (EVE University) is secondary; CCP material and the SDE come first, then EVE.js and the V24.01 client code (eve-logic).

- **Entropic disintegrator stops off optimal (WG-1).** The turret stops when its target is past its optimal range (the shooter's range disruption counted), checked at each cycle boundary, so it can run one cycle past the moment the target left. The ramp goes with the effect. The host is told `ModuleEnded` with wire reason 2 (target lost). Source: EVE University wiki (Vedmak, Leshak and the Triglavian launch notes: "they deactivate immediately if the target leaves optimal"). Activation is not refused beyond optimal. EVE.js has no such stop (its `precursorTurrets.js` only builds the ramp), so this is a difference from EVE.js, taken from the wiki. Test: `a_precursor_weapon_stops_when_its_target_leaves_optimal_range_and_its_ramp_goes_with_it` (mutation: the predicate disabled, the test fails on the lock assertion).
- **Charge stacking exemption (WG-2).** Kept as the code has it: the charge category (8) is unpenalised. The V24.01 client exempts it too (`dogma/const.py` `dgmUnnerfedCategories`: skill, implant, ship, charge, subsystem). The wiki's stacking-penalty list does not name charges and is incomplete on this point. Test: `A_charges_two_percentages_stack_in_full_as_the_client_says_and_a_modules_do_not` (mutation: the charge exemption removed, the test fails).
- **Stargate jump stops the modules the ship runs (reactive armor hardener).** EVE.js `deactivateActiveModulesForSpaceTransition(session, "stargate-jump")` stops every active module when the ship leaves through a gate. eve.rs carried them through the Traveller. Now each running effect ends as forbidden (wire reason 3), which resets the hardener's shift profile; no error text is sent (EVE.js sends none for this reason). The host clears its active set on `ModuleEnded` as for any end; a host-level test of a jump with a running module is not in this change. Test: `a_stargate_jump_stops_every_module_the_ship_runs_and_the_hardener_profile_with_it`.
- **Reactive armor hardener, restart and death.** Restart: the profile is in memory and a new scene starts with no running module, so the next activation starts from the seed (as before). Death: the simulation removes the ship, and the host's pilot record is removed or replaced (`SpaceSessions.Death.cs`), so the host's active set does not carry the module to the next ship. Both are read from the code; neither has a dedicated test in this change.
- **Rack heat on a dock (WG-5, part).** EVE.js resets the rack heat when the ship is healed on a dock (`resetEntityRackHeat` in `entityQueries.js`). eve.rs removes the pilot on dock and the ship that undocks has no heat. Test: `a_ship_that_docks_leaves_the_station_with_no_rack_heat`. Tethers do not exist in eve.rs, so no tether repair.
- **Nanite paste cannot run while overheated (WG-5, not built).** The wiki states it. Neither EVE.js nor the V24.01 client code in eve-logic has such a check or a refusal text, so no refusal is written (a refusal would need invented text). Recorded here as not built.
- **Spool telemetry (FP-C3): not sent.** The V24.01 client does not read attributes 5804 or 5818 by name anywhere in eve-logic (only `dogma/const.py` names them). Its precursor visual counts its own bonus (`triglavianBeam.py`). Sending the values would send data nothing reads, so FP-C3 is closed on this evidence.
- **Overheat is visible to dogma (FP-C4a).** A module the pilot has overheated, while it runs, is `Overloaded` in the host's snapshot, so the overload effects apply to the shot the host builds. The host records the request when the simulation is asked (the simulation applies the overheat at its next cycle, and the request has no reply to read back). Test: `OverheatDogmaTests.An_overheated_turret_builds_its_shot_with_the_overload_bonus_and_a_bare_one_builds_the_base` (PostgreSQL-backed). It tests the dogma side only; the host step that marks a running overheated module Overloaded (`FlownShipAsync`) has no test in this change.
- **Tracking disruption and computers reach gunnery weapons only (FP-10).** A weapon whose type requires Gunnery (SDE required skill 3300, read on the host) carries `RequiresGunnery` (protocol 35). An NPC hull gun (the laser, projectile and precursor families of `NpcArmament.TurretFamily`) is set to true, as those guns' types require Gunnery in the SDE; a hull has no fitted type to read. The vorton projector (required skills 55033 and 55034) is not reached. The precursor disintegrator requires Gunnery in the SDE, so it is reached. EVE.js `projectedWeaponModifiers.js` filters on the same skill. The client keys the filter on the victim's category (a ship: Gunnery; a drone, fighter or entity: no filter); eve.rs keys it on the weapon's type for every victim, so a vorton fired at a drone or fighter victim is not reached here (not modelled; no case in the tests). Tests: `a_tracking_disruptor_reaches_a_gunnery_turret_and_not_a_vorton_projector` (mutation: the filter removed, the test fails), and the simlink fixtures (`requiresGunnery`).
- **Smartbomb near a gate or station (WG-4): not built.** The wiki gives 3000 m, 5000 m and 6000 m (the last marked [Verify]) by bomb size. Neither EVE.js (`smartbombRuntime.js` has no such check) nor the client (`EMPWave.py`, `pythonEffects.py`) carries the rule or a refusal text, and no SDE attribute names it. No refusal is written.

## Module rules, track B3 (2026-10-09)

Each item names the source that decided it. The SDE and CCP material come first, then the EVE University wiki (secondary, equal in the sense that a wiki line is used where no CCP text or SDE attribute is found), then EveJS and the V24.01 client code (eve-logic).

- **Smartbomb near a station or stargate (WG-4), built.** A smartbomb does not start when the surface of a station or stargate is less than the larger of 5 km and the bomb's range from the ship, the range held to 6 km (the largest value the wiki gives; a 250 km QA field is held to 6 km, not to its own range, see the audit below). Source: the EVE University wiki (Smartbombs): "They cannot be used close to stations, stargates, or acceleration gates"; the three stated values (a 3 km Vehemence bomb held to 5 km, a 5 km one to 5 km, a 6 km Regressive to 6 km, the last marked [Verify]) fit "max(5 km, range)"; no SDE attribute names the distance (checked by name), and EveJS (`smartbombRuntime.js`) has no such check. Refusal `SMARTBOMB_NEAR_STATION`, with a notice of ours: no client or EveJS text exists. The check is at activation only; a ship that moves into the zone after the bomb starts is not stopped (no source says so). Acceleration gates do not exist in eve.rs. Tests: `a_smartbomb_does_not_start_within_five_kilometres_of_a_station_or_its_range_when_that_is_more` (mutation: the check removed, the test fails on its first refusal assertion); the older smartbomb tests now place their scenes clear of the station (`clear_of_the_station`), since their undock point is within the restriction.
- **Nanite paste and overheating (WG-5), not built.** The EVE University wiki (Overheating) states: paste repairs heat damage in space; "No module can be overheated while any other module is being repaired using paste"; "A module cannot be used while it is being repaired"; paste cannot repair a fully burned-out module; a repair is cancelled by a gate, filament or cyno jump, with the part done kept and only its paste used; a repair can run while the module reloads. The CCP patch notes were searched for the paste-while-overheated text and not found, so the wiki is the source. Not built: eve.rs has no paste repair at all. The client calls `godma.RepairModule` and `StopRepairModule` (eve-logic `godma.py` 2225-2270) and no host handler for them exists in `dotnet/src`. A paste repair needs that RPC, the paste item consumed per repaired point, the repair clock, the cancellation on a jump and the overheat interlock. Heat repair on a dock is in B2 (the rack heat clears).
- **Remote assistance and the safety colour (WG-6), built as a policy test.** The Crimewatch policy already refuses an assist with the pilot's safety: at green a suspect or criminal action is refused, at yellow a criminal one (`WithSafety` in `Eve.Crimewatch/Aggression.cs`), and the host re-asks the pilot's own level after a safety refusal (`SpaceSessions.Modules.cs`). Source: the EVE University wiki (Remote assistance): "Enable safety (green safety) will stop any action that is a Suspect or Criminal offence"; "Partial safety (yellow safety) will allow actions making you a Suspect but will stop any action that is a Criminal offence". Test added: `Remote_assistance_is_held_back_by_the_safety_colour_the_pilot_has_set`. No host-level database test was added for the refusal path. A conflict is kept as it is: the wiki says aiding a suspect is "generally a Suspect offence", while the policy makes a high-security suspect target a criminal action (`Remote_assistance_uses_security_class_and_combat_permissions`); the existing decision stays, and the source of the high-security rule is not in the sources read.
- **Weapons timer on a jam (WG-7), decided: not applied.** The wiki's jam line reads "If you happen to get a jam off on a person playing docking games he won't be able to get weapons timer for 20 seconds" (ECM drones 5 s); the same page says a jam lasts 20 s and removes the jammed pilot's ability to target anything but its jammers. The line does not say that the jammed pilot gets a weapons timer, and EveJS starts none on a jam (its only jam timer is the HUD's `usesJamTimer`, `hudIconRuntime.js`). So no timer is started. Revisit if a source states it.
- **HAW fitting restriction (WG-8), decided: not built.** The heavy assault missile launcher types (group 771 "Missile Launcher Heavy Assault", for example 20138) carry no fitting restriction attribute in the SDE (no `canFitShipGroup*` or `canFitShipType*`), and the wiki's "HAW only usable by Dreadnoughts" is not in the static data. A server check would have no source.
- **Cloak types (M-3 part), verified, no change.** A warp-safe cloak (`cloakingWarpSafe`, covert ops) carries its ship into warp; the standard (`cloaking`) and the prototype (`cloakingPrototype`) cloaks hold it (`SmartbombEffect.cs` 96-97, from `shipCloak.js` `isWarpSafeActiveModuleEffectState`). A cloak that is not warp-safe holds its ship out of warp (`deployable/tests.rs` `a_cloak_that_is_not_warp_safe_holds_its_ship_and_a_covert_one_does_not`); the prototype's package is tested in `SpaceServiceTests`. Cloaks refuse with `CLOAK_PROXIMITY_BLOCKED` within 2 km of an object (`activation.rs`).
- **Eject with a 906 module (906 eject rule), not applicable yet.** The SDE text of attribute 906 is "prevent ejection from the ship ... extend the log out ship removal timer". eve.rs has no eject command (no RPC, no handler in `dotnet/src`), so there is no path to refuse. The logoff timer has no code either. Recorded, not built.
- **Bastion micro jump drive block (WP16), built.** The client's table (`bastionMode.py` line 89, V24.01: `SKILL_MODIFIER_CHANGES_FOR_LOCATION` row `dgmAssModAdd, 4385, activationBlocked, activationBlockedStrength`) adds the bastion's strength (attribute 1350) to the activation block (1349) of the ship's modules that require skill 4385 (Micro Jump Drive Operation, type 4385, read from the SDE). The SDE gives the bastion module (type 33400, Bastion Module I) effect 6658 with no such modifier (`modifierInfo` of 6658 has no 1349 entry). The client's effect id 5788 (`dogma/const.py` `effectMarauderModeEffect`) is the same bastion effect under the client's id; the table is keyed on the SDE id, 6658. The row is a `LocationRequiredSkill` modifier on the ship domain, which `DogmaWorld` applies to the modules that require the skill, while the bastion runs. The host refuses a module whose world value of 1349 is positive with `MODULE_BLOCKED` (the client's `ModuleIsBlocked`), in `SpaceSessions.Modules.cs`. Approval: this track approves the three-row client table as WP16 asked (it is a citation-backed table, not a data dump; a senior should still confirm it). Tests: `BastionModeTests` (three: the table, the block only while the bastion runs, a module that does not need the skill is not blocked); mutation: the table row removed, two tests fail. No database test covers the host refusal, and no live client trace exists.
- **Not built in this track (each with its exact blocker):**
  - WG-3 module scripts: tracking speed and optimal range are verified with a loaded script (`ModuleScriptTests`, PostgreSQL). Sensor booster, ECCM, guidance and flex hardener are not started (each needs an SDE-backed case).
  - FP-8 target resistances (2112 to 2116, 2135): needs `resist` fields on the sensors block and on each `Remote` in the wire, a protocol change.
  - FP-C4b and FP-C5 (refresh running effects): needs a new `SimUpdateEffect` message (wire).
  - FP-C7 ECM burst: needs the simulation to report the ships a burst jammed (wire).
  - FP-C8 automatic reload: the simulation ends the effect at the last round with `NO_CHARGES` (`modules/effects.rs` 613) before the host sees `ChargeSpent`; a reload needs a pending-reload state in the simulation (the EVE.js `localCycleRuntime.js` queues it and resumes at the next cycle), which is a design change, not a host change.
  - M-6 scanner, analyzer and relic modules: the simulation has no scan or analyzer bodies yet.
  - M-3 leftovers: no item listed in the audit beyond the cloak types above.
  - WP17 assistance and jam overlays: the sim-side overlays are not built. Their stacking rule is the SDE stackable flag: every overlay attribute is stackable=true, so no penalty applies (client `dogma/attributes/itemAttributeFactory.py:35`).
  - WP19 velocity limits: the cynosural field generator has no body, so its 1334 band is not reached. The bomb launcher is reached and tested.
  - WP13 mode events (MR-D2): the cancelled deferral is not carried out in this track; no subscriber consumes the events, so none is published (AGENTS rule 5).

## Module rules, track B4 (2026-10-09)

Each item names the source that decided it. CCP material and the SDE come first, then the EVE University wiki, then EVE.js and the V24.01 client (eve-logic).

- **Nanite paste repair (WG-5), built.** The client calls `InitiateModuleRepair(Many)` and `StopModuleRepair` on dogmaIM (eve-logic `godma.py` 2204-2270). The rules are EVE.js `moduleRepair.js`: the repair runs in space, on a module that is damaged, not burnt out and not running, at the character's `moduleRepairRate` (1267, skill-modified) per minute, for paste priced by the module's damage (the character's `shipBrokenModuleRepairCostMultiplier` 1277, the type's `repairCostMultiplier` 187 and `repairCostPercent` 396). Differences from EVE.js: (a) the paste is taken when the repair ends, for the part mended ("the part done kept and only its paste used", wiki Overheating); EVE.js takes the whole paste at the start. The start still needs the whole cost in the hold and says so with the client's own `NotEnoughRepairMaterialToFinishAllRepairs`. (b) No module can be overheated while a repair runs, and no repair starts while a module is overheated (wiki Overheating). (c) A module under repair cannot be activated (wiki). The refusal texts for (b) and (c) are ours: the client and EVE.js have none, and they are shown as notices. (d) A repair ends when the pilot leaves the ship: a gate jump (named by the wiki with filament and cyno jumps), a dock, a board, an eject or a log off. A repair ended by a leave takes its paste and its damage; the client is not told the paste change at that moment (see TODO section 19). Tests: `RepairTests` (rate, cost, paste taken for the part mended, the stack used up, the repair that stops where the paste ends), `ModuleRepairHostTests` (start, short of paste, a second start, the stop, a running or undamaged module, the eject refusal).

- **Eject with a module that prevents it (906), built as a refusal.** The SDE text of attribute 906 is "prevent ejection from the ship it is fitted to". eve.rs has an eject command (`ShipService.Eject`, `SpaceSessions.EjectAsync`); the B3 note that it had none was wrong. The client's eject (eve-logic `menuFunctions.Eject`, `ship.Eject`) has no 906 check. EVE.js refuses an eject only while an entosis link runs (`transitions.js` 3145-3163). The SDE gives 906 to siege, bastion, cynosural, jump portal, burst, doomsday structure and similar modules (82 types), and not to the entosis link (checked in `sde.typeDogma`). A running module with 906 set refuses the eject with a CustomNotify, the reference's shape; the words are ours, since no client or EVE.js text names this module. The logout timer that 906 also names, and 1934, are not built (no reference text, MR-D9). Test: `A_siege_module_that_runs_prevents_ejection_and_the_pilot_stays_in_its_ship`.

- **Weapons timer on a jam (WG-7), decided: no change beyond the attacker's start.** A jam is an offensive effect (`SimRemote.Jam` is in `SpaceSessions.IsOffensive`), so the jammer's weapons timer starts when its module starts, as for every attack (`Aggression.Record`, which changes the attacker's own state only). The jammed pilot gets no timer from the jam. EVE.js starts a weapons timer only in `recordWeaponTimer`, which command bursts call (`commandBurst.js` 607). The wiki's jam line says the jammed pilot "won't be able to get weapons timer for 20 seconds": the jam keeps the target from one, it does not give the target one. The simulation does not report a landed jam to the host, so a landed-jam rule cannot be applied in the host either. The reading in the wiki gap report (a landed jam gives the target a 20 s timer) is not applied.

- **Fitting restriction for heavy assault launchers (WG-8), closed: already enforced.** `FittingRules.Check` applies the SDE's `canFitShipGroup01` to `08` (1298 to 1301, 1872 to 1883 and up) and `canFitShipType1` to `4` (1302 to 1305) on the fit RPC (`CharacterShips.cs` 197). The heavy assault launchers (group 771, 14 types) carry none of these attributes in the SDE, so there is no rule to add. The wiki's "heavy assault missiles only for dreadnoughts" is not in the static data and is not applied.

- **Mode events (WP13), built, publish only.** A module's start and stop are announced on the event bus (`ship.module.started`, `ship.module.stopped`), and a siege module's (SDE group 515) also as `ship.siege.entered` and `ship.siege.left` (`ShipModeTopics`). An accepted activation announces the start; a deactivation or an offline announces the stop. Nothing subscribes yet, so no consumer behaviour exists; the topic names are the contract for the subscribers the module rules name (missions, crimewatch). The announcements use the space layer's `Announce`, as the other space events do. Test: `A_siege_module_that_starts_and_stops_is_announced_as_a_module_and_as_the_siege_mode`.

- **Target resistance (FP-8), built host-side, no wire change.** EVE.js `resolveTargetResistanceMultiplier` (`hostileModuleRuntime.js` 565-575) and `resolveRemoteAssistanceMultiplier` (`assistanceModuleRuntime.js` 414-425) give the multiplier: the target's resistance attribute clamped to 0..1, 1 when the target does not have it. The attributes are EVE.js's: 2112 sensor dampener, 2113 weapon disruption, 2114 target painter, 2115 stasis webifier and tackle, 2045 energy warfare, and for remote assistance the assisting module's `remoteResistanceID` (2138), defaulting to 2116. The host applies the multiplier to the strength the remote carries (its amount, its percentages or its web part) when the effect starts, with the target as it flies (its running modules' overlays included). Scramble and jam keep their own rules (EVE.js names neither). EVE.js resolves the multiplier at each cycle; this resolves it once per activation, which differs only if a target's resistance changes during the effect. A wire field was not needed for this, so the protocol is not bumped. Drones are not resisted. Tests: `RemoteResistanceTests` (the attribute of each remote, the clamp and default, the weakened shape).

- **Sensor boosters (WG-3, part): the self booster adds its sensor strengths.** The SDE gives Sensor Booster I 40 on each of the four sensor strength percents (`scanGravimetricStrengthPercent` 1027 to 1030); an ECCM charge adds its own (ECCM - Radar I: 80 on radar). The self-boost effect `sensorBoosterActivePercentage` sent only lock range and scan resolution. It now sends the four strength percents too, as the remote sensor booster already does (`ProjectedEffect.cs`, `remoteSensorBoostFalloff`). Test: `A_sensor_booster_adds_its_sensor_strengths_to_the_ship_as_the_remote_booster_does`.

## Fighter LaunchBomb (track C3, 2026-10-09)

Protocol 37 (ADR 0013 epoch 37, ADR 0011 addendum of 2026-10-09). The sources in order of rank: the SDE (the squadron type's attribute 2324, the bomb type's attributes, the ability's duration 2349 and flags), the EVE.js reference (`server/src/services/fighter/fighterRuntime.js`: `executeFighterBombCycle` 3011-3132, `validateAbilityActivation` 4242-4375, `buildAbilitySecurityRestrictionError` 1735-1744; `fighterAbilities.js` 43, 72-77), the V24.01 client (`eve-logic` `eve/client/script/parklife/fightersSvc.py` `ActivateAbilitySlotsAtPoint`: the point goes to `fighterMgr.CmdActivateAbilitySlots`; `fighters/abilityAttributes.py` maps the ability to `effectFighterAbilityLaunchBomb`).

| Status | What differs from EVE.js, or what was decided | Evidence and reason | Open item |
|---|---|---|---|
| DECIDED | The blast is one area of ADR 0011: each ship within the radius of the centre (its surface distance, `ships_in_area`) takes the four damages through `damage_ship`, once. The centre is the point the pilot named, the radius is the bomb type's `empFieldRange` (99), else `explosionRange` (107): 15 000 m for the Micro Electron Bomb 41549. | EVE.js uses the same attributes and the same damage vector (`executeFighterBombCycle`). The surface distance is the Area's rule (ADR 0011); EVE.js measures from the ship's centre, a difference of the hull radius. | Not verified in the client. |
| DECIDED | The blast goes off once, when the 60 s window (2349) ends. There is no bomb flight and no ball. | EVE.js: a finished window runs the cycle (`executeFighterBombCycle`, `continueActive: false`). | The bomb type's `explosionDelay` (281, 12 000 ms on these types) is in the SDE and EVE.js does not read it; not applied (following the executable reference). Whether the V24.01 client shows a delayed burst is not verified. |
| DECIDED | No ship is spared: the launcher's own ship is hit if it is in the radius, and undock protection (`protected_until`) is not read. | EVE.js hits every damageable dynamic entity in range except the fighter itself (no launcher exclusion, no protection check). The bomb track spares the launcher for its own bombs (ADR 0011, its addendum); a fighter's blast follows the reference, which is the executable source. | A ship in its undock protection is hit by a fighter's blast. Not verified against the client. |
| DECIDED | The damage is the bomb type's raw damage, not multiplied by the pilot's missile damage multiplier (212), and not scaled by the squadron size. | EVE.js uses `emDamage`, `thermalDamage`, `kineticDamage`, `explosiveDamage` directly. Track A's bomb launcher multiplies by 212 because a launcher's charge is a missile; the fighter's bomb is not a missile in the reference. | none |
| DECIDED | The security refusal is the ability's flags (`disallowInHighSec` and `disallowInLowSec`, both set on LaunchBomb): high security is refused `CantInHighSecSpace`, low security `CantInEmpireSpace`, zero security runs. | EVE.js `buildAbilitySecurityRestrictionError` (1735-1744) and the catalogue (`Data/fighter-abilities.json`, ability 7). The host reads the system security from `SpaceSessions.SecurityOfSystem`. | none |
| DECIDED | A point is required: an activation with no point (an item target) is refused `TARGET_POINT_REQUIRED` ("That fighter ability requires a target point."). The point is not range-checked: the LaunchBomb ability carries no range attribute (`rangeAttributeID` null), so EVE.js's range check is skipped (`rangeMeters` 0). | EVE.js `validateAbilityActivation` (point-targeted branch) and the catalogue; the client sends the point (`fightersSvc.py`). | none |
| DECIDED | A bomb type with no radius or no damage is refused at activation (`NO_BOMB`). EVE.js deactivates at the window's end without a blast (`executeFighterBombCycle` returns `deactivate: true`). | A refusal is visible to the pilot; the reference's silent end is not. No such bomb type is in the SDE. | none |
| LIMITATION | Protocol 37 is one bump, in this track's batch. The blast is not run in the graphical client: no MITM capture of a LaunchBomb was made. | `tools/mitm/README.md`; `oracle/fixtures/simlink/messages.json` (one valid bomb, two invalid) | a capture when a scenario can be driven |

## Module rules, track B5 (2026-10-09)

Each item names the source that decided it. CCP material and the SDE come first, then EVE.js and the V24.01 client code (eve-logic), then the EVE University wiki.

- **Refresh of a running effect (FP-C4b, FP-C5), built.** EVE.js rebuilds a running module's live attributes from the ship as it flies (`server/src/space/modules/liveModuleAttributes.js`: an overloaded module projects its overloaded strength, and a computer projects onto the turrets it feeds). eve.rs had no refresh: a running effect kept the numbers it was started with. Built: the host rebuilds each running effect with the activation's own builder (`ModulePackages.Of`, against the ship as it flies) after an overheat change (single and rack), an activation, a deactivation, an offline and a simulation end, and sends `SimUpdateEffect` only when the rebuilt numbers differ. The simulation applies it at the start of the effect's next cycle, the boundary an overheat takes (`pending_overload`). The client is told the changed speed, capacitor or range at that cycle (`OnModuleAttributeChanges`). Difference: EVE.js recomputes per cycle; eve.rs recomputes at each host change and applies at the next cycle start. Tests: `a_refresh_of_a_running_effect_takes_effect_at_the_start_of_its_next_cycle` (mutation: the notice removed, the test fails), `RefreshHostTests` (an overheat is sent and its end is sent; mutation of the host call pending, see the track's report). The computer path (a tracking computer starting or stopping changes the turrets it feeds) runs through the activation and deactivation refresh; no test drives it, because it needs a fitted computer with its own state.

- **Turret reload from the hold (FP-C8), built.** EVE.js queues an automatic reload when the last round is spent and resumes the effect at the next cycle (`server/src/space/modules/localCycleReloads.js`, `server/src/services/dogma/dogmaService.js` `queueAutomaticModuleReload`, `server/src/space/runtime/moduleChargeReload.js`). eve.rs: the simulation parks a turret whose charge reloads (`Charge.reloads`, set when the module has a reload time, attribute 1795) instead of ending it with `NoCharges`; the host loads the kind from the hold through the path a pilot's load in space takes (the reload time is timed there, `DogmaIMService.StartReload`), and answers the simulation with the rounds it added; with nothing of the kind in the hold the answer is none and the gun stops with the existing `NoCharges` notice. A deactivate of a parked gun ends it at once (there is no cycle to finish). Differences: the reload window the client sees is the in-space load's notices (`OnModuleAttributeChanges` next-activation time, `OnChargeBeingLoadedToModule`), not EVE.js's own; this is not verified with the client. Tests: `a_turret_that_reloads_waits_for_its_load_and_resumes_with_it_at_the_next_cycle`, `a_reloading_gun_that_is_switched_off_while_it_waits_stops_at_once`, `ReloadServiceTests` (a dry gun is loaded after the reload time and answered; a dry gun with nothing to load is answered with none).

- **Speed band of a module (WP19), built as a rule.** SDE attribute 1334 `maxVelocityActivationLimit` (Cynosural Field Generator I: 500 m/s) and 2608 `minVelocityActivationLimit` (Bomb Launcher I and II: 1 m/s) are carried as rules (protocol 38) and refused at activation outside the band, by the ship's speed as the scene moves it: `VELOCITY_TOO_HIGH` and `VELOCITY_TOO_LOW` are the simulation's own names (no client or EVE.js text names them). The carriers have no bodies in eve.rs (they are refused as not implemented), so no module reaches the rule yet. Test: `velocity_tests::a_module_with_a_speed_band_starts_only_within_it`.

- **Not built in this track (each with its exact blocker):**
  - FP-C7 ECM burst jammed ships and its offensive gate: closed in track B6a (protocol 46, section "Track B6a" below).
  - M-6 scanners and analyzers (Cargo Scanner, Ship Scanner, Data Analyzer, Subverter, relics): the simulation has no scan bodies, and the EVE.js reference (`server/src/space/modules/scannerModuleRuntime.js`) was not ported in this track.
  - WP17 stacking of overlapping assistance and jam modifiers: decided by the SDE stackable flag (every overlay attribute is `stackable=true`, so the modifiers multiply without the penalty; client `itemAttributeFactory.py:35`).
  - WG-3 scripts and charges with a loaded script: Tracking Speed Script 29001 and Optimal Range Script 28999 are tested (`ModuleScriptTests`); the other script families are not.
  - WP12 and 1934: the SDE gives attribute 1934 `deactivateIfOffensive` (default 1: "If module is offensive should it deactivate on disconnect"). The disconnect path is `OnSessionClosedAsync` (`SpaceSessions.cs:820`) to `Scene::leave` (`scene/docking.rs:64`), which drops the running effects, so default-1934 modules stop. CHANGED: a bastion (1934 = 0) should keep running after its pilot logs off; eve.rs has no logged-off ship in space to keep it, so it stops (open).

## Module rules, track B8 (2026-10-09)

Each item names the source that decided it. The V24.01 client (eve-logic) decides who reloads a gun; the other sources are those of track B5.

- **Who reloads a gun that runs dry (FP-C8), decided.** The host reloads from the hold only for the accounts the operator names in `EVE_AUTO_RELOAD_ACCOUNTS` (a bot with no client UI). Every other account has its gun stopped with `NoCharges` when its rounds are spent, as before track B5. Source: the client file `eve/client/script/ui/inflight/shipModuleButton/shipmodulebutton.py`. Its `AutoReload()` (lines 727-738) loads the module's ammunition again when an activation or target effect ends with no charge in it (lines 1295-1297), and the per-module setting `settings.char.autoreload` is on by default (lines 147, 152, 168, 180, 188). So the real client reloads its own guns, and a server reload would load them twice. The reference recording `oracle/fixtures/scenarios/reload.json` agrees from the other side: its turret stops with `NoCharges` with more in the hold (`tools/headless-client/tests/test_reload.py`, docstring).
  - Built: `ModulePackages.Of` takes `autoReload`; for a client it clears `Charge.Reloads` on the effect it returns (`WithoutReload`), so the simulation stops the gun at its last round, the path a client had before track B5. The simulation still parks a gun whose charge reloads, which a bot needs. No protocol change: the flag is already on the wire (protocol 38). The host asks `AutoReloadsOf(pilot)` for the start (`SpaceSessions.Modules.cs`) and for the refresh of a running gun (`SpaceSessions.Refresh.cs`), so a running gun keeps the same setting.
- **NPCs: no change.** An NPC's weapon is a `SimNpcWeapon` built without a charge (`dotnet/src/Eve.Npc/NpcArmament.cs`, `HullWeapon` and `MissileHullWeapon`: "The NPC has no rounds to spend"). It never spends rounds, never parks and never reaches the cargo reload. Its ammunition is its own armament logic (`SpaceSessions.Npc.cs`).
- **The headless client stays a client session.** `tools/headless-client` replays the recorded reference (`reload.json`), where the turret stops with `NoCharges`; opting it in would fail that replay. An operator opts a bot in by listing its account id. The opt-in is not a flag in the login: the handshake is the real client's (`tools/headless-client/evecli/handshake.py` speaks the same shape), and a flag there would be a protocol change that a real client could send as well.
- **Per account, not per ship.** The account owns the ships it flies. A per-ship setting would need persistence, and no bot needs one.
- **A malformed setting stops the host.** `EVE_AUTO_RELOAD_ACCOUNTS` entries that are not positive ids throw at start-up (`HostConfiguration.AutoReloadUserIds`), so a typo cannot switch a reload on or off silently. The house rule is the one `EVE_NEW_CHARACTER_EXPERIENCE_MODE` already follows.
- **A conflict the decision does not settle.** Track B5 recorded that EVE.js queues an automatic reload (`localCycleReloads.js`); `reload.json` and `test_reload.py` say the reference turret does not reload by itself. The client file decides for client sessions, so this track does not depend on which is right. For a bot, the reload is the host's own rule and makes no claim about EVE.js.
- Tests: `AmmoHostTests` (`A_loaded_gun_is_started...` expects a client's gun with `Reloads: false`; `A_client_gun_whose_rounds_run_out_is_not_reloaded_by_the_host`; `A_bot_account_named_for_reloads_has_its_gun_loaded_from_its_hold_when_the_rounds_run_out`), `MissileHostTests` (a bot launcher), `AutoReloadAccountsTests` (the setting's parse).
- Not run in this track: `tools/headless-client/tests/test_reload.py` against a live host (it needs `EVE_LIVE_HOST` and three minutes with `EVE_LIVE_SLOW=1`), and the graphical client.

## Scanner modules, track M-6 (2026-10-09)

Each item names the source that decided it: the SDE first (effect records 46 and 47, attributes 6, 73, 125, 126, 188, 482, 510, 511, 1014), then EVE.js
(`server/src/space/modules/scannerModuleRuntime.js`, `server/src/space/runtime/moduleRuntimeCallbacks.js`), then the V24.01 client (eve-logic
`eve/client/script/ui/inflight/shipHud/shipUI.py` 1059-1077 and `eve/client/script/ui/inflight/shipscan.py`).

- **Scanner bodies, built (protocol 40).** A scanner is one cycle (attribute 1014 is 1 on both effects). The simulation keeps the lock and the reach and parks the module
  at the cycle's end; the host reveals the rows (`SpaceContainers.Scan.cs`). Source: the SDE effect records, EVE.js `executeScannerCycle`.
- **Decided: the resistance is read at the cycle's end, not at activation.** EVE.js refuses the start (`TARGET_RESISTS_SCAN`, no cycle spent) when the target's
  resistance wins the roll. The simulation holds no target attributes; the host reads them at the cycle's end, so the first cycle's capacitor is spent. The refusal
  carries the reference's words: "That ship is resistant to your scan." Open: a pre-activation check needs the resistance in the activation request (a wire field).
- **Decided: a target out of reach at the cycle's end gets no reveal and the scan ends as its target is lost.** EVE.js stops with the stop reason `range` and sends no
  notice. eve.rs uses the existing target-lost ending (reason 2), which the client shows as the module stopping.
- **Decided: a ship scan's capacitor is in GJ.** The charge is the target's charge fraction (`SimCondition.Charge`, asked of the target's session) times its capacity
  (dogma attribute 482 of the target's ship). EVE.js reads the live entity capacitor. The capacity is the host's dogma value; a fitting change in space shows late.
- **Decided: the reach is the effect's own attributes.** Range from the effect record's `rangeAttributeID` (125 or 126), falloff from `falloffAttributeID` (510 for the
  ship scanner, none for the cargo scanner, a hard cutoff). The reveal chance is EVE.js's `resolveScanRevealChance`: whole to optimal plus one metre, then
  `0.5 ^ ((distance - optimal) / falloff)^2`, nothing past falloff plus one metre.
- **Decided: a ship scan rolls each fitted item.** EVE.js draws once per fitted item when the chance is short of one. The host draws with `Random.Shared` (a seam,
  `SpaceContainers.ScanRandom`, for tests). The cycle's own roll decides only the resistance. The reference draws from the scene's random.
- **Decided: a scan is not an attack.** The SDE gives effects 46 and 47 `isOffensive` false. The simulation starts no weapons timer and no aggression.
- **Decided: a granted scan is answered `Complete` false.** The simulation ends a work target when `Complete` is true (a rock mined out, a wreck salvaged). A scan
  leaves the ship in space, so the effect ends as done (reason 1) and the target stays.
- **Decided: the pilot alone is told the reveal, after the record is kept.** The target is told nothing (EVE.js sends only to the scanning session; the V24.01 client
  has no "you were scanned" notification). A repeated cycle is answered from the record and reveals nothing again.
- **Decided: `TARGET_SELF` is the reference's `DeniedTargetSelf`.** Added to `ModuleRefusals` (EVE.js `dogmaService.js` maps `TARGET_SELF` the same way).
- **Limit: NPC ships are refused as `NO_TARGET`.** The hold and fittings come from the item store, which holds no NPC inventory. EVE.js scans any entity.
- **Not built in this track (each with its blocker).**
  - Data Analyzer (`doHacking`), Relic Analyzer and Data Subverter (`hackOrbital`): the reference is EVE.js `moduleOperation.js` (the analyzer section) and the client's
    hacking window. The minigame and loot container interior is the only named refusal allowed for them, and no body is written. The EVE University wiki "Hacking"
    page (read for this track) gives tier 1 analyzers a base virus coherence of 40 and strength of 20, tier 2 60 and 30, and +10 coherence per Hacking or
    Archaeology level. It is silent on range, cycle time, activation and the Data Subverter, so those come from the SDE. Blocked: eve.rs has no hackable site entity
    for the target to be validated against (EVE.js `_resolveAnalyzerHackingContext` reads a materialised container's hacking state).
  - Survey Scanner: no effect in the scanner runtime. `surveyScan` belongs to the mining chipsets (TODO section 7).
  - Probe lifecycle notifications (E-06): depend on `LaunchProbes` (RC-7).
- **Limitation: no graphical client run.** The MITM recipe needs a live EVE.js and a headless scenario that scripts a scanner. Neither exists, so the client's windows
  were not driven.

## Lance beacon (track C3, 2026-10-09)

## Module rules, track B6c: wiki rules and SDE numbers (2026-10-09)

Precedence applied: SDE wins for numbers and attributes; the EVE University wiki (wiki.eveuniversity.org) is the authority for mechanics and rules, implemented as written; CCP docs and EveJS / eve-logic support them and fix client wire shapes. Conflicts are recorded here.

- **Stacking penalty (WP17), wiki vs client.** The wiki "Stacking penalties" page gives 100%, 86.9%, 57.1%, 28.3%, 10.6%, 3.0% and the formula S(u) = e^-(u/2.67)^2, the same curve as the client (`stackingNurfedAttribute.py`). The wiki says only percentage effects are penalised and that positive and negative effects are penalised separately. The client also penalises the PreMul, PostMul, PreDiv and PostDiv operations (`dgmNurfableOperations`). Rule decided: the implementation keeps the client's operation set, since the wiki's "percentage" covers the multiplicative modifiers it lists; the separate positive and negative rows match the wiki. Exempt sources: the wiki lists skills, ship bonuses, implants, hardwirings and boosters; the client exempts skills, implants, ships, charges and subsystems. Rule decided: the client list is kept (charges and subsystems are not named in the wiki, so the client decides them), and boosters and hardwirings are to be confirmed against the SDE category before a change. Tests: `EngineTests` penalty set (existing).
- **Cynosural field generator (WP19), wiki.** Wiki rules: the field is lit only by a fleet member, by a ship slower than 500 m/s (the SDE 1334 value, consistent), and never in high-security space; a Mobile Cynosural Inhibitor within 200 km blocks lighting but does not remove existing fields; the ship's nominal speed becomes zero after lighting and it cannot dock or eject; base activation time is ten minutes; consumption is 500 Liquid Ozone per cycle (minimum 250, maximum 450 with Cynosural Field Theory). The wiki does not state how long a lit field lasts. Status: NOT BUILT. The cyno body is the remaining WP19 carrier; the speed band (1334) is in place and waits for it.
- **Cyno consumption, SDE vs wiki.** The wiki's 500 Liquid Ozone per cycle is a mechanic description; the SDE consumption quantity (714) and type (713) for the generator decide the number. Not yet compared.

Specified, not built. No code changed in this track. The sources below settle what a body needs. Two data facts block the buff values (rows marked OPEN), and the build needs one wire batch (ADR 0013, one bump by the integrator, next free epoch at build time: `origin/main` is at 39). The track rules also ask for a PG-backed run and a MITM capture; the worktree had about 2.1 GB free on `/home/user` (below the 3 GB floor), and the shared target (11 GB) belongs to the other tracks, so no build was started.

| Status | Item | What the sources show | What the body needs |
|---|---|---|---|

Mk I 42526 to 42530, Mk II 43551, 43552, 43554 to 43556, the named variants 88261 ("Vigilant" Shield) and 88263 ("Tyrannos" Armor), Expedition 89608, 89615, 89616, Presidential 92455 to 92458. | Each carries one default effect of the family: Shield 6733 `moduleBonusWarfareLinkShield`, Armor 6732, Skirmish 6734, Information 6735 (also Expedition), Mining Foreman 6736 (warp-safe; range attribute 54; duration attribute 73; discharge attribute 6). All 18 types of group 1770 carry cycle 60000 ms and capacitor 25. Mk I, Mk II and Expedition I and II have range 15000 m and `buffDuration` 60000 ms; the named ones (88261, 88263), Sisters Expedition (89616) and the Presidential ones (92455, 92456, 92458) have range 18000 m and `buffDuration` 80000 ms. Mk II: range 15000 m, cycle 60000 ms, capacitor 25. Attribute 604 (`chargeGroup1`) names the charge group: 1769 Shield, 1774 Armor, 1773 Information, 1771 Mining Foreman, 1772 Skirmish, 4905 Expedition. | The module's cycle, capacitor and range come from its own dogma (generic `ShipEffect.CycleOf`). The charge group gates the load (`CHARGE_NOT_COMPATIBLE`). |
| VERIFIED (SDE) | Burst charges (the burst materials): group 1769 (Shield: 42694 Active Shielding, 42695 Shield Harmonizing, 42696 Shield Extension), 1774 (Armor: 42832 to 42834), 1773 (Information: 42835 to 42837), 1771 (Mining Foreman: 42829 to 42831, 90733), 1772 (Skirmish: 42838 to 42840), 4905 (Expedition: 89612 to 89614). | Each charge carries `warfareBuffNID` (2468, 2470, 2472, 2536) naming a dbuff collection (for example 42694 names 11, 42695 names 10, 42696 names 12) and `warfareBuffNMultiplier` (2596 to 2599). Effect 6737 `chargeBonusWarfareCharge` (default on the charge, domain `otherID`, that is the module it is loaded into) copies the IDs (PreAssignment) and sets `warfareBuffNValue` (2469, 2471, 2473, 2537) to the module's value times the charge's multiplier (PostMul). | The buff set of a running burst is the charge's IDs with the value of the chain below. Each cycle consumes one charge through the shared charge path (`ChargeSpent`), as the turrets do. |
| VERIFIED (SDE) | Dbuff collections named by the charges: 10 (Shield Resistance Bonus, PostPercent on attributes 271 to 274), 11 (Shield Repair Modules: duration and capacitor-use bonus, PostPercent on 6 for the repair modules by skill), 12, 13 to 26, 60, 2464 to 2466, 2468 (Expedition Virus Coherence, ModAdd), 2474, 2481, 2516, 2517. | `sde.dbuffCollections` rows, with `itemModifiers`, `locationModifiers`, `locationGroupModifiers` and `locationRequiredSkillModifiers`; Each collection carries an `aggregateMode` (Maximum or Minimum); EveJS `createAggregator` reads it to combine two bursts on one attribute. | The registry consumes these rows through the same modifier model as skills and implants. Its first consumer is the burst. |
| OPEN (data) | The value chain of a burst evaluates to 0 on this SDE build. | `warfareBuffNValue` (2469) has SDE `defaultValue` 0.0 and the module type does not set it (43555 has no 2469). Effect 6737 is PostMul (4), so the value is 0 times the multiplier, which is 0 for every burst. EveJS reads the same chain: `liveFittingState.js` `resolveModifierBaseValue` takes the SDE default (0), and the fallback of 1 only applies when no default exists. `commandBurstRuntime.js` `buildCollectionValuesFromAttributes` then keeps the value 0, so the EveJS burst would apply 0 percent. The V24.01 client has no attribute name for these (`warfareBuff` does not occur in `eve-logic`), so it does not settle the base. ## Source priority for the scanner track (user rule, FINAL, 2026-10-09)

- STATS, NUMBERS and ATTRIBUTES: the SDE first.
- LOGIC and MECHANICS: the EVE University wiki next, implemented as written. EVE.js, the CCP documents and eve-logic come at equal rank as the last line.
- Client wire shapes and contracts: eve-logic decides.
- When no source states a spec, the wiki decides.
- A conflict between sources is recorded here with the source that was chosen.

Applied to the scanners:
- Numbers (ranges 125 and 126, falloff 510, cycle 73, capacitor 6, resistances 188 and 511, capacitor capacity 482, one-cycle attribute 1014): the SDE.
- Mechanics (lock and reach, the reveal, the per-item roll, the resistance, the notice, the one-container-level rule): the wiki has no page that states them. The EVE University pages read (`Scanning`, `Hacking`, and the `Cargo_Scanner` and `Ship_Scanner` slugs, which return 404) give no rule for the Cargo or Ship Scanner. So the mechanics come from EVE.js `scannerModuleRuntime.js`, the last line of the order, and no wiki conflict is recorded.
- Wire shapes (`OnCargoScanComplete(shipID, cargoList)`, `OnShipScanCompleted(shipID, capacitorCharge, capacitorCapacity, hardwareList)`): eve-logic's `shipUI.py` and `shipscan.py`.
- Hacking analyzers: the wiki's tier 1 and tier 2 coherence and strength (40/20, 60/30) agree with the SDE (1909 and 1910). The wiki's range, cycle, activation and Data Subverter are not stated, so the SDE attributes stand for those.
- The wiki's survey scanner range (15 km, 23 km with T2) belongs to a different module, which is not part of this runtime. No conflict arises.

**Decisions for the body (recorded so the build follows them; none is built).** D4 (the registry) is a persistent source list on `DogmaWorld` items, keyed by the burst module, cleared when the module stops, and expired by the scheduler at `buffDuration`. D3 (recipients): the same fleet as the burst's pilot (`FleetStore`), a ship in the burst's range, the burst's own ship excluded for the fleet families, and the host sends the list each cycle (ADR 0011 decision 5). The module's cycle is refused while the value chain is OPEN, because a burst with value 0 would be a success the reference does not give.

## Breacher pods, track A4 (2026-10-09)

Source order (the user's rule): the SDE for numbers first, the EVE University wiki for rules next, then EveJS, CCP documents and eve-logic at equal rank. The wiki
page used is the Revenant expansion notes' breacher section (mirrored on the EVE University wiki); its own breacher page is not at the URL the repo's other pages use.

- Numbers, SDE first: the SCARAB Breacher Pod S (85088) and M (85089) carry `dotDuration` 40000 ms (5735), `dotMaxDamagePerTick` 200 and 600 (5736) and
  `dotMaxHPPercentagePerTick` 0.6 (5737). The simulation reads the charge's values after dogma, so the pilot's skills apply: Operation (86260) at level 1 gives
  0.63 (the wiki's "+5 % max target HP damaged per second per level"; the host test pins it). The brief's "600 cap" is the medium's; the small charge's cap is 200. The wiki gives 160 (small) and 800 (medium) a second,
  and 200 and 1000 with max skills: the SDE is used, and the wiki's numbers conflict with it (the SDE wins). The wiki's 50 to 75 s duration (with skills) is the
  base 40 s plus skill bonuses; the breacher code does not read the bonus attributes 5749 and 5750 itself; the dogma layer applies the pilot's skills to the charge's attributes before the simulation reads them (see the percentage above).
- Launcher: 4807, the small (85084) and medium (85085) launchers. The charge's firing effect is `dotMissileLaunching` (12174), which the SDE marks offensive;
  its launch is an aggression though it does no damage. The rate of fire is the launcher's own attribute, as for the other missile launchers.
- A hit: the wiki says "they will not affect their target unless they actually reach and hit" and that a smartbomb can kill a pod before it arrives. The pod flies
  as a missile does (`missile_entity`), so both hold; the simulation takes the pod's damage as zero (its charge has no damage attributes in the SDE).
- Targets: the wiki says a pod "cannot be used against targets such as capsules, structures, stargates etc.", and only player ships and NPCs; the simulation's
  missile targets are ships, so this holds.
- The tick: one second. The wiki says damage "every second"; V24.01's `DOT_TICK_RATE_SEC` is 1 (eve-logic `dotWeapons/common/dotRegistry.py`).
- The damage of a tick: the lower of the percentage of the target's total hit points and the cap, rounded down. The wiki gives "based on the targets total
  hitpoints or a flat cap, whichever is lower", with hit points as shield, armor and hull combined; eve-logic `dotUtil.GetEffectiveDamage` is `int(min(ratio *
  total, cap))`. The total is the three capacities (eve-logic `_GetTotalHp` reads the dogma capacities), not the current points.
- Resistances: the wiki says the effect "completely ignores resistances". The tick goes through the layers with every resonance at one (`Health::apply_unresisted`).
- Stacking: one application works on a ship at a time. The wiki: "the player who has the strongest breacher pod effect will deal damage to the target, and the
  weaker effect stays inert until the stronger one expires". Strongest is the most a tick does at the ship's current total hit points; a tie goes to the one
  already working, then to the one that landed first. An inert application's clock stops until it works. eve-logic differs here: its applications expire on
  absolute time (`expiryTime = added + duration`, `dotPriorityQueue.py`) and pick the highest effective damage with the same tie rule. The wiki is the rule
  (its order is first), so the inert clock is kept; the difference shows only when a weaker effect outlasts the stronger one's run.
- A second pod from the same attacker: the wiki says "a new hit replaces the old effect once it expires". It is read as a queue: it waits, inert, like any other.
  Not a replacement of the attacker's own effect.
- The effect ends when the ship leaves the scene: a jump, a dock or a death (wiki: "until a player leaves the solarsystem or docks up"). It goes on through warp
  and tethers (wiki: "It can continue doing damage while the player is in warp or while the player is tethered"). A restart drops it (the simulation keeps no
  state across a restart, as for missiles in flight).
- A tick that destroys the ship: the killer is the pilot who fired the pod, while that pilot is still in the scene (a choice; the wiki and eve-logic name no
  killer for a DoT death).
- Client-visible: the slim item of the ship carries `dotHpPercentageApplied` = 100 times the tick's damage over the total hit points, told with
  `OnSlimItemChange` when it changes and removed by the plain slim item when the effect ends (eve-logic `dotRegistry.py` `ballpark.UpdateSlimItemField` and
  `DeleteSlimItemField`). A ship that comes into view while a pod's effect runs is not told the field until the next change (a shortcut: the slim item is built
  from the ship, without the effect; upgrade when a ship's slim item reads the applications).
- Not built, a blocker: `OnDotVictimUpdated(targetID, dmgAppsInfo)` (eve-logic `michelle.py`, the attacker's and the target's list of applications). Its
  `dmgAppsInfo` is a Python `set` (`dotInfoPerTarget.py` calls `.discard` on it). eve-marshal's `Value` has no set variant, and neither the repo nor EveJS
  (`server/src/network/tcp/utils/marshal.js`) carries a set opcode, so the payload cannot be encoded from a source here. Until the set's marshal form is known,
  the attacker and the target get no DoT list, only the slim field.
- Per-tick damage messages (`OnDamageMessage`) are not sent for a DoT tick: the sources have no wire shape for it. The target's attributes and damage state go
  through the ordinary hit path.
- Protocol 40 to 41: the missile block (`put_missile`, `read_missile`) ends with a damage-over-time flag, then duration, cap and percentage. A defender's action
  carries the same missile block with a 0 flag. The fixture generator writes the new block and one breacher frame in `oracle/fixtures/simlink/messages.json`.

## Module rules, track B7: ECCM, scripts, the cynosural field generator (2026-10-09)

Precedence applied as in track B6c: SDE for numbers and attributes; the EVE University wiki for mechanics; EveJS (`cynosuralField.js`,
`activationSpaceRestrictions.js`, `modules.js`) as the functional reference; CCP documents and eve-logic not re-read for this track's wire
shapes (the existing codecs were reused and extended by one action, tag 27, ADR 0017).

- **ECCM, self-applied (effect 2231 `scanStrengthBonusPercentActivate`).** SDE: four PostPercent modifiers on the ship's scan strengths (208 to 211)
  from the module's percents (1027 to 1030). Decided: its body is the generic dogma-modifier path (`ScanStrengthEffect` calls
  `DogmaModifierEffect.BuildModifier`), replacing the pending handler. Its types are unpublished in this build, so it is not one of the published-module
  modifier names that `ModuleEffectsTests` checks. Wiki (Electronic warfare, "Countering ECM"): an ECCM script "doubles the sensor strength effect", which
  matches the SDE's +100 on the sensor strength bonus (2282). Tests: `ModuleScriptTests.An_ECCM_script_loaded_in_an_ECCM_module_...`.
- **Projected ECCM (effect 2246 `scanStrengthTargetPercentBonus`).** Still pending: it is a target effect and needs the projected path.
- **Scripts, flex (groups 1701 and 1702: Armor and Shield EM, thermal, kinetic and explosive scripts).** SDE: +200 on the hardener's EM resistance
  bonus and -100 on the other three (2402 to 2405, PostPercent), on the hardener's bonus (984 to 987), which resonance activation (5230, 5231) reads.
  Numbers from the SDE only; the wiki page for these scripts was not read in this track. Tests: `ModuleScriptTests` (armour and shield).
- **Scripts, guidance (group 1400 Missile Range and Missile Precision Script, on the Missile Guidance Computer, group 1396).** SDE: Range adds +100 to
  missile velocity and explosion delay and -100 to the AoE cloud and velocity; Precision is the mirror. Tests: `ModuleScriptTests` (range and precision).
  Open: the Guidance Disruption scripts (group 1569, 40334 and 40335) carry the same modifiers with launcher group 1396, but the computer's charge group is
  1400, so their pairing is not decided here; not tested.
- **Category of ECCM.** The ECCM modules are category 7 (group 202); the ECCM Script is a charge of category 8 (group 910, type 41155). The pairing of a
  script with its module uses the category (`DogmaWorld.Partner`, `IsCharge`), so it works without a special case. Tested with `ModuleScriptTests`.
- **Cynosural field generators (21096 normal, 28646 covert, 52694 industrial; group 658).** SDE numbers: activation duration 600000 ms (normal and
  industrial) and 60000 ms (covert); fuel per cycle 500, 50 and 800 Liquid Ozone (type 16273); speed ceiling 1334 = 500 m/s; spawn radius 2455 = 5000.
  Wiki: activation ten minutes (matches), covert one tenth (matches), fuel 500 normal (matches), industrial base 800 (matches), covert fuel "always 25":
  the SDE says 50. Decided: SDE 50 (SDE first for numbers).
- **When the field is lit.** Wiki: the generator has an activation time of ten minutes before the field is lit. Decided: the field is released at the end
  of the generator's cycle (`SimAction.CynoField`, applied at `CycleEnd`). EveJS (`cynosuralField.js`, `spawnCynosuralFieldForEffect`) spawns the field at
  activation; the difference is recorded here and not copied.
- **Field lifetime.** SDE: each field type has explosionDelay 281 = 600000 ms (21094, 28650, 52696); the same expiry rule as bombs. Decided: the field lives
  its explosionDelay and does not explode. EveJS uses the generator's duration as the field's life and removes the field when the effect stops; with the
  covert generator (60000 ms) that differs from the SDE's field lifetime (600000 ms). Decided: the SDE number; the field is not removed when the generator
  stops. Test: `CynoFieldTypes`, `deployable::tests::a_cynosural_field_is_lit_...`.
- **One cycle per lighting.** The generator's repeat is one: it lights its field once and stops. Fuel is taken before that cycle through the shared
  mechanism (`BurnsFuel`, set by `ShipEffect` from the consumption type).
- **Lighting refusals.** Wiki: "only fleet members can jump to that cyno", "a ship must be in a fleet and slower than 500 m/s" (EveJS `modules.js` quotes
  the same), and "cynos cannot be lit in high-sec". The speed rule is attribute 1334, enforced by the generic velocity rule. Decided: the fleet is
  checked by the host's fleet store (`SpaceSessions.IsInFleetAsync`, set in `AgentRpc`), refused as `CYNO_NOT_IN_FLEET` with our wording, because the
  client's own wording for it is not in the tables read; the high-security rule (the high and the safe classes, `Security.IsHighSecOrHigher`, as the
  wiki's "high-sec" covers 1.0 too) is refused with the client's `CantInHighSecSpace`, as EveJS does for `MODULE_DISALLOWED_IN_HIGHSEC`. The order of the two refusals is the host's choice: the fleet is checked first, so both are reachable in the test rig
  (whose station is in Manifest, security 1.0). EveJS checks the security first.
- **Open, each with its blocker.**
  - Jump-in (fleet-only for normal fields, Black Ops for covert, jump freighters and capital industrial ships for industrial): not built. eve.rs has no
    capital jump drive, so there is no jump to a field to restrict.
  - Mobile Cynosural Inhibitor (200 km): not applied. No inhibitor body exists in eve.rs (a module and a deployable that do not exist yet).
  - Cynosural System Jammer (E-03, a system-wide jam of non-covert generators, EveJS `activationSpaceRestrictions.js`): not applied. The host's
    `BeyonceService.CynoJammerOnlineSimTimeForSystem` is not assigned (E-03), so the jammed state has no source yet.
  - The fleet beacon state (EveJS `setFleetCynoBeaconState` when a field is lit) is not set: the fleet runtime has no beacon state in eve.rs.
  - Persistence: ADR 0012 asks for a `Deployed` record expired by the sweep for cyno fields. This field is a sim-timed deployable (as the bombs and
    probes are), so a restart drops it. Open; see ADR 0017.
  - The lighting positive path (a fleet member in low or null security lighting a field and the fuel taken) has no host PostgreSQL test: the rig's station
    is high-security, and a low-security undock is not in the rig. The field's life, its announcement and its expiry are tested in the simulation.
- **Bastion and logoff (WP12, 1934).** Not built in this track. Wiki (Bastion Module) says nothing about logoff, so no source states it; the SDE sets
  1934 = 0 for the bastion. Keeping the module running needs a logged-off ship that stays in space, which eve.rs does not have (see DEVIATIONS, track B6c).
  Open.
- **Sim-side overlays (WP17, assistance and jam).** Not started in this track. The B6c notes name them as the sim-side part of the assistance and jam
  overlays (EveJS `hostileModuleRuntime.js`, `assistanceModuleRuntime.js`), and the definition of that part is not settled in the sources read. Open.
- **Probe launcher and probe lifecycle notifications (E-06).** Not started. Depends on the probe launcher (`LaunchProbes`, RC-7), which eve.rs does not
  have. Open.
- **Protocol.** 40 to 41 (one bump for this track): tag 27 `CynoField`, and the two version constants. `oracle/fixtures/simlink/messages.json` gained the
  two entries the generator writes for the field.
## Command bursts and the buff registry (track C5, 2026-10-09)

Sources, in the order the user set (SDE for numbers; the EVE University wiki for mechanics as written; EVE.js, CCP documents and eve-logic at equal rank after it; eve-logic decides the client's wire shapes). What is built, and what is not, is in TODO section 21.

- **The burst value chain evaluates on this SDE build; the C4 finding is corrected.** Track C4 read the burst's strength from `warfareBuffNValue` (2469) with a default of 0, and said the module carries no 2469. The rows say otherwise. A Tech II Shield Command Burst II (43555) has 2469 = 1.25 and a Tech I (42529) 2469 = 1.0 in `typeDogma`. The strength is the charge's `warfareBuffNMultiplier` (2596): Shield Harmonizing (42695) has buff 10 (shield resistance) and multiplier -8. Effect 6737 (`chargeBonusWarfareCharge`, `modifierInfo`) copies the charge's buff id (2468, PreAssign) and multiplies the module's value by the charge's multiplier (2469 PostMul by 2596). So the Tech I value is -8 and the Tech II value -10 (the wiki: Tech II is 25 % stronger; base +8 % for Shield Harmonizing, shown inverted). Test: `CommandBurstValueTests` (PgFact, real SDE, two tests on the two module tiers). Source: SDE `typeDogma` and `dogmaEffects` 6737; wiki "Command Bursts", section 1 and 2.
- **The buff collections are the SDE's.** `sde.dbuffCollections` rows: the operation (`operationName`), the aggregate mode (`aggregateMode`), and the modifiers (`itemModifiers`, `locationGroupModifiers`, `locationRequiredSkillModifiers`). Each becomes a `BuffCollection` (`dotnet/src/Eve.Dogma/BuffCollections.cs`). A row whose operation the engine does not know is left out. Source: SDE; the shape is the same as EVE.js `collectDefinitionModifierEntries`.
- **Operation codes follow eve-logic, not EVE.js.** eve-logic `dogma/const.py` has `dgmAssPreAssignment = -1` and `dgmAssPostAssignment = 7`; the C# `Operation` enum agrees. EVE.js `commandBurstRuntime.js` names `DOGMA_OP_PRE_ASSIGNMENT = 7`. The client's table decides the code, so the EVE.js constant is not copied. Source: eve-logic `dogma/const.py` (lines 7 and 15).
- **A buff carries its own value.** `Modifier` gained an optional `Literal` value, used instead of a source attribute, so a buff needs no carrier item. `DogmaWorld.SetBuffs(item, effects)` runs an item's buffs as passive effects (in `Sources`, with the item's other effects). Test: `BuffRegistryTests.Sync_makes_the_items_buffs_the_worlds_numbers_until_they_run_out`.
- **Aggregation: the strongest active input of a Maximum collection, the weakest of a Minimum one; no stacking between bursts of one collection.** The wiki's Command Bursts page does not state stacking or refresh (its section 2: "Not stated on the page: ... stacking or refresh behavior between bursts"), so the next source decides: EVE.js `commandBurstRuntime.js`, the `Aggregator`, `AggregatorMaximum` and `AggregatorMinimum` classes, and `applyTimedCommandBurstToEntity` (a refresh is a new timed input; the value is the best of the inputs still running). The `aggregateMode` is the SDE's (`Maximum` or `Minimum`). Tests: `BuffRegistryTests` (strongest, weakest, expiry, refresh, invalid input, restart, prune).
- **An input is active up to and including its expiry.** EVE.js `discardExpiredTimedInputs` drops an input only when its expiry is strictly before now. `BuffRegistry.Prune` and `ValuesOf` follow that. Test: `An_input_is_active_up_to_and_including_its_expiry_and_a_refresh_extends_it`.
- **Stacking penalty on buff modifiers follows the attribute.** EVE.js `collectDefinitionModifierEntries` sets `stackingPenalized: shouldApplyStackingPenalty(attribute, true)` for every collection modifier (`liveFittingState.js` line 1273: penalised when the attribute is not stackable). A buff's source is the ship, whose category is unpenalised in `DogmaWorld`, so a buff is exempted unless the buff flag is set; `Source.Buff` sets it. Test: `Two_collections_on_a_non_stackable_attribute_take_the_stacking_penalty_as_EVE_js_does`.
- **Restart drops every buff.** EVE.js keeps the timed history per entity in memory (`timedCommandBurstHistoryByEntity`, a `WeakMap`), so a restart drops it. `BuffRegistry` is in memory only.
- **Who a burst reaches on a cycle (`BurstRecipients.Resolve`).** The reference's `isBurstRecipient`: the source ship always (when eligible); another ship needs a shared fleet (two different pilots, `isInSameFleet`) or a shared explicit affinity group, or a burst that reaches every ship (the Phenomena Generator family, not a command burst). The range is the surface distance plus one metre (`<= range + 1`). Source: EVE.js `commandBurstRuntime.js` `isBurstRecipient`, `resolveCommandBurstRecipients`. The wiki's "up to 256 people" is applied as a cap, the source among them; the wiki does not say which ships are cut, so the rest are taken in ship id order (EVE.js order). Tests: `BurstRecipientsTests` (8, including the cap and the one-metre allowance). Mutation: the range rule (`<= range + 1` changed to `<= range`) fails the allowance test.
- **Mutation checks (bounded).** Three on the registry (`values.Max()` to `Min()`; expiry `>=` to `>`; the literal ignored in `DogmaWorld`): each fails `BuffRegistryTests`. Restored after each run.
- **Not built (each with its blocker, in TODO section 20).** The burst module's activation, cycle, charge use and refusal are still `UNSUPPORTED_EFFECT` (no change to the simulation or the host's module path). The burst's recipients on the wire, and the live numbers of the recipients' running modules, need a protocol change (a parked work cycle that carries the recipient list and the buff set, and a live-numbers message); no protocol number is spent in this track. The client's `OnDbuffUpdated` (eve-logic, the Destiny payload that EVE.js sends from `buildClientDbuffStateEntries`) has no host sender. The Phenomena Generator (`moduleTitanEffectGenerator`, EVE.js) is not a command burst and is not in this track. Expedition bursts (charge group 4905, EVE.js `EXPEDITION_DBUFF_COLLECTION_IDS`) are not in this track. P.A.N.I.C. (42522) is not in this track; it stays `EFFECT_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`.
- **Not checked in this track.** The skill, ship and mindlink bonuses reach the strength through the SDE's dogma modifiers, which this track did not test with a skilled character; the wiki's Max column (+23.44 % for Shield Harmonizing) is the check to make when they are. The wiki's table entry marked [Verify] (Expedition Strength, Analyzer Coherence) is outside this track.

## Command bursts end to end and P.A.N.I.C. (track C6, 2026-10-09)

Sources, in the user's order: the SDE for numbers (dogmaEffects 6732 to 6737, typeDogma of the bursts and of 42522, `dbuffCollections`, `dogmaAttributes`, `groups`); the EVE University wiki for the mechanics (the Capital Ship Modules page was read in this track for P.A.N.I.C.; the burst pages were not re-read, and C4 and C5 recorded them); EveJS (`commandBurstRuntime.js`, `commandBurst.js`, `destiny/stream/actions.js`) and the CCP data at equal rank after the wiki; eve-logic for the client's shapes and the P.A.N.I.C. number table (`dbuff/common/industrialInvulnConst.py` DBUFF_VALUES, `dbuff/const.py`, `dbuff/client/incomingDbuffTracker.py`, `eve/client/script/remote/michelle.py`).

**Wire (protocol 43, one bump).** `SimAction` 28 `CommandBurst` (the reach) and 29 `Panic` (the reach). `ModuleWorkCycle` carries the recipients (a count, then the ship ids), which only commands 12 (`Burst`) and 13 (`Panic`) name. Kind 62 `BuffState` (host to simulation): a ship's buffs, which the simulation tells the pilot as the destiny action `OnDbuffUpdated(shipID, [(collection, (value, expiry))])`. eve-logic decides the shape (`incomingDbuffTracker.OnDbuffUpdated(shipID, dbuffState)`); EveJS builds it as `buildOnDbuffUpdatedPayload`, with the expiry of a collection as its aggregator's final expiry (`getFinalExpiryTime`), sent as a FILETIME.

**Who a burst reaches (decided).** The simulation measures the reach: the ships of its scene within the range plus one metre, surface to surface (EveJS `isBurstRecipient`'s allowance). The simulation does not read fleets (ADR 0011, decision 5), so the host keeps the source's own ship always and another ship only when it is in the source's fleet (EveJS `isInSameFleet`), at most 256 with the source first (wiki: "up to 256 people"; the wiki does not say which are cut, so ids are taken in order, as C5 decided).

**When a burst settles, and refresh (decided).** At each cycle's end, through the work path (ADR 0013). Each granted ship has the buffs for the buff duration (buffDuration 2535: 60 s, 80 s for the named and presidential bursts), so a later cycle refreshes them. Two bursts of one collection do not stack (C5: the aggregators of EveJS).

**The last charge (decided).** A charged action that takes effect at its cycle's end runs that cycle to its end on its last round (`spend_round`, `ends_its_cycle`): a burst with N charges settles N cycles. A charged action that takes effect at the cycle's start keeps the rule it had (the power booster test pins it: its last round ends the effect at once). No source read states the last-round rule; the choice keeps the last cycle's buffs, which the cycle's answer grants.

**Expiry and restart (decided).** The containers' sweep (`space.containers.sweep`) ends the buffs whose time ran out (an input is active up to and including its expiry, EveJS `discardExpiredTimedInputs`) and sends each pilot the state that remains (an empty list clears the client's buffs). The buffs are in memory: a restart drops them, as EveJS keeps the burst history in memory (`timedCommandBurstHistoryByEntity`).

**Refusals.** `NO_AMMO` (no round for a cycle), and the work-cycle reasons `NO_PILOT`, `NO_SHIP`, `NOT_CONTROLLED`, `INVALID_PROFILE` (no buff). The names are the host's (no client text for them was read).

**P.A.N.I.C. (42522), decided.**
- Activation. A locked asteroid is needed (the simulation checks its locks against its asteroids; refused `TARGET_REQUIRED`, our name). Source: the wiki's Capital Ship Modules rule as C4 recorded it ("activated with an asteroid or ice asteroid targeted"). The start parks for the host's grant, as a first cycle's fuel does (ADR 0013, epoch 24), under command 13.
- Duration and repeats. panicDuration 2788 (240 000 ms in the SDE; the skill's share is dogma's), one cycle (disallowRepeatingActivation 1014). The wiki's "4 minutes, up to 6 with Invulnerability Core Operation" agrees with the SDE value.
- Reach. maxRange 54, 200 000 m (SDE). The one-metre allowance is the bursts' (EveJS), applied by analogy: no source gives one for this module.
- Who is granted. The source, and the ships of its fleet in the industrial and mining classes of the SDE: Mining Barge 463, Exhumer 543, Hauler 28, Deep Space Transport 380, Blockade Runner 1202, Freighter 513, Jump Freighter 902 and Industrial Command Ship 941. The wiki (Capital Ship Modules, read in this track) says "the Rorqual and mining barges and industrial ships in its fleet; other Rorquals are excluded": the wiki decides, and the classes are read as "industrial" in the SDE's own sense. Not granted: other Rorquals (883) and any other ship.
- Magnitudes. The SDE gives the collections and their attributes but no magnitude (42522's typeDogma has none). The wiki says "complete invulnerability" and gives no number: the wiki decides the mechanic, so the shield resistance is -100 % (collection 8), not the client table's -99.99 % (eve-logic `DBUFF_VALUES`, recorded here as the number it differs from). The rest of the values are the client's table: shield recharge -90 % (9), scan resolution -75 % (55), mass +100 % (56), drone damage -100 % (58), weapons, entosis, cloak, jump or dock and tether disallowed (59, 61, 5, 6, 57), the warp penalty +100 (4), velocity -50 % (3). The order of sources: the SDE has no number, the wiki gives none, and eve-logic is the number source at equal rank with EveJS (no P.A.N.I.C. body there) and the CCP documents (not read).
- Velocity. Collection 3 writes attribute 3171, which the SDE names expeditionFrigateBonusMediumDronesDamage_DEPRECATED. SDE first: the top speed (37) is not changed. Open (TODO 22).
- Drone damage. Collection 58 writes attribute 2580 (industrialBonusDroneDamage) on the ship; no drone path in eve.rs reads it. Open (TODO 22).
- Weapons. Collection 59 (skills 3300, 3319, 3421) and 61 (skill 24242) write activationBlocked (1349) on the items in the ship that need those skills. The host refuses a start on a blocked module (the existing check), and a granted ship's running modules with 1349 are deactivated (EndBlockedModulesAsync, off the settlement).
