The timing of the stop is the simulation's deactivate rule (it ends at the cycle's end); no source states the timing.
- Movement. The wiki says the protected ships cannot move. Held still for the grant's duration: the simulation is sent a stop as the grant is made, and the host refuses the movement orders of a held ship (a speed above zero, a direction, a follow, an orbit, an alignment) in `SpaceSessions.Command`. The refusal is silent (no client notice: none was read for it). Warp, jump and dock are refused as below.
- Cloak, jump or dock, tether. Cloak (2454) refused by the existing `CloakingEffect` check; docking (2354) refused with EveJS's words for a module that disallows docking, as the ship's own attribute is the cause here; jump (2453) refused with "You cannot jump at this time." (ours); tether (2343): eve.rs has no tether.
- Warp. Collection 4 adds 100 to attribute 104 (warpScrambleStatus): the warp is refused before the simulation with the scrambler's words ("You cannot warp because you are warp scrambled.").
- No status state in the simulation. The request named "general status effects" in the simulation. Not built: the simulation receives the numbers through the ship (`SimShips` reads 271 to 274, 479, 4, 564 and the warp speed, so its resonances, recharge, mass, scan resolution and warp speed follow the buffs), and the disallows are the host's checks on the ship's attributes, which the simulation has no state for. This is a deviation from the requested design; it is recorded here and in TODO 22.
- SCARAB Breacher pods bypass the grant in the wiki's rules. eve.rs has no such pods, so there is no bypass to build.

**Start pending (fixed).** `ActivateAsync` marked a module active after the simulation's answer. A parked start (a first cycle's fuel, a P.A.N.I.C.'s grant) is answered only when it is accepted, so the host's own check of the module, made while the start waited, saw it as not running and refused the fuel ask (`NOT_CONTROLLED`). The module is now marked while the start is pending, if it was not marked already, and unmarked on any refusal or failure.

**Protocol decode (fixed).** The Rust decoder of module work cycles accepted the commands 7 to 9 only, so a scan (10, 11) of protocol 40 was refused on the simulation side. Its set is the C# codec's (7 to 13), and its numbers are zero for every command but the mining ones.

**Not built, with blockers.** Login and re-entry state (TODO 22). Drone damage (the wiki says "cannot deal damage": SDE 2580 has no reader, so drones are not stopped; running drones are not recalled) and velocity (SDE: 3171 deprecated). The jump wording. MITM trace (`tools/mitm/README.md`, not driven).
## Track A5: Python set on the wire (2026-10-09)

- **Python `set` is a PyObjectEx1 (no set opcode), built.** The marshal protocol has no set opcode. A set is what
  `set.__reduce__` returns, `(set, (list(items),))`, so it goes out as PyObjectEx1 with the header
  `(token "__builtin__.set", (list,))`. Source: EveJS `services/_shared/serviceHelpers.js` `buildPythonSet` (line 307 to 315) and
  its reader at line 615; the client needs a real set, because `dotWeapons/client/dotInfoPerTarget.py` calls `discard` on the
  `OnDotVictimUpdated` payload (`eve-logic dotWeapons/client/incomingDotTracker.py`, called from `michelle.py` line 1578).
  Both codecs carry it: `Value::python_set` in `crates/eve-marshal/src/value.rs` and `PyValue.PythonSet` in
  `dotnet/src/Eve.Marshal/PyValue.cs`; `InventoryWire.Set` delegates to it. Tests: `crates/eve-marshal/tests/python_set.rs`
  and `CodecTests.A_python_set_is_a_set_object_ex1_and_round_trips`. The protocol number does not change (no new opcode, no new message).

## Track A5: OnDotVictimUpdated (breacher pods, 2026-10-09)

Source order: the SDE and the wiki first (A4's breacher section, which this track keeps), then eve-logic (the client and the shared DoT code), then EveJS, which has no DoT code.

- **The list a ship is told, built.** `OnDotVictimUpdated(targetID, dmgAppsInfo)` is a destiny action on the ship (eve-logic `michelle.py` line 1578). It has the shape of `OnDbuffUpdated(shipID, dbuffState)` (line 1574), which EveJS sends as a destiny action (`space/destiny/presentation/specialFxPayloads.js`, `payloadIdentity.js` line 25). The client's ballpark maps the action name to the method; that mapping is not in eve-logic, and it is the same route this action takes. The argument is the set of `DotApplicationInfo` objects of A4's set encoding. Each object is the class `dotWeapons.common.dotAppInfo.DotApplicationInfo` with its state (`_attackerID`, `_weaponTypeID`, `_expiryTime`, `_maxDamage`, `_maxHPNormalizedRatio`, `_activityState`, `_targetID`; eve-logic `dotAppInfo.py` lines 9 to 19).
- **The fields.** As eve-logic `DotApplication.toInfo` (`dotApplication.py` line 78): the attacker is the pilot's character (the shooter's `owner_id`, `charID` in eve-logic); the weapon is the launcher's type (`weaponItem.typeID`); the ratio is the charge's percentage over 100, clamped to [0, 1] (line 19); the cap is the charge's `dotMaxDamagePerTick`. `activityState` is 2 (`DOT_ACTIVE`) for the application that works and 1 (`DOT_IDLE`) for the others (`dotConst.py`).
- **Who is told: everyone who has the ship in view.** eve-logic `dotRegistry.py` tells the target (`ScheduleTargetUpdate`) and the attacker (`ScheduleAttackerUpdate(attackerID, targetID)`, lines 32 to 33 and 71 to 73). The notifier that sends the update is not in any source read. The action goes out as every ship action does (to the ship's pilot and to those who watch it). The client shows the attacker's button only for its own entries (`incomingDotTracker.GetOnGridDamageInfosForAttacker` filters on the character), so the wider audience changes nothing the client shows. A choice.
- **Expiry.** eve-logic gives an application an absolute expiry, `added + duration` (`dotApplication.py` line 20), which runs while the application waits. A4 chose that an inert clock stops, and this track keeps it. The working application's expiry is its own remaining time from the send; an inert application's expiry is the end of the queue (the sum of the remaining times). The queue ends when the working application ends, so an inert expiry sent earlier is still right until the next change, and every change sends the set again.
- **When the set is told.** On each hit, when the working application changes, and when an application ends (the set is empty when none is left). eve-logic tells the target when the attacker changes (`dotRegistry.py` lines 70 to 71) and when a target loses its effects (lines 89 and 97). A tick of the same working application is not told: its expiry does not move.
- **Not told: a ship that leaves the scene.** A jump, a dock or a death ends the applications without an empty set. The client clears its list on a change of ship or location (`incomingDotTracker.OnSessionChanged`). A ship that comes into view mid-effect has no set until the next change, as with the slim field (A4).
- **Protocol: no change.** The action rides the existing `DoDestinyUpdate`. Tests: `dot_weapons/tests.rs` `a_hit_tells_the_ship_its_applications_as_a_set_and_the_first_tick_makes_one_working` and `the_ship_is_told_an_empty_set_when_its_last_application_ends`, with the full `eve-space` suite passing.
- **NOT RUN: the client.** No MITM capture and no graphical client are on this host, so the HUD button, the attacker's timer and the expiry clearing are not seen in the client.

## Track A5: the cynosural field record (ADR 0012, 2026-10-09)

- **A lit field is kept as a record, built.** The simulation says a field was lit (`Deployed`, protocol 44: place, name, type, owner, corporation, alliance, ship and
  its whole life). The host keeps it in `space.deployed` until its time is up. Source: ADR 0012 decision 1 (a cynosural field is a `Deployed` kind). Tests:
  `CynoFieldRecordHostTests` (PostgreSQL) and `deployable/tests.rs` `a_lit_cynosural_field_is_told_to_the_host_with_its_place_and_its_whole_life`.
- **A restart puts the field back for what is left of its life, built.** The same message goes back to the simulation when a ship comes into the system; the
  field keeps its id; its life is the record's deadline minus the host's clock. A record whose deadline has passed is not put back. Source: ADR 0012 decision 1
  (the deadline is a row, and the sweep is the owner). Test: `a_field_the_host_puts_back_after_a_restart_lives_what_was_left_and_is_removed_on_request`.
- **The simulation keeps the field's own end, a choice that departs from ADR 0012 decision 3.** Decision 3 says the simulation does not decide expiry. The field's
  end in the simulation is B7's behaviour (`a_cynosural_field_is_lit_where_the_ship_is_as_its_activation_ends_and_is_taken_out_when_its_lifetime_is_up`), which the
  pilots see as the field going at its time. The record's sweep (up to 30 s later) sends `RemoveDeployed`, which the simulation answers with nothing once the field
  is gone. Changing B7's end is a client-visible change and is not made here. Source: EVE.js `cynosuralField` lifetime (explosionDelay 600000 ms, from the SDE).
- **Lateness: up to 30 s for the record.** ADR 0012 consequences accept a sweep interval of up to 30 s for lifetimes of 30 s and more; a field lives ten minutes.
- **NOT RUN: the client.** No graphical client on this host; the field's appearance after a restart is not seen in the client.

## Track A5: scanner probes, launch and recall (2026-10-10)

Source order: the SDE for numbers (explosionDelay 281 and radius 162; the core probe type 30013, explosion delay 4 000 000 ms, no radius), then the EVE University wiki where it decides, then EveJS and eve-logic.

- **Launch: the reference's checks, in its order, built.** `LaunchProbes(moduleID, count)` (EveJS `Handle_LaunchProbes`, `_resolveValidatedProbeLaunchContext`): not in space (`DeniedShipChanged`), the launcher is not the pilot's in the ship, is not a probe launcher (group 481), is not online, has no probe loaded (`NoCharges`), the loaded charge is not a probe (group 479), fewer loaded than asked (`You do not have enough loaded scanner probes.`), or more than eight active (`You cannot control more than eight active probes.`). Tests: `ScanProbeHostTests`.
- **Eight per pilot per system.** `MAX_ACTIVE_PROBES` (EveJS `probeRuntimeState`). A probe that is recalled or has gone no longer counts.
- **Placement: a ring of ten kilometres.** Probe *i* of the active ones goes at the ship's position plus `10 km × (cos, 0, sin)` of `2π i / 8` (EveJS `buildLaunchOffset`, `DEFAULT_LAUNCH_RING_RADIUS_METERS`). The scan range and range step are left for the client to set (`rangeStep` 7, `scanRange` 0, as EveJS sends them at launch).
- **Life: the charge's explosion delay, else an hour.** Attribute 281, rounded to whole seconds (EveJS `Math.round(expiryDelayMs / 1000)`); a type with none lives one hour (`DEFAULT_PROBE_EXPIRY_SECONDS`). The core probe lives 4 000 s.
- **Expiry at the sweep, every 30 s.** EveJS purges expired probes at each probe call; here `space.probes.sweep` removes them, so a probe can be up to 30 s late. Accepted (ADR 0012 consequences). The pilot is told `OnRemoveProbe` when it is in space.
- **Recall: no refund.** `scanMgr.DestroyProbe` removes the probe and tells the pilot `OnRemoveProbe` (EveJS `removeCharacterProbes` gives no charge back).
- **Ball radius: attribute 162 when the type has one, else 40 m.** The core probes of the SDE carry no radius; 40 m is a choice, not a source value.
- **Charge use: the stack is spent, not refunded.** The stack loaded in the launcher loses the probes launched; an emptied stack is removed (`SpendProbesAsync`).
- **One probe launcher per ship.** The ship's group limit refuses a second (`MaxGroupFitted`), as the fitting rules do.
- **Scanning, recovery, reconnection and probe warp: built in track A7 (below).** They were not built at this track; the line is kept as the record of it.
- **Restart: the balls come back with the records.** The records are in `space.scan_probes`, and each probe still in its life is put back in space when a ship comes into its system (`SpaceProbes.RestoreAsync`, on `SystemEntered`, as the cynosural fields are): the same id, the place it was launched at, the remaining life. A probe whose time is up is not put back, and the sweep removes its record. Tests: `ScanProbeHostTests`. LIMITATION: a restart of the process itself is not exercised by a test; the restore is run by a second arrival in the system.
- **NOT RUN: the client.** No graphical client or MITM capture on this host; the probe's place in the client's probe scanner is not seen.

## Track B6a (2026-10-09): ECM burst report, safe logoff, and the baseline failures

Source order used for the rules here (the coordinator's rule): the SDE for numbers, the EVE University wiki for mechanics (implemented as written), then EVE.js, CCP documents and eve-logic at equal rank; the client's wire shapes follow eve-logic. Each conflict names the source chosen.

- **ECM burst report (FP-C7), built.** The simulation reports one `SimEcmBurst` (kind 67, protocol 46) per cycle that reaches ships: the ship that runs the burst and each ship it reached (not a cloaked one), with whether its roll jammed it. The host records the attack on each pilot reached (`SpaceSessions.RecordEcmBurstAsync`), with the same `Aggression` record as a module started at another pilot's ship. EVE.js (`executeJammerBurstCycle`, `recordCrimewatchOffensiveAggression`) records the aggression on every reached ship before its roll, so a ship that resists is still recorded; this is what is built. A burst starts no weapons timer at activation (`IsOffensive` excludes it), as in EVE.js.
- **Who a burst reaches.** Every ship in the radius of the surface of the burst's ship, its own excepted, and not a cloaked one (`CloakState::Cloaked`). EVE.js `isCloakedTargetEntity` reads a cloak mode above zero; the simulation has no cloak mode value, so the fully cloaked state is the one that applies. The wiki says a burst "will also jam your friends", so friendly ships are reached as well. Its locks are broken, and no jam is left behind (wiki: "will only break all the existing target locks of affected ships"; EVE.js `breakLocksOnly`).
- **Conflict: the activation gate for an ECM burst.** The wiki (Electronic Countermeasures) says a burst "will get you CONCORD'd if used in high security space, and thus cannot be activated in hisec if your Safety is not set to Red". EVE.js refuses an untargeted activation for the smartbomb only (`evaluateEmpireUntargetedOffensiveActivation`, `isSmartbombActivation`). Chosen: the wiki. The host refuses the activation in high security space (security 0.45 and above) with `SafetyActivated` unless the pilot's safety is none (`SpaceSessions.Modules.cs`, `SafetyLevel.None`).
- **Bombs with an ECM kind** keep their path (`jam_at`): the bomb's ECM is not reported to the host, so no aggression is recorded for it, and cloaked ships are still rolled. Open: the bomb's aggression and cloak rule, which the wiki's bomb page has not been read for yet.
- **Safe logoff: the session-change refusal removed.** The undock opens a 10 s session change, and `SafeLogoffAsync` refused a safe logoff until it closed (a guard added in 601cb47). The wiki's Timers page lists what makes a safe logoff impossible: active modules, ejecting, aggression by players or NPCs, self-destruct, moving, fleet changes, warp, a cloak including the jump cloak timer. A session change is not among them, and EVE.js `Handle_SafeLogoff` accepts every request (it returns an empty failure list). Chosen: the wiki, so the guard is removed. The three Safe logoff tests fail at 863d259 too (4 runs of 4); their cause was the race between that refusal and the harness's timers. The active-module and target-lock checks stay, as the wiki lists both.
- **Mutadaptive spools.** `ProjectedEffect.MutadaptiveSpool` is one process-wide object keyed by module id. `SpaceSessions.Dispose` and `OnLost` cleared all of it, so one host reset the spools of the other hosts in the same process. Now each host resets only the modules it runs (`ForgetMutadaptiveSpools`); `ResetAll` is gone.
- **Fleet finder advert.** Migration 006 drops `fleet_advert_allowed_entities` (the allowed entities are in `advert_payload`), but `RemoveFleetFinderAdvert` still ran a `DELETE` from that table, so the removal failed. The `DELETE` is removed.
- **Test races fixed (the tests waited for the state, not for the notices).** `NpcBountyHostTests.Kills_of_several_npcs...` waits for both bounty notices; `RuntimeTests.A_persisted_engagement_expires...` waits for both engagement-ended notices.
- **Stale names.** `ConfigPrime` and `SessionEndsHangar` are not in the repository; they are dropped from the baseline list.

## Track C8: buffs across entry and restart, drone damage, velocity, MITM (2026-10-10)

Sources, in the user's order: the SDE for numbers (dbuffCollections 3, 8, 58; dogmaEffects 6789 `industrialBonusDroneDamage`; dogmaAttributes 37, 2580, 3171, 3436 "Drones"); the EVE University wiki (Capital Ship Modules, read in this track for the P.A.N.I.C. rules); then EveJS and eve-logic at equal rank (eve-logic's `dogma/const.py` names 2580, no reader; EveJS has no P.A.N.I.C. body).

**Buffs across entry and restart (decided).** A ship's active buff inputs (collection, value, expiry) are kept in `space.ship_buffs` (migration 0006, `PgShipBuffStore`) whenever the registry changes: the burst and P.A.N.I.C. settlements and the expiry sweep (`SpaceContainers.TellBuffsAsync`). A ship that the registry holds no buffs for (a restart) has its inputs restored from the record when it is next synced: undock, login in space and the flown-ship read (`SpaceSessions.SyncBuffsAsync`). Every entry tells the pilot the buffs its ship carries as `BuffState` (the simulation's `OnDbuffUpdated`): undock (`EnterHow.Undock`), login in space (`EnterHow.InSpace`) and a gate jump's arrival (`SimJumped`). Nothing is sent without a buff. An input at its expiry is active, and an expired one is not restored. Deviation: EveJS keeps its burst history in memory (`timedCommandBurstHistoryByEntity`), so a restart drops it there; eve.rs keeps the record, as the track's requirement asks. Tests: `BuffRecordHostTests` (undock after a restart is told the record's buffs; an expired input is not restored), `BuffRegistryTests` (a registry's inputs restore into another with the same values and expiries).

**Drone damage (decided, protocol 47).** The simulation scales each shot of a drone by its controller's `drone_damage_multiplier` (`drones/fight.rs` `drone_effect`), which the host sends in `SimShip` (`SimShips.DroneDamageMultiplierOf`). The SDE's effect 6789 applies the ship's industrialBonusDroneDamage (2580) to the drone's damage multiplier (attribute 64) as a PostPercent, and its owner's skill, the Drones skill (3436), is required. So a bonus (the Orca's 2580 of +100) counts only for a pilot with the Drones skill. A penalty counts for every pilot: collection 58 (PostAssignment, Minimum) sets 2580 to -100 on the granted ship, and the wiki says protected ships cannot deal damage, so their drones deal none. The multiplier is max(0, 1 + effective / 100), where effective is 2580 with the skill, and the lower of 2580 and 0 without it. The rule for the penalty without the skill is ours (the SDE gates both terms on the skill). Tests: `drones/tests.rs` (a controller at multiplier 0 does not kill the rat it fights; the kill with 1 stays), `IndustrialInvulnerabilityTests` (the bonus with and without the skill, the grant for trained and untrained pilots). Not verified in the client.

**Velocity (decided).** The wiki's P.A.N.I.C. rule is "unable to move", and it gives no speed number. The SDE's velocity collection (3) modifies attribute 3171, which the SDE names `expeditionFrigateBonusMediumDronesDamage_DEPRICATED`, so it moves nothing. The current top speed is maxVelocity (37). The grant's collection 3 is therefore the SDE collection with its shape moved to attribute 37 at -100 % (PostPercent, a top speed of zero); the eve-logic table's -50 % is not used, because the wiki decides. The simulation takes the ship's top speed from the host (`SimShip.MaxVelocity`), so a granted ship's motion stops with it; the host's movement refusal of track C6 still applies. Test: `IndustrialInvulnerabilityTests.The_velocity_buff_takes_the_top_speed_to_zero_and_leaves_the_deprecated_attribute_alone`. Not verified in the client.

**MITM (not driven).** `tools/mitm/README.md`: the headless client's module scenarios replay recorded client races (`test_module.py`, `arrive()` and the recorded module list); `test_live.py` scripts login, undock and movement only. No recorded or live-fitted command burst exists, and the headless recipe fits no burst module, so no burst scenario was driven through the proxy on either side. The reference side can run on this host (`/home/user/evejs-v0.12.9` with its `node_modules`, `tools/dev/run-evejs-live.sh`), but the burst scenario it would need does not exist in the tests.

## Track A5 (continued): WP-8 mobile warp disruptors, E-03 Mobile Cynosural Inhibitor, the probe gate (2026-10-10)

Source order: the SDE for numbers, the EVE University wiki for rules (as written), then EVE.js v0.12.9 where the wiki is silent. Protocol 48 (disruptor placement and anchoring, kinds 68 and 69), 49 (command 25, the drop point), 50 (inhibitor placement, kind 70).

- **Drop point: the simulation's heading, 2,500 m ahead.** The reference drops a deployable `DEPLOY_DISTANCE_METERS` (2,500 m) ahead of the ship along its heading (`buildDeployableSpawnState`). The wiki gives no deployment distance for these items, and the SDE has none for a ship's deployable (`anchorDistanceMax` is the structure anchoring range). The host asks the simulation for the point (Command 25, `DropPoint`, answered as a position), so the heading is the simulation's (EVE.js value stands).
- **Radius: the SDE radius of the type.** The disruptor's ball is the type's radius from the static data (attribute 162, the dogma statics' intrinsic radius): 70 m for the small Mk I, 100 m for the inhibitor. The 40 m fallback of the first increment is gone.
- **Disruptor lifetime: EVE.js's table (the wiki is silent for the standard models).** The wiki's disruptor page gives one lifetime, two days for the Tournament Bubble (a GM-only model, "continuously ... automatically decay"); for the standard models it gives none. The SDE gives none either. EVE.js's `CURRENT_SDE_LIFETIME_MS_BY_TYPE_ID` applies: two days for the Mk I, seven for the Mk II, fourteen for the Syndicate models. The anchoring times are the SDE's (attribute 556), which agree with the wiki's table (1, 2, 4 and 8 minutes by size and model).
- **Inhibitor anchoring: two minutes (the wiki); EVE.js says one minute.** The wiki: "It takes two minutes to set up." The wiki's rule is used; EVE.js's 60 s is overruled. The SDE type carries no anchoring attribute for the inhibitor.
- **Inhibitor lifetime and range: 200 km (owner decision, 2026-10-13).** One hour. The range was 100 km, the value of the Mobile Cynosural Inhibitor wiki page (`prevents all normal cyno fields (but not covert cynos) from activating within 100km`, re-fetched 2026-10-13) and of EVE.js; the Cynosural Field Generator wiki page says 200 km. The owner chose the 200 km figure; the inhibitor page disagrees, so the owner may revert `InhibitorRangeMeters` (dotnet) and the 200 km of the deployable (Rust) to 100 km.
- **Inhibitor security: none.** The wiki sets no security restriction for the inhibitor, and the SDE type has no `disallowInEmpireSpace` attribute. EVE.js's high-security cutoff (0.45) is not applied, since the rule it implements is the one the wiki and the SDE do not have.
- **Inhibitor skill: Anchoring III (EVE.js; the wiki names no skill for it).** The check reads the type's required skill attributes (182 with 277), which the SDE gives as skill 11584 at level 3.
- **Inhibitor spacing: 400 km from another inhibitor.** The wiki: a second one "cannot be placed where two cyno fields would overlap"; two 200 km fields overlap inside 400 km. The 75 km (station, stargate, upwell) and 40 km (control tower) blockers of EVE.js are not built: the host does not hold the positions of stations, gates and towers in space (shortcut; upgrade when the scene shares them).
- **A cynosural field is refused inside an anchored inhibitor's range.** `CYNOSURAL_FIELD_INHIBITED` at module activation, when the activating ship is within 200 km of an anchored inhibitor (EVE.js `modules.js` `findActiveInhibitorConflict`). A covert field (type 28650) is not stopped (EVE.js `TYPE_COVERT_CYNOSURAL_FIELD`).
- **Scoop and anchor-lift: not built, because the reference has neither.** EVE.js v0.12.9's mobile warp disruptor runtime has no pickup and no unanchor (its only exits are expiry, destruction and abyssal removal), and the inhibitor's wiki page says "this one cannot be reclaimed". A pickup for these items would be behaviour the reference does not have, so it is not built.
- **`GetCynoJammerState`: no source is wired; the answer is None.** The reference answers from the sovereignty Tenebrex structures and the starbase cyno jammers (`sovSuppressionState.js`, `getCynoJammerActivationTimeMs`). Mobile inhibitors are not one of its sources, so an inhibitor does not feed it. Neither of the reference's sources is built in eve.rs (no Tenebrex structure, no starbase jammer), so the answer is None for every system. `BeyonceService.CynoJammerOnlineSimTimeForSystem` is left unset on purpose.
- **Host queries routed in the simulation (fix).** Commands 21 (warp to a point), 22 (resolve a warp target) and 23 (position of) were not routed by the simulation's `command_of`, so the host's position queries were refused as `UNKNOWN_COMMAND` in the real simulation. They are routed now; a simulation test asserts the answers. The fake simulation in the host tests had always answered them, which is why the fault was not seen.
- **Probe restore and launch are serialised per system (fix).** The restore on a system's entry ran fire-and-forget, so it could interleave with a launch in the same system. A per-system gate is taken when the system is entered, so a launch that follows waits for the restore. The regression test holds the restore after it has read the records (a test seam, `AfterRead`) and asserts the launch waits. Against the ungated code the test fails (its launch places its balls at once); with the gate it passes. An entry may restore the same record more than once, and the simulation keeps one ball per id.
- **Drop, anchor and expiry are restart-safe.** The deployed record (migrations 0007 and 0008: the kind, the anchoring time, the range) is restored on the ship's entry into the system, with the anchored state its record gives; the anchoring job is durable (`space.deployed.anchor`). Tests restore the state by calling the restore directly; a process restart is not exercised.
- **Not built: the destruction of a deployable.** EVE.js clears a mobile deployable when it is destroyed (`dynamicEntities` `destroyed`); the simulation does not damage deployables, so the host has no such event for them.
- **NOT RUN: the client.** No graphical client or MITM capture on this host.

## Track A5 (scoop rules and fleet test isolation, 2026-10-10)

- **Scoop of a deployed mobile object: the wiki as written, which is silent for the disruptor.** The Mobile Depot page: "A Mobile Depot can only be accessed or scooped up by its owner"; it "can be scooped back to your ship's cargohold at any time"; its contents appear in space in a cargo container when it is scooped. The Mobile Cynosural Inhibitor page: "unlike the other mobile structures, this one cannot be reclaimed", so it is not scooped. The Mobile Warp Disruptor page and the mobile structures page give no retrieval rule for it, and EVE.js has none, so no scoop is built for it. The depot's rule is the depot's, not the disruptor's, and is not extended to it.
- **Fleet test isolation (fix).** `SpaceServiceTests` built `FleetStore` on the shared default schema `fleet` (18 sites), so rows of earlier runs collided on `fleet_members_character_id_key`; two raw statements also named `fleet.fleet_members`. Each test now uses its rig's own fleet schema (`Rig.FleetSchema`). Leaked rows removed from the dev database: fleets 2349 and 2350 with their members (characters 140000001 and 140000002, job 2) and their wing, squad, invitation and join-request rows (none). These were left by fleet tests that wrote to the shared schema and did not disband.
- **Result:** the Space suite (554 tests) passes twice in a row after the fix.

## Track A6: the capital jump drive (2026-10-10)

Sources, in the order of the scanner rule: numbers from the SDE (pinned build 3396210, `sde.typeDogma` for the hulls, `sde.dogmaAttributes`, `sde.groups`, `sde.types`); mechanics from the EVE University wiki, implemented as written (Jump drive: https://wiki.eveuniversity.org/Jump_drive; Jump fatigue: https://wiki.eveuniversity.org/Jump_fatigue; Capital ships: https://wiki.eveuniversity.org/Capital_ships; fetched 2026-10-10); EveJS v0.12.9 has no capital jump (a search of `server/src` finds only the fighter's micro jump), so it contributes nothing here; the client's callsites in eve-logic decide the wire shapes.

- **Source check, not a conflict.** The SDE's `jumpDriveDuration` (869) is 300 000 ms on every hull. The client's `jumpFatigue/const.py` (eve-logic) has `ACTIVATION_SECONDS_PER_DISTANCE = 60`, `FATIGUE_SECONDS_PER_DISTANCE = 600`, `FATIGUE_MAX_SECONDS = 18000`; these match the wiki's cooldown minimum (one minute per (1 + light years)), its fatigue minimum (ten minutes per (1 + light years)) and the five-hour cap. The spool is the SDE's 300 s; the 60 s is the cooldown's minimum, not the spool. Built so.
- **The wiki's carrier example does not match its own rule.** The third jump of the example has a fatigue of 4 h 54 min before it; a tenth of that is 29 min 24 s, which the rule gives. The example states 30 minutes. The rule is built as written; `JumpDriveRulesTests` pins 1 764 000 ms and records the example's figure here.
- **Range.** SDE attribute 867 times (1 + 0.2 × calibration level), the wiki's percentage (Jump Drive Calibration, 21611). Light years from the solar systems' positions in the SDE (`mapSolarSystems.position`, `SolarSystemCatalog.LightYearsBetween`, 9 460 730 472 580 800 m a light year).
- **Fuel.** The wiki's formula: light years × attribute 868 × (1 − 0.1 × Jump Fuel Conservation, 21610) × (1 − 0.1 × Jump Freighters, 29029, for a jump freighter only), rounded up to whole units. Fuel type: attribute 866 (the isotope type id: 16274 helium, 17887 oxygen, 17888 nitrogen, 17889 hydrogen). The wiki's economizer terms are not applied (shortcut: no economizer is fitted by the host yet). The wiki's capacitor cost is not applied (see TODO section 26).
- **Fatigue.** The wiki's order: the cooldown is a tenth of the fatigue before the jump (minimum one minute per (1 + LY), capped at 30 minutes); the fatigue is the fatigue before × (1 + LY), at least ten minutes per (1 + LY), capped at five hours. The distance is the light years times attribute 1971 (0.25 black ops, 0.1 freighters and the Rorqual, the wiki's 75 % and 90 % reductions), taken before the + 1. Decay is real time (the stored end time).
- **Field rules.** Covert field: black ops only; industrial field: jump freighter, capital industrial ship, black ops only (wiki, "Jump drive", the cyno kinds); a standard field: any hull with a drive. The field types are the SDE's 21094, 28650, 52696 (the same table as the cynosural generator, `CynoFieldTypes`).
- **Fleet.** Only a member of the field's fleet may jump to it (wiki). Both the lighter and the jumper are looked up in the fleet store (`FleetIdForCharacterAsync`).
- **High-security origin is refused.** The wiki's capital rule ("can only operate outside of high-sec space"). The message text is ours: no source read gives one.
- **Refusal text.** The wiki gives no error or refusal message text (its page says so). The host's messages are ours and are `CustomNotify` texts; the refusals are the rules' names in `SpaceJumpDrive` (not in the wire).
- **Command code and protocol.** The cynosural jump is command 26 (protocol 51). Code 24 is the login visuals prime, and 25 is WP-8's drop point, both already on the link; the code is taken at push time, as the A5 rule for the protocol says.
- **Beacons for the client.** eve-logic `fleetSvc.py`: `OnFleetJumpBeaconModuleChange(charID, solarsystemID, beaconID, typeID, active)` fills `activeModuleBeacons`; `menusvc.py` `_GetCynoTargetSubMenus` lists the entries and passes `(charID, beaconID, solarsystemID)` to `CmdJumpToFleetModuleBeacon`, which is how the host reads them. The beacon id is the field's deployable id (the host's field record), the type id is the field's type. The deployable variant (`CmdJumpToFleetDeployableBeacon`, `OnFleetJumpBeaconDeployableChange`) is answered for the same field record, since the lit field is the only beacon the host has (no deployable beacon is built).
- **Arrival.** The ship appears on a ring of 2 455 m round the field at a random bearing, facing the field. 2 455 is the static data's cynosural field spawn radius (attribute 2455, the value `CynoFieldTypes` already names); the ring, the bearing and the facing are a choice, since no source read gives the reference's landing. shortcut: one radius for every field type.
- **Spool and jump.** The simulation's stargate machinery is reused: the ship stops, the `effects.JumpDriveOut` effect is told (the client's own effect guid, eve-logic `eve/client/script/environment/effects/Jump.py`), the spool is `PendingJump` with its field, and the arrival is `Scene::arrive` at the field. Tests: `scene/tests.rs` (`a_cyno_jump_*`).
- **Order of the debit.** The fuel is checked, the simulation is asked, and the fuel and the new fatigue are written together once the simulation accepts (the spool is not undone by a debit). A debit that fails after the check is logged and the spool stands (shortcut: the module cycles are the only other debit).
- **The shared fuel debit.** `CharacterShips.ConsumeModuleFuelAsync` and the jump debit use `TakeFuelAsync` (the same fuel bay first, then cargo; the same stacks). The module cycle's answer is unchanged.
- **Test isolation.** The rig's fleets had a shared schema, which kept memberships across tests; the rig now gives the fleets a schema of their own (`pl_<suffix>`, dropped at dispose). The probe test counts only the probes it launched: a restore on arrival may put back the record the test saved (the race A5 later serialized per system).
- **NOT RUN: the client.** No graphical client or MITM capture; the fleet menu, the spool and the arrival are not seen.

## Track A8: the capacitor, the spool, the economizers and the skill numbers (2026-10-10)

Sources, in the order of the scanner rule (user rule, FINAL): numbers from the SDE (pinned build 3396210, typeDogma of the hulls and the skills, dogmaAttributes, groups); mechanics from the EVE University wiki, implemented as written (Jump drive: https://wiki.eveuniversity.org/Jump_drive, fetched 2026-10-10; Stacking penalty: https://wiki.eveuniversity.org/Stacking_penalty, fetched 2026-10-10); EveJS v0.12.9 and eve-logic at equal rank as the last line (EveJS has no capital jump and no economizer; eve-logic decides the wire shapes).

- Capacitor (built; SDE and wiki agree). SDE attribute 898 (jumpDriveCapacitorNeed) is 0.95 on every drive hull. The skill Jump Drive Operation (3456) carries attribute 899 (jumpDriveCapacitorNeedBonus) of -5 a level. The wiki: the activation consumes 95 percent of the energy in the capacitor, reduced by 5 percent a level, 71.25 percent at level five. The need is the hull share times (1 - 0.05 x level) of the full capacity (the SDE description: from full capacitor in modifier percent). It is taken from the charge when the spool starts, refused with NOT_ENOUGH_CAPACITOR when the charge is short, and told as OnModuleAttributeChanges. Tests: scene/tests.rs (a_cyno_jump_takes_its_share_of_the_capacitor and a_cyno_jump_is_refused_when_the_capacitor_cannot_pay), JumpDriveRulesTests (Capacitor_need), CynoJumpHostTests (the spool carries 0.95).
- Refusal wording (ours). The wiki gives no refusal text. The host answers a capacitor refusal with a CustomNotify text of its own. No source read gives a text.
- Skill numbers from the SDE, not constants. Jump Drive Calibration (21611) attribute 870 = 20 (a percentage a level), Jump Fuel Conservation (21610) attribute 1296 = -10, Jump Drive Operation attribute 899 = -5. The host reads them from the skill type own typeDogma (SpaceJumpDrive.PerLevelAsync; a missing attribute throws and is not read as zero). The values equal the constants A6 used, so no number changes; the wiki 20 and 10 percent agree.
- Economizers (built). The Jump Drive Economizers are SDE group 1299 (Limited, Experimental, Prototype: attribute 1296 = -4, -7, -10). The wiki (Jump drive): stacking penalized, highest bonus first; only Jump Freighters and Rorquals. The wiki Stacking penalty page: the n-th modifier (from 0) is multiplied by e^(-(n/2.67)^2), i.e. 100, 86.9, 57.1, 28.3 percent, applied strongest first (JumpDriveRules.EconomizerMultiplier). The multiplier is applied before the round-up of the fuel. The economizers are the modules fitted in the low, medium and high slots (ItemFlags 11 to 34). Shortcut: the host does not check the SDE canFit attributes (1298, 1299) at the jump; fitting validation belongs to the fitting service, not this track. Tests: A_fitted_economizer_takes_its_share_of_the_fuel_before_the_rounding (a Limited economizer, 4 percent: 3 000 units become 2 880) and JumpDriveRulesTests.Economizers_stack.
- The spool is kept, and a restart refunds it (decision; no source states it). The wiki Jump drive page does not say what happens to an in-progress jump on a server restart or a logoff, or whether it can be cancelled. Decision: a spool is recorded with the debit (migration 0010, space.jump_spool, one row a character: the fuel taken, the field, the system, the end time and the fatigue state the jump replaced). It completes when the ship jumps (FinishJumpAsync forgets it). At the host start every spool still in the table is cancelled: the fuel goes back into the fuel bay as a new stack and the fatigue state the jump replaced is restored (CharacterShips.CancelJumpAsync, SpaceJumpDrive.CancelInterruptedAsync). Not resumed: the sessions of a restarted host are gone, and the arrival needs a live session to change the ship system. Shortcut: a spool not completed a minute after its end (SpaceJumpDrive.SpoolGraceMs; a sweep every 30 s, space.jump_spool.sweep) is taken to have been lost by the simulation and is refunded the same way. No source read gives a rule for a lost spool. Shortcut: no fuel refund into a ship that no longer exists (it was destroyed). Shortcut: the capacitor drawn for a spool is not refunded (after a restart the ship has the charge it is restored with). Tests: CynoJumpHostTests.A_spool_in_progress_is_kept_and_a_restart_refunds and A_completed_spool_is_forgotten_and_a_sweep_refunds.
- One spool a character. The table key is the character, as the simulation refuses a second spool while the first runs. Shortcut: a debit that meets a stale row of the same character (a spool the simulation lost less than a minute ago) fails with a unique violation after the simulation has accepted, and the spool stands (the same shortcut as A6 for the fuel check).
- Protocol 52. Command 26 carries a fourth argument (the capacitor share). The bump is the link protocol (crates/eve-space/src/link/mod.rs, dotnet/src/Eve.Space/SimCodec.cs), taken at push time as the next free number after 51.
- Not built (A6 open list, blockers as they stand). Jump bridges (CmdBridgeToFleetModuleBeacon, CmdBridgeToFleetDeployableBeacon), the Titan Jump Portal Generator (type 23953, group 590, fits group 30, cycle 60 s), the Covert Jump Portal Generator (type 28652, group 4127, fits group 898), portals and conduits (CmdGroupJumpTo, CmdJumpThroughFleet). Blocker: each needs a world object with a lifetime in the simulation (a portal, a bridge open state, the Titan one-minute immobilisation, the covert and industrial 20 seconds), the fleet and 2 500 m rule of a bridge, the fuel per passing mass (the wiki formula: mass times 0.000000001 for a Titan, 0.000000135 for a Black Ops, base 3 000 and 700 isotopes, skill modifier 1 - 0.1 x Jump Fuel Conservation, times the distance), and the client calls (eve-logic cynoTravelSvc.py), which are not read yet. Not started in this track.
- Not built, and no source found. Compressed fuel use: the SDE has no compressed jump isotope (the jump fuel is the group 423 isotopes 16274 and 17887 to 17889; the group 422 gaseous isotopes are not jump fuel). The wiki rule that a jump cannot be activated inside a warp interdiction bubble is not checked: the host has no bubble object for a jump to test against.
- NOT RUN: the client. No graphical client or MITM capture on this host; the capacitor bar, the refusal text and the refund are not seen in the client.
### Character-selection reservation and notification arguments (2026-10-10)

`SelectCharacterID` reserves the target character before invoking session-attribute hooks. `SpaceSessions.GetSessionAttributesAsync` may recover a stale space ship into a hangar, so a duplicate login must be rejected before that preparation can mutate persistent state. The same online authority holds the previous character until all entry hooks finish; commit releases it, rollback restores it, and disconnect cancels both reservations. Counts and snapshots expose one current character per session. Failed attribute preparation leaves the previous runtime active; rollback restores the full session attribute snapshot, removes attributes created by the failed entry, and restores the online registry before reentering previous hooks. Deferred docked-ship flags are part of that snapshot. A disconnect cancels the pending reservation, so entry cannot report success or recreate the old runtime after the connection has closed.

`OnCharacterSelected` is sent to `clientID` with zero positional arguments. This matches the V24.01 consumers [`GameUI.OnCharacterSelected`](https://github.com/Fokiiiiiii/eve-logic/blob/1dbb5711a072a146e1d228365668ac05655eefe1/eve/client/script/parklife/gameui.py#L504-L507) and [`StationSvc.OnCharacterSelected`](https://github.com/Fokiiiiiii/eve-logic/blob/1dbb5711a072a146e1d228365668ac05655eefe1/eve/client/script/ui/station/base.py#L65-L68). EveJS v0.12.9 at `828823a75f211fdecb945b087a26c225f111491f` leaves the initial notification-plan flag false by default; the existing owner-requested initial notification remains an explicit deviation (row 143). Its notification arguments are corrected, not its presence.

Verification: the 34 PostgreSQL CharacterSelect tests cover duplicate rejection before preparation, preparation failure and retry, rollback with a competing previous-character claim, session/registry agreement during reentry, disconnect during entry, and the zero-argument notification order. Six online-registry tests cover immediate entry plus pending commit, cancel, and disconnect. These checks do not establish graphical-client behavior after a notification has already been sent and a later entry hook fails.



### Space entry and retry restore (2026-10-10)

Space attribute preparation no longer retains a second ship snapshot in a pending-login dictionary. Entry reloads the active ship and saved pose from `CharacterShips` and `PgSpaceItemStore`; leaving and reentering the previous pilot therefore works after its original restore was consumed. Missing or expired restore state is an explicit entry error. Attribute preparation for an already active pilot keeps that pilot in space, since an active session has already consumed its saved pose; it does not recover that live ship into a hangar.

The PostgreSQL regression covers updated pose after preparation, previous pilot leave/reentry, repeated attribute preparation without a hangar mutation, and missing restore data. PlayerDeath's capsule setup now waits for the capsule session and final item notification, because simulation wreck placement occurs before session publication. The earlier full Space run had one wait failure; after correcting that fixture barrier, all 13 PlayerDeath cases passed. After merging the concurrent probe-scanning changes, all 577 Space tests passed (zero failed/skipped); the Host Release build had zero warnings/errors, all 143 Host tests passed, and all seven Rust simlink fixture tests passed. Graphical-client acceptance is not established.


### Effective home-station read surfaces (2026-10-10)

`home_station.get_home_station`, `charMgr.GetHomeStationRow`, and the `GetHomeStation` alias now share one resolver. It reads the PostgreSQL character choice, official-SDE NPC stations and school starter location, and the GameStore's deployed structure records. The Host shares the structure-directory deployment validator and reads the structure table once. Active structure homes retain their 64-bit IDs, type, name, owner, and SDE system hierarchy; missing or destroyed homes resolve to the school starter station with `is_fallback=true`. With no usable school or current station, Row stays `None` and the lower service retains its unavailable-station error.

Sources: EveJS v0.12.9 [`isHomeStationLost`](https://github.com/Fokiiiiiii/evejs-v0.12.9/blob/828823a75f211fdecb945b087a26c225f111491f/server/src/services/character/homeStationRuntime.js#L71-L78), `homeStationService.resolveStation`, and `charMgrService.buildHomeStationPayload`; V24.01 [`CharacterSheet.GetHomeStationRow`](https://github.com/Fokiiiiiii/eve-logic/blob/1dbb5711a072a146e1d228365668ac05655eefe1/eve/client/script/ui/shared/neocom/charactersheet.py#L61-L65). Both C# read surfaces deliberately expose the same effective fallback location. They do not mutate the saved home choice or publish notifications.

Verification: 55 PostgreSQL CharacterInfo tests, including NPC, active 64-bit structure, missing/destroyed structure, unavailable fallback, and alias equality. The Host regression dispatches through a real persistence structure-directory read model. This slice does not implement candidate generation, remote-change cooldown, or home-station mutation, and does not establish graphical-client acceptance.

## Track A7: probe scanning (2026-10-10)

Source order, as in "Source priority for the scanner track": the SDE for every number (the probe types 30013, 30028, 30486 and 30488: attributes 1370 to 1373 and 1413; the launchers 17938, 18639, 4258: attributes 73 and 846; the skills 3412, 25739, 25810, 25811: attributes 66, 846 and 1156; the ships' 208 to 211 and 552), then the EVE University wiki "Probe scanning" as written (oldid 229626) for the rules, then EVE.js v0.12.9 where the wiki is silent, and the eve-logic client for the wire (the names, groups and tiers of the rows).

- **Targets: the ships, drones, fighters and scannable deployables of the scene.** The wiki: "Combat scan probes can find structures, ships, and drones. Wrecks, cargo containers, and cloaked ships cannot be scanned with probes." EVE.js `resolveEntityScanGroup` (ships, drones, deployables, NPCs). Fighters go to the Fighters group (2), which eve-logic names (`scanSvc.py` `NAME_BY_SCANGROUPID`); EveJS puts them in the drones group. The client's wire is eve-logic's, so the Fighters group is used.
- **Not targets: wrecks and containers** (the wiki's quoted sentence; EveJS has no kind for them), **cloaked ships** (the same sentence; EveJS `isEntityHiddenFromCombatProbes`), **celestials** (planets, moons, stargates, stations, belts: no probe group names them in eve-logic `scanSvc.py` (`NAME_BY_SCANGROUPID` has no celestial entry, although `probescanning/const.py` numbers one), EveJS has no celestial probe group, and the wiki's "Scanning" table finds belts with the Overview), **structures in space** (eve.rs's scene holds no structure entity: nothing to scan), **probes, bombs, interdiction probes, lance beacons** (not in the scan groups of EveJS, and the wiki names none of them).
- **Deployables: the cynosural fields, the Mobile Cynosural Inhibitor and the mobile warp disruptors** (group Deployable 12, the group eve-logic names; EveJS `resolveEntityScanGroup` maps the deployables to it). The wiki names no deployable; the choice is the reference's.
- **Core probes find no ship.** The wiki: "Core scanner probes" find cosmic signatures and only combat probes find "structures, ships, and drones"; the SDE gives attribute 1413 (probeCanScanShips) to the combat probes 30028 and 30486 and not to the core probes 30013 and 30488 (EveJS `isProbeEligibleForSite`). A core probe's scan leaves a ship absent.
- **Strength at a range step: the base strength over the range factor to the step minus one.** SDE 1371 is "given at the base scan range" (the wiki), and the factor per step is SDE 1373 (2), which gives the wiki's statement that a combat probe at 0.5 AU equals a core probe at 0.25 AU once doubled. EveJS `resolveProbeStrengthAtRange` (the same division).
- **Strength bonus: the skills' and the launcher's attribute 846, added as percentages.** The SDE gives 846 to Astrometrics and to Astrometric Rangefinding (5 a level) and to the T2 launcher (5). The wiki's "42 = 40 plus 5 percent due to Astrometrics I" is a test. EveJS adds the same bonuses (`addProbeScanBonus`). The rigs, arrays, implants, boosters and the ship's own bonus are not counted (see TODO section 27).
- **Required strength of a ship: sensor strength over signature radius, times 20, at least 0.25.** The wiki gives no formula for a ship's signal: its ship page gives the Probe's "LADAR Sensor 9 points" and "Sig. Radius 38 m" (the SDE's 208 to 211 and 552). The formula is EveJS `buildCombatTargetSite` (the strongest of the four sensor attributes; no sensor reads as 1). A bigger ship is easier to scan, a stronger sensor harder.
- **Result certainty: EveJS's caps on the count of the probes that reach, times their spread.** A probe reaches a target inside its range, with `1 - (distance / range)^2` times its strength over the requirement, at most 1 (EveJS `scoreProbeCoverage`). One probe is capped at 0.249, two at 0.499, three at 0.999 (times 0.9 of their mean), four or more at 1.2 times the mean of the strongest four, at most 1. The wiki's tiers match the counts: "Sphere" (one probe), "Circle" (two), "Two Dots" (three), "One Dot" (four or more, warpable at 100 percent). The spread factor is EveJS `resolveFormationGeometryQuality` (below four reaching probes it is 1; else the mean separation of the strongest four over their mean range, over 0.2, at most 1).
- **Result tiers: group from 25 percent, name from 75 percent, exact at 100.** The wiki: "The type is identified at 25% scan, the name is revealed at 75% scan and the site is warpable at 100% scan." Below 25 percent the row has neither group nor type (the client names it by its scan group: "unknown signal"). At 100 percent the row's data and position are the target's exact place (a tuple, as eve-logic's `IsPerfectResult` requires).
- **Maximum deviation: the wiki's formula, exact.** "Max Deviation = Base Maximum Deviation x (Scan Range / Base Scan Range) x (100 - Astrometrics x 5) / 100 x (100 - Astrometric Pinpointing x 5) / 100" (the wiki's table is a test: core 0.1188 AU at 0.25 AU, combat 30.4 AU at 64 AU). The skills' factors are multiplied one per skill, as the wiki writes them. EveJS adds the two skills' modifiers before it multiplies; the two differ only when both skills are trained (Astrometrics and Pinpointing), and the wiki is taken.
- **A result below 100 percent is placed off its target, not exactly.** The wiki: "the deviation of your scan will always be less than the distance shown" (the maximum). The position is the target plus a random direction at `maxDeviation x (1 - certainty)`, and the row's data is the maximum deviation. This is the wiki's rule plus the statement that stronger signals deviate less. EveJS places a hash-derived hint and blends it to the target (`buildDeterministicTargetHint`); that fixed placement is not used. The random source is `Random.Shared`; the tests inject a seeded one.
- **Absent targets: a non-signature target that no probe reaches is absent** (EveJS `buildSignatureScanResults`: the client clears it). A cosmic signature that no probe reaches is left alone (the same).
- **Scan duration: the launcher's attribute 73 (10 s on the Core and Expanded launchers, the SDE), times each scanning skill's attribute 66 as a product (EveJS `resolveCharacterScanDurationBonusMultiplier`).** A fallback of 8 s is used only when a launcher has no attribute 73 (EveJS `DEFAULT_PROBE_SCAN_DURATION_MS`). The skills are Astrometrics and Acquisition (-5 a level each, SDE 66).
- **The probes move before the scan: `OnProbeWarpStart` at once, `OnProbeWarpEnd` and `OnSystemScanStarted` 1.25 s later** (EveJS `scheduleProbeMovementThenScan`, `DEFAULT_PROBE_MOVE_DURATION_MS`). The probe's ball moves by a removal and a new placement (the simulation has no move command for a probe); the record's place is the destination (the client sends it; the reference does the same).
- **One scan per pilot per system, and a request for no idle probe answers none.** EveJS returns null for both; the client raises its own "scan in progress" (`UserErrorIfScanning`).
- **The stop answers the pilot alone, and the probes go idle whether or not the pilot is still there.** The reference sends `OnSystemScanStopped` to the scanning session; when the pilot has left the system the probes are set idle and nothing is sent (`scanHandler` on the client would drop it anyway).
- **Restart: both timed phases are durable jobs** (`space.probes.scan.start`, `space.probes.scan.stop`, keyed by the character). The reference keeps them in memory (`setTimeout`). A restart during a scan therefore finishes it; the reference's scan would be lost with its timer. Not exercised by a process restart in a test.
- **SetActivityState: an idle probe becomes inactive and back (state 0 and 1).** EveJS `setCharacterProbeActivity` (`probeRuntimeState`) is read for its effect on the count of active probes only; a probe that scans is left as it is.
- **Scan results are sent as the reference's rows** (EveJS `buildScanResultEntry`: id, scan group, group, type, strength attribute, dungeon fields as none, item, difficulty 1, certainty, data, pos). A ship's item id is its target id; a signature's item id is none in EveJS and will be set when a site source exists.
- **Warp to a result is the existing warp; the completed-scan warp authority is not ported.** The client warps to a probed object by its id (the existing warp commands, which the simulation answers for a ball in the scene). EVE.js keeps the perfect results of each completed scan per session (`probeScanWarpAuthority.js`, `recordCompletedScan` and `resolveCompletedScanWarpTarget`) and resolves a scan warp only through them. That authority is not built: a warp to a probed ship or deployable is not checked against the scan, so "warpable at 100 percent" is not enforced by the server. Signatures, which need that path, are not built either.
- **NOT RUN: the client.** The probe scanner's windows, the rows, the warp to a result and the MITM capture (`tools/mitm/README.md`) are not run on this host: the headless client has no scenario that scans.


### Home-station change workflow (2026-10-11)

Implementation extends the existing shared home-station resolver with candidates, the remote-change deadline, set and reset RPCs. The write authority is the PostgreSQL character row; office candidates come from active `CorporationOfficeStore` rentals and deployed-structure/SDE locations through Host composition. Coupled home/deadline updates must commit before the session and `OnHomeStationChanged` notification.

Contract sources: EveJS `828823a75f211fdecb945b087a26c225f111491f`, `server/src/services/character/homeStationService.js` (candidate generation, validation, datetime pickle, mutation and reset); V24.01 eve-logic `1dbb5711a072a146e1d228365668ac05655eefe1`, `homestation/client/service.py`, `homestation/types.py`, `homestation/validation.py`, `homestation/const.py`. Both executable reference and client define a 365-day remote cooldown; the school station is exempt. Corporation office eligibility is also documented by [CCP Corporation Offices](https://support.eveonline.com/hc/en-us/articles/203209652-Corporation-Offices); [Home Station](https://wiki.eveuniversity.org/Home_Station) corroborates the school exception and Thera allowance.

Reference verification: executed the actual pinned EveJS service source in an in-memory dependency harness covering candidate ordering/deduplication, remote confirmation refusal, remote change, deadline and cooldown refusal, local/school exemption, reset, and expiry. Python decoded the reference datetime pickle and matched its UTC deadline. This is executable service verification, not a running EveJS server or graphical-client test. Initial PostgreSQL verification: Characters 104/104, CharacterInfo 59/59, and actual Host-composition candidate/change/reset scenario 1/1, no skips. After integrating upstream through `695d0fb389c5dcdf39235e3556fa0a40c55ed79c`: Characters 104/104, CharacterInfo 65/65, CharacterSelect 58/58, Host 202/202, Services 80/80, selected space-entry/structure-docking/player-death cases 17/17, and Rust simlink fixtures 7/7, no skips. The first integrated Host run exposed an environment-variable race with existing composition fixtures; sharing their `host-composition` collection and isolating the newly added structure/agent schemas fixed it. The Host fixture disables unrelated ESI market capture and simulation, restores all overridden environment values, and drops its scratch schemas. A regression reproduces and fixes current-structure candidates without an office, including control-seat exclusion. Notification comparisons include the complete marshalled packet and sequence. The extended Host scenario imports a 64-bit structure through `Eve.Structures.StructureStore`, changes the home while docked there without an office, re-reads the shared authority, and decodes the notification's 64-bit ID; the focused scenario passes. Re-run the reference with `node tools/dev/test-home-station-reference.cjs <EveJS repository root>`. The desktop's Codex Serena exposure omits `create_text_file`; a transient desktop-app MCP session recovered that tool and created the comparison script without changing persistent configuration.

## Track A8: the jump bridges, the bubble check and the economizer fit (protocol 55, 2026-10-10)

Sources, in the order of the scanner rule (user rule, FINAL). Numbers: the SDE (pinned build 3396210): the generator modules 23953 (Jump Portal Generator I, group 590) and 28652 (Covert Jump Portal Generator I, group 4127): attribute 6 (capacitor need, 1000 and absent), 713 and 714 (the activation isotopes: 16275 and 500, Titan only), 1001 (the portal consumption mass factor: 1.5e-9 and 2e-7), 1002 (the portal duration: 60 000 and 20 000 ms), 1298 (the hull group it fits: 30 and 898), 3318 (the passenger flag it requires: 3319, 3320); the hulls: 866 to 868, 1971, 1972 (0.25 on Black Ops hulls), attribute 4 (mass); the passenger flags 3319 (default 1), 3320 and 3325 (default 0), read as the SDE defaults when a hull has no row; the skill Jump Portal Generation (24562) attribute 1775 (-10 a level). Mechanics: the wiki "Jump drives" (https://wiki.eveuniversity.org/Jump_drives, fetched 2026-10-10: a Titan bridge stays open one minute and immobilises the ship holding it; covert and industrial bridges 20 seconds; fleetmates within 2 500 metres; the fuel formula with base cost 3 000 (Titan) and 700 (Black Ops); the Strontium of 500 modified by Jump Portal Generation; Black Ops and Rorqual bridges have no opening cost; a Titan bridge goes only to a standard cynosural field or a beacon, a covert bridge to any cyno; the bridge cannot be taken inside a warp disruption bubble; a bridge needs Jump Portal Generation at level one) and the wiki "Black Ops" (https://wiki.eveuniversity.org/Black_Ops, fetched 2026-10-10: the covert generator and its passengers: covert ops ships). EveJS v0.12.9: server/src/services/ship/beyonceService.js (Handle_CmdBridgeToFleetModuleBeacon at line 2871, Handle_CmdBridgeToFleetDeployableBeacon at 3019, Handle_CmdJumpThroughFleet at 2954, resolveBridgePortalModule at 991, calculateBridgeFuelQuantity at 1236, the passenger resolution at 1560 to 1720, JUMP_PORTAL_MASS_FACTOR_BY_TYPE_ID at 216, isEligibleJumpPortalPassenger at 973). eve-logic: eve/client/script/parklife/cynoTravelSvc.py (read in full: the five bridge and jump-through calls and their arguments), eve/client/script/parklife/fleetSvc.py (OnBridgeModeChange at 200, GetActiveBridgeForShip at 242).

- Decision: the portal mass factor is the generator module's own attribute 1001 (1.5e-9 for the Titan generator, 2e-7 for the covert one). The wiki gives 1e-9 and 1.35e-7 (its table) and EveJS uses the module's attribute before its table (the same numbers as the SDE module). The SDE is first: the module's attribute is used. Conflict with the wiki recorded here.
- Decision: the covert bridge goes to any cynosural field, as the wiki says. EveJS gives the covert generator only the covert fields (getRequiredPortalPassengerAttributeForField, line 876). The wiki is the logic source at the higher rank; the conflict is recorded here.
- Decision: a Titan bridge holds the ship still for its minute (the wiki: it immobilises the ship holding it open). A covert bridge holds nothing: the wiki does not say it does (it says only the lifetime), so none is taken.
- Decision: the activation isotopes are the SDE's 714 less 10 percent a level of Jump Portal Generation (1775). They are checked against the bridging ship before the simulation is asked, and debited after it accepts (as the jump drive's fuel is). The capacitor is the module's attribute 6 in GJ (1 000 for the Titan generator), taken when the bridge opens.
- Decision: the passenger fuel is the wiki's formula with the SDE numbers: LY times passenger mass times the mass factor times the bridging hull's 868 less 10 percent a level of Jump Fuel Conservation, plus the module's 2793 (none on the generators), rounded up after rounding to six decimals (the SDE products carry float noise: EveJS notes the same defect). The isotopes come from the bridging ship (fuel bay first), not the passenger's.
- Decision: the passenger's fatigue is the wiki's rule (JumpDriveRules.AfterJump) on LY times the passenger hull's 1971 times the bridging hull's 1972 (a Black Ops bridge gives 0.25; a Titan 1.0). The two multipliers compose, as EveJS composes them. The passenger's cooldown applies too.
- Decision: a passenger leaves at once through the bridge (command 29 has no spool); the sources read give no delay (EveJS jumps the session at once).
- Decision: the eligibility of a passenger is its hull's flag for the generator's 3318 (SDE default for a row-less hull; the wiki: Titan bridges take all subcapital ships, Black Ops bridges take the covert-cloak ships). Refused with the text "Your ship cannot pass through that jump bridge." (ours).
- Decision: the Titan bridge is refused in high security (the wiki's capital rule, "Capital ships", as the jump drive's). The bridging ship must be within its own jump range of the field (the wiki), using the same SDE range and calibration as the jump.
- Decision: the bubble check. The wiki says a jump drive cannot be activated inside a warp interdiction bubble, and a ship in one cannot take a bridge. The simulation's warp_disrupted_at (every warp disruption source: a bubble, a probe, a mobile disruptor) refuses the cyno jump (WARP_DISRUPTED_BY_BUBBLE), the bridge opening and the passage. Shortcut: the sources in the check are the simulation's, which are wider than the bubble alone.
- Decision: economizers count only when they fit the hull (SDE 1298 and 1299 name the groups: 902 and 883). The wiki: only Jump Freighters and Rorquals carry them.
- Decision: the bridge lives in the simulation as a deployable of role JumpBridge with no client ball (the reference draws the portal on the ship; no ball was read). It ends with its time (the simulation's expiry); the host keeps the record for the fleet and its isotopes and closes it on a sweep every five seconds (OnBridgeModeChange, not active). A restart ends every bridge (the simulation and the host both lose them), as the spools did in A8 part one. Shortcut: the sweep's five seconds is the only delay in the fleet's closing notice.
- Decision: the fleet is told the bridge is open with OnBridgeModeChange(shipID, destination system, beacon id, true) (eve-logic fleetSvc.py line 200); the beacon id is the field's deployable id, as the A6 beacon is. The second argument of the notification ("fleetid") follows A6's fleet notifications.
- Decision: the deployable variant (CmdBridgeToFleetDeployableBeacon) takes the field by its deployable id and gives no answer, as the module variant does; EveJS's Handle_CmdBridgeToFleetDeployableBeacon returns no answer either (line 3019 to 3090).
- Refusal texts: the wiki gives none. Those that EveJS gives ("That fleet bridge is no longer available.", "The bridge ship does not have enough jump portal fuel.", "Your ship is not fitted with an online jump portal generator for that cynosural field.", "Your pilot cannot ...") are the same words; the rest are ours.
- Tests: scene/tests.rs (a_titan_bridge_opens..., a_fleet_ship_in_range..., a_passenger_beyond..., a_cynosural_jump_is_refused_inside...); CynoJumpHostTests (A_titan_bridge_opens..., A_bridge_is_closed...); JumpDriveRulesTests (A_bridge_takes..., A_passenger_flag..., An_economizer_counts...).
- Not built: conduit jumps and group jumps (CmdGroupJumpToFleetModuleBeacon, CmdGroupJumpToFleetDeployableBeacon, the conduit ships Black Ops, Rorqual, carriers). Blocker: a conduit moves its passengers WITH the anchor, so the spool timing and the passengers' departure need a rule the wiki gives only in part (passenger count and radius are given, the spool is not), and EveJS conduitJumpRuntime.js (568 lines) is not read in full yet. Not built, not claimed. The industrial bridge (Rorqual, generator 63140, flag 3325) is not built either: the OpenAsync only accepts the Titan and the covert generator.
- NOT RUN: the client. No graphical client or MITM capture; the bridge's notice, the jump menu and the passage are not seen in the client.
- **Warp to a result: the completed scan's 100 percent places only** (EveJS `probeScanWarpAuthority.js`, `recordCompletedScan` and `resolveCompletedScanWarpTarget`). `ProbeScanWarpAuthority` keeps the perfect results of each completed scan per session (a `ConditionalWeakTable` keyed by the client session) and `BeyonceService` `CmdWarpToStuff` takes a "scan" warp only to one of them, to its exact place. A warp to a target that no completed scan found at 100 percent is refused. The record is in memory: a restart drops it, so a pilot warps to a result only after a scan completes again. Cosmic signatures reach the same path through `ICosmicSiteSource` once a source exists.
- **Recover: the charges go back where they came from** (EveJS `Handle_RecoverProbes` and `restoreRecoveredProbeCharges`, 1391 to 1552). Each probe that left goes back into the launcher it came from while that launcher holds no other charge and has room for it (its capacity, `AmmoRules.Capacity`); the rest go to the ship's cargo hold, joining the stack of their type there (`CharacterShips.RestoreProbesAsync`, one transaction). The pilot is told the stacks that changed and each probe that left (`OnRemoveProbe`); the reply is the ids that were recovered.
- **Reconnect: the probes come back to the pilot's screen** (EveJS `Handle_ReconnectToLostProbes`, 2778). Each probe still in its life and not inactive is told as new (`OnNewProbe`), then all of them as idle (`OnProbesIdle`). The balls are not placed again: a restart puts them back in space when a ship enters the system (track A5).
- **Directional scan: the cone of the wiki, from the ship** (EveJS `Handle_ConeScan`, 2470 to 2590, and the helpers 436 to 624; the wiki's "Directional scanning"). Command 28 (protocol 55, `DirectionalScan`, answered as `DirectionalTargets`, kind 72) gives every object the scene can show: ships (player and NPC hull ships), drones, fighter squadrons, scanner probes, wrecks, and the bodies of the system. The host decides. The cone is the scan angle around the direction (a sphere at 2π), out to the range: at least 10 km, at most the ship's SDE 5796 range, and 14.3 AU when the ship has none (`DSCAN_FALLBACK_MAX_RANGE_METERS`). Distance and cone are measured from the ship's surface to the target's, each target's radius being the SDE 162 of its type (ships: the simulation's radius). Shown: a ship that is not cloaked, not in warp and not a combat recon ship; drones; probes; wrecks (group 186, not corpses); planets, moons, asteroid belts, stargates and stations (groups 7, 8, 9, 10, 15). Not shown: a type with SDE 1958 (`dscanImmune`). The rows are nearest first (ties by id), each with the id, the type and the group only. A pilot not in space, or a zero direction, gets an empty list (the reference's answer too).
- **Directional scan: what is not shown, and the gaps.** Normal NPCs: the wiki shows the ones named with a diamond, and the simulation keeps no NPC names, so none is shown. Fighter squadrons, containers, corpses, beacons and the star (group 6) are not in the wiki's list and are not shown. Cosmic signatures and anomalies are the separate system (`ICosmicSiteSource`). The scope check of `canEntityDiscoverTarget` is always true: eve.rs has no abyssal or dungeon scopes. The 5-second cycle is the client's; the server does not limit the scan rate.
- **NOT RUN: the client.** The probe scanner's windows, the rows, the directional palette, the warp to a result and the MITM capture (`tools/mitm/README.md`) are not run on this host: the headless client has no scenario that scans.

## Track S1: ships and fitting, part 1

- **The sweep reads the SDE, not a fixed list.** Ships come from `DogmaStatic.TypesInCategoryAsync("Ship")` (published category-6 types with dogma), and the subsystems from the "Subsystem" category. The count of 423 published ships is the SDE of build 3396210 (`sde/lock.json`), asserted as the baseline.
- **Subsystems belong to a hull by name.** The SDE has no `canFit` attribute on a subsystem (the Legion Defensive item 45586 carries only 182, 277, 1366, 1374, 1375, 1376). The sweep matches the name prefix `<hull name> <class> - ` (for example "Legion Defensive - Covert Reconfiguration"), which the SDE names give for all 48 items. The fitting rules themselves are not changed here; how eve.rs decides hull membership in production is SF-4's question.
- **Known gap, asserted exactly, not hidden.** The Strategic Cruisers offer five subsystem flags (SDE 1367 = 5, clamp to eight) against four classes. The test lists the four hulls and fails if the list changes (see TODO section 29). Choice of the four-class rule: the SDE holds four subsystem classes (1366 values 125 to 128, twelve items each); the wiki (Strategic Cruisers) states four subsystem types. Record: SDE is the data of record (AGENTS.md source priority).
- **Probe rigs by hull rig size.** The rig of the hull's SDE rigSize (1547): 1 small (26929), 2 medium (4395), 3 large (4397), 4 capital (33303, Capital Processor Overclocking Unit I). Each rig is 150 calibration (SDE 1153) and is fitted alone, so the calibration check does not decide the probe.
- **Probe high module: the Small Energy Neutralizer I (533)** (SDE effect 12, high power, no turret or launcher effect). The Civilian Miner (3651) is not used: it carries the turret effect (42), so it is refused on any hull without turret hardpoints, which is the rule working, not a bug.
- **Skills for the probes:** the pilot is trained to the highest level any probe type asks (`FittingRules.RequiredSkills`), so the sweep tests the slots and not the skills.

- **Fitting persistence lives in the fitting schema, not in the JSON game store.** The reference keeps `savedFittings` in its store (fittingStore.js); eve.rs keeps the same records in `fitting.saved_fitting` (migration 0005), so a restart keeps them and the owner's limit is counted in the same transaction as the insert.
- **Fitting ids come from a PostgreSQL identity column**, not from the reference's allocator (fittingStore.js `allocateNextFittingID`). The client only needs a positive integer, unique across owners; the ids are not compared with the reference's numbers.
- **The Fitting Manager role is read from the member's role mask** (`Member.Masks.Roles`, bit 1 << 59, the reference's `CORP_ROLE_FITTING_MANAGER`, fittingStore.js:50). The reference reads the session's `corprole`; eve.rs keeps no such session attribute. Alliance changes also need the session's corporation to be the alliance executor (`AllianceRecord.ExecutorCorporationId`), as in `assertSessionCanMutateOwner`.
- **Notifications reach every session of the owner** (`CharacterSessions.Of`, `OfCorporation`, and the sessions whose `allianceid` matches), with idtype `charid` as the reference sends them (fittingMgrServiceHelpers.js `notifyFittingMutation`).
- **Validation uses the SDE categories through the static data** (`DogmaStatic`): the ship type is category 6 or 65 and each item type must exist. The reference uses its own type table (`resolveItemByTypeID`); the categories are the same SDE data.
- **A missing or foreign fitting is refused before the draft is checked** (`FITTING_NOT_FOUND`), as the reference orders the checks. The reference's name and description limits (50 and 500) are applied by cutting the trimmed text.


## Track S2: ships and fitting, part 2 (2026-10-10)

Source order (user rule): numbers and attributes from the SDE first; mechanics from the EVE University wiki as written, then EveJS v0.12.9, CCP documents and eve-logic at equal rank; client wire from eve-logic. Where a source states nothing, the choice is recorded here with the sources it was checked against.

- **Stance mode items are in the ship's dogma world, in the hidden modifier bucket (flag 156), and are not fitted modules.** EveJS `liveFittingState.js` keeps the stance item in the fit state; eve.rs loaded only the slot flags (`ShipSnapshot.LoadAsync`). Test: `ShipStanceWorldTests` (SDE effects 6010 and 6014).
- **OnStanceActive (shipID, stanceID) goes to the pilot after the commit, and a ship in space is sent again.** EveJS `shipStanceRuntime.js` `setShipStance` (notification after the replacement of the item). The notification is the `charid` form (`shipmode/notifier.py:18`). Test: `ShipStanceNotifyTests`.
- **Deviation from EveJS: a stance is switched in space only.** EveJS has no station or space gate. The wiki's Tactical Destroyers page says the modes are switched "while in space", and the wiki ranks above EveJS, so the server refuses a switch in station with `SHIP_STANCE_NOT_IN_SPACE` ("Tactical modes can only be switched in space."). The client shows the buttons in station too (`station/base.py:269`), so a click there gets this refusal. Test: `ShipStanceNotifyTests`.
- **The stance table is checked against the SDE, not derived from it at run time.** The six hulls and their mode types match the client's `shipmode/data.py` and EveJS's table (34317, 34562, 34828, 35683, 89808, 89807). Group 1306 holds the modes; the switch time (1985) is the cooldown: 10 s for the four Tactical Destroyers and Anhinga, 2 s for Skua. The wiki's page gives one cooldown, "once every 10 seconds", for all hulls; the SDE value is used, since the SDE is the data of record and it says 2 s for Skua. Test: `ShipStanceTableTests`.
- **The slim `shipStance` field is not sent.** The client reads it (`environment/spaceObject/ship.py:214`) and treats a missing one as no stance; the field is a Destiny change in the simulation wire, which this track does not change (the integrator's batch). Its value would be the old stance, the switch time and the new stance (`notifier.py:37`).
- **AssembleShip answers the assembled ship's id (or a list, or None), not the rows EveJS answers.** The client reads the answer as an item id (`invItemFunctions.py:197-211`: `GetInventoryFromId(item_id)` and the default skin). EveJS `Handle_AssembleShip` answers `[{list of rows}, {dict}]`, which the client does not read as ids.
- **AssembleShip assembles any packaged hull, and a Strategic Cruiser also takes its subsystems.** EveJS `Handle_AssembleShip` (`moveItemsAndSetShipPackagingState(..., packaged=false)` sets the ship singleton). Refusals are silent, as in EveJS: a ship that cannot be assembled is left out, and the client shows nothing for it.
- **A Strategic Cruiser takes one subsystem of each class (125 Core, 126 Defensive, 127 Offensive, 128 Propulsion), each for its hull (SDE 1380, `fitsToShipType`).** EveJS `liveFittingState.js` (`STRATEGIC_CRUISER_SUBSYSTEM_FLAGS`, `isSubsystemCompatibleWithStrategicCruiser`); the wiki (Strategic Cruisers: four subsystems, one per class). The hull's `maxSubSystems` (1367) says five, and the SDE holds four classes: `Slots.Count` caps the subsystem family at four. The SDE's subsystem names are the wiki's classes. Tests: `StrategicCruiserAssemblyTests`, `StrategicCruiserRulesTests`.
- **The fit check refuses a subsystem for another hull** (SDE 1380 on the subsystem; EveJS checks the same in `isSubsystemCompatibleWithStrategicCruiser`). Before this track the fit check did not read 1380, so a Legion's defensive subsystem could be fitted to a Tengu.
- **Undock of an incomplete Strategic Cruiser is refused with the reference's words, before the simulation is asked.** EveJS `shipService.js` `_assertStrategicCruiserSubsystems` (`CustomNotify`: "A strategic cruiser requires one Core, Defensive, Offensive, and Propulsion subsystem before it can be used. Missing: ..."). The reference's further checks (CPU, powergrid, turret and launcher overloads at undock) are not made here: the fit check makes them when a module is fitted.
- **Hull cargo is the SDE's `types.capacity`.** The intrinsic attribute 38 is loaded from the `types` table (`DogmaStatic.IntrinsicAttributes`); the SDE's `typeDogma` has no attribute 38 for hulls. The earlier note that the SDE had no hull cargo was the wrong table. Test: `HullDataTests`.
- **The packaged volumes are the client's table, not SDE values.** `ItemVolumes` holds the client's `packagedVolumeOverrides` (per group and type). The SDE's `types.volume` is the assembled volume (Tengu 92000 m3, packaged 5000 m3). The two are different quantities; the table is kept.
- **Pinned SDE gaps (no value in the static data, and the SDE is the data of record):** no `capacity` for Capsule 670, Capsule Genolution 33328 and Monitor 45534 (cargo 0); no skill requirement (attribute 182) for Capsule 670, Capsule Genolution 33328, Echelon 3532 and Primae 2863 (nothing is required to fly them); no `typeBonus` row and no dogma effect for Opux Luxury Yacht 635. Test: `HullDataTests`. The wiki and CCP pages for these four hulls were not read in this track.
