  (`skillTradingRuntime.js`), which eve.rs does not record. `contextualOfferMgr.Track*` keeps its events in memory; no
  offer is served by eve.rs, so no tracking call is made. `localizationServer.UpdateLocalizationQAWrap` and
  `ReloadFSDPickle` change only in-process state. `communityTrackMgr` keeps its instances in memory (the community track
  state is not persisted). Status: open, each with its owner in TODO section 41.
- **Loyalty point store.** `LPStoreMgr` answers no offers and refuses every take (False). The reference's offers come from
  the loyalty point data that eve.rs does not have yet. Status: open (the agents and loyalty track).
- **Map reads without a data source.** `map.GetSolarSystemVisits`, `GetHistory`, `GetConstellationLPData`,
  `GetAllRoamingWeatherSystems`, `GetMyExtraMapInfo`, `GetMyExtraMapInfoAgents`, `GetDeadspaceAgentsMap`,
  `GetDeadspaceComplexMap` and `GetFacWarZoneInfo` answer the reference's empty shape (a rowset, a dict, None, or the
  faction id asked for with no upgrade levels): eve.rs has no visit, statistics, loyalty, weather, deadspace or
  factional-warfare state. The per-system edencom and triglavian lists (`GetEdencomFortressSystems`,
  `GetEdencomMinorVictorySystems`, `GetTriglavianMinorVictorySystems`) are the reference's constant lists; the SDE does
  not carry them and no state produces them. `map.GetBeaconCount` answers an empty dictionary, although the cynosural
  beacons of track A9 exist: the count belongs to the ships track (the other agent). Status: each open (TODO section 41).
- **Report row types (slice 2, 2026-10-12).** `lastUpdated` is a long (a FILETIME), as EVE.js's answer has it; the field was a real. The key sets of `GetIncursionGlobalReport` and `GetSystemsInIncursions` equal EVE.js's in the live walk (`test_incursion_walk.py`). Status: verified against EVE.js.
- **Incursions and anomalies come from the content state.** `map.GetIncursionGlobalReport`, `map.GetSystemsInIncursions`,
  `taleMgr.*` and `scanMgr.*` read the content instances of kind `incursion` and `anomaly` (`PersistenceIncursionAnomalyReadModel`).
  With none seeded, the answers are the empty contract shapes (an empty list, an empty rowset, an empty tale list).
  Incursions are simulated since B-L1 (see "Incursions (B-L1, B-S1, B-M1)" below); the content kind is no longer read.
- **Live suite isolation.** The live run kept the game store (`gamestore`, which holds the mailboxes), and the corporation,
  market, fleet, industry and asset-safety tables, in the shared default schemas. Each run now gets its own scratch
  schema for each of them, and the script drops them at its end (`tools/dev/run-eve-live.sh`). `LiveScriptIsolationTests`
  checks that every schema variable the host reads is scratch in the script and dropped in its cleanup. Status: done.

## Market slice M1: the wire, the labels and the transaction tabs (2026-10-10)

Sources: the V24.01 client (eve-logic) call sites named in each item; EVE.js v0.12.9 `server/src/services/market/marketProxyService.js` for the
observable behaviour of the five RPCs it implements (not its bugs). Design: `market-design.md` section 4.3 M1, decisions `market-decisions.md`.

- **Superseded 2026-10-13 (see the immediate buy entry at the end of this section):** BuyMultipleItems answers the empty list.** The reference (proxy 6915) executes one immediate buy per line at the line's price, range
  station, with the line's minimum volume, and answers the empty list, because immediate orders rest nowhere. eve.rs does the same, one
  transaction per line (keys `buy-multi:<line>`). An unfilled line is not an error, as in the reference (the client's own pre-check
  already drops the lines it cannot price). Client: `marketsvc.py:270-273` (BuyMulti iterates the answer), `buyMultiFromBase.py:306`.
  Status: done (M1). Tests: `MarketRpcTests.Buy_multi_fills...`, `MarketHostTests`, live `test_market.py`.
- **PlacePlexSellOrder.** The reference (proxy 7009) refuses a corporation sell and any type other than PLEX (44992). eve.rs does the
  same. The two texts are ours: "PLEX sell orders are placed on the personal market only." and "PLEX sell orders must use a PLEX
  inventory item." (the reference's own wording is not in the client snapshot; the client shows the text it receives). The answer is a
  boolean, true when the line traded or rests, as `executeSellEntry` (proxy 7034). Client: `marketsvc.py:278-279`, `sellMulti.py:464`.
  Status: done (M1). Test: `MarketRpcTests.Plex_sell_is_refused...`.
- **ModifyPlexCharOrder** delegates to the ModifyCharOrder path (proxy 7274 does the same). The stale-view precondition of Q15 is M2.
  Client: `marketsvc.py:291-295`. Status: done (M1).
- **CharGetTransactions and CorpGetTransactions (the read path, M1 scope).** Rows are the fills (`market.fills`) where the wallet bought or
  sold, as the reference's transaction rows (proxy 6582-6642, `buildMarketTransactionEntry` at 559-586): transactionID, transactionDate
  (FILETIME), typeID, quantity, price (per unit: the client multiplies), stationID, locationID, buyerID, sellerID, accountID (the viewer's
  division), buyerAccountID, sellerAccountID, journalRefID (-1: no journal reference is kept by the market), keyID. Newest first; the
  fromDate is a FILETIME, as the reference filters `transactionDate >= fromDate`. The viewer's division (accountID) is what the client
  reads to tell a company trade from a personal one (`marketSvc.py:41-64`). The read rule of the corporation call is the reference's
  (`corporationAuthority.js:134-147`): an Accountant or a Junior Accountant reads every division; any other member reads the division
  whose Account Take it holds; a null division needs the accountant role. A refusal is `CrpAccessDenied` with reason `(101,
  'UI/Corporations/AccessRestrictions/NoAccessToWalletDivision')`, as the reference sends it (proxy 6625-6630).
  Deviation (rule source): the Director role is not a read right here, as in the reference (`corporationAuthority.js:138-143`).
  Deviation (scope): the composite index and the division filter tests of M6 are not added in M1; the query uses the existing
  `fills_buyer` and `fills_seller` indexes (002). Status: done (M1); M6 completes the tests and the index check.
- **keyID is the order's account key (G1).** Owner-order rows (GetCharOrders, GetCorporationOrders, GetMarketOrderHistory) and the
  OnOwnOrdersChanged rows carry the order's wallet division (`accountKey`, 1000 to 1006), not 1000. The reference keeps the order's account
  key (`marketProxyService.js:579-582`), and the client names the division from it (`quote.py:442-443`). Test:
  `MarketRpcTests.A_corporation_order_row_carries_its_wallet_division_as_keyID`. Status: done (M1).
- **GetStationAsks and GetSystemAsks answer KeyVal rows (Q13, G10).** The client reads `.typeID` on each ask and passes the row to Buy
  (`quote.py:97-100`; reached through the evelink endpoint `endpoints.py:160`). A positional tuple has no `.typeID`. eve.rs answers the
  BestByOrder rows the regional best answers (price, volRemaining, typeID, stationID). The reference answers tuples
  (`marketProxyService.js:6241`): the same defect in the reference is not copied. Tests: `MarketRpcTests.Station_and_system_asks...`,
  `ProtocolTests.Ask_summary_rpcs...`. Status: done (M1).
- **Client labels for market refusals (G6).** A MarketError is sent as the client's UserError label with its arguments where the client
  source raises that label: `MktBrokersFeeUnexpected2` with `actualBrokerFeePerc` (100 x the server's rate) and
  `expectedBrokerFeePercentage` (the client's quote as sent: the client itself passes the raw fraction here, `sellMulti.py:456`), and
  `MktInvalidMinVolumeCannotExceedQuantity` with no arguments (`marketsvc.py:264-265`). Every other code is a CustomInfo sentence (ours),
  not the raw code. Texts: `MarketErrorText` in `MarketProxyService.cs`. Labels the client raises that need a value we do not hold are not
  used (`MktCantSellItem2` needs localized jump texts; `MARKET_REMOTE_SKILL_REQUIRED` stays a sentence). Tests: `MarketRpcTests` (fee,
  minimum volume), `MarketHostTests` (the fee label on the wire). Status: done (M1); the full label table is open where the client names
  no label.
- **No fabricated immediate-fill notification (G8).** A fill that completes an immediate order sends no `OnOwnOrdersChanged`: the previous
  row (order id 0, a fabricated order) is removed. The client's cache invalidation for the change is M4 (push invalidation). Reason: the
  client reads each row as a real order. Tests: `ProtocolTests` (counts of notifications), `MarketRpcTests.Buy_multi_fills...` (none
  sent). Status: done (M1).
- **Host registration.** The market service is registered through the real composition (`HostComposition.BuildAsync`, the path eve-host
  runs) in `MarketHostTests`, on scratch schemas, with `EVE_SIM=off` and `EVE_MARKET_SEED=skip`; the test project now sets
  `InvariantGlobalization` false, as the other projects that need ICU. The session's notifications are not asserted there (the
  composition attaches a session at character selection); the live walk covers them. Status: done (M1).

## Market slice M2: the rules as data and the decisions (2026-10-10)

Sources: the parent's binding decisions (`market-decisions.md`, Q1 to Q15); CCP-BR (EVE Development Team, "Broker Relations", 2020-02-24);
CCP-PN22.02 (patch notes 2025-03-12.1: "Sales Tax has been increased from 4% to 7.5%"); EVE University "Tax"; the client's constants under
`marketutil/`, `appConst.py:1485` and the feature-flag reader `featuremanagement/client/flag.py`; EVE.js `marketRules.js` for the formulas the
reference keeps. The rules are named constants in `MarketRules` (one source for the server, the fixture and the tests); a runtime rules table
was not built, because the client reads its flags elsewhere (Q1 below).

- **Q1 sales tax 7.5%.** `MarketRules.SalesTaxPercent = 7.5` (CCP-PN22.02; EVE University "Tax": 7.5%; 3.375% at Accounting V). EVE.js hard-codes
  8.0 (`marketRules.js:43`), as the client default does (`marketutil/const.py:30`); eve.rs does not copy it.
  **Decision beyond market-decisions.md (the flag cannot be served):** the client reads `market-transaction-tax` through a LaunchDarkly client
  that it starts with its own SDK key (`featuremanagement/client/flag.py:72-76`, `launchdarkly/loader.py:33-35`); the V24.01 client does not take
  a flag value from the eve.rs server, so the server cannot serve 7.5 "through the flag". The server charges 7.5% (the official value, the rule
  that binds the charge). The client previews with the value LaunchDarkly gives it, or its default 8.0 when LaunchDarkly gives none, so a
  preview can differ from the charge. Status: open (client preview); the charge is correct.
- **Q2 modification discount kept.** `MarketRules.ModificationDiscountPerLevel = 0.06` (the client's constant, `marketutil/skilllimits.py:35-36`;
  EVE.js and eve.rs use the same). CCP-BR says +5 percentage points per level. The client previews with 0.06, so the server keeps 0.06 to agree
  with it. Flip the one constant when the client changes. Status: decided (kept).
- **Q3 modification delay.** Five minutes (`MarketRules.ModificationDelayMs = 300000`; CCP-BR "the five minute delay"; the client's
  `mktModificationDelay = 300`, `appConst.py:1485`), counted from the later of the order's issue and its last modification. A modification that is
  too early is refused with the sentence "That order was placed or modified less than five minutes ago; it can be modified in N seconds." (N is
  the remaining seconds, carried in the refusal's argument). No client label exists for it. The time is stored in `orders.last_modified_ms`
  (migration `006_order_modification.sql`; existing rows are 0, so their delay runs from the issue). EVE.js has no delay (its `state.rs:2707-2713`
  updates the price only). Tests: `ModificationRulesTests.A_modification_inside_the_delay...`. Status: decided.
- **Q4 duration reset.** A modification sets the order's issue time to the modification time (`orders.issued_ms`, and `last_modified_ms`), so the
  expiry runs from it (CCP-BR: "Modifying an order will reset its duration back to the original."). The owner rows and the change notification
  carry the new `issueDate`, which the client uses for the expiry it shows (`quote.py:484-490`). Test: `ModificationRulesTests.A_modification_restarts...`.
  Status: decided.
- **Q5 matching priority.** Price, then issue time (the older first), then order id. The station id is no longer a tie-break: no source gives
  one, and the client sorts asks by price, issue time, jumps, then order id (`marketsvc.py:28-74`). Applied in the matching plan
  (`MarketMatching.Plan`), in the book order, in the ask summaries and in the locking scan (`CounterOrders`). Test:
  `ModificationRulesTests.Equal_prices_fill_the_order_issued_first...`. Status: decided. The priority is "confirm with a live match" (Q5) and is open for that.
- **Q6 buy escrow, unchanged:** 100% of the price times the quantity up front (CCP-BR). Status: kept.
- **Q9 minimum volume, kept.** The rule (a bid fills in full when its remainder is below its minimum) stays as it is. No CCP text was found for it:
  not confirmed by a CCP source. Status: kept (open for confirmation).
- **Q10 SCC surcharge, kept.** 0 at NPC stations; the structure surcharge is the structures track's. Status: kept.
- **Q11 highsec security tax, not charged.** The client previews 5% on non-immediate orders in highsec (`security/common/const.py:6`); no official source
  for a charge was found; neither reference server charges it; journal reference 194 is unused. The charge stays at zero. The client's preview can
  therefore show a 5% line the server does not take. Status: open (client preview; revisit if a source appears).
- **Q12 price ticks.** At most two decimals and at most four significant figures (`MarketRules.IsTick`; the client's `marketutil/ticks.py`
  `is_tick`). Enforced on place and on modify; a price off the ticks is refused with "Order prices have at most four significant figures."
  (`MARKET_PRICE_TICK`). Existing orders keep their price until modified (CCP-BR). Tests: `ModificationRulesTests.A_price_that_is_not_a_tick...`,
  `RulesTests.Prices_have_at_most_four_significant_figures...`. Status: decided.
- **Q14 duplicate placement.** An identical placement (the same request) from the same session within five seconds, after the first succeeded, is
  refused with "An identical order was placed a moment ago, so it was not placed again." (`MARKET_DUPLICATE_PLACEMENT`). The client sends no
  operation key, so this is a stop-gap: a deliberate repeat within five seconds is refused too. Test: `MarketRpcTests.An_identical_placement_within_five_seconds...`. Status: decided (stop-gap, documented).
- **Q15 stale view.** A modification carries the client's `oldPrice` and `issueDate`; if either differs from the stored order, the modification is
  refused with "That order has changed since it was shown. Reload the orders and try again." (`MARKET_ORDER_STALE`) and nothing moves. Source:
  the client sends both (`marketsvc.py:288`). Tests: `ModificationRulesTests.A_modification_on_a_stale_view...`,
  `MarketRpcTests.A_modification_on_a_stale_view_is_a_refusal...`. Status: decided.
- **Rules fixture.** `oracle/fixtures/market/rules.json` is regenerated by `tools/dev/gen-market-rules-fixture.js`. The generator replaces the one
  reference constant (`MARKET_TRANSACTION_TAX_DEFAULT_PERCENT = 8.0`) with 7.5 in memory before it compiles `marketRules.js`, and records every
  other value from the reference; the fixture's `source` field says so. Before the change `RulesTests` failed on the 7.5 fixture (the 8% values),
  and it passes after the change. Status: decided.

## Market slice M3: expiry as a durable job (2026-10-10)

Sources: the parent's decision Q7 (expire at issued + duration plus a small grace; refund at that moment; notify with reason 'Expiry');
EVE.js `marketProxyService.js:248-253` (a one-second grace, a one-second sweep throttle, the durable job kinds) and `2170-2350` (a job per
order at its expiry instant); CCP-BR (the order duration; "Modifying an order will reset its duration back to the original"); EVE University
"Trading" ("If the order is taken down or expires the fee is not paid back"); the client's `marketsvc.py:113-124` (the Expiry reason) and
`buyMultiFromBase.py:559`. The forum statement that expiry waits for daily downtime is not official and is not adopted (Q7).

- **One job per resting order.** Kind `market.order.expire`, key `order:{id}`, due at issued + duration + 1 s. It is scheduled in the transaction that
  places the order (immediate orders rest nowhere, so they get no job), replaced in the transaction that modifies it (the duration restarts, Q4)
  and cancelled in the transaction that closes it (a fill, a cancel or an expiry). The job's handler re-reads the order: a closed order is left
  alone; a duration that a modification has moved is waited for again. Code: `Eve.Market/MarketTrading.cs` (`ScheduleExpiryAsync`, `CancelExpiryAsync`, `ExpireOrderAsync`) keeps the job through the domain
  interface `IMarketExpiryJobs` (`Eve.Market/MarketExpiry.cs` holds the grace and the tries); `Eve.Services.Market/DurableExpiryJobs.cs` is the
  scheduler adapter and `Eve.Services.Market/MarketExpiryWorker.cs` runs the jobs. The domain module does not reference the scheduler (the
  architecture test `ArchitectureTests` keeps domain modules on domain modules only).
- **Safety sweep.** A recurring job `market.expiry.sweep` runs every 60 s and closes every due order. A read of the market (escrow, order history,
  placement, modification) runs the same sweep at most once a second (EVE.js 1 s throttle). The sweep applies the same grace as the job, so the two
  never disagree on when an order expires.
- **One notice per expiry.** The job and the sweep can reach the same order at once (the scheduler runs jobs in parallel). The close is keyed
  (`expiry:{id}:{issued}`), so the second call replays the stored receipt; an expiry replay returns no receipt, so only the call that performed the
  expiry announces it. Test: `ExpiryTests.A_restart_gives_the_open_order...` and the other expiry tests, which failed before this rule (two notices).
- **Restart.** `MarketExpiryWorker.StartAsync` registers the two kinds and gives every open resting order its job again (an existing job is kept). An
  overdue job runs at once. Test: `ExpiryTests.A_restart_gives_the_open_order_its_job_back...`, `An_overdue_order_expires_at_once_after_a_restart`.
- **Refund and items, once.** A buy returns its escrow; a sell returns its stack to its origin; the broker fee is not returned (EVE University
  "Trading"). A refund over the wallet cap stays pending in escrow (`MoneyLimitException`): the job fails and is tried again with the scheduler's
  back-off (up to 100 tries), and the sweep retries it too. Tests: `ExpiryTests` (buy, sell, lost job, cancel, modification, throttle).
- **Notifications.** `OnOwnOrdersChanged` with reason 'Expiry' goes to the owner. For a corporation order it goes to every member who has the
  corporation's market rights at the station: Accountant or Trader, the client's own test (`buyThisTypeWindow.py:324`,
  `marketOrdersWnd.py:40-41`; `CorporationStore.MarketRightsHoldersAsync`). The notice is sent after the close commits, from
  `MarketNotifier`, which is also the RPCs' notifier. Test: `ExpiryTests.A_corporate_order_that_expires...` (the founder and a second member
  with the Trader role are notified; a member without a market role and a character outside the corporation are not).
- **Host.** The host composition (`CorporationComposition.MarketSeed`) starts the expiry; the test `MarketHostTests.The_host_gives_each_resting...`
  checks that the job exists with its due time and survives a host restart.
- **Known gap (not closed in M3).** The notices are not durable: a process that stops between the committed close and the send loses that one
  notice. The balances, the items and the order state are correct after the restart, and the client refreshes its own lists on its next read.
  A durable notice outbox is not in M3.
- **Test changes for the grace.** `TradingTests` (the over-cap refund that stays pending) and `ModificationRulesTests` (the restarted
  duration) check the expiry one grace after the expiry instant; the expected values are unchanged.
- Status: done (M3), tested with a fake clock (TradeRig.Now); the live headless walk cannot wait a day, so the expiry is not walked live.

## Market slice M4: cache coherence and corporate notices (2026-10-11)

Sources: `market-design.md` section 4.3 M4 and decision Q8 (`market-decisions.md`); the V24.01 client's object cache
(`carbon/common/script/net/objectCaching.py`: `InvalidateCachedMethodCall` adds the client's own session attribute to the key, 222-238;
the cache details come from the server's wrapped result, 510-514); the client's own invalidation list (`marketsvc.py:113-124`); the
push mechanism of the fittings (`FittingMgrServices.cs:241-253`).

- **Book invalidation.** Every change to a region and type book sends `objectCaching.InvalidateCachedMethodCall` to the sessions of
  that region: `GetOrders(typeID)`, `GetStationAsks`, `GetSystemAsks`, and for PLEX also `GetPlexOrders` and `GetPlexBest`. A change is
  a placement that trades or rests, a fill, a modification, a cancel or an expiry; the receipt lists the books it touched
  (`MarketReceipt.TouchedBooks`). A session whose known `regionid` differs is not asked. The client keeps these results for the run
  (`versionCheck` "run"), so the push is what makes the next read fresh. Invalidations are sent before the notices.
  Deviation (scope): the station and system asks are invalidated for every change in the region, not only the station's or
  system's own; the client keys them by its own station or system, so a wider push is harmless and simpler.
- **Owner-list invalidation.** For each character a change reaches, the client's `GetCharOrders` (a personal order) or
  `GetCorporationOrders` (a corporate order) and `GetMarketOrderHistory` are dropped.
- **Corporate notices (G11).** A corporate order's change (placement, fill of a corporate resting order, modification, cancel,
  expiry) reaches every character with the corporation's market rights at the order's station (`CorporationStore.MarketRightsHoldersAsync`,
  the Accountant or Trader test the client uses, `buyThisTypeWindow.py:324`), together with the acting character. A personal order's
  notices are unchanged: its owner and the acting character. The recipients are computed inside the transaction (`NoticeRecipientsAsync`),
  so a rolled-back change sends nothing.
- **Not invalidated, with the reason.** `GetRegionBest` is not wrapped as a cached result by the server, and the client caches only
  wrapped results (`objectCaching.py:510-514`), so there is nothing to drop. Seed import is not a runtime change: the NPC seed is imported
  during host composition, before any session exists, so no cached result can hold the old book.
- **Client-side price history is not reached.** The client keeps price histories in its own `ExpiringDict` (`marketsvc.py:103`, three
  hours), not in objectCaching, so the server cannot drop them. A second trader's new fill can show in the history view up to three
  hours late. Open: no server-side remedy exists within the V24.01 client.
- **Outbox: not built (decision).** A notice goes to a live session only. After a stop no session survives, and the client's object cache
  is per run, so a reconnecting client reads fresh state. An outbox would therefore have no recipient to deliver to. The M3 gap ("a stop
  between the committed close and the send loses that notice") is recorded here as accepted: balances, items and order state are correct
  after a restart, and the client's next read shows them.
- **Open acceptance item (Q8).** The push is verified on the wire in-process (the test asserts the invalidation packet and the next read).
  Two native clients observing each other's changes are not checked: no second native client runs in this environment.
- **Tests.** `MarketCoherenceTests` (5): a placement, a cancel, a fill and an expiry, each seen by a second session in the region (the
  invalidation is asserted on the wire, and the next read shows the change), with a session in another region not asked; and a corporate
  placement, fill and cancel that reaches the trader with the market right and not the bystander. Status: done (M4) for the affected-project gate (Market, Host, Corporations and Scheduler green on the merge `2f80397`). The live run passed at `f046112` (70 passed, 8 skipped, 0 failed); it had waited for the Secure Depot start-up fix on origin/main (`SpaceComposition`), which is not an M4 change.

## Market slice M5: price history by day (2026-10-11)

Sources: the decision Q16 (`market-decisions.md`); the client's reading of the two rowsets (`marketsvc.py:344-366`,
`GetHistoryRowList`: the old rows, placeholder rows through yesterday, then the new rows) and of the average (`marketsvc.py:368-386`,
`GetAveragePrice`); the reference's split (`marketProxyService.js:469-487`).

- **Deviation (the split).** "Old" holds the days before today and "new" the day in progress (none when nothing traded today). The
  reference splits off the last row (`splitHistoryRows`, `rows.slice(-1)`). With traded-only days, that puts a past trading day in
  "new": the client then writes its placeholder rows for that day and yesterday, and appends the real row after them. The result is
  a duplicated, out-of-order day and a 7-day average that counts placeholders. The client reads "new" as today (`midnightToday`,
  `marketsvc.py:346-358`), so eve.rs splits by the day. Test: `MarketHistoryTests.Each_day_is_one_row_and_the_day_in_progress_is_the_new_row`.
- **Definitions (Q16), per UTC day.** Low is the lowest fill price, high the highest. The average is the sum of the gross amounts over the
  sum of the quantities (volume-weighted). The volume is the sum of the quantities and the orders count the fills. The client's own
  average (`avgPrice x volume` over the last seven days, divided by the volume) reproduces the day's gross exactly (test:
  `The_seven_day_average_the_client_takes_is_the_volume_weighted_mean_of_the_rows`).
- **Rows exist only for days with fills.** Days without trades are the client's placeholders (volume 2, `marketsvc.py:352`); the
  server sends no gap rows.
- **Same transaction as the fill.** The history is read from the `fills` table, which a fill writes in its own transaction, so a
  rolled-back fill leaves no history row and the earlier fills stay (test: `A_fill_that_rolls_back_leaves_no_history_row_and_the_earlier_fill_stays`).
- **Removed.** `MarketStore.HistoriesAsync` had no caller; it had no region filter, so a caller would have mixed the regions.
- **Cost.** Each read aggregates the region's fills for the type (`GROUP BY` over `fills`, the `fills_type_history` index). No server
  cache. Not measured beyond the tests.
- **Tests.** `MarketHistoryTests` (6): several days with one row per day and the day in progress as "new"; the client's seven-day
  average; a type without fills and a region without trades; the region scope (a trader in another region sees none); PLEX as its own
  type; and a rolled-back fill. Status: done (M5).

## Market slice M6: transactions by division (2026-10-11)

Sources: the reference's transaction rows and rights (`marketProxyService.js:6582-6642`, `CrpAccessDenied` at 6625-6630;
`corporationAuthority.js:134-147`); the client's reading of the rows (`marketsvc.py:41-64`: accountID and keyID are the viewer's
division) and of the corporation's division (`corpMarketTransactionsPanel.py:14-17`, `quote.py:442-443`).

- **Implemented in M1, completed here.** `CharGetTransactions` and `CorpGetTransactions` read the fills of the wallet that bought or
  sold, newest first, from `fromDate`; a corporation read takes an optional division. M6 adds the tests the design asks for.
- **Rights.** `CorporationStore.CanReadWalletDivisionAsync`: an Accountant or a Junior Accountant reads every division; any other member
  reads only the division whose Account Take role it holds; a read of every division (null) needs the accountant role. A refusal is
  `CrpAccessDenied` with the reason `(101, 'UI/Corporations/AccessRestrictions/NoAccessToWalletDivision')`, as the reference sends it.
  This matches the design (M6) and the reference; the Director role is not a read right, as in the reference
  (`corporationAuthority.js:138-143`).
- **Tests.** `MarketTransactionReadTests` (3): an accountant reads every division newest first, with keyID as the division, a division
  filter and a fromDate filter; a member with Account Take reads its division and is refused another division and every division with
  reason 101; a junior accountant reads every division and a member with no take role reads none (refused).
- **Not done (cheap check not run).** The EXPLAIN check of the buyer-or-seller query was not run, so no index was added. The query uses
  the existing `fills_buyer` and `fills_seller` indexes (migration 002). Open: the cost at production volumes is not measured.
- **Not tested directly.** A corporation as the seller of a fill (its office stock setup is heavy); the row is built by the same code
  path as the buyer side, so the buyer side is the tested proof.
- Status: done (M6).

## Market slice M7: bounded order-book match and locking (2026-10-11)

Source: the gap G17 (`work/market-design.md` 4.1, 4.3 M7). Decisions: none new; the design's advisory-lock rule (one lock per region and type) is kept.

- **Bounded candidate read.** `MarketTrading.MatchAsync` reads the book in book order (price, then issue time, then id) in batches of
  `MatchBatchSize` = 50 rows, each batch `FOR UPDATE`, and stops when the requested quantity is filled. Continuation is a keyset cursor
  on (price, issued_ms, id) with a range predicate on price, so the index starts at the cursor.
- **Same fills as before.** `MarketFillWalk` offers the candidates one at a time; each decision depends only on the candidates before it,
  so a batched read gives the fills of the whole book. `MarketMatching.Plan` is the same walk over a whole list.
- **Advisory lock kept; SKIP LOCKED not used** (the book lock already serialises one type in one region).
- **Indexes: none added.** `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` on a scratch schema with the migrations 001 to 006 and 10000 asks and 10000 bids
  of one type: the candidate read uses the partial index `orders_asks` (or `orders_bids`) with the region, type and price condition, and
  the LIMIT feeds an incremental sort on price (65 rows read for a 50-row batch). Before the range predicate the keyset form read 4000
  rows and removed them by filter; with the range predicate it reads none. The expiry sweep reads only the open non-NPC orders through
  the partial index `orders_expiry`; no index on the expression `issued_ms + duration_days * 86400000` was added (the sweep is one scan per
  minute over open orders).
- **Measured** (`OrderBookBenchmarkTests`, opt-in with `EVE_MARKET_BENCH=1`; 10000 NPC asks of one type at one station, 20 placements
  each buying one unit at the cheapest ask with a limit of 200, so every call sees the full candidate set; the machine was shared with
  other agents, load average 27 to 42 on 4 CPUs, so latencies vary between runs):

  | | rows locked per placement | placement p50 | placement p95 | book read (10000 orders) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | before (unbounded `FOR UPDATE`, one run) | 9999 to 10000 | 281 ms | 932 ms | 249 ms |
  | after (bounded, four runs) | 50 in every call | 22, 41, 89, 111, 236 ms (five runs) | 41 to 130 ms; 700 to 840 ms in the loaded runs | 111 to 1149 ms |

  The row count is deterministic and is the number the test asserts (at most 50 per call). The latency numbers are not a clean
  before/after pair: the before run was made once, at a lower load; the after runs were made under load and are listed as measured.

- **Not changed.** `GetOrders` still returns the whole book for a type and region (a book of 10000 orders is about 10000 rows; no cap,
  as Q17 requires a client check). A placement whose quantity is not filled, or whose candidates are all out of range, still reads the
  whole book: the bound is per fill, not per book.
- **Tests.** `OrderBookMatchTests` (1, always run): 130 asks in ten price groups of thirteen, with ties across the batch boundaries; a
  75-unit buy fills the first 75 in price, then issue order, locks 100 rows, and leaves exactly the orders from position 75 on.
  `OrderBookBenchmarkTests` (1, opt-in) carries the numbers above.

## Market slice M8: money and custody invariants (2026-10-11)

Source: `work/market-design.md` 4.3 M8. Q14 (the duplicate window) was decided and built in M2; M8 checks it and does not change it.

- **Reconciliation** (`MarketReconciliation`): four groups of queries over the market, wallet and item schemas, each returning the broken
  facts as text (at most 20 per check). Escrow per wallet; escrowed items per open sell order (and no escrowed items at a closed or bid
  order, or at no order); fill legs; order sanity (non-negative volumes, state against remaining volume, no escrow on a closed order,
  the bid escrow equal to price times remaining).
- **Escrow identity, as the ledger is written.** A buy order's ISK leaves the wallet at placement (journal reference 42 `Market buy escrow`)
  and is held in `orders.escrow_isk`. A fill consumes that escrow when the buyer side has no transfer or purchase leg (a seller hitting a
  bid, or a buyer modifying a bid across asks). For every wallet: minus the sum of the market's reference-42 journal rows = the open buy
  escrow + the consumed escrow. A player-to-player fill (a transfer) and an NPC purchase are settled from the wallet and are not escrow.
- **The fill-journal shape differs from the design text.** The design asks for one sale and one purchase pair per fill. The ledger has
  three shapes: a transfer (two legs, one debit and one credit, under the fill's key, net zero); a seller hitting a bid (one credit leg,
  the buyer's escrow consumed); an NPC supply (one purchase leg, or none when a buyer modifies a bid across NPC asks). The check encodes these
  three shapes and rejects anything else. The wallet writes one journal row per settlement leg, keyed `{operation key}:{leg index}`, so the
  legs of a fill are matched by prefix.
- **Expiry job runs it.** `MarketExpiryWorker` runs the reconciliation from the minute sweep, at most every 10 minutes (`ReconcileEveryMs`),
  and never from a read-triggered sweep. Each broken fact is logged as `market reconciliation: ...` (the host writes it to its error log).
  The queries scan the wallet journal for the market's reference-42 rows; that is a full scan of those rows per run, and the 10-minute gate
  is what keeps it cheap. Not measured at production volume.
- **Lock order, documented and checked.** `MarketTrading` documents the order: the command guard, the character row and the corporation
  row, the book's advisory lock, the order rows in book order, the wallet rows sorted by owner and account key, the item rows. Two entry
  points broke it and were moved (no change to the checks or the results: each move is before any write): `PlaceAsync` took a sell's stock
  item before the book lock (M7 code), and `ModifyAsync` took the modified sell's escrow item before the wallet rows. `MarketLockOrderTests`
  reads `MarketTrading.cs` and checks the first acquisition of each rank in `PlaceAsync`, `ModifyAsync` and `CloseAsync`. It is a
  source-order guard, not a proof of deadlock freedom. The same rule, applied by a script to a reconstructed pre-fix text of
  `ModifyAsync`, reports "items before wallets"; the C# test was run on the fixed source only.
- **Concurrency.** `MarketConcurrencyTests`: a modification racing a fill of one bid, and a cancellation racing a fill of one bid. Each
  ends in one of the two consistent outcomes (the fill first, or the change first) and the reconciliation holds afterwards.
- **Invariant tests.** `MarketInvariantTests`: a mixed history (placements, a fill of a bid by a seller, a partial fill of an ask, a
  modification after the delay, a cancellation, expiry of every order) keeps every invariant at each step; a planted escrow drift and a
  planted item shortfall are each reported, and the expiry sweep logs the drift.
- **Q14, checked.** The five-second duplicate refusal (`MarketProxyService.RefuseRepeatedPlacement`) is tested on the wire by
  `MarketHostTests` (M2). No change.
- Status: done (M8). The tests run on PostgreSQL (`EVE_PG_URL`); a skipped test is not a pass.

## Market slice M9: live acceptance and the NPC seed import (2026-10-11)

- **NPC seed (row 111 above).** `MarketSeedV4StoreTests` is in the tree (`CustodyTests.cs`) and runs on PostgreSQL: a bulk seed keeps
  the TQ volume baseline and retires old rows atomically, and an invalid row leaves the previous seed in place. The import path that the
  host runs at its first start (`CorporationComposition.MarketSeed`: capture to an artifact, read it back, `ToSeedsAsync`, `SeedNpcV4Async`)
  had no test; `MarketSeedImportTests` runs it on a captured artifact: the SDE station orders are imported, an unknown type and a station
  the SDE does not list are not, the book serves the imported prices, and `HasSeedV4Async` gates a second start.
- **Live walk** (`tools/headless-client/tests/test_market.py`, `test_a_seller_and_a_buyer_trade_through_the_market`): a seller takes stock
  from the hangar with `/giveitem` (slash service), offers 40 at 50 (`PlaceMultiSellOrder`), a buyer reads the station asks, buys 15 at
  once (`PlaceBuyOrder` with no duration), both read their transactions (`CharGetTransactions`: the buyer and the seller each show a fill of
  15), the seller's modification inside the five-minute delay is refused with the sentence that carries the seconds left, the same
  modification after the delay succeeds (run with `EVE_LIVE_SLOW=1`, which waits 300 s), the seller cancels the rest (`CancelCharOrder`:
  the escrow reads zero and the items return to the hangar), `GetCorporationOrders` of a character in an NPC corporation is empty, and a
  corporation order is refused.
- **Refusal wording.** A corporation-rights refusal reached the client as the raw code `CORPORATION_ACCESS_DENIED` (a `CustomInfo` with the
  code as its text). `MarketProxyService.Error` now sends the sentence "Your character does not hold the market rights of that corporation."
  for that code; the live walk asserts it.
- **Not covered live** (the headless client cannot issue them, or the step is slow): expiry (the fake-clock tests cover it: ExpiryTests,
  MarketCoherenceTests); a second character's view of a price change (the client cache is per run; M4 covers the push); a corporation
  trade (no player corporation is created in the walk); price priority over several asks (one ask in the walk); the five-second duplicate
  refusal over the live socket (covered by `MarketHostTests`).
- Status: done (M9). The live walk ran green with `EVE_LIVE_SLOW=1` (the change after the delay, 5 minutes) before the refusal
  sentence was asserted, and green without it after (the final file). Evidence: the run logs in the agent's scratchpad.

## Agents slice AG-1: LP wallet (LPSvc), the access rule and the bound agentMgr (2026-10-10)

Sources. eve-logic (V24.01): `eve/client/script/ui/shared/neocom/wallet/loyaltyPointsWalletSvc.py:27-67` (the balances,
`OnLPChange`, `OnCorpLPChange`); `eve/client/script/ui/station/loyaltyPointStore/lpStoreSvc.py:102-103` (`ExchangeConcordLP`);
`eve/client/script/ui/shared/neocom/wallet/panels/corp/corpDonateLPPanel.py:47` and `.../personal/personalDonateLPPanel.py:46`
(the two transfers). EveJS v0.12.9: `server/src/services/corporation/lpService.js:70-108` (service, rowset, corporation
holder), `lpWalletState.js:348-390` (notifications), `lpWalletState.js:392-510` (the idempotency key), `lpWalletState.js:610-740`
(the transfers). The client's agent rule: `eve/common/script/sys/eveCfg.py:112-143`, `standingUtil.py:15-43`,
`eve/client/script/ui/services/standingsvc.py:100-104`. Skill type IDs: `inventorycommon/const.py:1441, 1456, 1474`.
Faction lists: `eve/common/lib/appConst.py:618-632`.

- **LPSvc is registered by the Host.** It was missing (eve.rs routed the name to the station node and had no class). It is
  built on the wallet store and answers `GetAllMyCharacterWalletLPBalances` and `GetAllMyCorporationWalletLPBalances` with a
  `Rowset` of `(issuerCorpID, loyaltyPoints)`, the shape EveJS answers (`lpService.js:32-44`). The character is the session's
  `charid`; the corporation is `corporationID`, then `corpid` (EveJS `lpService.js:105-107`). A session with no character or
  corporation gets an empty rowset. Status: decided (AG-1); tests `LoyaltyPointsServiceTests`.
- **Balances persist in PostgreSQL, in the wallet schema.** Tables `lp_balances` (one row per holder and issuing corporation)
  and `lp_journal` (one row per applied change), migration `006_loyalty_points.sql`. The reference keeps them in its JSON
  `lpWallets` table; eve.rs does not use the JSON game store for new state, so the reference's table name is not reused. A
  change is one balance update and one journal row in the caller's transaction, so a coupled change (a mission reward with its
  ISK) commits as one. Status: decided (AG-1); tests `LoyaltyPointStoreTests` (PostgreSQL).
- **A reference applies once.** The journal is unique per (reference, holder, issuer). A replay returns no change and
  announces nothing. The reference refuses a reused idempotency key with a different amount (`lpWalletState.js:411-420`,
  `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED`); eve.rs answers the replay as already applied, because the caller's reference names the award
  and a different amount is a different reference. Status: decided (AG-1).
- **A debit below zero is refused** (`InsufficientLoyaltyPointsException`) and leaves no row changed. The reference refuses
  it with `INSUFFICIENT_FUNDS` only when the caller asks (`requireSufficientFunds`, `lpWalletState.js:531, 592, 631, 728`);
  eve.rs always requires the funds, because a balance cannot be negative. Status: tested.
- **Notifications follow committed changes only.** `OnLPChange("set", issuer, before, after)` goes to the character's
  session (`clientID`), `OnCorpLPChange(reason, issuer)` to every session of the corporation (`clientID`, each session).
  The change type is the reference's default `"set"` (`lpWalletState.js:348`); the client ignores it
  (`loyaltyPointsWalletSvc.py:59`, parameter `_changeType`). Status: tested (`LoyaltyPointsServiceTests`).
- **`ExchangeConcordLP` and the two transfers are refused, not answered.** The reference's `ExchangeConcordLP` is a stub that
  returns an empty dictionary (`lpService.js:166-168`), so an answer would be a fake success. The transfers (the donation
  panels) follow rules eve.rs does not have yet: the destination corporation check, the Evermark exception, the director role
  and the membership age (`lpWalletState.js:116-135`, `:136-236`, `:610-740`). Each is refused with
  `CustomInfo` "This loyalty point operation is not available on this server yet." Status: open (TODO section 43).
- **Not added: `GetLPsForCharacter`, `GetLPExchangeRates`, `GetLPForCharacterCorp`.** The reference answers them
  (`lpService.js:83-91`, `:111`), but no V24.01 caller exists in eve-logic (a search of the client tree finds none). A method
  without a caller cannot be checked against the client, so it is not added (the same rule as slice B3).
- **No source credits loyalty points yet.** The store's `ApplyAsync` is ready for the first credit (missions, factional
  warfare, insurgency). Until then every balance is empty; the tests credit through the store.
- **LPStoreMgr is unchanged** (still answers no offers and refuses every take); it is slice A9.

- **The access rule is a port of the client's rule** (`AgentAccessRule`): the level threshold `m = 2(level-1) - 1`, the
  floor (the lowest of the three standings must exceed -2), the research rule (the corporation standing must reach `m - 2`),
  the aura bypass, and the level-one rule (open to anyone except research agents). The three standings are the client's
  `npccharstandings` values for the agent's faction, corporation and agent (`standingsvc.py:100-104`); a missing one is 0.
  The skill bonus is 0.4 per trained level of Diplomacy (a negative standing), Criminal Connections (a positive standing toward
  a pirate faction) or Connections (any other positive standing), none for the four factions without a bonus
  (`standingUtil.py:26-37`). Decisions: (1) implants are not modelled, so the effective level is the trained level (the
  client's `effectiveSkillLevel` includes implants); (2) the rule has no quality term, because the V24.01 client has none
  (design document, section 4.1); (3) no server path calls the rule yet: the consumers are the agent dialogue (A4), offers
  (A5) and the accept path (Mi3), so the rule is tested as a function (`AgentAccessRuleTests`). Status: built, not yet used by
  a client-visible path.
- **`MachoBindObject` on agentMgr.** `Moniker('agentMgr', agentID)` binds the agent (`agents.py:319-321`,
  `eveMoniker.py:157`). The bound object is made for an agent the catalog holds; an unknown agent is refused with
  `CustomInfo` "That agent is not known." (ours; the reference binds any id, `agentMgrService.js:777-786`). A nested call on the
  bound object is refused as an `InvalidOperationException` (the host logs it and answers the wrapped unexpected error) while no
  bound method exists: `DoAction` and the mission methods are slices A4 and A5. A release forgets the object. Status: tested
  (`AgentMissionServiceTests`); the bound object answers no method yet.
## Agents slice AG-2: web research and the rule check (2026-10-11)

Sources read (2026-10-11). CCP's own pages were not reachable: `support.eveonline.com` answers 403 and
`developers.eveonline.com/docs/services/esi/` answers 404, so the CCP facts below come from the ESI OpenAPI document and
every other number comes from the EVE University wiki, a community source.
- ESI OpenAPI, `https://esi.evetech.net/meta/openapi.json` (CCP): `GetLoyaltyStoresCorporationIdOffers` response
  (`offer_id`, `type_id`, `quantity`, `lp_cost`, `isk_cost`, `ak_cost` optional, `required_items[type_id, quantity]`);
  `GetCharactersCharacterIdLoyaltyPoints` (`corporation_id`, `loyalty_points`); `GetCharactersCharacterIdAgentsResearch`
  (`agent_id`, `skill_type_id`, `started_at`, `points_per_day`, `remainder_points`).
- EVE University wiki, `https://wiki.eveuniversity.org/Agent` (levels, standing table, access, divisions, locator wording),
  `https://wiki.eveuniversity.org/Loyalty_Points` (LP sources, Connections bonus, LP store, CONCORD exchange),
  `https://wiki.eveuniversity.org/Locator_Agents` (fee table, cooldowns, restrictions), `https://wiki.eveuniversity.org/Missions`
  (read in the design phase). `https://wiki.eveuniversity.org/Research` is the blueprint research page, not agent research.

Agreement with the access rule (`AgentAccessRule`, eveCfg.py:112-143): the level thresholds 1, 3, 5, 7 for levels 2 to 5
(`m = 2(level-1) - 1`), the floor (all three standings above -2, "All three types of standing need to be above -2.00"), the
research rule (corporation standing of at least `m - 2`, "no more than 2 below the required level"), the -10 to +10 range and
the three standings (personal, corporation, faction). No code change.

Disagreement, the client wins:
- **Skill bonus targets.** The wiki says Connections, Diplomacy and Criminal Connections raise "effective corporation and
  faction standing". The V24.01 client applies the bonus to all three standings, the personal one included
  (`eveCfg.py:130-138`: `fac`, `coc` and `cac` each get `standingUtil.GetStandingBonus`). `AgentAccessRule` follows the
  client, and `AgentAccessRuleTests` covers a personal standing that passes only with the bonus. No code change.
- **Other-faction space.** The wiki says an agent in another faction's sovereign space is not unlocked by standings with
  its own faction. The client rule has no location term (`eveCfg.py:112-143`), so none is added.
- **COSMOS thresholds.** The wiki gives COSMOS -2, 2, 4 and 6 for levels 1 to 4. The client has no COSMOS rule (Q-AG-1); the
  COSMOS numbers stay a note.
- **R&D missions.** The wiki says R&D has "only two missions, a trade and a courier" and no LP; CCP's 2011 article says
  research missions (Q-AG-7). Still open.

Locator fees (A7). Source: the wiki's locator table (community, not CCP; the page says "for a fee" in the prose and gives
the amounts in the table). Columns: required faction standing, the cooldown, then the fee for the same solar system, the
same constellation, the same region and a different region.
- level 1: any, 5 min, 1,000 / 5,000 / cannot / cannot
- level 2: 1.0, 5 min, 5,000 / 10,000 / 25,000 / cannot
- level 3: 3.0, 15 min, 10,000 / 25,000 / 50,000 / 100,000
- level 4: 5.0, 30 min, 25,000 / 50,000 / 100,000 / 250,000
- level 5: 7.0, 60 min, 50,000 / 100,000 / 250,000 / 500,000
The wiki also says: wormholes are excluded; levels 4 and 5 need an Omega account (not modelled); the target is never notified;
corporation standing qualifies as well as personal. The wiki does not say when the fee is charged.

CONCORD LP exchange (`ExchangeConcordLP`, still refused). The wiki gives the rate for an empire faction corporation as 1:0.8
(a 20% loss), for other non-pirate, non-empire corporations as 1:0.4, and none for Factional Warfare, pirate, Triglavian or
some SoE corporations. It has no CCP source. It stays refused: the rate table, the corporation classification and the result
shape of the client call are not in eve-logic, so no rule is implemented from the wiki alone.

LP store (A9). The wiki gives no per-item LP cost and no standing requirement for a store. The ESI offer schema above is the
shape to serve. The pinned SDE (build 3396210, `.cache/sde`) has no LP offer file (`npcCorporations.lpOfferTables` names the
tables, but no table rows are in the export), so offer data is a source gap for A9.

Research (A6). ESI gives the shape (`points_per_day`, `remainder_points`, `started_at`); no CCP or wiki text gives the points
rate, the datacore price or the remainder rule that was read. A6 implements the stored state and the ESI arithmetic
(`remainder_points + points_per_day * days`); the rates come from the data the server holds, and the datacore price is a gap. Later note (2026-10-17, industry audit): the rate, the datacore price (100 RP and 10,000 ISK), the concurrency rule and the qualification are now the values EveJS takes from EVE University's "Research missions" page (`ResearchRules`); the dialogue round trips that start, cancel and buy research are still open (INDUSTRY_STATUS slice 7).

Status: the sources are recorded; the rule matches them; the open items are Q-AG-7 (R&D missions), the LP offer data (A9),
the research rates (A6) and the locator fee timing (A7).

## Agents slice AG-2, A5: offers and mission state (2026-10-11)

Sources. EveJS `services/agent/missionRuntimeState.js:26-28` (decline replay 4 h, offer lifetime 7 d, epic arc replay
90 d); the client's action strings and states (`eve-logic eve/common/lib/appConst.py:1601-1624`); the client's
`OnAgentMissionChange(actionID, agentID)` (`eve/client/script/ui/station/agents/agents.py:688`). Wiki (Missions, read in
the design phase): the offer lasts 6 to 7 days; design Q-AG-10 keeps 7 days.

- **The store is PostgreSQL, in the `agents` schema (`EVE_AGENTS_SCHEMA`).** Tables `missions` (one row per character and
  agent: state, the mission's content fields and the times of the state) and `journal` (one row per applied transition).
  EveJS keeps this in its JSON game store (`missionRuntimeState`); eve.rs has no writer for that table, so the new state
  is written to PostgreSQL. The game store's record is still read and the store's offers and accepted missions are laid
  over it (`AgentMissionStoreRecords.Overlay`). Status: decided (AG-2); tests `AgentMissionStoreTests` (Eve.Agents.Tests,
  PostgreSQL) and `AgentMissionStoreTests` (Eve.Services.Core.Tests).
- **One transition path.** Offer, accept, decline, quit, complete, fail and the expiry sweep go through one function: the
  row is locked, the state the transition leaves is checked, the row and a journal row are written in one transaction.
  A transition is named by its reference: a reference already in the journal applies nothing and returns null, so a
  replayed request changes nothing and announces nothing. A refused transition names its reason (`not_offered`,
  `offer_expired`, `already_offered`, `already_accepted`, `replay_wait`, `not_accepted`) and writes nothing.
- **Declined offers.** The agent does not offer again for four hours (EveJS `REPLAY_DELAY_MS`); the decline replay is refused
  as `replay_wait` until then.
- **Expiry is an explicit sweep (`ExpireOffersAsync`).** Each due offer returns to allocated once, with one journal row per
  expiry instant. Gap: the durable scheduler job that calls it is not wired yet (the offers are not created by any live
  path, see below), so no expiry runs on a live server yet.
- **Notifications follow committed transitions only.** `OnAgentMissionChange(action, agentID)` goes to the character's session
  with the client's action string (`offered`, `offer_declined`, `offer_expired`, `accepted`, `quit`, `completed`).
  `OnMissionsUpdated` is not sent (gap: the mission tracker's list is not refreshed on a change yet).
- **The journal shows offered, accepted and completed missions.** Declined, quit, failed and allocated records are left out
  of the view, because the client's journal row treats any other status as an offer.
- **No live path creates an offer yet (gap, not a fake).** An offer needs the mission's content (name id, title, type label),
  which comes from the mission pool. The pool is not in the SDE build (`agentAuthority` says so) and the mission data
  comes from the mission-data track (a second SDE pin, not this slice). `OfferAsync` is the only creator, and the tests
  call it directly. So on a live server the journal stays empty and "request mission" has nothing to offer (A4).
- **The title is the text when the name id is missing.** The client's journal row needs a name id or a title; a mission
  with neither is left out of the view (the reference behaves the same, `missionNameID` or `missionTitle`).

## Agents slice AG-2, A4: the dialogue behind DoAction (2026-10-11)

Sources. eve-logic `eve/client/script/ui/station/agents/agentDialogueWindow.py:324-428` (the button list, the
`DoAction` call, the message resolution through `agents.py:640-660`); `eve/common/lib/appConst.py:146-164` (buttons). EveJS
v0.12.9 `services/agent/agentMgrService.js:906-1120` (Handle_DoAction; the decline and quit confirmations at 366-449 are not
ported, see below), `services/agent/agentMissionRuntime.js:118-142` (the action tokens and their buttons),
`agentMissionRuntime.js:8038-8102` (the idle greeting and the idle conversation), `agentMgrService.js:97-115` (the reply
shape).

- **Reply shape.** `DoAction(actionID)` answers `((agentSays, actions), lastActionInfo)`: `agentSays` is `(message, contentID)`,
  a message is a plain text or `(localization label, keyword dictionary)` (the client resolves labels, `agents.py:640`);
  `actions` is a list of `(actionID, buttonType)`; `lastActionInfo` is the dictionary the window reads (`missionCompleted`,
  `missionQuit`, `missionCantReplay`, `loyaltyPoints`, `missionDeclined`). Status: tested (`AgentDialogueTests`).
- **The action tokens** (376 request, 377 accept, 378 decline, 381 quit, and the rest) are the reference's server-generated
  slots; the window echoes them back, and each maps to a stable button. A button id is accepted as itself. Status: decided.
- **Implemented:** the first contact (the greeting label `UI/Agents/DefaultMessages/RootAgentSays/GenericGreetings`, the
  request button only when a mission can be offered); the standing refusal (the reference's text for a basic agent,
  `UI/Agents/Research/TooLowStandings` for a research agent); request (the offer is written to the agents store, then
  `OnAgentMissionChange("offered")`); view; accept (`accepted`); decline (`offer_declined`, four hours of replay); quit
  (`quit`) at the agent's station only.
- **Standings and skills are read, not invented.** The three standings are the `standing` relation rows of the character
  toward the agent, its corporation and its faction (a missing row is 0, the reference's default). The skill levels are the
  character's trained levels of Connections, Diplomacy and Criminal Connections (`PgSkillStore.LevelAsync`). The rule is
  `AgentAccessRule.CanUse`, now called from the real path. The faction of an NPC corporation is the SDE `npcCorporations.factionID`.
- **No mission is offered on this server (gap).** The offer needs the mission's content from the mission pool, which is not loaded
  (see DEVIATIONS "Agents slice AG-2, A5"). The host answers `PickOffer` with null for every agent, so the greeting shows no
  request button and no offer is made. The request path is tested with a picked mission (`AgentDialogueTests`).
- **The confirmation windows are not ported (deviation).** The reference asks the player to confirm a decline and a quit
  (`sendAgentDeclineConfirmation`, `sendAgentQuitConfirmation`) through a deferred `OnAgentProvisionalResponse` and a YesNo
  call. This slice makes the transition when the button is pressed. Status: open; the confirmation needs the deferred response
  path of the bound call.
- **Not implemented, refused by name (CustomInfo "This agent service is not available on this server yet.")**: research
  (buttons 12-14, A6) and the locator (15-17, A7). Complete (6), defer (10) and continue (8) answer the current conversation
  and change nothing: the mission runtime (complete and the objectives) is outside this slice.
- **The mission briefing and the offer text are not sent.** The reference builds them from mission content (`GetMissionBriefingInfo`,
  `missionOfferText`). An offer shows the greeting with the accept and decline buttons; the briefing methods are not bound.
- **Refusals are named.** An unknown agent is refused with CustomInfo "That agent is not known." (A3). No refusal changes state.

## Agents slice AG-2, A9 (part 1): the loyalty store's offers (2026-10-11)

Sources. ESI (CCP, official): `GET /loyalty/stores/{corporation_id}/offers` (the response fields are read from the ESI OpenAPI
document, `esi.evetech.net/meta/openapi.json`). Client: eve-logic `eve/client/script/ui/station/loyaltyPointStore/lpStoreSvc.py:240-300,464-530`
(the offer fields the client reads, the take flow). Reference: EveJS `services/loyalty/lpStoreOfferCatalog.js:133-147` (the offer row) and
`services/evermarks/lpStoreMgrService.js:345-423` (the store service). The pinned SDE (build 3396210) and eve-logic carry no LP offer rows.

- **The offers come from ESI.** The official route is the only offer source (owner decision): the SDE holds no offer
  table in this build, and the client does not hold offer data. EveJS reads a 9 MB static JSON (`lpStoreStaticOffers.json`,
  provenance unknown); it is not used. Status: decided.
- **Daily cache with an offline switch.** An offer set is refreshed from ESI at most once a day (`LpStoreOfferCache`, one day).
  `EVE_LP_STORE_SEED=skip` answers what is stored and makes no call; the live run sets it (the same role as `EVE_MARKET_SEED=skip`);
  the host default is `auto`. A failed refresh keeps the stored offers and records a log line; the next request tries again.
  Tests: `LpStoreOffersTests` (fake HTTP handler; a fresh set makes no call, a stale set refreshes, a 500 keeps the stored set,
  `skip` calls nothing).
- **No standing condition is shown (gap).** ESI does not return a standing requirement for an offer, so the offer row has no
  `requiredStandings` (the client then treats the standing as met: `lpStoreSvc.py` `IsStandingsFulfilled`). The reference's data
  has standing requirements for some offers; they cannot be read from ESI, so the server does not invent them.
- **The offer row** is the reference's KeyVal (`typeID`, `iskCost`, `akCost`, `reqItems`, `offerID`, `qty`, `requiredStandings`,
  `corpID`, `lootItems`, `lpCost`). ESI has no loot items, so `lootItems` is empty. `akCost` is ESI's `ak_cost` (0 when absent).
  Test: `LpStoreMgrServiceTests`.
- **`LPStoreMgr` is a real service now** (`LpStoreMgrService`, Services.Wallet, registered with the agent services). It replaces the
  compatibility stub that answered an empty list and False (removed from Services.Core).
- **Taking an offer is refused by name (open, part 2).** `TakeOfferForCharacter` and `TakeOfferForCorporation` answer
  CustomInfo "This loyalty point store purchase is not available on this server yet." The purchase needs three things not
  yet in the code: (1) the ISK debit's wallet journal reference type for an LP store purchase, which has no source in the pinned
  SDE or in ESI's reference-type names (ESI names the types as strings, and the numeric ids were not readable); (2) the
  announcement of the granted item to the client (`OnItemChange` is sent by the inventory RPC layer after its own mutations, not
  by the stores); (3) the corporation wallet and director rules for the corporation take (EveJS `lpStoreMgrService.js:389-423`).
  The LP and item halves of the purchase are the same transaction as the offer's ISK, and are written when (1) is decided.

## Agents slice AG-3, A9 (part 2): taking an offer (2026-10-11)

Sources. eve-logic `eve/common/lib/appConst.py:667` (`refPaymentToLPStore = 26`, the ISK journal reference type of a store
payment), `eve/client/script/ui/station/loyaltyPointStore/lpStoreSvc.py:240-266` (the take calls and their reply),
`:457-462` (the Director test), `:499-530` (the requirements). EveJS v0.12.9 `services/evermarks/lpStoreMgrService.js:223-300,
345-423` and `services/evermarks/lpStorePurchaseState.js:242-362,546-640` (the purchase, the item legs, the Director rule).

- **ISK reference type: 26.** The client constant `refPaymentToLPStore` is the store payment. The pinned SDE (build 3396210)
  has no reference-type table (the native localization holds only the "undefined reftype" strings), ESI's reference types are
  names, and EveJS's store debit passes no numeric type. The client constant is the only numeric source, so it is used.
  Status: decided.
- **One transaction** (`LpStorePurchases`, Eve.Services.Wallet). The loyalty points (`LoyaltyPointStore.ApplyAsync`; the
  holder is the character, or the corporation when a Director pays), the ISK (`WalletTransactions.AdjustAsync` on the
  character's account or the corporation division), the required items (the character's hangar at the station, oldest stack
  first, as EveJS `selectRequiredItemLegs`) and the granted item (the hangar; ship and blueprint categories 6 and 9 are one
  item per unit, as the New Eden Store grant does) are written with the `lp_store_purchases` row (wallet migration 008).
  The journal rows carry the purchase id: the ISK journal has reference type 26 and counterparty = the store corporation;
  the loyalty journal has reason "LP store purchase". A refusal rolls the transaction back. Status: decided.
- **Refusals are named.** The keys and texts are EveJS `ERROR_MESSAGES`, sent as `CustomNotify` (the reference throws the same
  way). Keys: INSUFFICIENT_LP, INSUFFICIENT_ISK, INSUFFICIENT_REQUIRED_ITEMS, OFFER_NOT_FOUND, AK_UNSUPPORTED, ACCESS_DENIED,
  CHARACTER_NOT_FOUND, CORPORATION_NOT_FOUND, ITEM_GRANT_FAILED, INVALID_STORE, INVALID_CHARACTER, INVALID_QUANTITY. The
  INVALID_QUANTITY text is this server's (the reference's text is for the heraldry store only). Status: decided.
- **Corporation take: a Director only.** The client shows the corporation choice only to a Director
  (`CanCharacterUseCorpWallets`: session corprole and Director); the reference checks the persisted role. Here the member's
  roles come from the corporation store (`CorporationRoles.Session`, AtAll and Director), and the character must be a member.
  The corporation's loyalty points and ISK pay (the division is the session's corpAccountKey, default 1000); the required and
  the granted items stay with the character, as in the reference. Status: decided.
- **No standing check.** ESI returns no standing requirement for an offer (A9 part 1), so none is checked, and the client's
  own check passes. The reference's standing requirements are not served (gap, as in part 1). Status: gap.
- **Location.** The take is served only from a station (session `stationid`). The reference also serves Upwell structures that
  have the loyalty store service, which is not modelled; the heraldry store path is not served. Status: gap.
- **Idempotency.** The wire call carries no client operation key (`lpStoreSvc.py:249-251`), so a repeated call is a new purchase,
  as the client asked. Each committed purchase has one server id, which is the reference of its journal rows, and a refused or
  failed call writes nothing. A retry-safe take needs a key from the client. Status: gap.
- **Notifications, after the commit only.** `OnLPChange` / `OnCorpLPChange` (LoyaltyPointAnnouncer), `OnAccountChange`
  (WalletAnnouncer), and `OnItemChange` to the buying session: a consumed stack shows its previous stack size (9) and, when
  it is gone, its previous location (3) with the location moved to junk (6), as the reference and `CrateService` send it; a
  created item has no differences. Other sessions of the same character are not told (gap). Status: decided.
- **Offer data cross-check against the calculator site (AG-3, 2026-10-11).** The owner named eve.starweb.cc/lpstore. Its page
  loads `https://eve.starweb.cc/api/lp/offer` (corporationId, scope = 0.05, days 7, tax, buy/sell prices; scope must be a number).
  Compared with ESI `/loyalty/stores/{corporation_id}/offers/` for the 179 corporations the site lists (both sources answered
  200 for every corporation): 31,755 offers in both; 31,745 agree on type, quantity, LP cost and ISK cost; all ESI required items
  appear in the site's `Materials` with the same quantity. The site's `Materials` also hold manufacturing inputs (for example a
  blueprint material), so they are not the store's requirement. The 10 differing offers (corporations 1000129 and 1000270,
  offers 16429 and 16430) are kept at ESI's values. The site has 1,104 offers (46 corporations, 24 distinct offer ids in
  3561 to 3609) that ESI does not return for any corporation; none is in the EveJS ESI snapshot either. Decision: no rows added
  or changed. ESI stays the only offer source; the official feed does not list those offers, and the site is an unofficial
  calculator. Status: decided; the 1,104 rows are a question for the owner, not a gap in ESI. Scratch: scratchpad `work/lpcmp`.
- **Tests.** `LpStoreMgrServiceTests` (4, PostgreSQL, real `LpStorePurchases` and wallet, loyalty and item stores): the
  character take (LP, ISK, journal ref 26, items, granted item, two `OnItemChange` packets), the refusal with nothing changed,
  and the corporation take with and without the Director role. The Host wiring compiles; the live run exercises it.

## Agents slice AG-3, A6 and A7: research and locator rules (2026-10-11)

Sources (community, not CCP; the CCP pages were not reachable). EVE University wiki "Research missions" and "Datacore farming":
RP/day = (1 + (20 + 5N + AS) / 100) * (SS + AL)^2 (N Negotiation, AS personal agent standing, SS specialty level, AL agent level);
the wiki's maximum for a level IV agent, 125.55, is reproduced exactly by N 5, AS 10, SS 5, AL 4 (the wiki's flattened fraction
is read without a division by two; EveJS `researchRuntime.js:196-206` has the same form). Science V and a specialty at the agent's
level qualify; one project slot plus one per Research Project Management level; a datacore costs 100 RP and 10,000 ISK. Locator
fees, cooldowns and standings: EVE University "Locator Agents" (the table AG-2 recorded). EveJS `researchRuntime.js` and `agentMissionRuntime.js` cross-check.

- **Research rules and the project store (built).** `ResearchRules` (pure formula, slots, qualification, datacore price),
  `AgentAccessRule.EffectiveStanding` (the Connections bonus on the personal standing), `ResearchProjectStore` (PostgreSQL
  table `research_projects`, migration 0003 in the agents schema): a start counts the character's projects under an advisory
  lock and refuses `too_many_projects` or `already_researching` with nothing written; a project's points are the banked remainder
  plus the rate times the days since the start; a cancel deletes the row and the points are lost (EveJS: "All points will be
  lost when the connection is broken"). Status: decided. Tests: `ResearchRulesTests`, `ResearchProjectStoreTests` (PostgreSQL).
- **Research is not wired to the agent's buttons (gap, the cut line).** Start (12), cancel (13) and buy datacores (14) still answer
  "This agent service is not available on this server yet." The reference runs them as client dialogs (SingleChoiceBox for the
  field, YesNo for the cancel, GetQuantity for the datacores; EveJS `researchDialogue.js:247-380`), and eve.rs has no deferred
  response path for a client dialog (the same gap as the decline and quit confirmations, AG-2 A4). Blocked on that path.
- **Qualifying fields (decided from the wiki, not from data).** The SDE agent rows carry no research-field list and the client has
  none; the wiki says "one or more of the science specialty skills trained to the level required". The reference's per-agent
  field list (`researchAuthority`) has no source in this repository, so it is not used.
- **Datacores (gap).** The price is the wiki's. The datacore item (type per science field, "Datacore - <field>" in the SDE) and its
  grant to the station hangar are not built. No datacore is created and no RP or ISK is debited.
- **Research missions (gap).** Not built. Wiki: one mission per day for a research agent, one day's RP as a reward; the mission
  pool is not in the SDE build.
- **Locator rules (built, not wired).** `LocatorRules`: fee by agent level and distance tier (same system, constellation,
  region, other region; level 1 cannot locate beyond its constellation, and level 2 cannot locate in another region, as the table's "cannot" entries say), cooldown and standing.
  The wiki gives the fee amounts and says "for a fee"; it does not say when the fee is charged, so the rule only prices a locate.
  Status: decided for the rule; the locate flow (buttons 15 to 17) stays refused by name. Blockers: the target character's
  location is not held in eve.rs (no character location store read by the agents track), and the locate dialog and result
  need the client dialog path. Tests: `LocatorRulesTests`.
- **ISK and LP for research and the locator are not moved.** No journal row is written by either rule.

## Agents slice AG-4, A4: the client dialog path and the decline and quit confirmations (2026-10-11)

Sources. eve-logic `eve/client/script/ui/station/agents/agents.py:404-478` (YesNo returns a bool; SingleChoiceBox returns
`(ok, "radioboxOption<n>Selected")`; GetQuantity returns the quantity or nothing); EveJS `services/network/clientSession.js:1012-1150`
(a CallReq to the client service; the client answers with a CallRsp carrying the same call id; the answer is unwrapped from the
one-item body tuple and the substream); EveJS `services/agent/agentMgrService.js:366-449, 926-1075` (the decline and quit
confirmations: the provisional answer, the YesNo, a yes applies the transition, a no shows the current conversation).

- **The path (built).** `ClientSession.SendClientCall` sends the CallReq with a server call id and keeps the answer callback;
  the dispatcher routes the client's CallRsp (`PacketDispatcher`, `ClientAnswer`) to it once. `DeferredContext.Provisional` sends
  the empty result with the `OnAgentProvisionalResponse` named payload, unwrapped. The agent's bound `DoAction` returns a
  `PyDeferred` when the dialogue needs an answer: the provisional reply, the client call, then the reply of the continuation.
  A continuation may raise a further prompt. A failed continuation answers with `AgentDialogueMachine.FailureReply` (the window
  never waits forever). Status: decided.
- **Decline and quit ask first.** Decline (button 9) and quit at the agent's station (button 11) raise `YesNo` with the
  reference's labels, content ids (decline 4802, quit the mission's content id) and suppression ids. A yes runs the transition;
  a no shows the conversation as it was. Without a sink, the no path runs (EveJS runs a decline at once there; the difference
  only matters with no client to ask). Status: decided. Tests: `AgentDialogueTests` (quit yes and no, decline yes),
  `ClientCallTests`. The deferred runner in `AgentMissionService` is covered by the live run only (not by a unit test).

## Agents slice AG-4, A6: research buttons 12 to 14 and datacores (2026-10-11)

Sources. eve-logic `appConst.py:146-164` (buttons 12 to 14), `appConst.py:741` (`refDatacoreFee` = 112); EveJS
`services/agent/researchDialogue.js:247-419` (the start, cancel and datacore plans, their dialogs and continuations),
`services/agent/researchRuntime.js:68-88, 196-206, 307-332, 505-539, 764-860` (labels, RP/day, refusals, the datacore
price and the purchase); the SDE types and `typeDogma` (dogma attribute 182 = requiredSkill1).

- **Fields from the SDE (decided).** A datacore's required skill (dogma attribute 182) is the research field: 24 fields
  (the SDE's `Datacore - <name>` types whose attribute 182 names a skill other than Science; the two civilian datacores name
  Science and are not fields). No name is matched; `AgentResearchFields.FromSde`, tested on the pinned SDE. EveJS's per-agent
  field list (`researchAuthority`) has no source here and is not used, so every R&amp;D agent offers all 24 fields to a
  character who qualifies: Science V and the specialty at the agent's level (wiki, "Research missions").
- **RP/day and projects (decided).** `ResearchRules`: the formula of A6; the effective standing is the personal standing with the
  Connections bonus (`AgentAccessRule.EffectiveStanding`); one project per agent and `1 + Research Project Management` projects.
  Start is refused by name (standing, Science, fields, slots); a project is stored once (`ResearchProjectStore`).
- **Dialogs (client contract).** Start: `SingleChoiceBox` (title and body labels, the choices as `(label, skillID, skillLevel)`,
  the agent id); its answer `(true, "radioboxOption<n>Selected")` picks the field (1 based). Cancel: `YesNo` (two labels, the
  agent id); a no answers the invalid-input line, as the reference does. Datacores: `GetQuantity` with the affordable maximum
  (the RP accrued now, divided by 100). Status: decided, tests `AgentResearchDialogueTests` (fake port over the PostgreSQL store).
- **Datacore purchase (decided).** One transaction under the research lock (`PersistenceAgentResearch.BuyDatacoresAsync`): the
  RP is debited by re-anchoring the project (its accrued points, less the cost, from now); the ISK is debited with journal
  reference type 112 (the client's `refDatacoreFee`; EveJS `JOURNAL_ENTRY_TYPE.DATACORE_FEE`, reason "Datacore purchase"); the
  datacores are one stack in the station hangar (the station or structure the client is docked in). A refusal (RP, ISK, no
  station) writes nothing. The buyer's session gets `OnItemChange` after the commit. Status: decided. Test: the SDE field
  test; the purchase itself is covered by the live run only (no unit test of the Host port).
- **Cancel (decided).** Deletes the project; the points are lost (EveJS: "All points will be lost when the connection is
  broken"). An accepted mission with this agent is quit through the agents store, as the reference quits it.
- **Gaps.** Research missions (the agent's daily mission, one day's RP as the reward) are not offered; the mission pool does not
  carry them. EveJS grants the datacores through `researchDatacoreItemCustody`, which was not read; one stack is the rule used here.

## Agents slice AG-4, A7: locator buttons 15 to 17 (2026-10-11)

Sources. eve-logic `appConst.py:146-164` (buttons 15 to 17), `eve/common/script/util/notificationconst.py:73, 423` (type 69, the
"PilotLocated" name), `notificationUtil.py:540-591` (`FormatLocateCharNotification`: the data keys and the group ids 3, 4, 5, 15).
EveJS v0.12.9 has no locate dialogue: `agentMissionRuntime.js:6258` only publishes the locator's service row, so the rule below is
the wiki's ("Locator Agents") and the client's, not EveJS's.

- **Fee timing (decided, the rule).** The name is asked for free and the offer states the fee. The fee is charged when the pilot
  accepts (16), in the same transaction that starts the cooldown. A pilot who cannot pay is refused by name: nothing is written
  and the request stays open. The wiki gives the fee in its table and says "for a fee" without saying when it is taken; charging
  on accept means a pilot pays only for a result it is offered. Status: decided by this rule; not checked against CCP.
- **Where the pilot is.** The system is the online session's (`solarsystemid2`, else `solarsystemid`); the station is the session's
  docked location. A pilot who is offline is refused by name. The constellation and region come from the SDE solar system.
- **Reach, fee and cooldown.** `LocatorRules`: the fee table, cooldowns of 5, 5, 15, 30 and 60 minutes by level, and the rule that
  a level-one locator cannot reach beyond its constellation and a level-two one cannot reach another region (the table's
  "cannot"). Wormhole systems (ids 31000000 to 31999999) are refused ("wormholes are excluded"). A refusal is by name and writes
  nothing. Status: decided.
- **Standing.** The required standing per level (`LocatorRules.RequiredStandingFor`) is compared with the pilot's effective
  standing toward the agent (the Connections bonus applied, as the research start does). The wiki names this as faction standing;
  the corporation and faction standings are not split out here. Status: a gap.
- **Result (outbox, AG-5).** The fee, the cooldown and the result are one PostgreSQL transaction: the result is an event
  (`agents.locate_delivered`, the repo's transactional outbox, `EventBus`). A subscriber writes the located notification (type 69,
  group agents, sent by the agent) with `characterID`, `messageIndex` (0), `agentLocation` and `targetLocation` keyed by group id
  (3 region, 4 constellation, 5 system, 15 station; the station only when docked), keyed by the event id so a retry writes it once.
  So a pilot who paid is always told: a crash between the commit and the write is replayed by the bus. No refund path.
  Status: decided; the payload is read from the client formatter. Covered by the live locator walk.
- **Gaps.** The prompt caption, its label and the lines the agent says are plain English: the client's `UI/Agents/Locator/*` labels
  are not mapped to the dialog yet. `messageIndex` is always 0 (the first intro line). A subscriber that keeps failing dead-letters
  the notification (the fee stays charged; visible in the bus status). The dialogue tests (`AgentLocatorDialogueTests`, PostgreSQL,
  fake port) cover the ask, offer, accept, reject, refusals, cooldown and the unpaid case; the live walk covers the delivery.
