## Zarzakh gate, station and scanning rules (slice 2, 2026-10-12)

Sources. The EVE University wiki "Zarzakh" (fetched 2026-10-12). The client export (`/home/user/eve-client-sde/native/`): `spacecomponentsbytype.jsonl.gz` (the `alignmentBasedToll` rows of stargate types 77918 and 78264 to 78266) and `solarsystemcontent.jsonl.gz` (each stargate's `allowedShipsTypeListID`; 8 gates name 579). The SDE: `typeLists` 579 "Zarzakh Allowed Ships" (`includedGroupIDs`, "All Subcapital Ships, up to and Including the Orca"); `mapStargates` (type and destination). The client code (eve-logic): `stargate/client/gateJumpSvc.py`, `spacecomponents/client/components/alignmentBasedToll.py`, `spacecomponents/common/components/alignmentBasedToll.py` (the toll formula), `stargate/client/gateLockController.py`, `stargate/common/lock_details.py`. EveJS v0.12.9 has no emanation lock, no capital-ship list and no gate toll (no code found), so it is not a behaviour reference for these rules.

Implemented.

- **Capital-ship gate lists.** `GateRules.ShipAllowed`: a gate with an `allowedShipsTypeListID` admits only the ship groups of that SDE type list. The eight gates that name list 579 are the four Zarzakh gates and four gates of the neighbouring systems. Group 513 (freighters) is not in the list, so a freighter is refused; the Orca (group 1022) is admitted, as the wiki says. Refusal text "Capital ships cannot use this stargate." is ours (the client has no text for it). Tests: `GateRulesTests`, `SpaceServiceTests.A_gate_whose_client_list_leaves_out_the_pilot_ship_group...` (PostgreSQL).
- **Emanation Lock.** The wiki: "players are locked to that specific gate for six hours"; entering through another gate resets the timer and locks to that gate; leaving through any other gate of Zarzakh is refused while the lock runs; the Deathless Shipcaster, clone jumping and pod kills are not refused; entry from outside is not affected. Implemented as `EmanationLocks` (in memory): the lock starts when a jump into Zarzakh is accepted by the simulation (the arrival gate is the SDE destination of the gate jumped through), lasts `EmanationLocks.DurationMs` (6 h) and is checked before the simulation hears a jump out of Zarzakh. Refusal text "An emanation lock holds you to another stargate of this system." is ours. Tests: `EmanationLocksTests`.
  - **Limitation, not built:** the client learns the lock only through the protobuf public gateway (`eve_public.stargate.lock.api`: `ActivatedNotice`, `DeactivatedNotice`, `RestrictedSystemsNotice`, `GetRestrictedSystemsRequest`). eve.rs has no public gateway (owner decision 4: not built), so the client does not show the lock timer and its own pre-jump check (`CheckForGateRestriction`) sees no lock. The server refusal is the only enforcement. Owner decision: build the gateway for this, or accept the server-only rule.
  - **Limitation:** the lock is held in memory; a host restart drops it.
- **Alignment toll: the formula, not the charge.** The client computes `max(minCost, (shipMass * pricePerKilo) ^ exponent + fixedAddition)`, times `alignedDiscountMultiplier` when the pilot's war faction is in `alignmentRestrictionList` (500010 and 500011). The client values for types 77918 and 78264 to 78266 are: pricePerKilo 0.015, exponent 1.1, fixedAddition -100000, minCost 10000, alignedDiscountMultiplier 0.0 (aligned pilots pay nothing). The wiki's "starts at 10k ISK for smaller ships" matches the minimum. `GateRules.TollAmount` implements the formula (test: capital at 1e9 kg about 77.8 million ISK, the wiki says "presumably ... 100m"). **Not charged:** no source names the payee, the wallet model needs two wallets, and the client only asks yes or no (`CheckWithPlayer`). Owner decision: the payee (an NPC wallet, a corporation, or none) before the charge is wired. The ship mass the client uses is the destiny ball's mass, which the server does not read yet, so no real ship's toll is computed.
- **D-Scan disabled.** The wiki: "D-Scan is disabled in system." The cone scan answers an empty list in a system whose client flag `disallowScanning` is set (the same gate as the probe launch, `SpaceSessions.ScanningAllowedFor`). The empty answer is ours. Test: `ScanProbeHostTests.A_system_whose_client_flags_forbid_scanning_shows_no_directional_scan_result`.
- **Bookmarks disabled.** The wiki: "Bookmarks are disabled in system." A static bookmark (a station or a system) whose system is 30100000 is refused with `BookmarkNotAvailable` and the notice "Bookmarks are disabled in this system." (the notice is ours; the client reaches it as `CustomNotify`). Test: `AccessGroupBookmarkMgrTests.A_place_in_a_system_with_bookmarks_disabled...`.

Not implemented, with the reason.

- **Gate guns (missile gate guns that attack a capsuleer with a retribution timer).** The wiki gives no retribution timer length, no damage and no spawn rule for the guns. No number is invented; skipped.
- **Gravity hazard (hull damage when exiting the safe zone).** The wiki gives no damage amount or rate. Skipped. The safe zone is generated by the Jovian structures at the gates, The Fulcrum and the Shipcaster (wiki).
- **Deathless Shipcaster.** The wiki: pirate-enlisted players jump from it to their faction FOB in the current Insurgency. The FOBs belong to the Insurgency and structures tracks (no FOB data here). Skipped.
- **Warp disruption (deploy blocker).** New finding, not implemented, owner decision needed. The client's `deployBlocker` on the gate types 77918 and 78264 to 78266 names `blockAnchorWarpBubbles` (range 100000000 m) and `blockStructures` (range 100000000 m), with blocked messages 669713 to 669720 (`native/spacecomponentsbytype.jsonl.gz`). The wiki does not state it. The earlier decision (no basis) was taken before this client data was read.

Client-side data read by the server: the disallow flags (slice 1), the stargate lists, the alignment tolls and the deploy blockers, the wormhole system classes, and the mission texts and templates. The server carries embedded copies of the rows and fields it uses (`dotnet/src/Eve.Universe/Data/client-*.jsonl.gz`, `dotnet/src/Eve.Services.Core/Data/client-missions.jsonl.gz`, build 3396210, generated by `tools/dev/extract-client-sde.py`; 94 KB in all, the localization IDs but no text). The host starts with no client directory and enforces these rules. `EVE_CLIENT_SDE_DIR` is an optional override: when it is set and holds a file, that file is read instead. Wormholes, which need the classes, are on by default now; `EVE_WORMHOLES=off` turns them off. Not copied: the client's other static tables (the epic arcs, agents in space, the mission dungeons and the warzone map are read from the pinned SDE in the database, or are already embedded (`Eve.Insurgency/Data/warzone-systems.json`)).

## Zarzakh toll, anchoring and lock text (slice 3, 2026-10-12)

Sources. The client's English texts (`/home/user/eve-client-sde/native/localization_fsd_en-us.jsonl.gz`, messages 669965 to 669967 and 725456, and 669713 to 669720); the client code (eve-logic: `spacecomponents/client/components/alignmentBasedToll.py`, `stargate/client/const.py` `GATE_LOCK_MAX_TIMER_HOURS = 6`, `appConst.py` `refAllignmentBasedGateToll = 168`). The EVE University wiki "Zarzakh" (fetched 2026-10-12). The CCP article "The Siege of Zarzakh has Ended" (https://www.eveonline.com/news/view/the-siege-of-zarzakh-has-ended, fetched 2026-10-12): no toll, lock or fee text. EveJS v0.12.9: no toll, lock or anchoring rule (no code found).

- **Toll payee: none documented, so a sink.** Message 669967 (en-us): "the incoming stargates ... charge a toll in ISK based on the mass of the incoming spaceship. Outgoing stargates do not charge this toll. Pirate-aligned pilots are exempt from paying this toll." It names no recipient, and the wiki and the CCP article name none either. Implemented as a sink: the toll is debited from the pilot's wallet with the client's journal ref type 168 (`refAllignmentBasedGateToll`) and counterparty 0, so no wallet is credited. The debit is `WalletTransactions.AdjustAsync` in the transaction that the jump needs: the pilot cannot pay, the jump is refused (`CustomNotify`, "You cannot pay the toll of this stargate.", our wording); the simulation refuses the jump, the debit is reversed with a refund key (`<key>:refund`, same ref type and reason "Gate toll refund"). The charge runs before the simulation hears the jump (`SpaceSessions.ChargeTollAsync`).
  - Amount: the client's formula (`GateRules.TollAmount`), mass from dogma attribute 4 with the fittings (`SpaceSessions.ShipMassAsync`). The client's ball mass is the destiny ball's mass; this is the closest source the server has, and it is not compared with a live ball. Rounded to whole cents, half away from zero.
  - Only incoming gates carry the toll (the four Zarzakh-adjacent gate types 77918 and 78264 to 78266 in the client export). Outgoing gates of Zarzakh carry none, as the text says.
  - Owner decision open: a payee, if one is ever published, gets a credit in place of the sink.
- **Emanation lock: the client's text fixes the end.** Message 725456: "When entering Zarzakh, The Fulcrum locks a ship to the gate being used, preventing exit through any other gate for {hours} hours. ... The lock is removed after exiting Zarzakh." The client's timer is `GATE_LOCK_MAX_TIMER_HOURS = 6`. Implemented: the lock also ends when the pilot leaves Zarzakh by an accepted jump (`EmanationLocks.Left`). The wiki's "Entering through another gate resets the timer" is kept.
  - Public gateway: the client learns the lock only through the protobuf public gateway (`eve_public.stargate.lock.api`). The client module is absent from the V24.01 export that eve-logic holds (owner decision 4): the gateway is not built. The server refusal is the only enforcement, and the client does not show the lock. Recorded here only, as the owner decided.
- **Warp bubbles refused near a Jovian stargate.** Message 669713 (en-us): "Cannot be anchored due to gravitational anomalies in the vicinity of an ancient Jovian stargate." The client's `deployBlocker` on the four Zarzakh-adjacent gate types (77918, 78264 to 78266) gives `blockAnchorWarpBubbles` and `blockStructures`, range 100000000 m (client spacecomponentsbytype). Implemented for the warp disruption field generator (group 899, effect `warpDisruptSphere`): its activation is refused within the range of a gate of those types in the same system (`SpaceSessions.Modules`, `GateRules.GateBlocking`, `AnchorKind.WarpBubble`). The refusal text is the client's own message; the distance is the straight line between the ship's position and the gate (inclusive). **Not checked:** the mobile warp disruptor (`mobile_warp_disruptor` in `SpaceContainers.Disruptors.cs`) and the interdiction sphere launcher are not refused by this rule (their anchoring path was not read for the position in this slice). **Structures:** `GateRules.GateBlocking` with `AnchorKind.Structure` is the refusal check; no caller exists, because the structure anchor route (`structureDeployment.Anchor`) is not built (structures track). Tests: `GateRulesTests` (range, kind, system). No host test: the field generator activation needs a fitted module.
- **Superseded entries.** The slice 2 entries that said the toll is not charged and that the deploy blocker needs an owner decision are superseded by this section.

### Live check: Turnur to Zarzakh (slice 3)

What ran. `tools/dev/run-eve-live.sh` on the eve_p2 lane (`EVE_PG_URL` eve_p2, `EVE_LIVE_PORT=26101`, `EVE_MARKET_SEED=skip`, `EVE_START_STATION=60004723` (a Turnur station), `EVE_CLIENT_SDE_DIR` set, `EVE_SDE_DIR` the pinned build). The scenario `tools/headless-client/tests/test_zarzakh_jump.py` (new, `EVE_LIVE_SLOW=1`) creates the character from the recorded jump walk, undocks in Turnur, warps to the Turnur gate 50016562 (type 77918, the incoming toll gate), jumps through it into Zarzakh gate 50016566, and checks the jump answer, a session change into Zarzakh (30002086 to 30100000) and, with `EVE_PG_URL` set, one new gate-toll journal row (`ref_type_id` 168) in the run's wallet schema. Result: 1 passed in 96.9 s, through `tools/mitm/mitm capture` on port 26111 (capture `captures/eve-rs-zarzakh.jsonl`, git-ignored). The capture shows `CmdStargateJump` answered with the bound object and a time, and the session change into 30100000.

Not run, and why.

- **EVE.js comparison (`mitm diff`).** The EVE.js checkout here has no `node_modules` and no generated data (the same gap as in slice 1), so `tools/dev/run-evejs-live.sh` cannot start the reference. The capture is eve.rs only. EVE.js has no toll, no emanation lock and no capital-ship list, so the expected difference is the toll journal row and the lock; the rest of the jump is what the comparison would test.
- **Cockroach seeded through the database.** The live character is the recorded jump character (a frigate), not a seeded Cockroach: no live seeding tool exists for the live schemas (the Cockroach is seeded only in the .NET rig, `Rig.cs`). The formula gives the 10000 ISK minimum up to about 2.6 million kg (the mass where (mass times 0.015) to the power 1.1 minus 100000 exceeds 10000), so a frigate and a Cockroach pay the same minimum.
- **The client's toll prompt and the lock notices.** The headless client sends `CmdStargateJump` directly; the client's own confirmation (`GateTollConfirm...`) and its lock display are client-side and are not driven.


## Pochven: research, the capital stargate rule and the faction rules (2026-10-12)

Sources. `docs/project/POCHVEN_DESIGN.md` section 1: the EVE University wiki "Pochven" (https://wiki.eveuniversity.org/Pochven) and "Triglavian Invasion" (https://wiki.eveuniversity.org/Triglavian_Invasion), the pinned SDE (build 3396210: `mapRegions` 10000070, `mapSolarSystems`, `mapConstellations`, `factions`, `groups`, `types`, `typeDogma`), and EveJS v0.12.9 `server/src/space/pochvenPolicy.js` (region 10000070 test only).

- Decision, capital refusal at stargates in Pochven. Source: the wiki Pochven page, "Capital ships can't use stargates or be manufactured in Pochven." Capital groups: Titan 30, Dreadnought 485, Carrier 547, Supercarrier 659, Force Auxiliary 1538, Capital Industrial Ship 883 (the set the wiki's "Capital ships" page names; Rorqual is in it, as in the bridge rules). Black Ops 898, Jump Freighter 902 and Freighter 513 are not capitals here. The SDE gives no ship list for Pochven stargates (none of the 27 systems carries one), so the rule is the only capital check in the region. The refusal text is the existing stargate text "Capital ships cannot use this stargate."; the wiki gives no text.
- Decision, the check runs before the client data's gate rules, and it runs even when the client data is not loaded (`GateRules` null): a region rule does not depend on the client export.
- Decision, faction pairs. The wiki says "the five main factions attack each other on sight" and does not name them. The four factions with SDE ids that live in Pochven are taken (Triglavian 500026, EDENCOM 500027, Rogue Drones 500025, Drifters 500024). The Sleepers have no id in the sources, so they are not modelled. CONCORD, pirates and empires are outside the rule.
- Decision, NPC standing rule. Wiki: Triglavian rats hostile at 0.00 and below; EDENCOM rats hostile below 0.00; Rogue Drones, Drifters and Sleepers hostile to all players (the wiki's "Pochven" page). Sleepers are not in the rule (see above).
- Not done: the hostility is not yet used by the simulation (no NPC-vs-NPC targeting exists there); gate sentries that shoot (no published firing rule: EveJS draws cosmetic sentries only, `server/src/space/empireGatePresence/empireSentryLayout.js`); skirmish spawns; invasion states; filaments (see TODO "Pochven").
- Tests: `PochvenRulesTests` (4, pure). The gate path in `CheckGateRulesAsync` is not exercised by a Pochven host test: the rig places the pilot in a high-security system (see the A8c note above), and no rig helper puts a pilot in Pochven. Status: NOT RUN for the host path; the existing `SpaceServiceTests` gate tests cover the unchanged path.


## Pochven population: sentries, camps, skirmishes and the NPC-vs-NPC fight (2026-10-12)

Sources: `docs/project/POCHVEN_DESIGN.md` (the wiki's Pochven and Invasion pages; SDE build 3396210 `typeDogma`, `types`, `groups`, `dungeons`); the hull and sentry numbers in `crates/eve-space/src/pochven_population.rs` (each `NpcType` names its SDE type id). The data file `crates/eve-space/data/pochven_states.json` holds which faction holds each of the 27 systems (source and date in its header).

Published, used as published: the sentries' numbers (Entropic Disintegrator Werpost 54577, EDENCOM GunStar 54579, Heavy GunStar 54580: range 250 km, falloff 50 km, shield, armour, structure, resonances, tracking; the GunStar damage is 25 each times the multiplier 10, the Heavy 25 times 20, the Werpost 250 times 0.8); the hulls' numbers (Liminal Damavik 52182, Imperial Navy Sentinel 52923, Interfering Phase-II Legionary 56493, Drifter Assault Cruiser 56221); the factions and their corporations; the standing rules (the wiki: Triglavian rats hostile at 0.00 and below, EDENCOM rats below 0.00, Rogue Drones and Drifters hostile to every pilot); the Final Liminality systems (27, held by the Triglavians since 2020-10-13).

Project rules (not published; stated in code):
- Sentries: one at every stargate of a Pochven system, 20 km from the gate towards the system's centre. A Triglavian system gets Entropic Disintegrator Werposts; a system the EDENCOM hold gets EDENCOM GunStars (none in the data file). A sentry does not move (orbit velocity 0).
- Camps: two Liminal Damavik at the first two gates of a Triglavian system, 5 km from the gate, attacking pilots and the other factions' NPCs (they stay near the gate: they orbit at 300 m/s, as the belt rat does).
- Who a sentry attacks: a Triglavian sentry attacks every ship that is not Triglavian, pilots included (a pilot starts at 0.00 standing, which the wiki makes hostile to the Triglavians). An EDENCOM sentry attacks the NPCs of the other three Pochven factions and no pilot (a pilot at 0.00 is not hostile to the EDENCOM; the simulation has no pilot standing to lower).
- Skirmishes: every 900 s per scene (the first 900 s after the system is first looked at); the pair of factions follows the skirmish's number (Triglavian against EDENCOM, Rogue Drones against Drifters, Triglavian against Rogue Drones, EDENCOM against Drifters, in turn); two groups of two hulls, 40 km apart, from the first gate of the system.
- Shots: once every 4 s per turret (the SDE gives no cycle for these types); the shot is the SDE's raw damage times the hull's damage multiplier; the hull's resonances are the SDE's; the Drifter cruiser's agility and structure resonance are not in the SDE (agility 1, resonance 1).
- Caps: at most 40 Pochven NPCs per scene; a skirmish waits until it fits (4 NPCs).
- The owners a Pochven NPC attacks (`pochven_population::owners_for`): a fighter attacks the NPCs of the other Pochven factions and nothing outside Pochven.

Implementation: `Scene::poll` looks at a Pochven scene once (`populate_pochven`), places the sentries and camps, and runs the skirmish schedule. The NPC-vs-NPC fight is the stock combat tree (`brains/rats.rs`, `create_pochven_behavior_tree`) with two additions: the proximity sensor reports entities too (`includedCategories`, a Combat parameter), and `invalidOwnerIds` (a Combat parameter) excludes the owners a tree does not attack. The sensor is tagged for the Pochven tree (it was always tagged for the belt rat, which is why a Pochven NPC never saw anything before).

Found while building it (not a rule): the visibility grid's box edge is the origin (`GRID_BOX` 7,864,320 m). A fight that straddles the origin changes bubble, and the bubble filter drops the target: the test fight is placed at the middle of box 0.

Not done (cut, with reasons): the bounty and loot of the Pochven NPCs (the bounty code is in the host, keyed on NPC profiles that do not include the Triglavian, EDENCOM or Drifter hulls: the sim cannot credit a kill yet); the EDENCOM fortress and Heavy GunStar placements (no Pochven system is an EDENCOM fortress in the wiki; the GunStar path is built and tested through the data file's `edencom` state only); the Triglavian Entropic sentries' effect on jamming and tracking beyond the stock turret; the filaments and the Home-system standing (step 5, not started); the live walk (the headless client cannot place a pilot in Pochven; see the A8c note).

Tests: `pochven_population` (16 in the Pochven filter, including the scene-level ones: the sentries and camps on first look, the 20 km placement and no movement, the skirmish schedule with the cap, determinism, a sentry locks a rogue drone and never a fellow triglavian); `brains::pochven_tests` (the NPC-vs-NPC attack, its absence within the faction and outside Pochven); `pochven` (faction and owner rules). The full `eve-space` (762) and `eve-behaviors` (548) suites pass; clippy `-D warnings` is clean for both crates.


## Wormhole space: placement, the jump and the ends (slice 7, 2026-10-12)

Sources: the owner's decisions of slice 7 (the host places both ends of a connection with the system's scene; the end position rule
below). The wiki "Wormhole" (https://wiki.eveuniversity.org/Wormhole: polarization five minutes per pilot, no stargates, collapse
rules), "Wormhole Types". The client's `wormholeMgr.WormholeJump(itemID)` (eve-logic `movementFunctions.py`).

- End position (not published): an end is 50 km from its system's centre on a bearing chosen from the connection's seed (the far end
  uses the same seed with another salt); radius 3,000 m. Shortcut: the reference places wormholes at positions the sources do not
  give. An end at 50 km from the star's centre may lie inside the star for some systems (the owner accepted the rule; not checked).
- Ends: body ID = connection ID times two (the near end) and times two plus one (the far end). The ends of every active connection
  of a system are placed when the system's scene starts (the undock) and before a jump into the system; the simulation makes the
  scene for a placed end when it has none (a scene with no ship stays until the next ship leaves it).
- Texts (ours, the sources give none): "Your ship is too large for this wormhole." (the individual limit), "You are polarized to
  this wormhole: you cannot go into it from this side yet." (the five-minute polarization, kept in memory per pilot and end).
- Jump order: the mass is checked before the simulation is asked; the simulation takes the jump (a stargate-like jump of the wormhole
  end, the ship leaves for the far end after the usual delay); then the mass is taken (`WormholeLifecycle.Transit`) and the
  change is published (a collapse removes both ends; an opening places both). Shortcut: another ship that transits between the
  check and the take can make the take refuse after the jump is taken; this is logged, not refunded.
- The collapse of an end by the scheduler (the lifetime) publishes its removal to the simulation at the time the job runs; the job's
  transaction may still fail after that, and the next scene start places the stored ends again.
- Tests: `WormholeWorldTests` (ends, positions, generation once, a captured collapse), `WormholeLifecycleTests` and
  `WormholePlannerTests` (eve.wormholes); the simulation's placement and jump (`eve-space` scene tests). The space service jump:
  `WormholeJumpServiceTests` (eve.services.space, PostgreSQL): the mass refusal with its text and no jump heard, the jump taken with the
  destination's end placed and the polarization with its text, and a collapse that removes both ends in the simulation. Not run: the client.
## Slice 3, step 1: zones outside Pochven (2026-10-12)

Sources. The EVE University wiki "Triglavian Invasion (Chapter 3)" (https://wiki.eveuniversity.org/Triglavian_Invasion_(Chapter_3), read 2026-10-12; the page was last edited 2023-08-28 and says it documents content no longer in the game): Arshat moved from Escalating Liminality to an EDENCOM Fortress at the end of Chapter 3 (2020-10-13). The wiki's "EDENCOM systems" page (last edited 2024-01-19) names no system and links an external "List of invasion systems" that returned 404 when fetched. The forum names of the 2020 renewed attacks (Abha, Inaya, Muttokon, Seiradih, Aldik, Iosantin) are not states and are not zones.

Decision. The zones are the 27 Pochven systems (Triglavian) and Arshat (30002253, SDE region 10000043, EDENCOM). Arshat is the one zone outside Pochven that a source names with a state; its state is the end-of-Chapter-3 state, and no source confirms it today. The EDENCOM zone's sentries are GunStars at each gate (the data file's state 'edencom'), its camps are two Imperial Navy Sentinels at the first two gates, and its camp is a mobile tree that attacks the other factions' NPCs and no pilot (EDENCOM rats are hostile below 0.00 standing). The Triglavian zones keep the rule of step 2-4. Skirmishes stay Pochven's: a zone outside Pochven gets sentries and camps only.

Not done: a dated list of EDENCOM Minor Victory and Stronghold systems, and more fortress systems: no source read names them.


## Slice 3, step 2: the protocol bump for a kill, and the NPC bounty (2026-10-12)

Protocol. The simulation's `Destroyed` message carries the destroyed ship's type id and corporation id (i32 each, after the wreck kind). The simulation's protocol version is 64 (the wormhole agent's 63 was pushed in 66b6b3c). The C# codec (`SimCodec.ProtocolVersion`, `SimDestroyed.ShipTypeId`, `CorporationId`) and the fixtures (`oracle/fixtures/simlink/messages.json`, from `tools/dev/gen-simlink-fixtures.py`) move with it. The host's own protocol (`HostCodec`, version 1) is unchanged.

Bounty. The host pays a kill of an NPC the simulation keeps (a session at or above 2^62 that the host has no profile for) the `entityKillBounty` of its type in the static data (SDE build 3396210, `typeDogma` attribute 481), paid to the pilot who dealt the last blow, through the same store and ESS split as a profiled NPC. Values from the SDE: Entropic Disintegrator Werpost 54577 pays 12,500,000 ISK; EDENCOM GunStar 54579 and Heavy GunStar 54580 pay 0; Liminal Damavik 52182, Interfering Phase-II Legionary 56493 pay 0; the Imperial Navy Sentinel 52923 and the Drifter hulls carry no bounty attribute and pay 0. Eleven Rogue Drone entity types carry non-zero SDE values and pay them.

Sourced table (checked 2026-10-12 against the EVE Ref type pages, https://everef.net/type/{id}, which carry the static data's Bounty attribute; the SDE build 3396210 agrees):

| Type id | Name | SDE entityKillBounty | EVE Ref Bounty |
|---|---|---|---|
| 54577 | Entropic Disintegrator Werpost | 12,500,000 | 12,500,000 |
| 52182 | Liminal Damavik | 0 | 0 |
| 56493 | Interfering Phase-II Legionary | 0 | 0 |
| 54579 | EDENCOM GunStar | 0 | 0 |
| 54580 | EDENCOM Heavy GunStar | 0 | 0 |
| 52923 | Imperial Navy Sentinel | no attribute | not listed |
| 56221 | Drifter Assault Cruiser | no attribute | not listed |

Other Damavik variants have a bounty on EVE Ref under other type ids (Stoic 77562: 56,250 ISK; Stalker 77575: 62,500 ISK; Umbral 77608: 100,000 ISK; Blinding 52209: 0). None is adopted for the sim's Liminal Damavik (52182), which EVE Ref gives 0; mapping a value across type ids would be an invention. The owner can decide whether those variants should pay once the sim spawns them.

Decision, no published bounty for the Triglavian and EDENCOM hulls. A search of the EVE University wiki (Triglavian Invasion, Triglavian Collective) and the wider web gave no per-ship ISK bounty for the Damavik, Vedmak, Leshak, Kikimora, Rodiva, Drekavac or the EDENCOM navy hulls. Those kills pay 0 until a source publishes a value; no number is invented.

Loot. Unchanged: the sim-resident NPCs have no host profile and so no loot table (the loot rule is keyed on the profile). No published drop table exists for these types in the SDE.

Tests: `NpcBountyHostTests` (a werpost kill pays 12,500,000 ISK to the killer with the type in the bucket; a Damavik kill pays nothing), `Eve.Space.Tests` (300, the codec and fixtures), `eve-space` (764), `eve-net` and `eve-sim` build clean under clippy -D warnings.

### System environment as dogma effects (slice 7c)

- A solar system's environment is its effect beacon (SDE group 920, `Class N <effect> Effects`, the attributes of the beacon type). Each
  attribute "<name>Multiplier" is a post-multiplication of the attribute "<name>", "<name>Bonus" a post-percentage of it; both reach the
  ship and the items located in it (its modules), as a buff of the same shape does. Source: the SDE values (`WormholeEffects`), the
  dogma modifier rules of `Eve.Dogma` (`SystemEnvironment`, tests `SystemEnvironmentTests`).
- Applied in the C# dogma world of the ship (`SyncBuffsAsync` with the ship's buffs). The Rust simulation does not receive these
  values: its combat and flight numbers are the ship spec's at the time the ship enters, so a wormhole's effect is seen in the
  attributes the client reads and not in the simulation (not built).
- Names with no base attribute (for example `shieldBonusMultiplier`, `capacitorCapacityMultiplierSystem`) are listed as not applied.
## Slice 3, step 3: filaments (rules and data; jumps not built)

Data. `crates/eve-space/src/filament.rs` holds the 17 Pochven filament types (56065 to 56081, group "Triglavian Space Filaments") with the SDE's ship cap (`mjdShipJumpCap`) and spool-up (`filamentSpoolupTimeSeconds`) per type, and the link radius (`mjfgRadius`, 6000 m). The Proximity-Extraction types carry the SDE's 2.5 ly limit (`lightYearDistanceMax`). Families: Devana and Extraction are outbound (Pochven to k-space); Cladistic, Border, Internal and Home are inbound.

Conflict. The wiki's Pochven page gives 60 s for the inbound filaments; the SDE gives 30 s (the 5-ship types) and 45 s (the 15-ship types). The SDE values are used, as SDE comes first. The Home-system standing (6.0) is skipped, per the owner.

Not built. The filament destinations are not in the client extract (the location list ids 4510 to 4569 are absent), so no filament can be jumped and no destination is invented. The trace rules (outbound cooldown 210 s, trace lifetime 900 s, unlink distance 30 km) are constants with tests; nothing consumes them until the destinations are sourced.

Tests: `eve-space` filament module, 5 tests (the 17 SDE rows, the family directions, the fleet cap and empty-fleet refusal, the trace numbers, the 2.5 ly reach), all passing; clippy -D warnings and rustfmt clean.


### Wormhole signatures, sites and scan strength (slice 9, project rules)
Superseded in part by "Wormhole sites: real names and client dungeon keys (slice 10)" below: every category now names its site.

- Scan strength of every wormhole signature: 0.0, the SDE dogma default of `scanWormholeStrength` (attribute 1908). EveJS holds no
  per-type value for wormhole types (its provider reads none), and the wiki pages read give none, so one uniform strength is the rule.
- Signatures per J-space system: a count from 4 to 9 drawn from the system's seed (`WormholeSites.Plan`).
- Shares of the categories: combat 40%, relic 15%, data 15%, gas 20%, ore 10%. The wiki names only combat sites per class, so a relic,
  data, gas or ore signature has no template name and is planned with no name; those sites are not built (no template is named in
  the sources). Ore is not limited to the classes that list ore: the share is the rule.
- Combat site names: the wiki's list for the system's class (each name exists in the SDE's dungeon data, archetype 24, faction 500021).
  C13 uses the class 6 list (no extra for C13 is documented).
- A Drifter system (classes 14 to 18) has exactly five combat sites, named from the class 6 list (no Drifter name list is on the pages read).
- Not built: the link of the plan to `CosmicSiteDirector` and to probe scanning; the site entities are not created in the world.

### Wormhole sites: real names and client dungeon keys (slice 10, project rules)
- Names: the per-class lists of EVE University "Wormhole sites" (raw wikitext, fetched 2026-10-12): anomalies (combat), data, relic, gas (reservoirs) and ore (deposits). Each name is matched to the client dungeon by its en-us localization text (`native/localization_fsd_en-us.jsonl.gz`) and carries the dungeon key (`native/dungeons.jsonl.gz`, `dungeonNameID`). EveJS's `wormholeSites` content pack (`resolution-<kind>-<key>`) gives the same key for 68 of the 69 names.
- Spelling: the wiki says "Sizable Perimeter Reservoir"; the client's localization says "Sizeable Perimeter Reservoir" (key 3559). The client spelling is used.
- Zones of gas and ore follow the wiki's headings: gas (reservoirs) perimeter in C1 to C4, frontier in C3 to C6, core in C5 and C6 and Shattered; ore (deposits) perimeter and frontier in every class, core in C5 and C6, Shattered Debris Field in Shattered systems. Core ore that the wiki says may spawn rarely in C1 to C4 is not planned.
- C13 (Shattered) uses the class 6 lists for every category, plus Shattered Debris Field (ore). Shattered Ice Field (an ice belt, the wiki's "Ice belts" heading) is in the client data but not planned: the share table has no ice category.
- Thera (class 12) and the other classes outside 1 to 6 and 13 get no planned sites (`WormholeSites.Plan` returns none). Thera's own sites are not in the wiki's lists.
- Drifter systems keep exactly five combat sites from the class 6 anomalies (unchanged from slice 9).
- The scan strength (0.0) and the counts and shares are unchanged from slice 9. The link to the director and to probe scanning is in the next section.

### Wormhole sites in the director and the probe scan (slice 10, project rules)
- Placed: every planned combat (anomaly), data and relic signature of a J-system is a site of the director, in its own slot (the plan's index). Gas and ore signatures are planned but not placed: the director has no gas or ore site kind, and the mining site generation is not built (the "Cosmic sites" section above).
- Templates: 48, one per combat, data and relic dungeon of the plan (`Sites/Data/wormhole-site-templates.json`, id `wormhole-<dungeon id>`), made by `tools/dev/extract-wormhole-site-templates.py` from the EveJS wormhole content pack (client build 3475087). The waves are the pack's encounters in key order, each a Sleeper spawn query (`sleepers_deadspace`, a pool of `npc_authority.json`); a guard encounter (`talocan_guard`, in 11 of the templates) counts as the last wave. The containers and their analyzers are the pack's. Their difficulty, faction and archetype are the client dungeon's.
- Plan seed: the system id, so a J-system has the same sites in every world (the world seed `EVE_WORMHOLE_SEED` does not reach the director). Not an owner decision yet.
- Respawn: a site that ends is replaced in its slot, somewhere else (the seed carries the slot's previous instance count), with the same template. The wiki gives no respawn rule for a wormhole site.
- Scan strength: a J signature needs `WormholeSites.ScanStrength` (0.0, SDE attribute 1908), and `ProbeScanMath.Coverage` now treats a target that needs no strength as reached by its geometry alone (a probe in range covers it fully). Before, such a target covered nothing. A ship's required strength is never 0, so no ship changes.
- Lifetime: 24 hours as the K-space sites (`CosmicSiteDirector.Lifetime`); a collapsed wormhole does not remove its system's sites (no rule published).
- Not built: the signatures of gas and ore; the hacking and the analysis of a J-space container are the existing K-space path, not tested in a J-system; the live run in a J-system (the headless check) is not done.

### Wormhole service jump tests (slice 10, service layer)
- `WormholeJumpServiceTests` (PostgreSQL, under the heavy lock): a pilot undocked in a J-system (its location set before the undock), a world of one static of the test's types, and the space service's `WormholeJumpAsync`. Covered: the individual mass refusal with its text and no jump given to the simulation; a jump taken, the destination's end placed, the polarization and the second try's text; a connection of 10 tonnes that collapses on the first transit, with both of its ends removed from the simulation.
- Not covered: the polarization's expiry after five minutes (the rig's clock is fixed), the RPC `WormholeMgr` path with its notifications, and a second ship's race between the check and the take (logged, shortcut above). The client is not driven (NOT RUN).

### Wormhole environment in the simulation (slice 10)
- Route: the simulation has no environment of its own. The host applies a system's beacon as dogma modifiers on the ship (`SystemEnvironment`, slice 7c), and gives the simulation the ship's spec from those values (`SimShips.Of` reads the dogma world). The Rust sim is unchanged for this slice.
- Gap found and closed: a ship that jumped into a wormhole system kept the spec of the system it undocked in, until its next undock or login. Now a jump into or out of a system with an environment syncs the buffs and gives the simulation the ship again (`RefreshEnvironmentAsync`, after the arrival). A test: `A_jump_into_a_system_with_an_environment_gives_the_simulation_the_ship_again_with_that_environment`. Gate jumps into systems with no environment are not changed.
- Resistances: the beacons name resistance bonuses (`shieldEmDamageResistanceBonus` and the other 15 of the four layers). The SDE names the dogma attribute a resonance (`shieldEmDamageResonance`, 271 and the rest), a resonance is 1 minus the resistance, so a bonus of p percent is applied to the resonance as minus p percent (PostPercent). Before this, these bonuses were listed as not applied. The spec reads the shield, armor and hull resonances, so they reach the simulation.
- Applied, and read by the spec's ship fields: `maxVelocity`, `agility`, `signatureRadius`, `maxTargetRange`, `rechargeRate` (the capacitor's recharge), `shieldCapacity`, `armorHP`, the shield, armor and hull resonances.
- Applied in the dogma layer, not read by the spec's ship fields: `damageMultiplier`, `heatDamage`, `empFieldRange`, `aoeCloudSize`, `aoeVelocity`, `armorDamageAmount`, `shieldBonus`, `trackingSpeed`. Whether a module spec carries them to the simulation was not checked; the simulation's module model is not changed in this slice.
- Not applied (the name has no dogma attribute or is not a bonus): `energyWarfareStrengthMultiplier`, `energyTransferAmountBonus`, `missileVelocityMultiplier`, `overloadBonusMultiplier`, `smallWeaponDamageMultiplier`, `smartbombDamageMultiplier`, `stasisWebStrengthMultiplier`, `targetPainterStrengthMultiplier`, `capacitorCapacityMultiplierSystem`, `armorDamageAmountMultiplierRemote`, `shieldBonusMultiplierRemote`. These are left out of the environment and listed in `NotApplied` of `SystemEnvironment.EffectOf`.
- Not in the client's attributes: the dogma world's values change on a system change, but the client's ship attributes are not sent again (the client reads them at its next change).

### Warp to a wormhole end and the polarization and RPC tests (slice 11)
- A warp to a wormhole end follows the warp of a body with no warp-in point (the stop distance: the end's radius of 3,000 m plus the ship's size). A warp whose stop point is less than the 150 km minimum (`WARP_DISTANCE_TOO_CLOSE`) is refused, as the game refuses a short warp. The test `a_warp_to_a_wormhole_end_under_the_minimum_warp_distance_is_refused_and_one_beyond_it_ends_within_jump_range` covers both halves; a far end (2e11 m) ends within jump range and the jump is accepted.
- Live (headless, the third and fifth runs): the warp to the home end was accepted and the ship stopped 3.1 km from the end's centre (inside jump range). A destiny update then put the ego ball 5e8 m away and the jump was refused as too far for the rest of the window. The cause is not found: the sim's warp reproduces in the unit test without the drift. Open; see TODO.
- Tests: `WormholeJumpServiceTests`: `The_polarization_lapses_five_minutes_after_the_jump_and_the_service_reports_none_then` (the rig clock) and `The_wormhole_jump_through_the_rpc_service_is_the_space_jump_and_its_answer_is_the_list_the_client_reads` (the RPC service built as the composition builds it).

### The live drift and the star under a wormhole end (slice 12)
- Cause of the drift: the host places an end 50 km from its system's centre (EndPosition, the rule above). In a system whose star has a radius of about 5e8 m, that point lies inside the star. A ship that warps to the end is bumped out by the star's collision to the star's surface, about 5e8 m from the end, and cannot jump (TOO_FAR_FROM_STARGATE). The control (a warp to a stargate of the same system, 40 s of destiny updates) shows no drift.
- Fix: an end (placed) and the far point of an arrival are moved out of every star and planet of their system (`wormholes::clear_of_bodies`): along the end's bearing from that body, to its surface plus its radius and 50 km. Shortcut: the reference's placement of a wormhole end is not in the sources; this is the rule that keeps the end out of the star.
- Scenes kept: `Scene::is_empty` ignored wormhole bodies, so a poll dropped the scene of a system where the host had placed the ends and no ship was yet there; the arriving ship then found no end (STARGATE_NOT_FOUND). A scene with a wormhole end is now kept.
- Arrival: a ship comes out about 12 km from the far end (the gate's clearance), outside the 2.5 km jump range and inside the 150 km minimum warp distance. The return leg flies in (full speed, CmdFollowBall to the end) instead of warping.
- Tests: `a_wormhole_end_inside_a_star_is_placed_outside_it_on_its_bearing` (scene), `a_wormhole_end_placed_in_a_system_nobody_is_in_is_still_there_after_a_poll` (sim), and the live `test_wormhole_live.py` (headless, through the MITM proxy): a jump in, and a jump out through the junction end: passed.
- Not done: the trace logging behind an env var (not needed: the destiny updates showed the 5e8 m position and the star's radius); the gas and ore sites.

## Mission execution X10: courier missions (2026-10-12)

Owner decisions (X9, X10). The courier rules below are eve.rs's project rules, not CCP's or EVE.js's; each is stated with its source.

- **Pickup.** The cargo is handed over at acceptance into the hangar of the agent's station (`GrantCargoAsync`, the agent's `locationID` as the station). The quantity and the singleton flag come from the mission's `courierMission` record (`objectiveQuantity`, `objectiveSingleton`). The pickup location in the client objective is the same station.
- **Destination.** A station chosen by a seed among the stations (other than the origin's) whose system lies within the level's jump range from the origin's system, counted on the stargate graph (`mapStargates`). The seed is the mission ID times 1,000,003 plus the character ID; the candidates are in station ID order. Jump ranges, from eve-survival's "Courier Missions" page (a third-party page, about ten years old; the EVE University wiki gives none): levels 1 and 2 0 to 5 jumps, level 3 0 to 12, level 4 1 to 12, level 5 as level 4. A level outside these gives no destination.
- **Completion on delivery.** Docking at the destination station (`SpaceSessions.DockAsync`, after the ship's dock succeeds) completes the accepted courier mission when the cargo is held in any location; the cargo is consumed with the completion, in the completion's transaction.
- **Expiry.** An accepted courier mission expires after the mission data's `expirationTime` (minutes; 1020 of the 1155 courier missions carry it, for example 10080 = 7 days), or after three days where the data gives none, counted from the acceptance. Expiry is checked by the mission sweep (every 10 seconds).
- **Failure.** The expiry fails the mission with no standing change, as every failure of the runtime does today (no failure penalty is implemented). The cargo stays with the pilot (shortcut: the failure does not take it back).
- **Reward.** The official ISK of `missionRewards.reward` when the record gives one (324 of the 1155 courier records do); the basic level rate is not applied to courier missions.
- **Client objective.** The transport entry of the client contract (eve-logic `evemissions/client/mission.py`: pickup owner and location, drop-off owner and location, and the cargo with the SDE volume times the quantity and `hasCargo`).
- **Reach.** The 25 courier missions that are epic-arc steps are offered through the arc. The other 1130 courier missions need a basic courier offer (the Distribution agents' missions), which is not built yet: those missions are held back.
- Tests: `MissionDungeonRuntimeTests` (`A_courier_accepted_hands_the_cargo_over_at_the_agents_station`, `A_courier_delivered_at_its_destination_completes_consumes_the_cargo_and_pays`, `A_courier_past_its_data_expiry_fails_and_one_before_it_does_not`, `The_courier_destination_is_a_station_in_the_level_jump_range_reached_by_stargates_and_is_seeded`).

## Mission execution X10 C: the kinds of the objective data (2026-10-12)

- **The 560 kill objectives.** Every `killMission` with an `objectiveTypeID` (560 missions, 253 item types) also carries a `dungeonID` and an `objectiveQuantity` above zero. Each is a dungeon whose objective is an item: the shape of arc 29 step 14119 (the Strange Datacore). The item groups are commodities (the largest share), asteroid ores (Veldspar, Pyroxeres, Omber, Kernite and others), harvestable gas clouds, datacores, ship logs, criminal tags, drugs, identification cards and gate keys.
- **Implemented:** the fetch from a dungeon (X8 A): `FetchOf` reads all 560 rows; the item is granted at the agent's station when the dungeon completes and handed in at the agent. The agent's kind decides which missions are offered (`AgentMissionKindRules`): Security agents give encounter missions, Mining agents mining, Distribution agents courier.
- **Mining:** the ore objectives are the same shape (a dungeon with ore loot), because the SDE has no mining record without a dungeon. Mining is therefore the fetch from a dungeon; no separate mining mission is built. Mining agents offer no mission yet (no basic mining offer exists).
- **Courier:** the 1155 `courierMission` records (X10 B above). Built: the lifecycle; the 25 that are arc steps are offered; the 1130 others need a basic courier offer, not built.
- **Trade:** no SDE record is a trade objective (`killMission` and `courierMission` are the only objective records). Not implemented: no data. The client's trade kind is EveJS's mapping to a fetch, which is not a sourced rule.
- **Encounter:** the basic pool offers dungeon kills only (`BasicKillOffers`), not the 560 item objectives. Not implemented for the basic pool; the 59 arc steps with item objectives work through the arc.

### Gas and ore sites of wormhole space (slice 12)
- The plan's gas and ore signatures are sites now: a reservoir is a signature (probe-scanned, scan strength 0.0), a deposit is an anomaly (warpable, in the scanner) — the wiki's rules.
- Templates: from the EveJS content packs `evejs-gas-sites-1.0.0.json` (reservoirs: the `gasTypeIDs` are harvestable cloud types, group 711 in the SDE) and `evejs-ore-anomalies-1.1.0.json` (deposits: each rock's type, quantity and position, client build 3475087). The generator `tools/dev/extract-wormhole-site-templates.py` writes 68 templates.
- Placement: a reservoir's cloud is one of its candidate cloud types (picked by the site's id) with 1,000 units (shortcut: the stock of a cloud is not in the SDE; the same figure as a belt's cloud). A deposit's rocks are at their recorded offsets from the site's origin, with the client's quantity. Both are items launched by the site, placed in the simulation's scene (`SpaceContainers.PlaceSiteResourcesAsync`).
- Restore: a pilot's entry puts the live sites' resources back in the scene (`RestoreSiteResourcesAsync`); the simulation drops a scene nobody is in, so the resources are placed again on each entry.
- shortcut: the resources are not removed when their site ends (a belt keeps its rocks the same way until mined). Not built: the mining of the ore and the gas cloud is the existing belt path (not tested on these sites).
- Not run: a live check of a gas or ore site (the headless stack has no scenario that scans one).

## Mission execution X11: basic offers, the courier failure side (2026-10-12)

- **Basic courier offer.** A Distribution agent of basic type (kind `courier`) at level L offers the lowest courier mission of level L that is not an arc step and that the replay delay does not hold (`BasicKillOffers.PickCourier`). The level comes from the committed level tags: 241 of the 1155 courier records carry one (levels 1 to 4; none at 5), so the other records get no offer. The arc's 25 courier steps are offered by the arc. The priority is the agent's own level; L4 and L5 are not ranked above lower levels, because the data has no level-5 courier record.
- **Basic item-objective offer.** A basic encounter agent of level 4 or 5 already offers the dungeon-kill missions of its level, the item-objective ones included (`BasicKillOffers.Pick`, unchanged); their item is granted by the fetch of X8 and handed in at the agent. Covered by `A_basic_encounter_agent_offers_the_item_kill_mission_of_its_level_through_the_dungeon_offer`.
- **Courier failure: the cargo.** The expiry fails the mission and the agent takes the cargo back: the stacks tagged `courier:{mission}` (the custom info of the cargo at its pickup) are removed, and no other item of the type is touched.
- **Courier failure: the standing.** The EVE University wiki's NPC standings page says failing a mission lowers the agent, corporation and faction standings, and that declining more than one mission in four hours does the same; it gives no amount for either. The quit example on another wiki page (a level 4 quit: about 6.2% agent, 2.5% corporation, 0.1% faction) is for quitting, not failing. No amount for a failure is published, so no failure standing is applied; the direction is recorded for when an amount is sourced.
- **Dock.** Docking reports the pilot to the mission runtime (`SpaceSessions.PilotDocked`) once the simulation has docked the ship. Test: `A_pilot_that_docks_is_reported_to_the_mission_runtime_with_the_station_it_docked_at` (Eve.Services.Space.Tests, a real `SpaceSessions` on the PostgreSQL ship store; the test sends the simulation's `SimDocked`). The courier completion itself is tested in `A_courier_delivered_at_its_destination_completes_consumes_the_cargo_and_pays`. Not done: a Host-level test that boots the composition; the wiring from `PilotDocked` to the runtime is compile-checked only.

## Mission execution X12: consumption by tag, quit, the simulation dock, level tags (2026-10-12)

- **Delivery consumes the tagged cargo only.** A courier delivery consumes the stacks tagged `courier:{mission}` and no other stack of the type. A fetch turn-in still consumes any stack of its type (the X8 rule). Test: `A_courier_consumption_takes_only_the_tagged_stack_and_leaves_the_pilots_own_stacks`; refusal without a tagged stack: `A_courier_consumption_with_only_the_pilots_own_stacks_is_refused_and_writes_nothing`.
- **Quit takes the cargo back.** A quit of an accepted courier mission, at the agent and through the research cancel, takes the tagged cargo back as expiry does, with no standing change. The dialogue has no decline of an accepted mission (decline applies to offers), so decline after accept does not exist. Test: `A_courier_quit_takes_the_cargo_back_and_pays_nothing`.
- **PilotDocked fires for simulation docks.** A client dock command goes to the simulation and the dock is completed by `SimDocked` (`FinishDockingAsync`); a dock the simulation starts on its own arrives the same way. The hook runs for both. Tests: `A_dock_that_the_simulation_starts_on_its_own_reports_the_pilot_too` (no client command) and `A_pilot_that_docks_is_reported_to_the_mission_runtime_with_the_station_it_docked_at` (through `DockAsync`).
- **Level tags for the untagged courier records: not extended.** The tag file is unchanged. Tagged courier records by level: L1 105, L2 36, L3 54, L4 46 (241 in all, no L5). The client mission records carry no level field. Kill-mission levels came from the client dungeon difficulty; courier levels come from the wiki distribution tables (191 tags in all). The SDE carries English names for all 1155 courier records, so EVE University per-mission level pages (for example "Cargo Delivery (Blood Raiders) (Level 4)") can source them by name; that transcription for the 914 untagged records is not done. A cargo-volume rule was checked: above 1000 m3 the tagged levels are 3 and 4, and at or below it 1 and 2, but no threshold separates one level, so no volume rule is applied.
- **Correction to C.** The client records name the content template: `StorylineTradeMission` (443 records) and `BasicMiningMission` (42) exist. Trade data is therefore present; the trade kind is not implemented yet.

## Mission execution X13b: the trade and mining kinds (2026-10-12)

- **The kind comes from the client template.** The SDE carries no content template. The client mission data (the embedded copy of `native/missions.jsonl.gz`; `EVE_CLIENT_SDE_DIR` overrides it) carries `contentTemplate` for every mission (2,892 rows); it is read at startup (`ClientMissionTemplates`). The trade and mining rules below depend on it.
- **Storyline trade (443 records, `StorylineTradeMission`).** The objective is the record's `courierMission` (item and quantity). It is a fetch at the mission's agent, as EveJS treats a trade (agentMissionRuntime: the trade kind is a FETCH): the pilot holds the quantity and hands it in at the agent, which consumes the held stacks and completes the step with its reward. No cargo is handed over at acceptance, a docked delivery does not complete it, and it has no courier expiry. Tests: `A_storyline_trade_is_a_fetch_at_its_agent_with_no_cargo_no_delivery_and_no_courier_expiry`.
- **Trade offers: not built.** The storyline offer rule (EVE University Storyline missions: one storyline mission for every 16 completed missions of the same level and faction, from the nearest storyline agent of that faction, with Faction Warfare excluded) is not implemented. Storyline trade missions are therefore never offered yet, and no trade mission is reachable in play.
- **Basic mining (42 records, `BasicMiningMission`).** A dungeon kill whose objective is ore (`killMission`). The fetch of X8 grants the objective quantity at the dungeon's completion and the agent takes it at the turn-in. Shortcut: the quota is granted in full at the completion, not mined by the pilot. The basic mining offer (`BasicKillOffers.PickMining`) offers the lowest mining mission of the Mining agent's level (the 42 records are level tagged: L1 11, L2 10, L3 9, L4 12) that the dungeon can start and that the replay delay does not hold. Test: `A_basic_mining_agent_offers_the_lowest_mining_mission_of_its_level_that_the_dungeon_can_start`.
- **Courier exclusions.** A trade mission is never a courier: its objective record is excluded from the cargo at acceptance, the docked delivery, the courier expiry and the quit take-back.
- **Name-based level tags.** Not done here; another agent's job, which edits only the tag file.

## Mission execution X14: the storyline offer counter (2026-10-12)

- **The rule (wiki).** EVE University, Storyline missions: one storyline mission for every 16 completed missions of the same level and faction (any corporation); Faction Warfare missions do not count; the offer comes from the nearest storyline agent of that faction.
- **Built.** The counter table `storyline_counters` (migration 0005: per character, faction and level, the completed and the offered counts), `StorylineCounters` (increment in a transaction, read, mark offered) and the due-offers rule `StorylineCounters.PendingOffers` (completions in whole intervals of 16, less the offers made). Tests: `The_storyline_offers_due_are_the_whole_intervals_less_the_offers_made` and `Sixteen_completions_of_one_faction_and_level_are_one_pending_storyline_offer_until_it_is_made`.
- **Not wired.** The completion does not yet increment the counter, the offer picker does not yet offer a storyline mission, and the nearest storyline agent is not chosen. The wiki gives no jump range for the nearest agent, so none is applied when it is wired. Until then no storyline mission is offered and the counter stays empty.
- **Mining quota.** Not made an actual quota: the ore quota is still granted in full at the dungeon's completion (X13b shortcut); the mining code is not reused.

## Mission execution X15: the storyline counter wired, the mining quota handed in (2026-10-12)

- **Counting.** A completion counts toward its faction and level when it is a basic mission of a basic agent (agent type 2), not an arc step, not a trade mission, with a known faction (npcCorporations.factionID). Faction Warfare and storyline agents are other agent types and never count. The increment is in the completion's transaction (`CompleteInTransactionAsync`, counted argument). Test: `A_counted_completion_adds_one_to_its_faction_and_level_and_an_uncounted_one_adds_nothing`.
- **The offer.** At a storyline agent (types 6 and 7) whose faction has a pending offer for a level 1 to 5 (`StorylineCounters.PendingOffers`), the picker offers the lowest storyline trade mission the pilot has not completed, and marks the offer made. Shortcut: the offer is marked when it is picked, not when it is accepted. The nearest storyline agent is the agent talked to, since the pilot is at its system; the wiki gives no range, so none is applied. The storyline trade is a fetch at that agent (its turn-in is the fetch of X13b).
- **Not tested directly:** the picker itself (it is a composition lambda). The counter's 16-completion boundary is tested in `Sixteen_completions_of_one_faction_and_level_are_one_pending_storyline_offer_until_it_is_made`; the offer path is compile-checked only.
- **Mining quota: handed in.** A basic mining step's dungeon completion grants nothing. The pilot hands in the ore of the objective type it holds, at the agent, and the full quantity completes the step (the turn-in consumes it). Test: `A_mining_step_is_not_granted_by_its_dungeon_and_completes_when_the_pilot_hands_in_the_full_ore_quantity`. Shortcut: the pilot supplies the ore (mined or bought); the site does not put ore loot into rocks or containers, and the mining code is not reused.
- **Client data.** The embedded client-SDE subset has not landed on origin/main yet, so the kinds and texts are still read from `EVE_CLIENT_SDE_DIR`, which stays optional.

## Mission execution X16: the storyline offer marked on accept, and tested as a path (2026-10-12)

- **Marked on accept.** The storyline offer is counted as made when the pilot accepts the storyline trade (the accept hook of the agent dialogue), not when the offer is picked. A declined or abandoned offer keeps the pending count. `StorylineOffers.MarkAcceptedAsync` counts the lowest due level of the faction; it changes nothing for a declined or non-storyline record.
- **Testable path.** The picker's storyline step is `StorylineOffers` (`PickAsync`, `MarkAcceptedAsync`), which the composition uses. Test: `The_sixteenth_completion_offers_the_storyline_trade_a_decline_keeps_it_due_and_the_acceptance_consumes_it` (the 15th completion offers nothing, the 16th offers the trade, a decline keeps the count, an acceptance consumes it).
- **Embedded client data.** Not switched: no embedded client-data commit is on origin/main yet (the two matches for "client data" are wormhole and Zarzakh commits). The kinds and texts are still read from `EVE_CLIENT_SDE_DIR`, which stays optional.






### Wormhole space: final status (2026-10-12)

The implemented work, its evidence and its open items are in `docs/project/TODO.md` ("Wormhole space: final status"). This section lists the places where eve.rs differs from the reference or where a project rule stands in for a published value.

- **Project rules (nothing published):** signature counts 4 to 9 per system and category shares (combat 40%, relic 15%, data 15%, gas 20%, ore 10%); a Drifter system has five combat sites; the plan's seed is the system id; the scan strength of a wormhole signature is the SDE value 0.0 (attribute 1908); the ends are placed 50 km from their system's centre, pushed out of any star or planet; a cloud holds 1,000 units.
- **Shortcuts (known limits):** the site's resources are removed when the site ends, but a rock or cloud that is still in space when the server stops is only restored by its site's next entry; the host's placement of an end is not the reference's; an arrival is 12 km from the far end (the gate's clearance), and the return flies in.
- **Cut by the owner:** wandering connections in K-space (Thera's connections are implemented, the K-space ones are not); region-adjacency exclusion of statics is not applied.
- **Not done:** local chat in J-space (no chat service); the ice belt of a Shattered system (not planned); a live check of a gas or ore site; a capture comparison of a wormhole jump with a stargate jump; the client's attribute refresh after a system change; trace logging behind an environment variable (not needed for the drift).
- **Live evidence:** the headless jump in and back through a static wormhole passed (`tools/headless-client/tests/test_wormhole_live.py`, through the MITM proxy). It was a run with a copied static into the pilot's station system, not a static of the world's generator.

## Module activation rules audit (2026-10-12)

Policy (Decision 9, 2026-10-13): a refusal needs an SDE attribute or group rule, a client error key or text, or a wiki basis; EVE.js alone is not a basis; when in doubt the module is allowed. The cloak rules are the owner's ruling and are not subject to it.
Every refusal of the module activation path, as the code had it, checked against the real-game basis. The sources: the SDE attributes (`disallowActivateOnWarp` 1245, `disallowInEmpireSpace` 1074, `disallowInHighSec` 1970, `maxGroupActive` 763, `activationBlocked` 1349, `moduleReactivationDelay` 669, `maxVelocityActivationLimit` 1334, `minVelocityActivationLimit` 2608, `disallowEarlyDeactivation` 906, `canCloak` 1163, `disallowCloaking` 2454, `doomsdayNoJumpOrCloakDuration` 2427, `disallowAssistance` 854), the V24.01 client (`dogma/moduleRestrictions.py`, `pythonEffects.py` FakeCloaking, `baseDogmaLocation.py`), EveJS v0.12.9 (`moduleOperation.js`, `scene/modules.js`, `shipCloak.js`), and the EVE University wiki (Smartbombs, Cloaking, Cynosural Field Generator). Verdicts: real = the rule has a source and matches it; invented = no source, removed; wrong = a source exists but the rule differs, changed; open = a conflict or a gap, kept and listed.

| Rule (refusal) | Where | Basis | Verdict | Change |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ModuleRestrictions.Check`: `CantInEmpireSpace` (1074, fully-corrupted exception 5599/5600) | dotnet `ModuleRestrictions.cs`, `SpaceSessions.Modules.cs` | client `moduleRestrictions.py` (low sec: 1074; high sec: 1074 and 1970); SDE 1074 on bombs, lances, probes, warp disruptors | real | none. Smartbombs are on neither list (SDE), so no ban on them in high sec |
| `CantInHighSecSpace` (1970) | same | client `moduleRestrictions.py`; SDE 1970 on capital, carrier, dreadnought, titan, structure types | real | none |
| `ModuleRestrictions.CheckWarp`: `MODULE_WARP_BLOCKED` (1245) | dotnet `ModuleRestrictions.cs` | SDE 1245: siege, triage, bastion, capital industrial core, sensor arrays, cyno fields, clone vat; EveJS bastion-in-warp | real | none. The SDE also covers aligning, which the check does |
| `CANNOT_ACTIVATE_IN_WARP` for strikes (lances) and doomsdays | Rust `activation.rs` | no SDE attribute on those types (1245 is not on them); EveJS refuses only the smartbomb and the bastion in warp | invented | removed. A doomsday still fails on its lock, which cannot be held through warp (test `a_lance_is_activated_while_its_pilot_warps`) |
| Smartbomb in warp (removed) | — | EVE.js only: no SDE attribute (1245 is not on the bomb types), no client text, no wiki statement found | dropped (Decision 9) | the refusal is removed; a smartbomb starts in warp |

| `ACTIVE_INDUSTRIAL_CORE_REQUIRED` (3265) | Rust | EveJS text; SDE 3265 | real | none |
| `ACTIVE_SIEGE_MODULE_REQUIRED` | Rust, dotnet | SDE `activationRequiresActiveSiegeModule` (5426) = 1 on the four disruptive lances (Super Weapon group); the reference refuses without an active siege module (EVE.js) | real | none |
| `TARGET_REQUIRED`, `TARGET_NOT_FOUND`, `TARGET_NOT_LOCKED` | Rust | client targeted effects act on the locked active target (`crimewatchSvc.GetRequiredSafetyLevelForEffect` reads `GetActiveTargetID`); no client refusal text found in eve-logic | real (targeting rule) | none |
| `TARGET_SELF` | Rust | not a game refusal: an impossible target guard (no client text); without it a module would name its own ship | kept as a validity guard | none |
| `TARGET_OUT_OF_RANGE` for remote modules and scanners | Rust | SDE `maxRange` (54) and the falloff of the module give its reach; the reach is what the module can act on (`Reach::share`). The client's range refusal key is not in eve-logic (open: the wording) | real (SDE range) | none |
| `TARGET_OUT_OF_RANGE` for turrets | Rust | turrets are not refused out of range (only missiles, below) | real | none |
| `TARGET_OUT_OF_RANGE` for missiles (flight range) | Rust `activation.rs` | no client text and no EVE.js refusal for a launcher beyond its missile's flight; wiki Missiles: missiles "chase" the target and are spent "when they ... run out of flight time"; the only hard range in the wiki is that of auto-targeting missiles, which do not acquire targets beyond it | invented, removed | the launcher starts and its missile expires (test `a_launcher_fires_at_a_target_further_than_its_missile_flies_and_the_missile_does_not_reach_it`) |
| `NO_AMMO` | Rust, dotnet | client `NoCharges` | real | none |
| `NO_FUEL`, `FUEL_REFUSED` | Rust, dotnet | SDE `consumptionType` (713) > 0 on the module (its fuel per cycle, `consumptionQuantity` 714); `ShipEffect` sets `BurnsFuel` from it; a module that needs fuel cannot cycle without it | real | none |
| `NOT_ENOUGH_CAPACITOR` | Rust | SDE `capacitorNeed` (6) is what a cycle takes; the client refuses a module it cannot pay for | real | none |
| `MJD_COOLDOWN`, `BAD_MJD` | Rust | wiki Micro Jump Drive: "the MJD enters an 180-second cooldown period before it can be used again" (Marauders shorter); BAD_MJD is a parameter guard | real | none |
| `TETHERED` (removed) | — | no SDE attribute, client text or wiki statement backs a refusal of a smartbomb or bomb while tethered. The SDE `disallowTethering` (2343) is on command bursts, cynosural field generators, super weapons, siege and sensor modules; those already break the tether on start (no refusal) | dropped (Decision 9) | the refusal and its code are removed; test `a_tethered_ship_starts_a_smartbomb_and_the_start_breaks_the_tether` |
| `TARGET_LOOT_RIGHTS_REQUIRED` | dotnet | client `crimewatchSvc.CheckCanTakeItems` and `HaveLootRight` (a container's loot right) | real | none |
| `TARGET_INVALID_FOR_MODULE`, `CHARGE_NOT_COMPATIBLE` (mining family, crystal list) | dotnet | client groups ice (`groupIce`, `groupIceProduct`) apart from ore; SDE `specializationAsteroidTypeList` (3148) on a crystal | real | none |

| ECM burst in high sec without safety none | dotnet | wiki Electronic Countermeasures (as cited in code) | real | none |
| Jovian gate blocks a warp-disruption field generator | dotnet | client deploy blocker (100000000 m) | real | none |

Tests: `crates/eve-space` 799 passed. dotnet: `Eve.Space.Tests` 313 passed, `Eve.Services.Dogma.Tests` 134 passed, `Eve.Services.Space.Tests` filtered to Module/Smartbomb/Ecm/Cloak/Drone 111 passed. Not run: the live walk (`tools/headless-client/tests/test_fob_insystem_walk.py`), which fits a smartbomb in high security at red safety.


## Refusals reviewed against a basis (2026-10-13)

Owner policy: a refusal needs an SDE attribute, a client error key or text or constant, or a wiki basis; EveJS alone is not one. Reviewed in
the mission, agent, market, trade, LP store, repair, reprocessing, bookmark, blueprint, item-locking, corporation-station and war-registry code.

- **Immediate buy that matches nothing is booked (owner decision, 2026-10-13).** The client's `BuyStuff` (duration 0, its default) and `BuyMulti` announce
  the order they placed (`OnClientEvent_OrderPlaced`, `marketsvc.py:267, 272`). An immediate buy takes what matches at its limit and books the rest as a buy
  order at its price and range, with escrow and broker fee as for any buy order. The requested duration is 0, and an order of duration 0 rests nowhere, so the
  booked order lasts the default buy duration of 90 days (`MarketRules.ImmediateBuyBookedDays`; the EVE default; not stated in the sources read). `BuyMultipleItems`
  answers the rowset of the orders it created (the client iterates it). Tests: `An_immediate_buy_that_matches_nothing_is_booked_with_its_escrow`,
  `An_immediate_buy_fills_what_matches_and_books_the_rest`, `Buy_multi_fills_each_line_at_its_limit_and_books_the_unfilled_rest_of_each_line`.
- **Removed (no basis):** the "No matching sell orders" refusal of an immediate buy (`PlaceBuyOrder`, duration 0), which the owner has now replaced by the booking above. The PLEX
  personal-market-only refusal: the client's `SellPlex` forwards the corporation flag like any item. The seven-day offer expiry for every
  agent: the wiki gives it for COSMOS agents only and no data identifies them, so offers do not expire.
- **Kept, with the basis:** the four-hour block on a re-offer after a decline (the client's per-agent `GetReplayTimestamp` timer); the
  four-hour standing window (declining more than one mission in four hours, wiki) has no published amount and is not applied. The market
  modification delay (`mktModificationDelay = 300`, client constant); the price ticks (`TICK_SIGNIFICANT_FIGURES = 4`); the bookmark folder,
  subfolder and active-folder caps (client `MAX_*` constants and keys); the bookmark restriction in Zarzakh (wiki); the market skill and order
  limits (SDE skills, wiki); the partial-repair refusal (the client's `RepairNonPartialConfirmation` dialog); the stack limit of the
  reprocessing split (the client's 32-bit stack, `CLIENT_INVENTORY_STACK_LIMIT`); the corporation access rules (client keys and roles).
- **Kept, not a game rule:** messages for services not built on this server ("not available on this server yet", "Direct trading is not
  available", "Shared bookmark folders are not available"). They are placeholders and say so.
- **Kept, protective:** the state guards of agent missions, the stale-view and duplicate-placement checks, the custody, escrow and money checks.

## Industry slice I1: job rules, NPC stations and cost index (2026-10-13)

Full status, formulas and sources: `docs/project/INDUSTRY_STATUS.md`. Decisions and cuts recorded here:

- **System cost index**: a project rule. `EVE_INDUSTRY_COST_INDEX` (fraction, default 0) applies to every system and
  activity, and the map cost RPC uses the same rule. Basis: the pinned SDE and the client data carry no live indices;
  the ESI `/industry/systems/` activity keys were not confirmed. Next step: an ESI loader like the adjusted prices.
- **NPC station facility tax**: the SDE `npcCorporations.taxRate` of the owning corporation (CCP data). The war-faction
  modifier (`npcstationtaxmodifier`) is not applied (cut).
- **NPC station activities**: the SDE station operation's service list decides. Factory (14) runs manufacturing;
  Laboratory (15) runs research, copying and invention. Time factors are the SDE `manufacturingFactor` and
  `researchFactor`, as EveJS applies them.
- **Reactions**: refused at NPC stations (no station service runs them). Structure reactions are not checked against
  the structure reaction service (cut).
- **Delivery**: completed jobs wait in Ready until the owner delivers (`CompleteJob`), per the EVE wiki's Deliver
  button. The durable scheduler is not used for delivery.
- **Distance rule** (`FACILITY_DISTANCE`): enforced: jumps from the installer's system over the SDE stargates against 5 jumps per distance-skill level (max 25). Basis: the client's job error and DISTANCE_PER_LEVEL.
- **Invention skill probability**: 1/30 per level, 1/40 for skills on SDE type list 799 (applied).
- **Duration skills**: Industry, Advanced Industry, required-skill (1982), Science (452), Metallurgy (468), Reactions
  (2660) and Research (453, time efficiency) come from SDE skill attributes. Invention has no time skill in the SDE.
- **Slots**: base 1 per activity class plus the skill levels (Mass Production, Advanced Mass Production,
  Laboratory Operation); the base of 1 is the project's basis (cut: not read from the SDE).
- **Reverse engineering**: no activity in the pinned SDE; not built.

## Industry slice I2: corporation jobs, MITM shapes, structures (2026-10-13)

Full status: `docs/project/INDUSTRY_STATUS.md`. Decisions recorded here:

- **Corporation jobs** follow the client's rules (`industry/job.py` validation): a corporation owner needs Factory Manager
  (1024) and, at the job's station, the manufacturing role (1<<50) for a manufacturing job or the science role (1<<51) for
  any other activity; a director holds every role. The wallet division is paid only by a member with its take role. The
  client's `roles` is `RolesAtLocation(facility)`; the server reads the member's roles at the job's station (the same
  CorporationRoles.Session rule as the hangar). Covered by `IndustryCorporationPersistenceTests` (4).
- **Corporation notifications** (not built): `OnIndustryJob` goes to the owner and the installer character only. The other
  members of the corporation who hold Factory Manager are not notified of a corporation job.
- **Corporation delivery location** (not built): the corporation's delivery flag is not known from the client code read
  (`flagCorpDeliveries` is the client's flag for the output of a corporation job; the server delivers to the hangar of the
  owner's station). No guess is made here.
- **Structures** (not changed): a structure with an online industry service runs every activity (no service-module to
  activity mapping), its tax and time multiplier come from the structure record, and rig and role bonuses are not applied.
  Changing this needs the structure service names from the structure data, which the walk did not read; cut for this slice.
- **Reverse engineering and duplicating**: not client activities. `industry/const.py` lists manufacturing (1), research
  time (3), research material (4), copying (5), invention (8) and reaction (9) only, and the client's ACTIVITY sets are
  these. Nothing is added.
- **Shape comparison with EveJS** (code reading, and the MITM capture of the walk; EveJS was not run live for this slice):
  `InstallJob` returns the job id (both); `GetJobsByOwner` returns a list of job rows (both); `CancelJob` and `CompleteJob`
  return the job payload in EveJS (`industryManagerService.js` Handle_CancelJob, Handle_CompleteJob) and null in eve.rs.
  The client ignores both returns (`industrySvc.CancelJob`, `CompleteJob`), so this is a shape difference with no client
  effect. The earlier note that both return nothing was wrong for EveJS.
- **Ready and install notifications**: `OnIndustryJob` is sent once per state change (install, ready, cancel, delivery).
  The MITM capture of the walk shows the install notification (status 1); the walk asserts it.

## Industry slice I3: corporation outputs, structure activities (2026-10-13)

- **Corporation outputs.** The facility lists a corporation's places as its deliveries (flag 62) and its hangar divisions 1..7
  (flags 115..121), the client's `flagCorpDeliveries` and `flagCorpSAG1` (`inventorycommon/const.py`). A corporation's job
  outputs only to one of those at its facility; a personal hangar is refused (`IndustryInvalidJob`, the client's error key for
  an invalid job). A corporation job with no chosen output goes to the deliveries (`industry/job.py` MatchLocation's default).
- **Corporation announcements.** `OnIndustryJob` goes to the owner, the installer and the online members who hold Factory
  Manager (1024). The EveJS reference sends it to every online member of the corporation (`industryNotifications.js`
  `notifyIndustryJob`); eve.rs narrows it to the role holders, because the client lists a corporation's jobs to them only
  (`industryManagerService` `sessionCanAccessIndustryOwner`). The walk checks both sides.
- **Structure activities** (`IndustryRules.StructureActivities`): a structure runs the activities its online services allow,
  by the EveJS service mapping (`structureServiceAuthority.js` `getIndustryActivityServiceIDs`): manufacturing by 21 to 24;
  each science activity by the Laboratory (30) or its own laboratory service (31 to 34); reactions by 40 to 43. The structure
  payload lists those activities. The SDE service module types are Standup Manufacturing Plant I (35878), Standup Research
  Lab I (35891) and Standup Invention Lab I (35886) (`types.jsonl`); the service ids are the EveJS constants, not read from the
  SDE. The earlier name match (industry, manufacturing, research in a service name) is removed.
- **Rig and role bonuses: not applied.** The SDE carries the engineering rig attributes (`attributeEngRigTimeBonus` 2593,
  `attributeEngRigMatBonus` 2594, `attributeEngRigCostBonus` 2595; `dogmaAttributes.jsonl`), but eve.rs keeps no rigs fitted
  to a structure (the structure schema has no rig or role data). With no fitted rig there is nothing to read, so the bonuses
  are cut for this slice. Role bonuses of the structure hull are not read either.
- **Reverse engineering and duplicating**: not client activities (`industry/const.py`); nothing added.

## Planetary interaction (PI)

Source: `docs/project/PI_DESIGN.md` (inventory, client contract, formulas with their files and lines, SDE facts). The rules are the V24.01 client's (eve-logic `eve/common/script/planet/*`, `planetCommon.py`); the numbers are the SDE's (build 3396210) or the EVE University wiki's (Planetary Interaction, Skills: Planet Management).

- **D1 Resource distribution.** The value a program draws from a point of the planet comes from CCP's native resource harmonic (`eve/common/lib/PlanetResources`, `_eveplanetresources`), which is not in the client snapshot. The server uses a deterministic field of the planet id and the resource id (`Eve.Planets.DeterministicResourceField`, bounded by the client's `planetResourceMaxValue` 1.21). The mechanics (overlap of heads, cycles, decay and noise, bandwidth) are the client's; the yields are not CCP's. `GetResourceData` answers no band (the client keeps its empty survey).
- **D2 Installable resources.** Any planetary resource (category 42) is accepted as a program's resource. The planet-type list of resources is not in the snapshot.
- **D3 Command center removal.** The command center item is consumed when the planet is first placed; removing the command center abandons the colony and destroys its pins (their items go with them). No refund. The client's rules say nothing; the wiki is silent on what a removed command center returns.
