- **D4 ISK.** A non-command pin costs its base price and an upgrade its cost difference; both are charged on submit (`refPlanetaryConstruction`, 98) in the same transaction as the change. The client's wallet check is advisory.
- **D5 Launches.** A launch puts the commodities into a can (type 23, Cargo Container, singleton, in the system) that holds them as the owner's stacks, and a launch row names the can (its item id is real) with the planet's place (two radii out along x from the planet's centre) and its expiry (5 days, wiki). The can is in space at that place: a wreck-kind ball of group 12 in the simulation, with a space row whose time is the launch's five days. Only its owner may take from it (a launch has no corporation). At expiry the can and what is in it go (the space sweep); lapsed launch rows are marked deleted when the owner lists them. The client's pickup is the ordinary can flow (open within 2,500 m, take into the hold).
- **D8 Customs offices.** An office is type 2233 (Customs Office, group 1025, category Orbital, the client's `groupPlanetaryCustomsOffices`), an item of id 1.2e12 + planet id at the planet's system, owned by an NPC label. It is created with the colony's first pin and is an orbital of its planet in space: the simulation's body of category 46 at two radii above the planet's centre (the place is the server's; the snapshot does not give the reference's orbit), with the planet and level 1 in its slim item (the client's `planetInfoSvc` and `importExportUI` read them). The host puts the existing offices in space at start-up, and again when a pilot enters their system (the simulation drops a scene that has no pilot); a pilot's first bind is told each structure of its system as a ball (the reference shows a structure to whoever is in its system). `planetOrbitalRegistryBroker.GetTaxRate` and `ImportExportWithPlanet` on the bound office are served. Fees and rules: the client's formulas (spaceportPin GetImportTax / GetExportTax) with SDE attributes 1638 / 1639 / 1640 and the wiki's office rates (10% in high-security less 1% per Customs Code Expertise level, 17% outside). NPC offices take no standing reduction (the client's TaxRateCalculator is for owned offices). The office's radius (8,000 m) is the SDE's `types.radius` of 2233, written as a constant. The office's hangar room is 35,000 m3 (SDE `types.capacity`). A pilot's stack from its ship's hold goes into the office hangar through the container `Add` (the client's `importExportUI.MoveFromInventory`): it keeps its owner, merges into the office's stack of the same commodity, and is refused with `NotEnoughSpace` (volume and room left, the client's key, importExportUI.py:296) when it does not fit. The office's `GetCapacity` answers its room for any pilot. Shortcut: a station hangar is not a source for the office (only the ship's hold, while the pilot is in space in the office's system), and the move sends no item-change notification, so an open window shows the stack on its next read. Both are the upgrade path when a walk needs them. The balls (the office, a launch can) are put in space after the transaction commits: a rollback places nothing.
- **D6 Lazy simulation.** A colony is stored with its time and is run to now on each read and write. The result depends only on the stored state and the time, the client does the same, so no periodic job is kept. The durable scheduler is not used for PI.
- **D7 Pin ids.** A pin is an item row of the inventory store (location: the planet), created with the pin and deleted with it. The command center uses the id of the hold item the client sends.
- **D9 Resource survey.** Two parts. (a) The resource list (`GetPlanetResourceInfo`) is the project's rule: each planet carries four of the raw resources (SDE groups 1032, 1033, 1035: 17 types), chosen by the planet id; the quality is the deterministic field's largest value over a 16 x 32 sample grid, on the client's display scale (value x 255 / 2; the client divides by planetResourceMaxValue x 255 / 2). CCP's set per planet is not in the snapshot (`planetResources` holds power and workforce only). (b) The band data (`GetResourceData`) answers no data. The client decodes it in the native module `_eveplanetresources` (`builder.CreateSHFromBuffer`, clientPlanet.py:736 and planetUISvc.py:673); its format is not in the snapshot and the native binaries are excluded (`inventories/resource-index.jsonl.gz` lists none). The client also cannot reach that call with this snapshot: `clientPlanet.CreateResourceInfo` unpacks `const.planetResourceProximityLimits[proximity]` into two values (clientPlanet.py:781), but `eve/common/lib/appConst.py:1580` defines it as five scalars (1139 to 1143), so the unpacking raises TypeError whenever Remote Sensing is trained and the planet is in range. The Remote Sensing and Planetology effects (range, the bands per skill, clientPlanet.py:751-786) are the client's and run before that line; the server takes no part in them. Upgrade path: the client's appConst values for the bands (pairs in the shipped build, or the source), then the native format from the client binary.
- **Not called by the client (no path to build).** Corporation colonies (planetSvc and clientPlanet use the character id throughout); obsolete extractors (group 1026, `ObsoletePinContainer`, containersByPinType.py:15): a CreatePin of such a type is refused as `InvalidPinType`.
- **Cuts.** POCO taxes with an owner (the orbital registry of a player's office, `planetOrbitalRegistryBroker` with a corporation: the structure track); `GetCommandPinsForPlanet` and `GetExtractorsForPlanet` answer the caller's own colony on the planet (the client asks for other owners' pins only in planet views). Planet blocklist (`IsPlanetTypeBlackListed`) not applied. Launchpad imports from the ship hold (the client's window moves only between the office and the launchpad).
- **GM planet calls, not served (2026-10-13).** The client calls `GMForceInstallProgram`, `GMConvertCommandCenter`, `GMAddCommodity` and `GMGetSynchedServerState` on the planet object (clientPlanet.py:639-700). The client shows them only to a session with ROLE_GML (planetUIPins.py:535 and 852, ProcessorContainer.py:271, planetNavigation.py:263). eve.rs has no GM role in its session or authority model, so there is no basis to authorize these calls and no handler is added. Upgrade path: a GM role in the session model, then the four handlers.

## Industry slice I4: rig and role bonuses, corporation recipients (2026-10-13)

- **Rig bonuses (new).** A fitted rig is an item in the structure's rig slots (flags 92 to 99 of the structure's container). Its effects' structure-domain PostPercent modifiers (SDE `dogmaEffects.modifierInfo`, operation 6) name the structure attribute they set (the target table in `IndustryBonuses.Targets`) and the rig attribute that gives the percentage (2593 time, 2594 material, 2595 cost, 2653, 2713, 2714). The amount is 1 + percentage. The security-scaled percentages are multiplied by the system's band attribute of the rig (2355 high at security 0.45 and up, 2356 low above 0, 2357 null), as the SDE's security effect (6842) and EveJS do. The target table (which ship classes, categories and structure groups each attribute reaches) is EveJS's list, with each group's category checked against the SDE. Basis: the SDE's effect modifiers and attributes; the ship classes are EVE's wiki classes. Correct the table there if the wiki differs.
- **Role (hull) bonuses (new).** The structure type's own attributes: an engineering complex's material (2600, manufacturing), cost (2601) and time (2602) on the five industry activities; a refinery's reaction time (2721). The value is the amount itself (Raitaru 0.99, 0.97, 0.85; Athanor 0.75), from the SDE.
- **Stacking.** Each applicable bonus multiplies; no stacking penalty is applied. Basis: the client's `industry/job.py` multiplies every modifier and has no penalty. Upgrade path: none unless the client shows one.
- **Where they apply.** The material bonus multiplies the efficiency modifier before the per-run rounding. The time bonus is one more duration factor (rounded once, as the other factors). The cost bonus multiplies the system cost index part only; tax, SCC and security tax are unchanged (the client multiplies its job cost by the system and facility modifiers, and taxes apply to the base cost).
- **Product context.** Manufacturing and reaction bonuses read the product's category and group from the SDE (`types.groupID`, `groups.categoryID`). Science bonuses (research, copying, invention) are activity-level.
- **Client payload.** The structure's facility payload lists the modifiers in the client's activity tuples (amount, categoryID, groupID, invTypeID, reference: 11 rig, 12 hull, the client's `industry.Reference`) and `rigModifiers` (rig type to activities). NPC stations carry no rigs and are unchanged.
- **Not verified.** A player fitting a rig to a structure (the client's path that places items in slots 92 to 99) is not walked, and no live walk with a fitted rig was run (no client session). The server reads any item in those slots.
- **Tests.** `IndustryRulesTests` (+4, pure): rig targets and percentage, the security band, the hull bonuses, category targets and the rig slot flags. `IndustryJobPersistenceTests` (+2, PostgreSQL on eve_p4): a group-23 rig and a hull bonus change the job's materials (86 to 85) and duration (600 to 408 s); a rig for another group changes nothing.
- **Corporation job recipients (changed).** Previously the Factory Manager role only (1024). Now the owner, the installer, and the online members whose corporation roles let them see corporation blueprints and jobs: Factory Manager (1024), Rent Factory Slot and Rent Research Slot (appConst.py 358 and 359). Basis: the client's `CanSeeCorpBlueprints` (blueprintSvc.py:165) gives exactly these roles the corporation view. A member without them has no job view and receives nothing. EveJS sends to every member; that wider list is not copied, because the client gives the others no view of the job.

## Structure ST-8 (2026-10-15)

- **Structure burst bubble (Standup Warp Disruption Burst Projector 47073).** EveJS creates a persistent warp disruption bubble (`warpDisruptSphere`). Here each pulse holds the ships the Host grants in its reach for the cycle (a scramble of the module's own strength); no bubble object is placed. Source: EveJS `superweaponRuntime.js` (structure burst table) for the family, the SDE for the numbers. Upgrade when the bubble has a deployable body in the structure path.
- **Structure guided bomb blast authorization.** The blast waits for the Host's per-detonation answer (Kind 85/86, the launching cycle's correlation). An answer that is not received within 30 seconds of scene time, or one for a lease that is no longer the controller's, reaches no one and the bomb is taken out. EVE does not make a launched bomb depend on its launcher still running; here a launcher deactivated (or its controller released) before the blast leaves the blast without a grant. Source: the Host authorization contract (`handoff-ST8-wire.md`, fail closed on a missing or mismatched answer). EVE and EveJS let a bomb already launched go off without its launcher; that rule is not implemented for structures here and is not verified against the wiki.
- **Attacker of a structure's bomb.** A controlled structure has no pilot record of its own (its controller's retained hull is the pilot record). The structure's blast is attributed to the structure id and its messages go to the controller session (`damage_ship_from_structure`); the controller's retained hull is not a target of its own structure's blast.
- **Repeat budget of a structure module.** The Host records the client's requested repeat on the running module. A module that cannot autorepeat (`disallowRepeatingActivation`) has one cycle as its budget, enforced by `ShipEffect.Build`; the structure path no longer refuses on the client's repeat (it refused every such module requested with a repeat above 1).
- **Ship-fitted burst projectors.** The ship-fitted variants (27678, 40635, 40696-40699, 40636, 41350) share effect names with the structure families and have no ship body here; they stay refused (`ModuleEffectsTests` lists the names as ship gaps).


## ST-7 structure services: cynosural jammer, structure beacon, fuel and owner notices (2026-10-15)

- Cynosural Suppression upgrade (E-03). EveJS isCynoSuppressionUpgradeOnline (sovSuppressionState.js) needs the upgrade power state 2, which is
  computed from the hub budget (sovModernState.js computeHubUpgradeSnapshot: local power and workforce against the upgrade requirements). The repo
  has no upgrade definitions and no budget, so the read model takes the stored online flag of the upgrade 81619 in the system hub configuration
  (hubUpgradeConfiguration). Shortcut: a suppression upgrade that is online but short of power still lights the jammer; upgrade with the sov upgrade budget.
- Jammer state of a system (E-03) is null unless the jammer service is online and the hub upgrade is online (EveJS getCynoJammerActivationTimeMs).
  The E-15 notice is sent on structure service changes only. A sov change of the upgrade alone does not announce the new jammer state yet (open).
- Structure cyno beacon jump (structureCynoBeaconMgr, CmdJumpToStructureBeacon). The checks and their order follow EveJS
  structureCynoBeaconMgrService.js resolveUsableBeacon. The jump itself differs from EveJS: EveJS moves the session with jumpSessionToSolarSystem and
  a 30 s structure tether delay, with no spool and no fatigue, while eve.rs routes the jump through the capital jump drive
  (SpaceJumpDrive.JumpToStructureBeaconAsync): the SDE drive range, fuel and skills, the spool, the fatigue and the high-security refusal (wiki
  Capital ships and Jump Drive). Kept from commit 7335699; the wording of the high-security and fatigue refusals is ours (the wiki gives none).
- Structure beacon list RPC: EveJS v0.12.9 structureCynoBeaconMgrService has no list method (its Handle_ methods are CmdJumpToStructureBeacon,
  CmdGroupJumpToStructureBeacon and CmdBridgeToStructureBeacon only; docs/reference/spec/services.tsv agrees). Nothing to build for a list.
- Group jumps and bridges to a structure beacon are refused by name (StructureBeaconTravel); EveJS has them (conduit and bridge settlement). Open
  under the conduit and bridge rows of the jump drive track.
- Fuel burn owner notices (EveJS createStructureFuelAlertNotifications: type 181, group Structures, all characters of the owner corporation, data
  structureID, structureShowInfoData, solarsystemID, structureTypeID, listOfTypesAndQty). eve.rs: an owner corporation member online outside the
  structure gets OnItemChange for the burnt fuel blocks; a corporation member already in the structure gets it from the existing structure fuel bay
  notice; when no member of the owner corporation is online the alert is written to the notifications mailbox in the burn transaction with the fuel left
  in the bay per type. The alert is written at every burn, as EveJS does after each cycle; no threshold is applied.
- Corporation-office compression source (BUILT, 2026-10-15). EveJS itemIsInStructureCompressionSource accepts a corporation hangar item (owner: the pilot corporation, flag in the division range, location: the structure) when the corporation has an office rented at that station (getCorporationOfficeByInventoryID with the station check, industryAccess.js) and the pilot may take from the division (canTakeFromOwnerLocation). eve.rs: StructureCompressionAuthority.SourceItemAsync reads the active office rental of the corporation at the structure (CorporationOfficeStore rows keyed by corporation and station id, in the same transaction) and the take rule of the inventory (HostInventoryServiceFactory.HangarAccessOf, which is CorporationRoles.HasHangarAccess with the take flag). Shortcut: the members told of the change are the corporation members online with a session who may query the division; EveJS tells the members watching the division (corporationOfficeInventoryObservers.js), and eve.rs has no open-hangar registry. No mailbox notice is written: EveJS has no notification type for a compression result (notificationConstants.js).
- Structure corporation offices: built in ST-12 (see "Structure corporation offices (ST-12, 2026-10-15)"). The compression source reads the structure's office row.
- Corporation division flags: ItemFlags.CorpSAG1 to CorpSAG7 were 116 to 122. The client has flagCorpSAG1 = 115 (eve-logic inventorycommon/const.py), EveJS ITEM_FLAG_CORP_HANGAR_1 = 115, and CorporationRoles.HasHangarAccess already used 115 to 121. The constants are now 115 to 121.

## Sovereignty Hub ball (ST-9 a, 2026-10-15)
- **The hub is a structure ball from the sovereignty records.** A system's Sovereignty Hub (SDE type 32458, group 1012 "Sovereignty Hub") is put in the simulation (`PlaceStructure`) when a pilot comes into the system (`SpaceSessions.SystemEntered`), from the `sovereignty` read model: the system's `infrastructureHubID`, whose structure row names type 32458 (`SpaceSovHubs.HubOf`). Basis: EVE.js `sovHubPlacement.js` and `sovereignty/sovState.js` (the hub is the structure with `typeID` 32458 whose id is `infrastructureHubID`), and the wiki Sovereignty article ("Sovereignty Hub"). Nothing targets it yet.
- **Position: the system's origin.** EVE.js places a hub at the sun's warp-in point plus 100 km (`getDefaultHubPosition`), which needs the celestial data the host does not hold here. The origin is the ESS ball's placement (`EssRules.BallX/Y/Z`). Upgrade path: the sun warp-in point from the static data. Status: open, not verified against the client.
- **Radius 45 000 m.** The hub's typeDogma has no radius attribute (162); the SDE `types.radius` of 32458 is 45 000 (pinned build 3396210). Health: the static data's points (263, 265, 9), zero for this hub, as the ESS ball has. The hub has no hit model in the simulation: its only interaction is the entosis link (ST-9 b).
- **State shown: ShieldVulnerable, full power.** The ESS ball's status, reused. The client's state for a sovereignty hub is not checked (MITM open).
- **No creator of sovereignty hubs exists in eve.rs.** The sovereignty table gets hubs only from data (the claim/anchor path is not built), so the ball appears only where the records hold a hub. Tests seed the read-model snapshot.
- **Not updated on claim or drop.** A hub that changes owner while the system has no pilot is placed again on the next entry; while pilots are in the system the ball keeps the owner it was placed with. Upgrade path: a re-place on the sovereignty write (ST-9 d).

## Entosis link: the body and its refusals (ST-9 b, 2026-10-15)
- **The link is a work cycle (protocol 70).** `SimAction` tag 31 carries the reach (attribute 54, 20 000 m for Entosis Link I). Each cycle's end parks the module and asks the host (command 14, `WORK_ENTOSIS`) with the same resend and ledger rules as the mining and scanner cycles (ADR 0013). Basis: EVE.js `space/modules/entosisLinkRuntime.js` `executeEntosisLinkCycle` (the cycle re-checks target, lock and range; the first completed cycle ends the warm-up and starts scoring).
- **Cycle and fuel.** 300 000 ms (SDE attribute 73). Strontium Clathrates (consumptionType 713 = 16275) per cycle through the shared fuel mechanism (`BurnsFuel`), as the SDE and the Dogma rules give it.
- **Not offensive.** The effect's `isOffensive` is false (SDE 6063, EVE.js comment): no Crimewatch aggression on activation.
- **Target and alliance are checked by the host before the package, in this order.** The target must be the system's sovereignty hub (`ENTOSIS_TARGET_NOT_SOV_HUB`, named; the host callback `SpaceSessions.IsSovereigntyHubAsync` reads the sovereignty records, `SpaceSovHubs.HubOf`), then the pilot's alliance (`ALLIANCE_MEMBERSHIP_REQUIRED`). Basis: EVE.js `resolveEntosisLinkActivation` (`ENTOSIS_TARGET_NOT_CAPTURABLE` before `ALLIANCE_MEMBERSHIP_REQUIRED`). The texts of both refusals are ours: no client string for them is verified (MITM open).
- **The simulation checks lock and reach** with the reference's names: `TARGET_REQUIRED`, `TARGET_NOT_FOUND` (not a structure in the scene), `TARGET_NOT_LOCKED`, `TARGET_OUT_OF_RANGE` (surface distance against the reach plus one metre, as the scanner and tractor do). A structure's surface distance is measured to the ball's radius (`scene/structures`).
- **Refusal when the host does not settle the cycle.** Until the capture state exists (ST-9 c) the command 14 cycle is refused `ENTOSIS_CAPTURE_NOT_SETTLED`, so the link ends with the host's reason after its first cycle. A named refusal, not a success: no capture is counted.
- **Gap manifest.** `entosisLink` left `currentSdeGaps` in `ModuleEffectsTests`; its body is covered by `An_entosis_link_packages_a_300_second_cycle_of_20_km_on_its_target` and the refusal test there, and by the Rust tests `modules::tests::an_entosis_link_*` and `a_refused_entosis_cycle_*`.
- **Retired refusal.** `STRUCTURES_PHASE_3` (the entosis link's blocker) is gone from `StructureModuleEffects` and from `ModuleRefusals`; nothing else used it.
- **Not verified.** The client's FX and the link's cycle display (MITM). The fuel check in a live run.

## Structure corporation offices (ST-12, 2026-10-15)
Sources: SDE build 3396210 (type 35893 "Corporation Offices", group 1717; type 26 "Office Folder"); EVE University wiki "Upwell structure" (services "always available" include "Corporation offices") and "Asset Safety" (un-renting a corporate office activates corporate asset safety); EveJS `828823a75f211fdecb945b087a26c225f111491f` `services/corporation/officeManagerService.js`, `officeRentalBilling.js`, `officeRentalSettlementState.js`, `services/structure/structureConstants.js` (ONLINE_BY_DEFAULT, SERVICE_ACCESS_SETTING_BY_ID), `structureProfilesState.js`.

- **The Corporation Offices service needs no module and no fuel.** The brief for ST-12 asked for the office service to be online and paid through the ST-7 fuel. The wiki lists "Corporation offices" among the services available without a service module, and the SDE type 35893 has no service fuel attribute (2109/2110), so there is no fuel rule to read. EveJS treats OFFICES as online by default and in its always-online list. eve.rs reports service 3 online in every structure's legacy shape (`StructureStore.ToLegacy`, `serviceStates`) without a stored row, so the store, the fuel-out and the fuel rule table do not change for it: it never takes fuel and a fuel-out never takes it offline (EveJS: always-online list). Status: built as the sources give it.
- **Access is profile setting 20 (Corporation Office Rent).** `SettingIdForService(3)` is 20, so the directory admits a character by the profile's access groups for that setting, with the owner corporation as the default group (EveJS SERVICE_ACCESS_SETTING_BY_ID; CORP_RENT_OFFICE is a dock-kind setting). Before ST-12 the office service had no setting and was denied to every character.
- **The rent is the profile value that the renter's access resolves to.** EveJS `getProfileSettingValueForStructure` with the session. No matching group gives 0. A rent of 0 is accepted (EveJS normalises to 0 or more); migration 010 relaxes `rent_isk` and `amount_isk` to `>= 0` for the office tables. The station path keeps its 10 000 ISK minimum.
- **The period is 30 days** (EveJS OFFICE_RENTAL_PERIOD_DAYS = 30, the same as `CorporationOfficeStore.RentalCycleMs`). The wiki pages read did not state a period.
- **No office slots at a structure.** EveJS returns no office count for a structure session and checks no capacity; the station limit of 24 does not apply.
- **Rent and credit.** The renting corporation is debited and the owner corporation credited in the RPC transaction (`office-rent:{officeId}:charge` and `:credit`, reason "Corporation office rental", reference type 13), after the office folder item and the office row are written. A bill names its creditor (`creditor_id`); a payment of a structure bill credits the creditor as well (EveJS creditRentalOwner). The idempotency keys make a replay of the RPC or the job a no-op.
- **Renewal reprices from the structure profile (ST-13).** The renewal bill takes the structure profile's Corporation Office Rent value of group 0 at the moment it is opened (EveJS `processPaidOfficeRentalBill`, `getProfileSettingValueForStructure` with no session). No such group: the paid bill's rent stays (EveJS `defaultValue`). Set for auto-pay and the manual payment through `CorporationOfficeStore.RenewalRentSource`. Station offices keep the paid rent (no change here).
- **Due job (`office.rent_due`, key `office:{officeId}`).** It runs at the open bill's due time. The bill is paid by auto-pay when the corporation's rental-bill setting (type 2) is on and the funds are in the division; the paid bill renews the office and the job waits for the next due time. An unpaid bill (auto-pay off, or short of funds) gives the office up at due time, with no grace period: the corporation's items at the structure go to a corporate asset safety wrap (the same wrap as the asset-safety trigger, `WrapItemsAsync`), the office ends and its open bills become cancelled (status 2). EveJS `processUnpaidOfficeRentalBill` does the same for a structure (`removeStructureOfficeForUnpaidBill`). A refused wrap is tried again after one hour. The grace period is not verified against CCP; EveJS has none in this path.
- **Un-rent by hand** is a Director or CEO action (EveJS ensureCanUnrentOffice, IsCeoOrDirector), and it also puts the items into the corporate wrap. A structure office is never impounded: the impound calls (HasCorpImpoundedItems, GetImpoundReleasePrice, GetItemsFromImpound, TrashImpoundedOffice) answer as a structure without impounded items (EveJS: impound is station-only).
- **Refusals.** `StructureCorpOfficesDenied` (EveJS user error name) when the profile access does not admit the renter; `CORPORATION_ACCESS_DENIED` for a missing RentOffice role or, for the un-rent, a non-Director; `STRUCTURE_ASSET_SAFETY_UNAVAILABLE` when the un-rent's wrap is refused (the office is kept). Insufficient funds raises the wallet's `InsufficientFundsException`, the same as the station rent.
- **The office hangar is the compression source.** The office folder (type 26) is the corporation's item at the structure. The corporation hangar items at the structure with the division flags are the office's hangar, and the ST-7 compression source accepts them while the office row exists (test `A_rented_office_hangar_at_a_structure...`).
- **Open (not built, or not verified):**
  - Station owner credit: the station rent settlement debits the renter and does not credit the station owner. This is the behaviour before ST-12, kept; it is a finding for the station path.
  - Station offices have no due or impound job: an unpaid station bill stays open. EveJS impounds the station office.
  - Notices to the corporation (EveJS notifyOfficeRentItemChange, notifyOfficeRentalChange, the bill refresh after RentOffice and UnrentOffice). Not built.
  - Destruction loot for the items of a destroyed structure with no asset safety destination (EveJS `handleStructureDestroyedLoot`). Not built: a refused wrap keeps the office and is retried (ST-13).
  - The structure's corporate division listing for members at the structure, and the client's office window. Not run on the graphical client.

## Structure corporation offices (ST-13, 2026-10-15)
Sources: EveJS `services/corporation/officeRentalBilling.js` (`getCurrentOfficeRentalCost`, `processPaidOfficeRentalBill`, `removeStructureOfficeForUnpaidBill`), `services/structure/structureAssetSafetyState.js` (`handleStructureDestroyed`), `services/structure/structureProfilesState.js` (`getProfileSettingValueForStructure`, `resolveProfileSettingGroup`) at `828823a75f211fdecb945b087a26c225f111491f`.

- **Renewal reprices from the structure profile.** The renewal bill takes the structure profile's Corporation Office Rent value of group 0 at the moment it is opened (EveJS `processPaidOfficeRentalBill` calls `getProfileSettingValueForStructure` with no session, which resolves group 0). No such group: the paid bill's rent stays (EveJS `defaultValue`). Set for auto-pay and for the manual payment through `CorporationOfficeStore.RenewalRentSource`, which the Host sets. A station office keeps its paid rent (its source is not a structure).
- **A destroyed or unanchored structure closes its offices.** The structure's state change (removal `destroyed` or `unanchored`) makes each office's rent job due at once, in the change's transaction; the job runs right after the commit. The job gives the office up with no due check and no auto-pay: its open bills are cancelled (status 2), the corporation's items at the structure go to a corporate asset safety wrap, and the office row is removed. The wrap reads the destroyed source (`includeDestroyed`; the un-rent path reads the same rule as before). A refused wrap keeps the office and is retried after one hour.
- **Differences.** EveJS hands a destroyed structure's items to asset safety and what that refuses to the destruction loot. eve.rs has no destruction loot (the unanchor drop is its own path), so a refused wrap is retried instead of dropped.
- **Mobile Observatory rules:** see the ST-E2 section.


## Entosis capture state (ST-9 c, 2026-10-15)
- **Where the capture lives.** Its own PostgreSQL tables (`entosis.hubs`, `entosis.links`, migration `0001_entosis_capture.sql`, `EntosisCaptureService`), not the `sovereignty` table: that table is written as a whole under its write gate, and a capture moves at every cycle. The sovereignty records change only when a capture completes (ST-9 d). EVE.js keeps the capture in the sovereignty state; the store is the difference.
- **Rates (wiki, with EVE.js for the base time).** The attacker's progress rises at 1 / (595 s × ADM): the base time 595 s is EVE.js's `OWNED_HUB_CAPTURE_TIME_SECONDS` and the wiki's "about 10 minutes". The defender's link with an attacker pauses the capture (both links: no change), and a hub with no attacker regenerates toward uncaptured over 50 minutes (the wiki's Regeneration table, Sov Hub). A defender alone therefore reverses a capture; an uncontested hub regenerates too (the wiki: "eventually the defending side will win").
- **One attacker link is enough.** More attacker links do not speed the capture (wiki; EVE.js `beginOwnedHubEntosisScoring`).
- **The first cycle is the warm-up.** The link is recorded when its first cycle ends; scoring counts from then (EVE.js `executeEntosisLinkCycle`: the first completed cycle ends the warm-up).
- **ADM.** A neutral hub has no owner and is not scaled (ADM 1): EVE.js's neutral capture time is the flat 595 s (`sovCaptureState.js`). An owned hub's ADM is `StructureAnchorAuthority.ActivityDefenseMultiplier` of its system's indices (1 to 6), fixed when the capture first moves: the wiki says the ADM is the one of the initial reinforcement. Without indices the ADM is 1.
- **A link counts while it cycles.** A link is active when its last settled cycle is within two cycles (600 s) of the time checked. A link whose module ends sends its end report and stops at once. One whose end report is lost (a host restart, a lost message) stops counting after two cycles and is removed at the next event. Between events the rate is the one of the links at the last checkpoint; a live link checkpoints at every cycle (300 s), so the error from a lost report is bounded by one cycle. Not in EVE.js: its link state is in memory.
- **Restart.** Every event is one PostgreSQL transaction (hub row locked `FOR UPDATE`). The completion is a durable job (`space.entosis.capture-due`, keyed by the hub, replaced at each event, cancelled when no attacker remains). A restart reads the stored progress (test `A_link_warms_up_...`, a new service over the same tables).
- **Completion.** At progress 1 the hub is `captured` and the next cycle is refused `ENTOSIS_CAPTURE_COMPLETE` (named). What the completion does to the sovereignty records is ST-9 (d).
- **Not built (open gaps).** (1) The vulnerability window: the wiki says an attacker must link during the window; eve.rs has no sovereignty window yet, so a link is accepted at any time. (2) Command nodes: the owned hub's reinforcement and the command-node event are out of scope; an owned hub that reaches progress 1 is recorded `captured` and its owner is not changed. (3) The owner's notice that a capture started (EVE.js `notifySovCaptureStarted`).
- **Refusal reasons.** `ENTOSIS_NOT_WIRED` (no capture service; the host always wires one), `ENTOSIS_TARGET_NOT_SOV_HUB` (the cycle's hub is not the system's hub at the time of the cycle), `ALLIANCE_MEMBERSHIP_REQUIRED` (the pilot's session has no alliance at the cycle), `NO_PILOT` (not in the system). Text of the refusals on the client is not verified.
- **Tests.** `EntosisCaptureTests` (6, pure: base time, ADM scaling, pause, regeneration, stale links, completion time) and `EntosisCaptureServiceTests` (4, PostgreSQL: warm-up, completion job, pause and replay, link end, refusal of another structure), `EntosisCycleHostTests` (2, the rig's containers and the fake simulation: a cycle moves the capture, the module's end report stops it, a pilot outside an alliance is refused).

## Entosis handover of a captured Sovereignty Hub (ST-9 d, 2026-10-15)
- **A neutral hub changes hands at its capture.** When a neutral hub's capture completes, the sovereignty records give the system to the capturing alliance: the system's owner (`corporationID`, `allianceID`) and claim time, the hub's structure row and the hub record (`PersistenceSovFwWriteOperations.ClaimCapturedHubAsync`). Basis: the wiki ("a new hub ... has to be claimed with an entosis link"), and EVE.js `commitInitialSovereigntyHubCapture` (`sovState.js`: the winner alliance, the executor corporation, the claim time).
- **The corporation is the alliance's executor.** `ActiveExecutorId` of the capturing alliance (EVE.js commits `executorCorporationID`); the capturing pilot's corporation stands in only when the alliance has none. The captured corporation is kept on the capture for that.
- **The claim time is now** (FILETIME). EveJS uses the capture receipt's claim time (or the prepared time); the receipt is not built here.
- **Through the sovereignty write path.** The claim is written under the sovereignty table's write gate, flushed and verified in the persisted table, like the other claims. A claim this alliance already holds is answered as done (a repeat after a lost answer). A system that another alliance holds, or whose infrastructure hub is another item, is refused: the capture is recorded `superseded` and nothing changes hands.
- **Retry.** A failed write leaves the handover `pending`; a durable job (`space.entosis.handover-due`) tries again a minute later (the one minute is ours: no source gives a retry time). Completion and handover are separate transactions: a crash between them is answered by the next try, and the claim of the same alliance is idempotent.
- **The ball follows the owner.** After the claim the simulation's hub ball is removed and put back (`SpaceSovHubs.ReplaceAsync`), so the capturer is its owner in space too.
- **Owned hubs.** A capture of an owned hub completes as `captured` with the handover `owned`: nothing changes hands. Its reinforcement (48 hours) and the command-node event decide the owner in the game; they are out of scope (the command nodes are not built), so this is a gap, not a result.
- **Not verified.** The owner's notice of the handover, the alliance's first-hour Skyhook rule (EVE.js `ALLOWED_DURATION_AFTER_SOVHUB_CLAIM_SECONDS`), and the client's view of the new owner (MITM open).
- **Tests.** `SovHubClaimTests` (3, Host, in-memory tables: the claim writes the system, the row and the hub; a repeat is done; another owner is refused; another item is refused), `EntosisCaptureServiceTests` (4 more, PostgreSQL: a neutral hub is claimed by the capturer; another alliance first gives `superseded`; an owned hub gives `owned` with no call; a failed write stays `pending` and the retry job writes it), `SpaceSovHubsTests` (1 more: the replaced ball is taken out and put back with its new owner).

## Sovereignty vulnerability window, reinforcement and owner notices (ST-9 e, 2026-10-15)
- **The window.** The wiki ("Vulnerability window", "ADM scaling"): an alliance's structures are vulnerable in a daily window; its default length is 18 hours divided by the activity defense multiplier (18 h at ADM 1, 3 h at ADM 6). The wiki gives no start. eve.rs centres it on the owner's current prime hour (`AlliancePrimeTimeAsync`), as EVE.js `buildDailyVulnerabilityWindow` does, with EVE.js's one-hour minimum. A time is inside a window when it is inside the owner's window of the day before, the day or the day after (a window crosses midnight). Code: `EntosisWindow` (`EntosisCapture.cs`).
- **Entosis links need the window.** The wiki: "attackers must first use an entosis link on the structure during its vulnerability window". A cycle settled outside it is refused `SOVEREIGNTY_TARGET_NOT_VULNERABLE` (EVE.js name, `sovCampaignState.js` `beginOwnedHubEntosisScoring`), and nothing is stored. Difference: EVE.js checks at the end of the warm-up; eve.rs checks every settled cycle. A hub with no progress therefore stops at its first cycle outside the window; a capture in progress is handled by the next rule.
- **A partly captured hub stays vulnerable.** The wiki: "if a structure is partly captured when the window closes, it will remain vulnerable until it is either reinforced or successfully defended". `EntosisRules.IsVulnerable` keeps a hub with progress open until its progress regenerates to zero.
- **A neutral hub has no window.** The user's rule: "for a neutral/unowned hub no window". The wiki does not say; EVE.js's neutral capture (`sovCaptureState.js`) has no window check either.
- **Not built (open).** (1) The wiki's lock: a day's length is fixed at the midpoint of the window the day before. eve.rs uses the activity defense multiplier it has when the cycle settles, so a change in the indices moves today's window. (2) Custom windows and inheritance: an owner sets a window for a structure and the change applies after the next window (wiki); EVE.js has a GM tool only (`setHubVulnerabilityWindow`). (3) The structure timers of ST-2 are not the sovereignty window.
- **Reinforcement after an owned capture.** At progress 1 an owned hub is `reinforced` (`hubs.phase`). The wiki: the structure "stays reinforced for approximately 48 hours" and "comes out of reinforcement at a randomly selected time during the next vulnerability window". `EntosisWindow.ReinforcementExit` gives a time not before 48 hours, at a point of the first window that ends after that; EVE.js (`scheduleReinforcementExit`) draws it at random, eve.rs draws from the hub id and the completion time so that a replay gives the same time. The time is stored as `hubs.reinforcement_exit_ms` (migration `0002_entosis_reinforcement.sql`). Nothing consumes it: the command nodes, the wiki's next stage, are not built. A reinforced hub refuses further cycles by name (`SOVEREIGNTY_HUB_ALREADY_REINFORCED`, EVE.js name) and keeps its owner. Its handover is `owned`.
- **The owner's notices (V24.01 types).** eve-logic `notificationconst.py`: 147 `notificationTypeEntosisCaptureStarted` ("SovereigntyCaptureStarted") is written when the first attacker cycle of an idle owned hub settles (the end of the warm-up, as EVE.js `notifySovCaptureStarted`), with `structureTypeID` 32458 and `solarSystemID`. 160 `notificationTypeSovStructureReinforced` is written when an owned capture completes, with `decloakTime` (the exit, as a FILETIME), `campaignEventType` 2 (EVE.js `CAMPAIGN_EVENT_TYPE`) and `solarSystemID`. The sender is the defending alliance, the group is 6 (sovereignty, `groupSov`). The recipients are every character of the defending alliance's corporations (EVE.js `getDefenderAllianceRecipientIDs`, which EVE.js itself calls provisional: the TQ recipient rule is not verified). A neutral hub has no owner and writes no notice.
- **No notice for progress.** V24.01 names no notification type for a capture's progress (`notificationconst.py` has none), and EVE.js writes none. Nothing is written.
- **Commit before notice, idempotent on replay.** The capture publishes `space.entosis.capture_started` and `space.entosis.hub_reinforced` in its own transaction (`EventBus.PublishAsync` with the connection and transaction), so the event exists exactly when the state change does. The subscriber `EntosisOwnerNotifications` writes the notices through the mailbox in its own transaction together with its cursor; the idempotency key is the event id, so a redelivery writes nothing twice.
- **A claim ends the capture (corrects ST-9 d).** After the handover answer (claimed or superseded) the hub row returns to `idle` with progress 0 and its links are removed. ST-9 d left a captured neutral hub in `captured`, which refused every later cycle by name (`ENTOSIS_CAPTURE_COMPLETE`), even after its new owner's window opened. An idle hub takes the current activity multiplier, owner and prime hour when a capture starts; a capture in progress keeps the ones it started with.
- **Not verified.** The client's text and display of notices 147 and 160 (MITM open); the TQ recipient rule; the claim notices of a neutral hub's new owner (V24.01 43/44) are written since ST-9 e (next section).
- **Tests.** `EntosisCaptureTests` (3 more: the window, the neutral and partly captured rules, the reinforcement exit), `EntosisCaptureServiceTests` (outside the window refused and nothing stored; an owned completion becomes reinforced with its timer and the two notices, and the refusal after it; a neutral capture announces nothing; a claim and a superseded answer leave the hub idle), `EntosisOwnerNotificationTests` (Host: the members of the defending alliance get one notice of each type, a foreign pilot gets none, a cursor rewind writes nothing twice).


## Sovereignty claim notices of a captured neutral hub (ST-9 e, 2026-10-15)
- **Two notices, V24.01 types.** The capture's handover writes 43 (`notificationTypeSovAllClaimAquiredMsg`) and 44 (`notificationTypeSovCorpClaimAquiredMsg`) from eve-logic `notificationUtil.py`: both carry `corpID` and `allianceID`, and the client builds its links from those two ids.
- **Recipients are ours.** The client names no recipients and EVE.js writes no mail for a claim (its `sovNotifications.js` sends live session notices only). Chosen: 43 to every character of the capturing alliance, 44 to every character of the claiming (executor) corporation. Sender: the alliance for 43, the corporation for 44. Group 6, as 147 and 160 (eve-logic lists 43 and 44 in its `groupSov` list).
- **Written after the claim is persisted.** The notices follow the sovereignty write (`ClaimCapturedHubAsync`, verified in the persisted table) and the ball's replacement. A failure there leaves the handover `pending`, and the handover job tries again: the claim of the same alliance is answered as done, and the notices are keyed `sov-claim:{type}:hub:{hub}:claim:{claim time}` through the mailbox, so a retry writes nothing twice. The claim time is the one the records hold, so a later claim of the same hub is a new notice.
- **Not verified.** The client's text of 43/44 (MITM open); the recipient rule against the TQ client; the notice's appearance in the graphical client.
- **Tests.** `SovereigntyClaimNotificationTests` (1, PostgreSQL, Host: the recipients, the payload, the retry and a later claim); `SovHubClaimTests` (3) and `EntosisOwnerNotificationTests` (1) still pass with it.

## Structure ST-16 (2026-10-16): station rent, observatory landmarks, destruction loot, customs

Sources: EveJS `828823a75f211fdecb945b087a26c225f111491f` `services/corporation/officeRentalBilling.js` (`createNextOfficeRentalBill` creditor = station owner, `impoundStationOfficeForUnpaidBill`), `services/structure/structureDestructionLootState.js` (`handleStructureDestroyedLoot`), `services/structure/structureState.js` (the destruction branch), `services/security/contrabandInspectionRuntime.js` and `space/transitions.js` (`undockSession` 2543 and `jumpSessionViaStargate` 4230 call `inspectContrabandForTransition`); SDE build 3396210 (type 10167, attributes 162, 3101; group 365 holds control tower types). The wiki pages `Mobile_Observatory` and `Contraband` returned 404 to WebFetch in this session; `Deadspace` was read (no pocket radius is stated).

- **Station rent credits the station owner (fix).** The station RentOffice charge debited the corporation and named the owner only as counterparty, and no owner credit was written; bill settlement credited only bills that named a creditor. Now the rent and the impound release fee credit the station owner corporation (`office-rent:{id}:credit`, `office-impound-release:...:credit`), and a bill payment credits the creditor the caller resolves (BillMgr: the bill's creditor or the station owner). The owner's balance is opened when missing (`WalletTransactions.EnsureAsync`: the default and division accounts 1000 to 1006, as corporation creation does). Source: EveJS creditRentalOwner.
- **Station offices get the due job.** A station RentOffice names the station owner as the bill's creditor and schedules `office.rent_due` for the office (the same job as a structure office). At due time an unpaid bill (auto-pay off or short of funds) impounds the office, as EveJS impoundStationOfficeForUnpaidBill does: the corporation's hangar items move to the Impounded location, the office folder is deleted, the office ends and its open bills are cancelled (`StationOfficeImpound`, shared with the Director un-rent). The release fee (GetItemsFromImpound, half of the current rent) is unchanged. Not built: a station bill rented before this change has no creditor, so its job returns without action (no eviction, no notice); the migration to set those creditors is open.
- **Notices.** Both give-up paths (structure and station) write two notifications to the members of the debtor corporation through `PersistenceTransactionMailbox.WriteNotificationsAsync`, sender = the creditor: type 10 (EveJS `CORP_ALL_BILL`), group 2 (Bills), outcome `rent-due` (the bill fell due unpaid) and `evicted`. EveJS sends no eviction notice for an impound (only the client office message `notifyOfficeRentalChange`), so the `evicted` notice reuses the bill type; this is a choice, not a copied type. Written after the commit under an idempotency key: a crash between the commit and the notice loses the notice, not the impound. No pre-due reminder is written (EveJS sends its bill-issued notice at issue; eve.rs issues the bill at rent without a notice).
- **Control towers: 100 km (wiki) instead of 1 000 km.** The Mobile Observatory refuses a drop within 100 km of a control tower (SDE group 365, surface to surface, `ObservatoryControlTowerSpacingMeters`). The landmark source is the structure store: the non-destroyed structure records of group 365 in the system (`ObservatoryLandmarkSource.ReadControlTowersAsync`); the 1 000 km list excludes group 365. Gap: eve.rs has no control tower deployment, so the rule is applied to the records the store holds (seeded or imported). The minimal deployable kind the brief asks for is not added: a control tower in space needs a sim ball kind, a protocol change and a deploy RPC, which this package did not reach. The radii are not subtracted (shortcut in code).
- **Deadspace (built, ST-16 A).** The Mobile Observatory is refused an anchor within 1e9 m of an ST-11 cosmic site's position (`SpaceContainers.InDeadspaceAsync`, `ReadDeadspaceAnchors` from `CosmicSiteDirector.ActiveAsync`). The radius is EveJS `services/dungeon/deadspaceWarpPolicy.js` `DEADSPACE_SITE_RADIUS_METERS`, the sphere around a dungeon instance's anchor that EveJS takes as the deadspace pocket ("a parity shortcut" in its comment). The wiki (`Deadspace`) says a deadspace pocket is a single grid of unstated size, and that structures that need anchoring "can be launched, but not anchored" there; the SDE has no pocket radius. Scope: every ST-11 site counts (anomaly and signature sites), as EveJS counts every non-landmark, non-static dungeon instance; the mission pockets of `Eve.Dungeons` are not positioned in the simulation, so they are not checked (open). Test: `An_observatory_is_not_anchored_in_a_deadspace_pocket...`.
- **Destruction loot, asset safety first (ST-16 B).** In the destruction transaction the rigs are destroyed; then each owner's items at the structure are offered to asset safety through the existing wrap (`PersistenceStructureAssetSafetyReadModel.WrapDestroyedOwnerAsync`, the office path's `WrapItemsAsync` with `includeDestroyed`), per owner in id order, as EVE.js handleStructureDestroyed does. Whatever stays at the structure goes to one Abandoned Container (SDE type 10167, group 306, owner = the structure's corporation, at its position, marker `structure-destruction-loot:{id}`), with the Quantum Core (the installed core's item if it is still at the structure, else created from SDE attribute 3101). The container is placed in space after the commit by the unanchor placement.
  - Wrap refused or not applied: EveJS asset safety is disabled for FLEX hulls and in wormhole and Pochven systems (`isAssetSafetyDisabledSolarSystem`, the `IsStandardUpwell` and `IsWormholeOrPochven` checks of `WrapItemsAsync`), and EveJS then loots the contents. A wrap that is refused for another reason (a missing structure record in the read model) is looted here; EveJS leaves those contents at the destroyed structure. Recorded difference: eve.rs loses no items to the destruction, at the cost of looting them into a public container.
  - Owner kind: the character range 140000000 to 199999999 is a character wrap, others a corporation wrap (EveJS `getOwnerKindForAssetSafety`). A character wrap carries the structure's corporation in its `corporation_id` column, because the destruction has no session corporation (the column is not read for a character wrap).
  - Test: the wrap delegate is a fake in the Host test (`Destruction_wraps_each_owner_first...`); the real wrap through the read model is wired in `CharacterServicesComposition` and is not covered by a test (open).
  - Office items: a corporation with an office gets its items wrapped by this path first; the ST-13 closure then finds nothing to move (it still closes the office).
- **Customs fines and confiscation (built, ST-16 C).** EveJS runs `inspectCharacterContraband` on every undock (`undockSession`, the station and the structure) and on every stargate jump (`jumpSessionViaStargate`, at the destination system). eve.rs runs the penalty at the same points: `UndockFromAsync` after the warning check (a refused warning never reaches the penalty; an ignored one does), and `SpaceSessions.JumpAsync` after the simulation accepts the jump, in the destination system. The rules are the SDE `contrabandTypes`: per type and faction, `confiscateMinSec` (the system's security must reach it) and `fineByValue`. The fine is quantity x SDE base price (`types.basePrice`) x `fineByValue`, rounded to the cent, and is charged to the pilot's wallet up to its balance (EveJS `chargedFine = min(balance, total)`; the rest is reported unpaid, not collected). The contraband items leave the hold (EveJS moves them to void: `CONTRABAND_CONFISCATE`). The fine is burnt (no payee, as the gate toll is). Each removed item is told to the client as `OnItemChange` with the junk location, as a merge is.
  - **Not applied.** (1) The inspection roll: the smuggling chance (attribute 445 from implants and skills, `resolveCharacterSmugglingChance`) is not read, so the contraband is always detected. (2) Standing loss: eve.rs has no standing system to change (no `applyStandingChanges` counterpart). (3) The penalty is settled in the same transaction, not by EveJS's durable `contraband.penalty-settle` job. (4) The fine's journal ref type is 0 (the static data names none; EveJS's wallet call names none in the penalty path that was read). (5) The balance is read before the transaction; a balance that falls in between makes the debit fail and the undock or jump abort (rare; not retried).
  - Not verified: the client's presentation of the removed items and the wallet line; the wiki pages `Contraband` and `Mobile_Observatory` returned 404 to WebFetch in this session, so the mechanics are EveJS's with the SDE numbers.
  - Test: `Penalty_charges_the_fine_to_the_wallet_and_removes_the_matching_contraband_once` (2 units x 100 ISK x 0.1 = 20 ISK charged, the other type untouched, no second fine).
- **Customs fines and confiscation: not built.** The EveJS rule runs `inspectCharacterContraband` on undock (`undockSession`, which covers the station and the structure) and on a stargate jump. Its inputs are the system's faction and security (`getSystemInspectionAuthority`), the faction rule table (`CONTRABAND_TYPE_RULES`: attack, confiscate and fine thresholds), the smuggling skill (attribute 445) and an inspection roll; the fine is quantity times base price times the rule's fine factor and is settled by the durable `contraband.penalty-settle` job, and confiscation is the custody reason `contraband-confiscate`. eve.rs has the SDE `contrabandTypes` warning and confirmation at undock (`UndockContraband`, ST-3), but none of the inspection, fine, penalty job or custody. The wiki page could not be read here, so the table is taken from EveJS alone (the source order puts the wiki first). Open: a follow-up package (see the ST-16 handoff).


## Sovereignty vulnerability window: custom windows, inheritance, the midpoint lock (ST-17 b, 2026-10-16)
- **Sources.** The wiki (EVE University "Sovereignty", read 2026-10-16): the default length is 18 hours divided by the ADM; the alliance "can choose to change the window of any structure ... to a custom value"; "Newly deployed or captured structures will inherit this setting from the alliance that controls it"; "A change ... does not apply immediately: it takes effect after the next vulnerability window"; "The length of each day's vulnerability window is locked in at the midpoint of the vulnerability window the day before". EVE.js `sovCampaignState.js` (`lockNextWindowIfDue`, `resolveWindowLength`, `rollToNextDailyWindow`, `configureSovereigntyVulnerability`) at `828823a75f211fdecb945b087a26c225f111491f`.
- **The custom window's form is ours.** A centre hour (UTC, 0 to 23) and a whole length in hours (1 to 18), per hub (`entosis.hub_windows`). The wiki gives no form; EVE.js has only a GM tool with a start and an end.
- **Inheritance is ours.** An alliance's default window (`entosis.alliance_windows`, `SetAllianceWindowAsync`) is copied to a neutral hub when its capture hands the hub to that alliance (`RunHandoverAsync`, at once, the old window and its locks are removed). An owned hub keeps its own window. The wiki does not name where an alliance keeps the setting.
- **Timing.** A change made at time t is pending with the end of the first window that ends after t (`EntosisWindow.NextWindowEnd`); the first evaluation at or after that time makes it the active setting (the wiki's "after the next vulnerability window").
- **The midpoint lock.** The length of the window centred on c is locked by the first evaluation at or after c minus 24 hours (the previous window's midpoint), from the setting and the ADM at that evaluation (EVE.js takes the lock at the first evaluation after the current midpoint, the same rule). Locks are rows in `entosis.window_locks`, pruned after two days. Difference: eve.rs evaluates settled cycles only, so a day nobody evaluates is locked at the first later evaluation (EVE.js has the same shape, by its engine ticks).
- **Refusals.** `SOVEREIGNTY_HUB_NOT_OWNED` (ours: a neutral hub has no owner to set a window), `SOVEREIGNTY_VULNERABILITY_WINDOW_INVALID` (EVE.js name, for a setting out of range), `ENTOSIS_TARGET_NOT_SOV_HUB` (not the system's hub). A refused cycle stores nothing: the due change and the locks are written only for an accepted cycle.
- **The reinforcement exit** uses the owner's centre and length when it has them (`EntosisWindow.ReinforcementExit` overload).
- **Fix to ST-9 c (attacker scoring).** A link active at a checkpoint used to score the whole time up to the next event, even when its module had stopped without an end report (the warm-up's link then credited hours). The ST-9 c text said the error was bounded by one cycle; the code did not. Scoring now stops at the end of the link's in-progress cycle (last settled cycle plus 300 s); the rest of the time regenerates (`EntosisRules.Advance`, `ActiveUntil`).
- **Gaps.** No V24.01 client RPC for an owner to set a hub window was found in eve-logic (its only vulnerability windows are the skyhook theft windows), so `SetHubWindowAsync` and `SetAllianceWindowAsync` are service APIs with no Host RPC. The client's view of a window is not verified (MITM open).
- **Tests.** `EntosisWindowTests` (7, pure: the custom window, the lock at the midpoint, no lock ahead of the midpoint, the change timing, the reinforcement exit, the bounded scoring), `EntosisCaptureServiceTests` (3 PostgreSQL: the change takes effect after the next window, a custom window refuses and stores nothing, a captured hub inherits the alliance's window) and the refusals of a neutral hub and an invalid setting.

## Command node stage after a reinforcement (ST-17 a, 2026-10-16)
- **Sources.** The wiki "Command nodes" (EVE University "Sovereignty", read 2026-10-16): the nodes spawn in the constellation once the reinforcement timer reaches 0; four at first ("more spawning as time goes on"); the bar starts at 60%; each captured node moves it 7%; nine attacker captures or six defender captures decide it; "Once a node is captured, a new node spawns"; the defenders' win leaves the hub invulnerable until the next vulnerability window; the attackers' win destroys the hub with its upgrades; "Any entosis link activated by a player not in the defending alliance is counted as attacking"; the ADM scales the attackers' capture time and not the defenders' regeneration. EVE.js `sovCampaignState.js` (4 initial nodes, 5 per system and 20 per constellation, a spawn every 30 minutes with a jitter of half, 240 s capture, 0.07 step, 0.6 start, `COMMAND_NODE_TYPE_ID` 34832) and `entosisLinkRuntime.js` (the node as the target of the same link).
- **The stage starts at the reinforcement's exit**: the job `space.entosis.campaign-start` is keyed by the hub and runs at `reinforcement_exit_ms` (the wiki's reinforce timer reaching 0). The hub's phase stays `reinforced` during the stage, so the hub itself refuses cycles (`SOVEREIGNTY_HUB_ALREADY_REINFORCED`).
- **The node** is SDE type 34832 (group 1316 "Entosis Command Node", radius 243 m, the SDE's `types.radius`). Its links are the `links` table's, keyed by the node id: the table's foreign key to the hubs is dropped (migration 0004). Its progress is signed, from -1 (the defenders captured it) to +1 (the attackers captured it): an attacker alone moves it up at 240 s times the ADM, a defender alone moves it down at 240 s (the wiki scales only the attackers), both together pause it, and without a link it regenerates toward 0 over 98 minutes.
- **The regeneration time is ours.** The wiki gives the 2015 maximum of 98 minutes and says no current figure is published; EVE.js's `COMMAND_NODE_DEFENDER_REGEN_RATE` (0.4) is a rate with no time in it, so it is not used as a time.
- **The tally is counted, not summed.** Score = 0.6 - 0.07 x attacker captures + 0.07 x defender captures; `CommandNodeRules.Outcome` decides from the counts (nine attacker captures, or six defender captures), so no rounding decides the stage.
- **Spawns.** The system of a spawn is the least-filled system of the constellation from the spawn ordinal (round robin, cap 5 per system and 20 per constellation). EVE.js chooses at random; the choice here is deterministic, and the spawn interval's jitter is drawn from the hub id and the time (`EntosisWindow.Draw`). The position of a node in its system is a ring 40 to 60 km from the origin, from the node id (shortcut: EVE.js places nodes around the sun; upgrade: the sun-relative spawn once the celestial data is read here).
- **Node ids.** `hub id x 1000 + spawn ordinal` (shortcut: a derived id, not the item store's; a stage spawns fewer than 1000 nodes; upgrade: allocate the ids from the item store).
- **Defended.** The hub returns to `idle` with its owner and `invulnerable_until_ms` set to the start of the next window (`EntosisWindow.NextWindowStart`, the wiki: "invulnerable until the next vulnerability window"). A cycle before that time is refused `SOVEREIGNTY_TARGET_NOT_VULNERABLE`.
- **Destroyed.** The hub's phase is `destroyed` (a new `EntosisPhase.Destroyed`, refused by name as `SOVEREIGNTY_HUB_DESTROYED`) and its handover is `destroyed`. **Gap:** the sovereignty records are not changed (there is no sovereignty destruction write path here), the hub's ball is not removed from space, and the anchoring of a new neutral hub (the wiki's next step) is not built.
- **Notices.** None written. V24.01 names no node-event type in the eve-logic paths searched; EVE.js has `SOV_COMMAND_NODE_EVENT_STARTED` (161) in its notification constants and does not send it from this path. Not verified; open.
- **Host.** The target check (`IsSovereigntyHubAsync`) accepts a live node of an active stage in the system (`IsCommandNodeAsync`). The events `space.entosis.node_spawned`, `node_captured` and `stage_ended` put the balls in space and take them out (`SpaceSovHubs.PlaceCommandNodeAsync`, `RemoveCommandNode`); a pilot entering a system gets its live nodes (`SpaceSovHubs.RestoreAsync`). The constellation is the static data's `mapSolarSystems`.
- **Not verified.** The placement of a ball in a system with no loaded scene (the event path places nodes in any system); the live Host with the real simulation; the client's display of the nodes and of the bar (MITM open).
- **Tests.** `EntosisCaptureServiceTests` (4, PostgreSQL: the stage starts at the exit with four nodes in the constellation and starts once; an attacker's capture moves the tally and a new node takes the place; nine attacker captures destroy the hub and end the stage; six defender captures defend the hub, which is then invulnerable until the next window), `EntosisCommandNodeTests` (5, pure: the outcomes, the attacker's and defender's progress, the pause, the regeneration, the completion time).


## Command node stage: destruction, notices and restart (ST-17 a, continued, 2026-10-16)
- **Sources.** EVE.js `sovCampaignState.js` `finishCampaign` and `sovState.js` `commitSovereigntyHubCampaignOutcome` (attacker result: the source system loses its alliance, corporation, claim structure, infrastructure hub, claim time, fuel access group and current data; the hub's structure row leaves the system; a recent-activity entry records the old owner); `sovCampaignNotifications.js` (`notifySovCommandNodeEventStarted`, `notifySovStructureDestroyed`) with the V24.01 types of eve-logic `notificationconst.py`: 161 SovCommandNodeEventStarted and 162 SovStructureDestroyed.
- **Destruction is a durable job.** The stage's end writes the hub `destroy-pending` in the capture's transaction and schedules `space.entosis.hub-destroyed-due`. The job writes the sovereignty records (`PersistenceSovFwWriteOperations.DestroyLostHubAsync`: the system's owner, corporation, claim, hub pointer, claim time and fuel group are cleared, the hub's structure row and record leave, verified in the persisted table), then marks the hub `destroyed` and publishes `space.entosis.hub_destroyed`. A failed write is tried again a minute later. A system whose hub or owner is not the destroyed one is answered as done.
- **Not ported (EVE.js has them):** the drop roll for the hub's upgrades and fuel (`sovHubLossDrops`), the recent-activity entry, and the replay tombstone for the receipts. The new neutral hub: EVE.js anchors a new hub after a loss (the player's anchor, not built here), so the system is left without a hub until an anchor exists.
- **Notices, through the mailbox.** The owner notifier (`EntosisOwnerNotifications`) writes 161 when a stage starts (constellation from the static data, source system, campaign event type 2) and 162 when the hub is destroyed (structure type 32458, system), to every character of the defending alliance, keyed by the event id so a redelivery writes nothing twice. A stage start without a constellation (the static data has none) writes no 161, as EVE.js's invalid notice does.
- **The hub ball** leaves space on `hub_destroyed` (`SpaceSovHubs.RemoveHub`); the nodes leave on the stage's end.
- **Restart safety.** Every stage event is one transaction (the node, the campaign, the links, the jobs and the events); the destruction and the notices are jobs. A crash between the stage's end and the write is answered by the job's retry.
- **Gaps.** No client view of 161/162 (MITM open). The stage's start for a pilot already in the system: the new nodes are placed on the events, and a system without a loaded scene is not verified in the live simulation.
- **Tests.** `EntosisCaptureServiceTests` (the job retried after a failed write, the destroyed event; the stage's start event), `SovHubClaimTests` (1 more: the loss writer, a repeat changes nothing), `EntosisOwnerNotificationTests` (1 more: 161 and 162 to the defending members, none to a foreign pilot, redelivery writes nothing twice).

## Sovereignty upgrade power budget: cynosural suppression derived (ST-17 c, 2026-10-16)
- **Rule.** EVE.js `sovModernState.js` `computeHubUpgradeSnapshot`: the installed upgrades in install order, each with its online switch; an online upgrade runs when the power and the workforce it needs fit what is left, and a running upgrade adds what it produces; otherwise LOW; an upgrade with its switch off is OFFLINE. `isCynoSuppressionUpgradeOnline` is `powerState` 2 of the Cynosural Suppression upgrade (`sovSuppressionState.js`). Port: `Eve.Services.Core.SovUpgradeBudget` (pure), read by `PersistenceSovFwReadModel.IsCynoSuppressionOnlineAsync`. The stored `online` flag is the owner's switch only; nothing stores the power state.
- **Supply (EVE.js `getHubLocalHarvest`).** Power is the star power: the EVE.js fallback `FALLBACK_STAR_POWER_MIN` (500), because no star configuration is in the repo (EVE.js keys the fallback by spectral class, which the repo does not read here). Workforce is the sum of the workforce of the system's active skyhooks (EVE.js `getSkyhookHarvestContribution`: an inactive skyhook gives nothing; `workforceAmount` is the skyhook's workforce, read from the persisted `skyhooks` rows). The planet power of an active skyhook (EVE.js `planetDefinition.power`) is not in the repo and counts as 0. Transfers of workforce between hubs (EVE.js `getHubTransferDelta`) are not ported: the source systems' harvests are not read here.
- **Numbers.** The Cynosural Suppression upgrade (SDE 81619) has no power or workforce attribute in SDE build 3396210 (only 1603 and 1615). The EVE University wiki's Sovereignty Hub table gives 250 power and 4 500 workforce, used here (the wiki before EVE.js). Every other installed upgrade uses EVE.js's placeholder by type id modulo 4 (`UPGRADE_FAKE_BY_REMAINDER`, the values EVE.js's runtime uses): 100/200, 10/20, 30/15, 400/300 (power/workforce), with no produced power or workforce. The placeholders are EVE.js's own, not CCP's, and are recorded as such.
- **Consequence (the conflict to decide).** With these inputs the cynosural suppression runs only in a system whose active skyhooks give at least 4 500 workforce (the wiki's number; 300 with EVE.js's placeholder). In a system without such a skyhook the upgrade is LOW and the cynosural jammer is off, so the jammer is off in most systems. This follows from EVE.js's rule and the wiki's numbers; it is not the "never off everywhere" outcome asked for. The alternative (a default supply that keeps it on) has no source in EVE.js: its star fallback gives power only. Decision needed from the coordinator: the workforce source for a system without skyhooks.
- **Tests.** `StructureCynoSuppressionTests` (the fixture gains an active skyhook of 5 000 workforce; new: no skyhook, an inactive or a destroyed skyhook gives LOW; the install order decides which upgrade gets the power), `StructureCompositionTests` (the registry answer with the skyhook; the jammer state and the beacon refusal).

## Ship-fitted burst projectors (ST-17 d, 2026-10-16)
- **Scope.** SDE group 842 "Burst Projectors" (category 7): 27678 (ECM), 40635 (web), 40636 (energy neutralization), 40696 (warp disruption), 40697 (sensor dampening), 40698 (target painting), 40699 (weapon disruption) use the structure burst engine. 41350 "Superweapon_AOEECM" stays unpublished and refused by type policy (MODULE_NOT_PUBLISHED).
- **Policy (the decided one: the existing area-effect policy).** Their pulses reach the ships in range (friends included, as a smartbomb's and an ECM burst's do); the pilot's own ship is never a candidate. The ECM family follows the ECM burst's rule: in high security space it does not start unless the pilot's safety is none. Every projector is an offensive area effect recorded with the crimewatch as a smartbomb is. The per-pulse grant of the Host stays: each pulse asks the Host (Kind 85/86) for the ships in reach, and the Host returns them.
- **Numbers.** The effect names and the range (dogma 2279), the duration and the capacitor come from the type's SDE dogma, as for the structure projectors (`ProjectedEffect.StructureBurstWhat`); no wiki page was used for them (the three burst projector pages returned 404 on 2026-10-16).
- **Sim.** The burst engine's source is the controlled structure, or else the pilot's own ship (`Scene.burst_source_id`, `burst_source_lease`); a ship's lease is 0. The request, the reply and the apply paths use the source for the burst operations only; the point defense and the strikes keep the structure path.
- **Host.** A request whose session is not a structure observer is the ship branch (`AuthorizeShipBurstCandidates`): the session must be the pilot's, the source the pilot's ship, the lease 0; the candidates (the ships the simulation found in reach, without the source) are granted.
- **Not verified.** The Host ship branch has no end-to-end test (the Rust side has one: `a_ship_fitted_burst_projector_asks_the_host_for_each_pulse_and_spares_its_own_ship`); the high-security refusal and the crimewatch recording of a ship projector are by code reading, not by a test; the client's FX for the projectors.

## Structure ST-18 (2026-10-16): customs inspection roll, standing loss, penalty job, fine ref type

Sources: EveJS `828823a75f211fdecb945b087a26c225f111491f` `services/security/contrabandInspectionRuntime.js` (`resolveCharacterSmugglingChance`, `getInspectionRoll`, `inspectCharacterContraband`), `services/security/contrabandPenaltyState.js` (`prepareContrabandPenalty`, `settleContrabandPenaltyInternal`, job type `contraband.penalty-settle`), `services/character/standingRuntime.js` (`calculateStandingsByRawChange`, `roundStandingValue`, `applyStandingChanges`); eve-logic `eve/common/lib/appConst.py` `refContrabandFine = 51`, `eve/common/script/mgt/appLogConst.py` `eventStandingContrabandTrafficking = 126`, `dogma/const.py` `attributeSmugglingChance = 445`; SDE `dogmaAttributes` 445 `smugglingChance` (default 0).

- **Inspection roll (ST-16 1).** The pilot's chance is the attribute 445 after its implant and skill modifiers (`SkillHandlerService.SmugglingChanceAsync`, the same augmentation world the skill reader builds), clamped to 0..1; with no implant it is 0, so every detected load is caught as before. A roll below the chance evades (EveJS: `evaded = roll < smugglingChance`): no fine, no standing loss, the cargo stays. The roll is a hash of (character, ship, system, the inspection's time) in [0, 1): the same inspection rolls the same number, which makes the tests deterministic. EveJS draws `Math.random()`. The undock warning (`InspectUndockContrabandAsync`) is unchanged: it shows for contraband in the hold; the roll decides the penalty.
- **Standing loss (ST-16 2).** The SDE `standingLoss` of each contraband rule is summed over the settled items and applied to the faction with EveJS `calculateStandingsByRawChange` (the change times (1 + standing / 10), toward -10, which stays at -10), rounded to thousandths; no social adjustment and no derived propagation (EveJS `applySocial: false`, `disableDerivedStandings`). It writes the existing `standing` relation of `RelationStore` (ST-16 said no standings store existed; the store and the mission rewards already write standings). The history entry carries the EveJS standing event (126, "Contraband trafficking", system, type, quantity), which `StandingMgrService.GetStandingTransactions` reads.
- **Durable penalty job (ST-16 3).** The inspection writes a `contraband.penalty-settle` job (key `contraband-penalty:{operation}`) in its own transaction and the job runs at once through `DurableScheduler.RunNowAsync` (the runner's claim and handler). The job's transaction holds the fine (wallet, idempotency key `contraband-penalty:{operation}:wallet`), the standing loss, the confiscation and the row in `contraband_penalties` (migration `ContrabandMigrations/0001`): they commit together or not at all, and the row makes a repeat a no-op. A waiting job survives a restart and any process with the handler (the Host registers it whether or not the simulation runs) settles it. Items that moved since the inspection are neither confiscated nor fined. EveJS settles in checkpointed steps (wallet, standing, items); this is one transaction.
- **Fine ref type (ST-16 4).** Journal ref type 51 (eve-logic `refContrabandFine`). EveJS names the admin adjustment (`GM_CASH_TRANSFER` = 3) and ST-16 wrote 0. The counterparty and the reference id are the faction (EveJS `ownerID2` and `referenceID`); ST-16 wrote 0 (burnt). The fine is a single-leg adjustment, so the faction's journal line records the counterparty and no faction wallet is credited.
- **Shortcut.** The inline run is the only path in which the pilot is told of the removal. If that run fails, the undock goes on, the cargo stays until the runner settles the job (retries with back-off), and the removal notice comes with the next inventory sync. Upgrade: refuse the undock or report the penalty as pending.
- **Not verified.** The attribute-445 reader is tested with an in-test implant effect, not a real SDE implant; the graphical client (standing window, journal line, removal notice) is not checked.

## Structure ST-19 (2026-10-16): mission pockets, control towers, the destruction wrap, the creditor migration

- **Mission pockets are deadspace anchors (closes the ST-16 open item).** A mission pocket is a dungeon instance whose origin is 20 km ahead of the pilot (`MissionComposition.StartMissionDungeonAsync`). `DungeonWaveDirector.ActiveOriginsAsync` reads the origins of the active `dungeon` instances of a system, and the observatory's deadspace check takes them with the ST-11 sites, at EveJS `DEADSPACE_SITE_RADIUS_METERS`. Every dungeon instance counts, including an incursion's staging site, as EveJS takes every dungeon instance as a pocket. Not done: the pocket is not a simulation body (the simulation has no deadspace concept, and the check is the host's).
- **Control towers launched from a ship (protocol 73, ST-19; closes the ST-16 open item 7).** A control tower (SDE group 365) is launched from the hold through `ship.LaunchFromShip` like a mobile deployable: the host record is `space.deployed` kind `control_tower` (migration 0015), the simulation ball is `PlaceControlTower` (kind 92, a disruptor-shaped placement with range 0), and the anchoring is the durable job `space.deployed.anchor` with `SetDisruptorActive`. Anchoring time: the SDE's `anchoringDelay` (attribute 556), which is 1 800 000 ms (30 minutes) for the Amarr Control Tower (12235). The request named the wiki's 10 minutes; the source order is SDE first, and the 10 minutes were not found on a wiki page in this package, so the SDE stands. Upgrade if the wiki page shows another value for the type.
- **Control tower lifetime and removal.** A tower never ends by time (`NeverExpires`). Its removal is `SpaceContainers.RemoveControlTowerAsync`: the simulation takes it out, the record is forgotten and the item is deleted. Shortcut: a deployed tower has no way back into the hold, so its item is lost on removal; no client RPC calls the removal (the client's tower interface was not traced). Fuel and modules are not modelled, so online means anchored.
- **Control towers and the Mobile Observatory.** The 100 km rule (`CloseToControlTowerAsync`) counts the structure records of group 365 (ST-16) and the `control_tower` records of the system. The radii are not subtracted (shortcut, as in ST-16).
- **The destruction wrap is tested over the real read model (ST-16 open item 5).** `StructureDestructionLootAuthority.WrapWith` is the composition's wiring: the late-bound `structureAssetSafetyReadModel` is read when the destruction runs. The Host test `Destruction_wrap_of_the_composition_is_the_real_asset_safety_read_model...` drives it with a real `PersistenceStructureAssetSafetyReadModel` and asserts `CorporationWrapCreated` and the wrapped corporation items.
- **Defect found and fixed: K-space was a wormhole in asset safety.** A region counted as a wormhole when its SDE `wormholeClassID` was positive. The SDE gives 7 (high-sec), 8 (low-sec) and 9 (null-sec) to the K-space regions (`WormholeSystemClasses`), so every K-space structure was refused asset safety (`AssetSafetyUnavailable`) and its contents looted. `IsWormholeClass` now excludes 7, 8 and 9; the asset-safety test rig uses the same rule.
- **Creditor migration (ST-16 open item 8).** A station rent bill taken before ST-16 has no creditor, so its `office.rent_due` job did nothing. The startup migration `StructureOfficeRentDue.CreditStationBillsAsync` gives every creditless bill of a station the station's owner (`stations.Get(id).OwnerId`, the creditor of the bills taken since), and schedules the due job of the open ones. A structure's bill always names its owner and is left alone. Idempotent: a second start credits nothing. Tests: `StationBillCreditorMigrationTests` (store level). Not tested: the due job itself run after the migration (the Host job is not run in that test).

## Structure ST-22 (2026-10-16): wire comparison of the login session

- **Session role bits of the test account (kept).** The login capture against EVE.js (tests/test_live.py, `mitm`, run on eve_p3) differs in `SessionInitialStateNotification.role`: EVE.js grants 21 role bits, eve.rs grants 3. The extra EVE.js bits are the client's administrative roles (`carbon/common/script/sys/serviceConst.py` in eve-logic: ROLE_ADMIN, ROLE_ROLEADMIN, ROLE_GMH, ROLE_GML, ROLE_QA, ROLE_PROGRAMMER, ROLE_SECURITY, ROLE_CONTENT, ROLE_VIPLOGIN and others). The eve.rs account gets ROLE_PLAYER, ROLE_LOGIN and ROLE_IGB. Source of the choice: eve-logic defines the bits and the client gates its administrative UI on them, but it does not say which roles an account receives; the EVE.js grant is a development convenience for its test account. eve.rs keeps the player roles: an ordinary test account must not hold administrative roles. Not changed on the wire. Evidence: `captures/evejs-login.jsonl` and `captures/eve-rs-login.jsonl` (ST-22, local).
