- **Session user id and dictionary order (volatile, not a divergence).** The `userid` value differs (EVE.js 3, eve.rs 1) because the test account is a different row on each server. The keys of the same dictionary come in a different order; the client reads the session dictionary by key, so the order carries no meaning. `mitm diff` still reports the order as the first divergence (ST-22 finding; see WIRE-COMPARE.md).

## Structure ST-23 (2026-10-16): control tower fuel and unanchoring

Sources: SDE build 3396210 (`typeDogma` of the Amarr Control Tower 12235: attribute 1031 size 3, 722 posControlTowerPeriod 3 600 000 ms, 676 unanchoringDelay 3 600 000 ms, 556 anchoringDelay 1 800 000 ms; `types.raceID`; `controlTowerResources`). EveJS `828823a75f211fdecb945b087a26c225f111491f` `services/ship/controlTowerRuntime.js` (`getControlTowerFuelRequirements`, `CONTROL_TOWER_FUEL_BLOCK_BY_RACE_ID`, `startControlTowerAnchorTransition`) and `services/planet/posMgrService.js` (`Handle_UnanchorStructure`). The wiki's control tower page was not read in this package: the fuel numbers below are the SDE attributes and the EveJS rule, not a wiki check.

- **Fuel per hour (built).** A tower burns `10 x 2^(size-1)` blocks of its race's fuel block each period (small 10, medium 20, large 40; the SDE size is attribute 1031, clamped 1 to 3). The race fuel block: Caldari Nitrogen 4051, Minmatar Hydrogen 4246, Amarr Helium 4247, Gallente Oxygen 4312 (EveJS table; the SDE raceID is a bit mask 1, 2, 4, 8). The blocks are the items at the tower's id (its fuel bay, EveJS `listTypeStacks(towerID)`); no flag filter. `CharacterShips.TakeTowerFuelAsync`, `ControlTowerFuelRules`.
- **Period and online state (built).** The period is the SDE's 722 (one hour). The first period is paid when the tower is anchored (the fuel job is scheduled at the anchoring time). A period the bay can pay (all or nothing, in the job's transaction) sets the tower `online` and schedules the next; a period it cannot pay sets it `offline` and is checked again one period later. Stored as `deployed.tower_state` (migration 0016).
- **Difference: no manual online.** EveJS needs the pilot to put a tower online (an online fuel charge, `consumeStarbaseOnlineFuel`); the host onlines it at each period that the bay can pay. Not built: the online command and its charge.
- **Difference: no client surfacing.** The simulation has no online state, so a tower is anchored in space whether or not it is fuelled. The client reads no tower state yet, the fuel bay changes send no `OnItemChange` to the owner, and the client's fuel-bay access to the tower's container is not verified (open).
- **Unanchoring (built).** `posMgr.UnanchorStructure(itemID)` (the client calls it with the tower's item id, as the retail client does) asks for the unanchoring of a tower that is anchored, offline, and in the pilot's system of the launching corporation (refused by name otherwise). The state becomes `unanchoring`; the job runs for the SDE's 676 (one hour) and burns no fuel meanwhile; asking again is accepted. EveJS refuses an online tower (`CONTROL_TOWER_MUST_BE_OFFLINE`); the same rule is kept, with the text "The control tower must be offline before it can be unanchored."
- **Difference: the item is kept (the removal rule).** The removal method that deleted the item (ST-19) is gone. At the end of the unanchoring the tower leaves the simulation and its record is forgotten, and its item is not deleted: it is marked with the unanchor-drop marker (the structure hull marker of the tower's id) and dropped at its place as a wreck, as the structure unanchor drop does (`PlaceUnanchorDropAsync`). The scoop path accepts that marker for a Starbase item (it accepted only Structure items). Not verified on the graphical client; the anchor-lift effect path (`effectAnchorLiftForStructures` through dogma) is not implemented.
- **Not built.** The anchoring charter (SDE `controlTowerResources` purpose 1, the faction charter in sovereign systems at or above 0.45 security): unchanged from ST-19 (the tower anchors without one). The reinforcement fuel (purpose 4, Strontium Clathrates), the tower's modules, and the tower's fuel-bay notifications.
- **Restart.** The fuel and unanchor jobs are durable (`space.control_tower.fuel_hour`, `space.control_tower.unanchor`, key `tower:{id}`); the record and state are in the database. Tests: `ControlTowerHostTests` (4, PostgreSQL), `PosMgrServiceTests` (2).
- **Invalid moon duration (ST-22 T09, kept).** EVE.js `Handle_StartNewExtraction` answers null for a duration that is not positive (no refusal); eve.rs refuses `MoonminingEventTooShort`. eve-logic's scheduling client raises that label itself before it calls the server (`eve/client/script/ui/moonmining/schedulingSvc.py`: `durationSeconds < MINIMUM_EXTRACTION_DURATION` raises `UserError('MoonminingEventTooShort')`), so the server's refusal with the same label is the client's contract; a null answer would look like a started extraction. The client never sends such a duration, so the case is reached only by a crafted call. Test: the duration refusal in `MoonExtractionStartHostTests` and `test_a_moon_extraction_for_a_duration_that_is_not_positive_is_refused_by_name`.
- **Standup drill (ST-22 T16, kept).** EVE.js `hasOnlineMoonDrillModule` requires a fitted, online Standup Moon Drill (type 45009) in a structure service slot (flags 164 to 171) besides the moon mining service being online. eve.rs gates the start on the service state only (`MoonExtractions.StartAsync`, `StructureMoonAuthority.GateAsync`), because eve.rs has no fitted-module model for structures yet (the structure service modules are ST-8a, open). The refusal label and the wire are the same; the difference is a service set online without a drill, which EVE.js refuses and eve.rs does not. Eve-logic has no server rule for it. The wiki's mechanic (a drill is needed for moon mining) agrees with EVE.js. Follow-up: ST-8a fitted service modules, then this entry closes.

## Structure ST-23 (2026-10-16): dungeon pockets, the return to the ship, the moon live check

Sources: EveJS `828823a75f211fdecb945b087a26c225f111491f` `services/dungeon/deadspaceWarpPolicy.js` (`DEADSPACE_SITE_RADIUS_METERS` 1e9 m; a dungeon instance is a pocket), `services/structure/moonExtractionsService.js` (fracture: `Handle_FractureChunkWithStructure`, `FRACTURE_TOTAL_DURATION_SECONDS` 30, the notices by type), `services/structure/structureControlState.js` (`ejectFromStructureControl`, `restoreShipID`). SDE 3396210: station 60015249 (radius of its ball is the simulation's, from the static data) and refinery type 35835 (radius 45 000 m, attribute 162).

- **Pockets in the scene (built, protocol 74).** The simulation has a pocket body, `PlaceDeadspacePocket` (kind 93): a disruptor's placement with no type, group, range or owner. It is put in the scene at the dungeon's origin with radius 1e9 m (EveJS). It is in no client ball and no scan, and it stops no field, as EveJS shows it through the rooms the client already has. The host keeps a record of kind `dungeon_pocket` (migration 0017, id = base + instance id) and a job (`space.deadspace.pockets`, every 10 s) places the pockets of the running dungeons and takes out the pockets of the dungeons that are not running. A pocket placed by a start is seen by the observatory check within ten seconds.
- **Difference: the warp rules of a pocket are not in the simulation.** EveJS restricts warps inside a pocket and redirects a warp into a gated pocket (`deadspaceWarpPolicy.js` rules A and B). The simulation does not apply them: the body is there, the warp rule is not (open). The observatory deadspace check (the EVE rule the host enforces) reads the pocket record of the same position and radius.
- **Difference: incursion sites are pockets too.** EveJS takes every dungeon instance as a pocket; the incursion staging sites are dungeon instances, so they are placed the same way.
- **The return to the own ship (built).** `EjectFromStructure(shipId)` measures the ship's surface distance to the structure (V24.01 maxBoardingDistance, 6 500 m). The simulation answers a position query for the pilot of a ship only; a boarded ship is no longer its pilot's session. So the place the ship has when its pilot boards from space is saved (`RecordBoardingPlaceAsync`, the ship's space item row), and the return measures that place when the simulation does not answer (`ScenePositionOfAsync`). The place of a ship that is not boarded is still the simulation's. Host-tested (two cases: within the distance, beyond it).
- **Live seed for the return (test data).** A refinery seeded 5 km from Manifest's station (the first seed) is inside the station's ball: the simulation pushes a ship out of it to 70 km from the station's centre, beyond the return distance. The return refinery is a second row 200 000 km from the station (`RETURN_STRUCTURE`); the docked refinery keeps its place. The pilot's saved place is 48 km from the return refinery (45 000 m ball, about 3 km of surface). The seed is the ship's saved place, written between a log off and a select, which the server restores on entry.
- **Live seed for the moon (test data).** The seed's moon ore shares were the types 34 (Tritanium) and 20 (Kernite): not moon ores. The fracture validates the ore types (`SDE type 20 is not a published moon ore type`). The seed now uses Zeolites 45490 and Cobaltite 45494 (groups 1884 and 1920), and the moon is Manifest's 40491234 (SDE mapMoons). The seeded moon 40000001 is not an SDE moon, so the field's position was not found. No server behaviour changed for the moon: the laser-fired notice (205), the fractured completion and the ore field are as EveJS writes them.
- **Live moon checks.** The owners' notices are written by the event bus after the fire, and the ore field after the completion (a durable delivery, retried), so the live test polls for them.

## Character phase S2 (2026-10-16): alpha injector, login milestone, achievements, population seat, pilot augmentations

Sources: EveJS `services/skills/trading/skillTradingAuthority.js` and `skillTradingRuntime.js` (Daily Alpha Injector: 50,000 SP, `resolveNextDowntimeFileTime`, 11:00 UTC), `services/character/milestoneMgrService.js` (login milestone), `services/character/achievementTrackerMgrService.js` and `services/achievement/achievementState.js` (achievement tracker), SDE 3396210 (`typeDogma` of type 46375: `containedSkillPoints` 50000; type 9941: `memoryBonus` 3). The EVE University wiki "Skill Injector" page gives the injector amounts and the alpha limits but not the daily timing; the timing comes from EveJS.

- **Difference: the Daily Alpha Injector is spent until the next 11:00 UTC downtime, not for 24 hours.** The eve.rs rule before this change was a rolling 24-hour window (`TryUseDailyAlphaSkillInjectorAsync`). It is now the EveJS rule (one use per downtime; a use before the last downtime is spent). `alphaInjectorMgr.GetNextAvailableInjection` answers the filetime of the first downtime after the last use, and 0 for a character that never used one. EveJS answers the stored filetime; the client shows `max(0, next - now)`, so a past filetime and 0 read the same. Test: `Daily_alpha_injector_is_spent_until_the_next_11_utc_downtime`, `Daily_alpha_clock_answers_zero_then_the_next_downtime_after_a_use_and_keeps_it_across_a_restart`; live `tools/headless-client/tests/test_alpha_injector_live.py`.
- **Difference: the Core stub of `alphaInjectorMgr` is replaced.** `Eve.Services.Core` answered 0 for every character. The service is now `Eve.Services.Skills/AlphaInjectorMgrService.cs`, registered in `CharacterRpc`.
- **Login milestone (CH-5), source: EveJS only.** The wiki has no page for it. The milestone is per account: 2 hours of online time, then the character is told (`OnLoginMilestoneAchieved`, milestone 1, 500,000 ISK). `ClaimRewards` pays the character once (the wallet entry has reference type 0 and the operation key `login-milestone:<account>:1`; EveJS records no reference type, and the eve.rs reference type catalogue has no milestone entry). Progress is written when the character leaves (EveJS `handleSessionDisconnected`); the timer is in memory, so a host restart without a leave loses the time since the last write. Test: `The_reward_is_paid_once_after_the_milestone_is_reached_and_the_claim_survives_a_restart` (store), `A_reached_milestone_is_paid_once_and_the_claim_survives_a_restart` (service); live `tools/headless-client/tests/test_login_milestone_live.py`.
- **Difference: a character with no achievement record gets empty maps, not the golden fixture (CH-9).** EveJS (`achievementTrackerMgrService.js`) answers `GOLDEN_LOGIN_EVENT_COUNT_ENTRIES` and `GOLDEN_LOGIN_COMPLETED_ACHIEVEMENT_ENTRIES` when the state has no tracker data. eve.rs answers empty maps and stores what `UpdateClientAchievmentsAndCounters` sends (both maps replaced whole, as EveJS `updateLegacyTracker` does). Tables `achievement_trackers`, `achievement_completions`, `achievement_event_counts` (migration 0009; 0006 and 0007 are other packages' migrations). Test: `An_empty_write_is_a_record_with_no_entries`, `An_update_hands_both_maps_to_the_store_and_a_character_without_a_record_reads_empty_maps`; live `tools/headless-client/tests/test_achievement_tracker_live.py`.
- **Population cap: check and seat in one step (CH-7).** The reference's cap is not published (see DEVIATIONS "Character selection and entry"); the cap of 300 is unchanged. Entries into the last room are serialised by the population's lock: a seat is taken with the room check and given back when the entry ends, so two entries into the last room admit one. A refused entry leaves the count as it was. Test: `Simultaneous_entries_into_the_last_room_admit_exactly_one`, `A_seat_is_taken_in_the_same_step_as_the_room_check_and_is_given_back_when_disposed`; live `tools/headless-client/tests/test_population_cap_live.py` (run with `EVE_SYSTEM_POPULATION_CAP=1`).
- **Pilot augmentations in the pilot's dogma world (CH-11, partial).** An implant (flag 89, category 20 with an implant slot) located in the pilot and an active booster (flag 88 with a live booster row) act on the pilot in `ShipSnapshot.LoadAsync`, as EveJS `activeImplantModifiers.js` does for the pilot's attributes. The modifier is the SDE's (`memoryBonus` 3 on memory 166 through effect 304/306, domain `charID`). Not done: the learning attributes that set the training rate still read the base values (`TrainingSettings.Attribute`), so the implant does not yet change the training rate; and an implant's change sends no notification yet (the path that installs an implant is the clone's, CH-15 and CH-16). Test: `An_implant_plugged_into_the_pilot_changes_the_attribute_dogma_reads_until_it_is_removed`, `An_item_in_the_implant_slot_that_is_not_an_implant_does_not_act_on_the_pilot`.

## Character phase: votes, reports and playtime (CH-10)

- **Decision GATE-C (owner): votes and reports answer `true` and keep nothing.** `userSvc.CastUpvote`, `CastDownvote`, `ReportBot` and `ReportISKSpammer` answer `true` as the reference does (EveJS `services/user/userService.js` 1104-1127). Before this, eve.rs answered `false`. The V24.01 client does not read the answer, so the change is not visible to the client; no feedback or report store is kept, and none is planned in this phase.
- **Playtime answers 0.** `userSvc.GetSessionPlaytimeMinutes` answers 0, as the reference does (EveJS `userService.js` 1139). No V24.01 client caller was found in `eve/client` (eve-logic search), and no stored playtime source exists in eve.rs, so there is nothing to read.
- **Season and challenge reads (CH-4, CH-19), source: EveJS only, and EveJS is a stub here.** No active season: `seasonManager.get_season_data_for_character` and `select_season` answer None, `get_season_end_time` answers the current filetime (EveJS). The challenge reads answer EveJS's no-challenge values. EveJS `process_client_event` sends a notification for a placeholder challenge id (192491367) that no data defines; eve.rs does not send it. Challenge progress, rewards and season definitions are not implemented: no source in the SDE (only `sde.characterTitles`), EveJS or the wiki (no season or challenge system page). Tests: `ProjectDiscoveryTutorialTests`, `ProjectDiscoveryHostTests`, live `test_project_discovery_live.py`.
- **Project Discovery tutorial (CH-4).** The state is stored per character (`project_discovery_tutorials`), default not finished at level 0 (EveJS `projectDiscoveryService.js`). The writers are not implemented in this package.
- **Player bounties (CH-13), source: EVE University wiki, then EveJS.** The wiki page named "Bounties" does not exist (404). The wiki's "Bounty Hunting System" page (marked deprecated, the only bounty mechanics page) gives the minimums that EveJS also uses: 1,000,000 ISK for a pilot, 20,000,000 for a corporation, 100,000,000 for an alliance; placements from anywhere, not on NPC corporations. The placement debit uses journal type 16 (EveJS `JOURNAL_ENTRY_TYPE.BOUNTY`). Owner kind and affiliation are stored when the pool is first written (EveJS resolves them at read time): a shortcut until a corporation or alliance change must move a pool. A placement retried by the same call is recorded and debited once, under the call id. Placement notifications (types 112-114) are not sent yet (not in CH-13's acceptance).
- **Player bounty payouts (CH-18), source: wiki "Bounty Hunting System", then EveJS (`playerBountyState.js`, `killmailTracker.js`).** The payout is a fifth of the loss (ship base price and what it held), capped by the pools that are drained in order: pilot, corporation, alliance. The payout is due at once (EveJS schedules it for now, with no delay). The wiki says a whole fleet shares a claim when its final blow is dealt by one of its members; EveJS and eve.rs pay the final-blow pilot only (fleet sharing is not implemented). Pod destruction is a separate kill in the wiki; eve.rs claims ship losses only. The credit uses journal type 17 (EveJS `JOURNAL_ENTRY_TYPE.NPC_BOUNTY_PRIZE`). Stats: hunter, corporation and alliance each get the claimed amount and one kill (EveJS `recordHunterPayout`).
- **NPC kill standing (CH-14), source: EveJS only.** The wiki's "Faction standing" page has no text. The rule is EveJS's `factionKillStandingRuntime.js` (on by default, `factionStandingLossOnKillEnabled`): a faction NPC's kill lowers the killer's standing with that faction only, by the fixed raw change -0.00006875 that EveJS calibrates from its golden mission logs (CCP's per-entity table is not public), with the distance-to-floor formula of the standing runtime, debounced per pilot, faction and system for ten minutes (in memory). Test: `NpcFactionStandingTests`. The client notification of the change is not sent yet.
- **Re-sculpt (CH-8, GATE-B owner decision: wiki and SDE as written).** The wiki has no re-sculpt page. The SDE describes the two certificates: 34132 "a single resculpt of your pilot's facial features, body shape, and gender" and 89956 "a single resculpt of your pilot's facial features and body shape" (no gender). Both are consumed at activation ("consumed immediately"). The state each sets follows the difference the SDE text names: 34132 sets 3 (full recustomizing), 89956 sets 1 (resculpting), and the next save of the appearance ends the state (the reference clears it after `UpdateExistingCharacterFull`/`Limited`). EveJS `userService.js` answers `true` and changes nothing (its stub is not followed). The state mapping to the two certificates is this package's reading of the SDE text, not an EveJS rule.


## Character phase S4 (2026-10-16): implants, boosters, expert systems

- **Implant model (CH-11).** An implant is an item at the pilot's location under the implant flag (89); `GetImplants` and the dogma world read it there (`Eve.Ships/PilotAugmentations.cs`). EveJS keeps the implant in the character record (`record.implants`, `services/dogma/implants/implantRuntime.js`) and removes the item. The client sees the same implant list. Plug-in takes the item from the station hangar or the active ship's cargo (EVE University wiki, Implants: "right-click on the implant and choose Plug In"; EveJS `isItemInPilotLocation`, the same places). Unplug destroys the implant (wiki: "If you remove the implant, it is destroyed"; EveJS `destroyImplantItem`).
- **Skill requirement of an implant (CH-11).** The implant's skills come from the SDE type (`typeDogma` 182/277, for Memory Augmentation - Basic (9941) Cybernetics I). The wiki says any implant needs Cybernetics I, which agrees. A missing skill is refused with the EveJS text.
- **Omega requirement (CH-11, not checked).** The wiki says "certain implants require the use of an Omega clone in order to inject" without naming them. Neither EveJS nor eve.rs checks it. Not implemented.
- **Learning bonus of the training rate (CH-11).** The rate uses each learning attribute as the base (remap, `PgSkillStore.LearningAttribute`) plus what implants and active boosters add through dogma (`PilotLearningBonus`, dogma modifiers with a base of zero). EveJS `applyActiveImplantAttributeBonuses` adds the ModAdd bonuses of the active implants to the same base (`services/dogma/implants/activeImplantModifiers.js`); the learning effects in the SDE (302 to 310) are all ModAdd (operation 2). The rate formula is the wiki's: SP per minute = primary + secondary / 2 (Omega), halved for Alpha (EVE University wiki, Skill training).
- **Two types named Standard Cerebral Accelerator (CH-11).** The SDE has type 49539 (booster slot 1087 = 10, +10 on each learning attribute, boosterDuration 330 = one day), which is the wiki's slot 10 (EVE University wiki, Medical boosters: "They all share the booster slot 10"). It also has type 2838 (slot 4, +5 on each learning attribute, no boosterDuration in the SDE). eve.rs refuses 2838 as before ("This booster has no valid SDE duration", the existing rule of the booster path); 49539 is the one the live walk uses. Not checked: whether 2838 is usable in the client.
- **Cerebral accelerators and a clone jump (for CH-16).** The wiki says cerebral accelerators stay active when the pilot clone jumps or is podded; normal boosters and implants do not (EVE University wiki, Medical boosters and Implants). Recorded here so CH-16 keeps them.
- **Rebase at a change (CH-11).** A running skill keeps the points it had at the old rate, and the new attributes apply from the change on (`CharacterTraining.RebaseRunningAsync`, the same rule as the neural remap in `RespecAsync`).
- **Booster expiry (CH-11, known gap).** The rate of a running skill changes at the next read after a booster lapses, not at the instant it lapses. The points before the next read use the rate after the lapse. Not corrected.
- **Notification order (CH-11).** Plug-in: `OnItemChange` (the implant row, with the place and the flag it left), `OnServerImplantsChanged`, `OnJumpCloneCacheInvalidated`, `OnModuleAttributeChanges` (the character's attributes) and `OnServerBrainUpdated`, then the queue (EveJS `implantsAndBoosters.js` `Handle_InjectImplant`, `syncCharacterDogmaState` before the queue sync). Unplug: the same without the item row. Consume: `OnItemChange`, `OnServerBoostersChanged`, then the attributes and brain, then the queue. Tests: `test_implant_live.py` (live), `CharacterTrainingLearningTests` and `PilotLearningBonusTests` (`PilotLearningBonus` refresh and removal).
- **Expert system catalogue (CH-17).** The pinned SDE has no expert system table (`sde/lock.json`). The catalogue is EveJS's `tools/DatabaseCreator/staticTables/expertSystems/data.json` (the client's `expertsystems.jsonl`, build 3365090), embedded as `dotnet/src/Eve.Skills/Data/expert-systems.v1.json` by `tools/dev/gen-expert-system-catalog.py`. Its limits (three installations, the 30-day top-up window) are EveJS's constants.
- **Expert system levels (CH-17).** A granted level shows above the trained level in `GetSkills` and in the pilot's dogma; the points stay the trained ones, and the training runs from the trained level (EveJS `services/skills/expertSystems/expertSystemProjection.js`). The fitting skill checks do not read the granted level (not checked).
- **Expert system expiry (CH-17, timing).** A recurring job of the durable scheduler runs every 10 seconds and removes what has lapsed. EveJS schedules one job per character at the expiry time (`expertSystemExpiryScheduler.js`). The lapse is therefore told up to 10 seconds late.
- **Expert system expiry (CH-17, offline).** EveJS also writes a persistent notification of type 253 (ExpertSystemExpired) for a character that is offline. eve.rs has no notification store for this, so an offline lapse is not announced at the next login. Not implemented.
- **Expert system notices (CH-17).** `OnExpertSystemsUpdated(list, added, typeID)`, the skill changes, then the ship's attributes and brain (`ExpertGrantsChangedAsync`), and on a lapse `OnExpertSystemExpired(typeID)` (EveJS `expertSystemNotifications.js`). A used item is told as `OnItemChange`, with the location 6 when the item is used up (EveJS `syncInventoryChangesToSession`). Tests: `ExpertSystemTests` and `ExpertSystemStoreTests` (Eve.Skills.Tests), `test_expert_system_live.py` (live).
## Character phase S5 (2026-10-16): deletion purge, multi-character training, skill bundles

- **Multiple Character Training: a queue is paused when its slot ends.** The wiki ("Multiple Character Training"): "Once the MCT queue runs out, the skill training for the character with the lowest number of skill points will be paused." EveJS (`services/skills/training/skillQueueRuntime.js`, `getAvailableTrainingSlotCount`) counts the live slots when a queue is saved and does not pause a running queue when its slot ends. eve.rs follows the wiki: a durable job at the slot's end pauses the queues beyond the remaining slots, lowest skill points first, and tells the account. The wiki is the chosen source; the EveJS difference is recorded here.
- **A stack of certificates.** The wiki does not say how a stack is used ("the page does not say whether a stack is split"). eve.rs consumes one certificate per activation and leaves the rest of the stack in place.
- **Character deletion purge.** The set follows EveJS `purgePrivateCharacterState` (`services/character/characterDeletionRuntime.js`). Not purged in S5: the GameStore plan, queue and skill-trading rows (`skillPlans`, `skillQueues`, `skillTradingState`): eve.rs keeps the skill queue in PostgreSQL and no eve.rs code reads the plan tables (CH-21 owns plans); and the portrait image files, which were not checked.
- **GetSkillBundleInfo (superseded in S6: answered with the client's empty shape, see the S6 section).** The client (eve-logic `eve/client/script/ui/crate/lootItem.py`, `skillsvc.py` 1093) reads a name label and an icon for a skill bundle id. No source in the allowed set holds them: the SDE has no skill bundle type or table, EveJS keeps only the bundle contents in the crate's custom info, and the EVE University wiki has no skill bundle page. The owner decides the source.
- **Clone installation copies no implants.** The plan asked for an implant copy at installation. Both sources say no: the wiki "Jump clone" page says implants stay with the old body on a jump and gives no copy at installation, and EveJS `installCloneAtCurrentLocation` builds the clone with `implants: []`. eve.rs installs a clean clone.

## Character phase S6 (2026-10-16): skill bundle info, clone installs in structures and ships

- **GetSkillBundleInfo answers the client's empty shape (S6).** The client unpacks `name, icon` for the crate loot line (eve-logic `eve/client/script/ui/crate/lootItem.py` 87) and shows the label through `GetByLabel`. No source holds a bundle name or icon: the SDE has no skill bundle table, EveJS keeps only the contents in the crate's custom info and has no `GetSkillBundleInfo`, the EVE University wiki has no skill bundle page, and eve-logic has only the callers. The answer is an empty label and no icon (the client falls back to its default icon). The answer does not check the id, since no table of bundles exists. Unverified: how the client's `GetByLabel('')` renders an empty label; the owner decides the name and icon source. Test: `CharacterHostTests.Skill_bundle_info_answers_the_client_empty_shape_through_the_registry`, live `test_skill_bundle_info_live.py`.
- **A structure's clone bay (S6).** The rule is EveJS `characterHasStructureCloneService` (`services/station/jumpCloneRuntime.js` 1446): the Medical service online, or the Jump Clone service online and the character can dock (`StructureDockingAllowedAsync`). Shortcut: the Medical service's own access setting is not read (EveJS `characterHasStructureService`). The fee is EveJS `getCloneServiceCostForLocation`: the profile setting CLONINGBAY_TAX (id 23, `structureServiceAuthority.js` 15), clamped to 0..100,000,000; a structure with no profile (profile id 0) takes the 900,000 default, where EveJS reads the owner's default profile. The fee is credited to the owner corporation's master wallet with the journal reference 55 (EveJS credits the owner through its own durable job; here one transaction).
- **A ship's clone offer (S6).** EveJS `offerShipCloneInstallation`, `acceptShipCloneInstallation`, `cancelShipCloneInstallation` (`jumpCloneRuntime.js` 2586-2717). Offers live in memory, as in EveJS (a restart clears them). Shortcut: a fitted Clone Vat Bay I counts whether it is online or not (EveJS `isEffectivelyOnlineModule`; the fittings keep no online state outside a space scene). Shortcut: the range is the centre-to-centre distance; EveJS takes the two hulls' radii off first (`calculateSurfaceDistanceMeters`). The fee of a ship install has the offering pilot as the journal counterparty and credits no wallet (EveJS: no owner corporation, so no credit). Refusals: InviteClone1 (no bay), InviteClone2 (out of range or no system), Clone bay is full, JumpCloneSkillReqNotMet, NotEnoughMoney. Notifications: Offered to the target; Done to the target, then the offering pilot, then the clone caches; Canceled to the caller, then the target. Test: `JumpCloneShipInstallTests` (Services.Core, with fakes for the store and the space side), live refusal `test_a_ship_without_a_clone_vat_bay_is_refused_a_clone_offer_with_the_client_label`.
- **Settlements (S6).** The reference keeps a durable pending settlement with compensation jobs (`jumpCloneInstallSettlements`, `prepareCloneInstall`, `settlePendingCloneInstallInternal`). eve.rs performs the debit and the credit and writes the settlement row (`character_jump_clone_settlements`, migration 0012) in one PostgreSQL transaction with the clone row, so an install is either complete or absent; no pending state exists to compensate.
- **Bounty placement notifications (S6, CH-13).** EveJS `services/bounty/bountyNotifications.js`: BountyPlacedChar 112 to the target (not to the placer), BountyPlacedCorp 113 to each member of the corporation, BountyPlacedAlliance 114 to each member of each alliance corporation; sender is the placer, group BOUNTIES (10), data `bountyPlacerID` and `bounty`. eve.rs sends them after the placement commits, keyed per placement and recipient so a retry sends once (EveJS sends them inside the placement call with no key).
- **NPC kill standing notification (S6, CH-14).** EveJS `standingRuntime.notifyStandingSet` sends `OnStandingSet(faction, pilot, standing)` to the pilot's session after the standing is written; the client's `standingsvc.py` 48-55 turns it into `OnNPCStandingChange` when the pilot holds a war faction. eve.rs sends the same to the killer after `NpcFactionStanding.ApplyAsync` writes the new standing. Untested by a Host or live test so far.

## Character phase S7 (clone jump, lapses)

- **Clone jump implants (CH-16).** The body that jumps keeps all its implants in a clone at the origin (wiki, Jump clone: implants stay with the old body). EveJS `followsJumpClones` has no SDE attribute in this data, so no implant is exempt. Test: `CharacterHostTests.Clone_jump_moves_the_body...`.
- **Clone jump boosters (CH-16, not done).** The wiki says normal boosters end on a jump; eve.rs does not end them yet (the jump only moves the body and its implants). Cerebral accelerators stay active (DEVIATIONS S4).
- **Clone jump destinations (CH-16).** Only station clones are jumped to: structure and ship clones are not installed in this data path (CH-15 open).
- **Cooldown.** 24 h less the Infomorph Synchronizing level (skill 33399), checked when the destination is not the current place (EveJS `jumpCloneRuntime.js` 1809-1850).
- **Activation fee.** 900,000 ISK, wallet reference 128, no counterparty (the same shortcut as the install, which the S6 settlement records).
- **Death respawn (CH-16).** Unchanged: the home station. A jump does not change the home station (EveJS sets `cloneStationID` only on a home station change). Not checked with a live kill.
- **Expert system lapse notice (S4).** A lapse writes the persistent notice (type 253, misc group, sender 0) whether or not the pilot is online (EveJS `notifyExpertSystemExpired`). The mailbox now accepts sender 0 for system notices.
- **Booster lapse (S4).** A durable job per activated booster (`skills.boosterLapse`) ends the booster at its expiry (EveJS `boosterRuntime.js` times it only for online pilots; the durable job also covers offline pilots).
- **Fleet sharing of a bounty claim (S6, CH-18).** The EVE University wiki ("Bounty Hunting System"): "If, however, that hunter is in a fleet, the entire fleet shares the bounty." The wiki does not say how the bounty is divided, nor whether the members must be near the kill. EveJS has no fleet sharing (`playerBountyState.claimBountyPayout` pays the one hunter). eve.rs splits the payout equally across all members of the killer's fleet (`FleetStore.SnapshotAsync`), the hunter keeping the rounding, each share with its own payout and job. The equal split and the absence of a proximity rule are eve.rs choices, not the wiki's.
- **Pod kills not implemented (S6, CH-18).** The wiki: "destroying the target's ship and pod as 2 separate events, each granting a bounty reward", and "For pod destruction, the target's implant value is also included in the value destroyed." EveJS claims only on a ship destruction (`killmailTracker.isCapsuleerPilotedShipVictim`). eve.rs has no pod-destroyed event with a killer; open.



## Character phase S8 (2026-10-16): clone jump boosters, restart and fitting tests

- **Normal boosters end on a clone jump (CH-16, A5).** Wiki (EVE University, "Medical boosters" and "Jump clones"): a jump takes the cerebral accelerators with the pilot, not the normal boosters, which the wiki says must be waited out ("except by jumping out of the clone and back into it"). The jump transaction deletes their rows and items (`SkillBoosterLapse.EndNormalBoostersAsync`, `HostJumpCloneStore.JumpAsync`); the running skill is rebased and an online pilot is told `OnServerBoostersChanged` after the commit. EveJS `services/skills/boosters/boosterRuntime.js` has no jump rule (read in full for jump and clone terms); the wiki decides. Pod death does not end boosters yet (open, TODO S8).
- **Cerebral accelerator = booster slot 10 (attribute 1087 = 10).** Wiki: all cerebral accelerators share booster slot 10. The SDE group does not separate them (normal boosters such as type 10151 are in the same group 303). Some SDE types named "Cerebral Accelerator" carry slot 4 (e.g. 2838, 33087, 33111, 50091); by the slot rule they are normal boosters. Open: check these types in the client data.
- **Death respawn station.** Wiki "Jump clones": a pod destruction wakes the pilot in a fresh clone at its home station. This is the clone station used by CH-12 and CH-16 (unchanged in S8).

- **Public gateway transport (CH-20, owner decision GATE-A: build it).** Client: eve-logic `publicGateway/grpc/connectionConfig.py` connects a native gRPC module to `<tier>-public-gateway.evetech.net` over TLS, or to `EVE_PUBLIC_GATEWAY_ADDRESS` in clear text. eve.rs serves the clear-text HTTP/2 (h2c) listener on `EVE_PUBLIC_GATEWAY` (Kestrel, the shared ASP.NET Core framework; the routes and envelopes of EveJS `publicGatewayLocal.js`). TLS is not built: the production address needs a certificate the owner has not provided.
- **Events/Publish is acknowledged and not stored; Notices/Consume answers UNIMPLEMENTED.** EveJS acknowledges events too and keeps none. EveJS holds a Notices stream open; eve.rs has no public notices yet (no producer), so Consume is refused rather than held open. Open.
- **Identity of a gateway request.** The active character comes from `authoritative_context.identity` without a session or JWT check. EveJS does the same (`getActiveCharacterID`). The listener is for the local client; a shared deployment needs the user token checked first. Open.
- **Skill plan namespace (CH-21).** Limits and refusals are EveJS `skillPlanState.js`: 10 plans, 150 skill entries, 5 milestones, names 50 and descriptions 1000 characters, levels 0 to 5; a missing plan is 404, a refused write 400 (the client's SkillPlansErrorBadRequest, requestsMessenger.py). Plans do not train skills. `GetSharedRequest` answers an empty message: no plan is shared (EveJS `findSharedPersonalPlan` finds none). Not done: EveJS `canonicalizeSkillRequirements` (it adds the prerequisite skills of each requirement); eve.rs stores the requirements as sent. Open.

- **Character titles (CH-22).** The title definitions are the SDE's (`characterTitles`, 43 keys, the ids EveJS `titles.json` also has). An owned title is granted by a reward (EveJS `grantTitle` through the achievement reward claim); eve.rs has no reward claim through the gateway yet, so the store is granted by the reward path when it lands (`CharacterTitleStore.GrantAsync`). Equipping needs an owned SDE title; otherwise 409 (EveJS `equipTitle`, the reference's status). Open: the definition CDN checkpoint (`definition.api.GetLatestRequest`, EveJS serves `latest.chti` from its data, which is not committed here), the title notices (`GrantedNotice`, `RevokedNotice`, `SetNotice`, which need the public notices channel), and the showcase namespace (`eve_public.achievement.showcase.api`, not built).

## Character phase S9 (2026-10-17): pod death session, gateway identity, notices, canonical plans

- **The gateway's identity is the login token's account (CH-20 open item, S9).** A Requests or Notices request counts a character only when the account of its bearer token owns it: no token is 401, another account's character is 403 (`GatewayAuthority`). The token is the `authorization` metadata: the login JWT whose subject is `USER:EVE:<account>` (eve-logic `publicGateway/publicGatewaySvc.py` gives the gateway the user's JWT through `monolithconfig.get_user_jwt` and `set_auth_token`; `eve/common/lib/jwt/__init__.py` reads the same `USER:EVE:<id>` subject in the local dev token). The native gRPC module that attaches the token is not in this repository, so the metadata key is the gRPC standard. Difference from EveJS (`publicGatewayLocal.js`, `observeRequestCharacterIdentity`), which trusts the identity without a token. Open: the token's signature is not checked. The identity service's signing key is not in this repository, and every decode in the V24.01 client uses `verify=False`; a deployment with the key needs the check first.
- **Transport (CH-20 TLS, S9).** The client connects in clear text to `EVE_PUBLIC_GATEWAY_ADDRESS` (eve-logic `publicGateway/grpc/connectionConfig.py`: `connection.connect(address)` without a root file) and over TLS with the certifi roots to the discovered `<tier>-public-gateway.evetech.net`. eve.rs serves the clear-text HTTP/2 listener on `EVE_PUBLIC_GATEWAY` and does not terminate TLS. A discovered production address needs a TLS-terminating proxy in front of that listener; none is in this repository.
- **Notices/Consume (CH-20, S9).** Served. The stream stays open after the request body ends; each request frame names the active character, which counts only when the token's account owns it. Notices are routed by character and by account (EveJS `shouldDeliverNoticeToState`). The solar system, corporation, alliance and application-instance target groups are not published by eve.rs yet. The response headers are flushed when the stream starts (Kestrel otherwise holds them until a body is written).
- **Title notices (CH-22, S9).** `SetNotice` is published to the character's notice streams on equip and on unequip (EveJS `achievementGatewayService.js`, the `title-set` event). Not done: `GrantedNotice` and `RevokedNotice` (EveJS publishes them on the `title-granted` and `title-revoked` events, which a reward claim raises; eve.rs has no reward claim through the gateway yet).
- **Skill plan canonical form (CH-21, S9).** `SkillPlanCanonical` is EveJS `skillPlanState.js` `canonicalizeSkillRequirements`: each skill once at its highest level (1 to 5), the published prerequisites absorbed at the level they need, and every level of each skill listed after the skills it needs. It runs on create and on the requirement update (EveJS `normalizePlanRecord` serves both). A plan therefore lists every level of each skill, one entry each.
- **Pod death (CH-16, S9).** The survivor and the wreck enter the simulation after the session change, the cosmetics and the skin, and the wreck's item change comes before the wreck's ball, as in the reference recording (`oracle/fixtures/scenarios/death.json`). See rows 50 and 51.
